# Parity debt

> **Status 2026-11-10.** The sections below were written when no environment could run Rails or the replays, and are kept as the
> history of what was unproven. They have since been closed against a real stack (setup in [parity-local-stack.md](parity-local-stack.md)):
> every tagged example has a fixture (`node scripts/parity-debt.ts` reports 0 without one) and all 59 replay files pass (2,758 tests).
> What is open now is in [parity-tasks.md](parity-tasks.md) and the "Open items" of [workstreams.md](workstreams.md). The counts
> table is current; sections 1 to 4 describe the state at `b8fbe3067` unless a line says otherwise.

## Counts

| | Count |
| --- | ---: |
| Spec files containing `parity: true` | 104 |
| Examples tagged `parity: true` | 1,987 |
| Of those, with a fixture | 1,987 |
| Of those, without a fixture | 0 |
| Fixture files (incl. `PARITY_RECORD=all` controller specs) | 2,801 |
| Replay test files | 52 (60 test files in the parity run) |
| Replay result | 60 files, 2,802 tests passed, 12 skipped, 0 failed |

## Tooling

```bash
cd nextjs
node scripts/parity-debt.ts                 # tagged examples without a fixture, per spec file
node scripts/parity-debt.ts --spec url_redirect   # one spec (substring of the path)
node scripts/parity-debt.ts --json          # same, machine-readable
```

`scripts/parity-debt.ts` parses the specs without Ruby (describe/context nesting from indentation) and matches
each example's full description against the `example` field of the recorded fixtures of the same spec. It
reports `unresolved` for descriptions it cannot resolve (interpolation with no matching fixture, or examples
inside `shared_examples`); a `describe provider do` over a loop variable counts as an interpolation, which is what left 15 social-connect examples unresolved while their 45 fixtures existed (all 15 match now); the run at this branch has 0 unresolved. It should print `0 without a fixture` when
section 1 is done. Interpolated descriptions (`#{role}`, `#{path}`) are matched as patterns, so a loop that
records only some of its iterations would not be flagged: could not determine whether any does.

## 1. Tagged examples without a fixture

Record each spec file with `PARITY_RECORD=1 bin/rspec <spec>` from the repo root (needs MySQL, Redis and, for
Discover, Elasticsearch, per `bin/rspec`'s usual setup). The examples have never run, so expect some spec
failures: fix the spec or the factory setup, never the fixture. Then replay with the command in the last column
from `nextjs/`, with `PARITY_DATABASE_URL` set. Replay file shorthand: `R = npx vitest run --config parity/vitest.config.ts parity/__tests__/`.

## 2. Golden generators

All 115 golden and generated JSON files exist. For each generator that writes a golden I compared the
output names it writes with the files on disk, then compared the string keys the Vitest tests look up
against the JSON. Findings:

| Golden | Lacks | Why it matters |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `nextjs/src/server/mail/__fixtures__/receipt-mailer.golden.json` | `receipt_rental_no_video`, `receipt_rental_watch` | Defined in `generate-receipt-mailer-golden.rb` (:418, :422), absent from the file. Gate for `RENTAL_REDIRECT_PROVEN` (section 4.5) |
| same file | `receipt_gift_sender` | Defined in the generator, absent from the file. Gate for `unproven.giftSender` in `receipt-presenter.ts`: set it to `false` after regenerating and replaying, and check the `gifts` read lands where Rails sends it (`loadReceiptData`, `receipt-mailer.ts`) |

Goldens built on `scripts/golden/support/rails_cases.rb` (e.g. `payment-processor-jobs.rb`) accept a scratch database named `_ws<N>` or `_<N>`
(a lane's `gumroad_test_<N>`); `payment-processor-jobs.golden.json` was regenerated that way on lane 16 with three new threshold cases.
No debt line was open for the sales-threshold hand-back, so none was closed.

`delete-wishlist-products-job.golden.json` was re-recorded on lane 17 (`gumroad_test_17`, Redis 47) with the real `WishlistProduct#mark_deleted!`; the job runs in Node. Left: a wishlist product with a variant, recurrence, quantity above one or rent, or of a rentable or recurring product (the unported `WishlistProduct` validations) hands back.

`discover_parity_spec.rb` gained two affiliate-cookie examples (`?affiliate_id=` on the root page, `?a=` of a global affiliate on a search box visit), recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16`, Redis 61-63, replay Redis 60, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c16`); `discover.replay.test.ts` checks the cookie value is the replay's own second, as `ws12b.replay` does. They close the discover affiliate-param hand-back; no debt line was open for it.

`schedule-affiliate-workflows.golden.json` was regenerated on lane 15 (`gumroad_test_15`, Redis 56-58, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15`) with seven token cases (Integer, 2**70, negative, true, false); the 40 existing cases are unchanged. `ScheduleAffiliateWorkflowJobsJob` still hands back a Float, Array or Hash token (Rails casts an Array to an IN list). No debt line was open for it.

The dashboard home (`GET /dashboard`, `nextjs_dashboard`) serves `stripe_verification_message` in Node: for a seller with a non-Connect Stripe account, the last requested `user_compliance_info_requests` row (by id) whose `verification_error` yields a message wins, via the settings payments page's `verificationErrorMessage` (Stripe's own message first, else the code table). A code in `ADDRESS_MISMATCH_ERROR_CODES` still hands back, because `po_box_address_deadlock?` reads the address history. Two examples were added to `dashboard_home_signed_in_spec.rb` (a code, and two requests where the later one carries Stripe's message), recorded on lane 17 (`gumroad_test_17`, Redis 47-50, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c17`) and replayed in `dashboard-home.replay` (22 tests pass); no older fixture was rewritten. No debt line was open for it.

Variant workflows (`POST /workflows`, `PUT /workflows/:id` with `workflow_type: "variant"`) run in Node: `Workflow::ManageService` looks the option up with `BaseVariant.find_by_external_id` (`findBaseVariantId` in `workflow-manage.ts`; an id that does not decrypt looks up `id IS NULL`, so the workflow keeps no option, and an unpublished workflow's emails follow the new option). The old hand-back example is replaced by three recorded examples in `workflows_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb` (create, repoint with emails, unknown option id), recorded on lane 15 (`gumroad_test_15`, Redis 56-59) and replayed in `audience-mutations.replay` (42 tests pass); the `save-installment.golden.json` case "workflow create: a variant workflow" now runs instead of being refused. Still handing back: a `variant_external_id` that is not text. Posts of type variant followed (see below). No debt line was open for it. Three older examples of that spec (follower/affiliate, paid filters out of order, accountant) failed while recording on lane 15 because of leftover rows in `gumroad_test_15`; their fixtures were left untouched.

Posts (emails) of type variant (`POST /emails`, `PUT /emails/:id` with `installment_type: "variant"`) run in Node: `SaveInstallmentService#save_installment` sets `base_variant` from `BaseVariant.find_by_external_id(params[:variant_external_id])`, the same lookup as the workflow (`findBaseVariantId` moved from `workflow-manage.ts` to `save-installment.ts`, still re-exported; an id that does not decrypt looks up `id IS NULL` and the post keeps no option). Six cases were added to `save-installment.golden.json` (lane 15, `gumroad_test_15`, Redis 56, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15`; the 108 existing cases are byte-identical) and three recorded examples to `emails_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb` (create, unknown option id, repoint a draft), replayed in `audience-mutations.replay`. No debt line was open for it. A `variant_external_id` that is not text still hands back, for posts too.

Three examples in `spec/requests/settings_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb` (a boolean `facebook_pixel_id`, a boolean `notification_endpoint`, boolean billing detail fields) were recorded on lane 15 (`gumroad_test_15`, Redis 56-59, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15`) and replay green in `settings-mutations`. Closed: `stringValue` cast a boolean to `"t"`/`"f"` for every column, but Rails gives `"1"`/`"0"` on a varchar column and `"t"`/`"f"` on a text one. It now takes the column kind (varchar by default; `notification_endpoint` and billing `additional_notes` are text). `facebook_pixel_id: true` saves `"1"` in Rails (the pixel regex accepts it) where Node failed validation on `"t"`; a boolean Google Analytics id or meta tag fails validation either way, so only the pixel id and the billing fields show it. The new fixtures use the replay secret key base; the 38 existing ones were left as recorded.

The product page (`product_page_parity_spec.rb`) no longer hands an ebook back: `setMeta` builds the JSON-LD `Book` (author, `workExample` per alive epub/pdf/mobi file in position order, with its ISBN) as `Product::StructuredData#build_ebook_structured_data` does. One example ("renders an ebook with its book files in the structured data") was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16`, Redis 61-63) and replays green in `product-page` (54 tests); with the port reverted the replay hands the page back and fails.

The product page no longer hands a non-USD product back: `setMeta` prices the JSON-LD `Offer` as `Product::StructuredData#build_offer_data` does (USD from the cached rate when one is stored, else the product's own currency and amount, divided by `unit_scaling_factor`; `cachedRate` reads Redis only and never fetches) and `product:price:amount` divides by the currency's unit factor instead of 100. One example ("renders a euro product with its price converted to dollars in the structured data", the stored EUR rate seeded from the fixture's Redis) was recorded on lane 16 and replays green in `product-page` (55 tests); with the port reverted the replay hands the page back and fails. The no-rate fallback (own currency and amount) and single-unit currencies are covered by unit tests only. Still handing back: a product with asset previews or profile sections, and the `layout`, `embed` and `overlay` params.

The seller's own product page (the editing props) no longer hands back when the seller has posts: `posts` is `ProfileSectionsPresenter#visible_posts` (alive, published, workflow-free audience posts with `shown_on_profile`, `published_at DESC, id ASC`, at most 999; `{id, name, slug, published_at}`), shared with the settings profile through `visiblePosts` and the pure `shownProfilePosts` in `page-props/settings/profile.ts` (4 unit tests in `profile.test.ts`). One example ("lists the posts a profile section could show in the seller's edit controls": two shown posts out of order plus a hidden one) was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16`, Redis 61-63, replay Redis 60, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c16`) and replays green in `product-page` (56 tests); with the port reverted the replay hands the page back and fails. No debt line was open for it.

`Installment#validate_call_to_action_url_and_text` runs in Node (`installment-writes.ts`; the `URI.regexp` pattern moved to `src/lib/ruby-uri-regexp.ts`, shared with the custom field writes). A post with a call to action no longer hands back from `PUT /emails/:id`, the workflow email writes or `PublishScheduledPostJob`: text missing, link missing or not an http(s) address add the two Rails messages, and blank text/link are judged with `[[:space:]]` (Unicode `White_Space`, not ASCII only). Three examples in `emails_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb` (valid call to action kept, link without text, link that is not a web address) were recorded on lane 21 (`gumroad_test_21`, Redis 42-45, replay Redis 41, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c21`) and replay in `audience-mutations`; six cases were added to `publish-scheduled-post.golden.json` (the existing cases are byte-identical) and the valid-link case is no longer in the test's hand-back list. No request sets the call to action, so only a post that already carries one (legacy rows) reaches this validation. No debt line was open for it.

`PUT|PATCH /settings/team/members/:id` with a role outside `TeamMembership::ROLES` (a string, number, boolean or nil) and `PUT .../restore.json` of a membership the member has since joined again no longer hand back: Rails raises `RecordInvalid`, which ActionDispatch answers with 422, and `processAction` now maps `RecordInvalid` to 422 for every action (an unhandled `RecordInvalid` used to be a 500 in Node). The test environment renders the debug page in HTML whatever the Accept header says, so a 422 there is HTML; the replay ignores that body, as for 404s. Four examples in `settings_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb` were recorded on lane 21 (`gumroad_test_21`, Redis 42-45, replay Redis 41, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c21`) and replayed in `settings-mutations` (45 tests pass); with the port reverted they fail. Still handing back there: a request without a `team_membership` hash and removing a member of the Gumroad account. No debt line was open for it.

`flush-delivered-email-infos-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 20 (`gumroad_test_20`, Redis 37 for `REDIS_HOST` and `SIDEKIQ_REDIS_HOST`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c20`); its replay needs `JOBS_REDIS_URL` and is skipped without it. The one hand-back of `FlushDeliveredEmailInfosJob` is a buffer entry that is not `{i, p, t}` integers (never written by `EmailInfo.buffer_delivered`); no debt line was open for it.
`DELETE /v2/products/:link_id/variant_categories/:id` (`nextjs_api_v2_products`) runs in Node, audit row included (see "Variant category destroy" in workstreams.md). Three examples in `products_api_resource_writes_parity_spec.rb`, recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16`, Redis 61-63, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c16`) and replayed in `api-v2-products` (66 tests pass); with `routes.json` reverted three of them fail. No debt line was open for it.

`GET /mobile/preorders/preorder_attributes/:id` serves an `authorization_successful` preorder in Node (`mobile-preorders.ts`): the link's mobile JSON plus the authorization purchase's `purchase_id`, `purchased_at`, `user_id`, `is_archived`, `product_updates_data: []` and `preorder_data` (`external_id`, `release_at`). It hands back a charged preorder (`UrlRedirect#product_json_data`), one whose last purchase is `successful`, and, through `NotPortedError`, a product with asset previews or a seller or product with any post (the product-updates preloader). Three examples in `mobile_products_subscriptions_parity_spec.rb` (authorized preorder, archived authorization purchase, seller with posts) recorded on lane 18 (`gumroad_test_18`, Redis 52, 53, 57, 58, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c18`) and replayed in `mobile-api.replay`. No debt line was open for it.

`GET /mobile/purchases/purchase_attributes/:id` serves a plain purchase in Node (`mobile-purchases.ts`): a USD purchase of the library owner with no url redirect, preorder, subscription, refund or review, whose seller and product have no post (`product_updates_data: []`), and the 404 for a refunded, charged-back or unsuccessful one. `models/purchase-product-updates.ts` ports the DB-only part of `Purchase.preload_product_updates_data!`: the blocked-membership guard (a lapsed membership of a product that blocks access after cancellation gets `[]` whatever the seller posted) and the no-posts case. Still Rails': any seller or product with a post (`Purchase.product_installments` buyer filters, `Installment#installment_mobile_json_data`, which creates url redirects), so the installments-with-context, sales show, subscriptions-with-posts and preorders-with-posts hand-backs stay open; the membership purchase is also Rails' until `subscription_data` is serialized. Examples "serves the buyer's own purchase with no product updates" (re-recorded from the old hand-back), "does not find a refunded purchase" and "hands a read of a purchase whose seller has a post to Rails" recorded on lane 18 and replayed in `mobile-api.replay`. No debt line was open for it.

`PUT|PATCH /mobile/licenses/:id` no longer hands back an `enabled` that is an array or hash: Rails' boolean cast turns it into true (lane 16, Redis 60-64; example
"enables a disabled license when enabled is an array" recorded and replayed in `mobile-api.replay.test.ts`). No debt line was open for it.

`DELETE /links/:id` (`nextjs_products_writes`) no longer hands back a product with public files: `Link#delete!` runs `alive_public_files.update_all(scheduled_for_deletion_at: 10.minutes.from_now)`, ported as `schedulePublicFilesForDeletion` (no `updated_at` change, the file rows are not soft-deleted). Example "schedules the public files of a deleted product for deletion" recorded and replayed in `products-mutations.replay.test.ts` (lane 15, Redis 55-59). Still handing back: a product shown in a profile section and one with a custom domain. No debt line was open for it.

`POST /links/:id/unpublish` by a collaborator on the product runs in Node (`fetch_product_and_enforce_access` accepts `logged_in_user.collaborator_for?(product)`; `find` no longer raises `NotPortedError` for it, and `authorize` judges the collaborator by their own account role, as Rails does). A collaborator on a different product of the same seller still answers 404. Two examples in `products_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb`, recorded on lane 15 (`gumroad_test_15`, Redis 56-59, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15`) and replayed in `products-mutations.replay` (25 tests pass). No debt line was open for it.

`PUT /links/:id/sections` (`LinksController#update_sections`, `nextjs_products_writes`) runs in Node for the owner, a team member with `update?` and a collaborator on the product (`find(input, "access", ...)` now also hands the collaborating set to the policy; `linkPolicy.update` is `LinkPolicy#update_sections?`). It stores the decrypted ids and `main_section_index.to_i` in the product's `json_data` (`Link` saves may now change the `sections` and `main_section_index` keys of `json_data` and no others; any other key still raises `NotPortedError`), then deletes the product's `seller_profile_sections` the list leaves out, in one transaction, and answers 204. Rails draws the route for the discover host and seller domains only (`product_info_and_purchase_routes`), so Node serves it on the discover host and hands the app host (Rails: 404) and seller subdomains/custom domains back, and so it does a section id that does not decrypt (Rails stores `nil`) and a `main_section_index` that is not text, a number or null. Four examples in `products_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb` (collaborator reorders and drops a section; owner empties the list and keeps another product's section; collaborator on another product: 404; accountant: 401), recorded on lane 15 (`gumroad_test_15`, Redis 56-58, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15`) and replayed in `products-mutations.replay` (29 tests pass; with the `json_data` allowance reverted the two writing examples fail). Still in Rails for collaborators: `POST /links/:id/update`, `publish`, `release_preorder` and `disable_downloads_for_all_files` (none is registered in Node at all: they need the product save pipeline, `Link#publish!`, preorder release charges, and `ProductFile` writes with `GenerateProductFilesArchivesJob`), and `Link#collaborator_for_display` (`assertNoCollaborator` in `product-props.ts`, which gates the product page, not these writes). No debt line was open for it.

`GET /internal/products/:product_id/existing_product_files` by a collaborator on the product runs in Node when the owner's library has no live file (`collaboratingProductIds`, the same check as `fetch_product_and_enforce_access`); a user who neither owns nor collaborates on the product gets the 404 Rails' `e404` gives, no longer a hand-back. Three examples in `internal_api_parity_spec.rb`, recorded on lane 15 (`gumroad_test_15`, Redis 56-59, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15`) and replayed in `internal-api.replay` (105 tests pass). The remaining hand-back is a library with files (`ProductFile#as_json(existing_product_file: true)`; for a collaborator Rails also narrows the list to the product). No debt line was open for it.

The rest of `LinksController` answers its guards and empty answers in Node (`links-mutations.ts`; flags `nextjs_links_guards`, `_links_landing`, `_links_search`, `_links_card`; `links_controller_parity_spec.rb`, 52 examples recorded 2026-11-11 on lane 23 and replayed in `links-controller.replay`, 54 tests; the landing `version` is checked against the seeded `pages.updated_at` because the replay seeds every row relative to its own clock). `POST /links/:id` (update), `publish`, `disable_downloads_for_all_files`, `POST /products/:id/release_preorder`, `POST /products/:id/price_check` and `send_sample_price_change_email` run `fetch_product_*`, `authorize` and (except `publish`) RequireAccountEmail, then hand back whole: 404 for an unknown or foreign product, 401 for a refused role, `price_check` 404 while the `price_checker` flag is off for the seller, `send_sample_price_change_email` 404 JSON for a tier that is not one of the product's alive tiers. `GET /l/:id/landing/version` is native (a visitor or signed-in buyer gets `present: false`; the owner, a collaborator or a team member get the root page's `updated_at` as epoch seconds), as is the 404 of `GET /l/:id/landing/embed` (feature off, no page, unpublished product for a visitor); a visible page hands back (the sandboxed document and `Pages::Interpolator`). `GET /products/search` answers the empty page for a `user_id` that is no user's `external_id` column or a section the seller does not own; every other search (including `ids`, the virtual `default-products` section and any discover search, which need Elasticsearch) hands back. `GET /products/:id/card` is a 404 because the controller has no `card` action. Still in Rails, unregistered: `POST /links` (create) and `GET /links` (the same page as `/products`, under another path). Gumroad-domain hosts only: the seller-domain and custom-domain copies of `search` and the landing routes stay in Rails.

`PurchasesController` answers its guards in Node (`purchases-controller.ts`; flags `nextjs_purchases_seller_guards`, `nextjs_purchases_public_guards`; `purchases_controller_parity_spec.rb`, 36 examples recorded 2026-11-11 on lane 22 and replayed in `purchases-controller.replay`, 38 tests). `PUT`/`PATCH /purchases/:id`, `change_can_contact`, `cancel_preorder_by_seller` and `refund` need a signed-in JSON request and answer 404 (unknown purchase), 404 JSON (another seller's purchase; `refund` does not look the purchase up) and 401 (a role that cannot update customers), then hand back whole: the saves run `Purchase` callbacks and a gift update re-sends a receipt, the refund moves money. `receipt`, `confirm_receipt_email`, `subscribe`, `resend_receipt`, `unsubscribe`, `confirm`, `charge_preorder` and `PUT /subscriptions/:id` (`update_subscription`) answer the 404 of their lookup (an external id, the encrypted manage cookie of a subscription, or a signed `unsubscribe`/`confirm` token plus, for `unsubscribe`, the legacy purchase or charge id) and hand back every found row: they render ERB or the mailer, mint signed redirects, write every purchase of the buyer, read the processor or charge a card. A SecureExternalId configuration Node cannot read hands the token routes back. `GET`/`POST /purchases/export` are not registered; `GET /purchases/search` is served by WS-20o below.

`DELETE /links/:id` also runs the rest of `Link#delete!` in Node: `clear_featured_product_sections!` (drops `featured_product_id` from featured-product sections), `remove_from_profile_sections!` (strips the id from every products section of the seller under the `seller_profiles` row lock) and `custom_domain&.mark_deleted!` (the existing `CustomDomainDeletable`). Three examples replace "hands a deletion of a product shown in a profile section back to Rails" (its fixture is deleted): products sections, featured section, custom domain, recorded on lane 15 (`gumroad_test_15`, Redis 56-59, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15`) and replayed in `products-mutations.replay` (23 tests pass). The custom domain example pins `created_at`/`updated_at` in the past: the column has second precision, so a row created in the replay's own second would show no `updated_at` change. Still handing back: nothing on this route; a profile section whose `json_data` already breaks its schema raises `NotPortedError` before the first write (JSON::Validator's messages are not reproduced). No debt line was open for it.

`POST /v2/products/:link_id/variant_categories` and `PUT|PATCH .../variant_categories/:id` (`nextjs_api_v2_products`) no longer hand back a JSON boolean `title`: `permit(:title)` accepts it and the varchar column stores `"1"` (true) or `"0"` (false), the same cast as the settings fields. Two examples in `products_api_resource_writes_parity_spec.rb` were recorded on lane 19 (`gumroad_test_19`, Redis 31-34, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c19`) and replay green in `api-v2-products`. A numeric title still hands back: the JSON parser cannot tell `5` from `5.0`, and Rails keeps the float's `"5.0"`.

Regenerate (the generator's own header has the DB setup: scratch database `gumroad_ws76` loaded from `db/schema.rb`,
Redis db 10):

```bash
USE_DB_WORKER_REPLICAS=true bundle exec ruby nextjs/src/server/mail/__fixtures__/generate-receipt-mailer-golden.rb
cd nextjs && npx vitest run src/server/mail
```

A regeneration redraws Message-IDs and MIME boundaries (the commit history shows earlier goldens were spliced
rather than regenerated for that reason); review the diff and keep only the two new cases if the rest churns.

No other golden gap was found by the checks above. The checks are heuristic (string literals in test lookups; helper calls
with literal names in generators), so they cannot see a case the generator should have but never defines, or a
`toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(n)` that has silently become loose.

Golden generators, for reference, and the command that rebuilds them (all run from the repo root):

- `nextjs/scripts/golden/*.rb` (51): `bundle exec rails runner nextjs/scripts/golden/<name>.rb` (or `bundle exec ruby ...` for `flags`, `geo-ip`, `paper-trail`, `public-suffix`, `serialize`, `countries`, `money-format`, `query-cache`, `ruby-uri`); each file's header lists its env (`REDIS_DB`, `RACK_ATTACK_REDIS_HOST`, `GOLDEN_DATABASE`).
- `nextjs/src/**/__fixtures__/generate-*.rb` and `nextjs/src/server/infra/__fixtures__/generators/*.rb`: mail, html, search (Elasticsearch through `es_recording_proxy.rb`), jobs, stripe, healthcheck, safe redirect, rails cookies; header of each file.
- `spec/presenters/product_presenter_golden_spec.rb`, `spec/services/product/editor_revision_golden_spec.rb`: `PARITY_GOLDEN=1 bin/rspec <spec>`.
- `nextjs/scripts/dump-*.rb` (10): `RAILS_ENV=test bin/rails runner nextjs/scripts/dump-<name>.rb`; they write `*.generated.json` constants, regenerate after any Rails change to the lists they read.
- `bin/golden <ruby-file> <spec.json>` for `nextjs/parity/golden/*.json`.

After a regeneration, `git diff` must be empty (except the two rental cases above). A diff means either Rails
changed since the file was written or the committed file was hand-edited: could not determine which for any
file; the diff will tell.

## 3. Feature flags

`routes.json` gates 188 flags. The proxy reads them from Flipper (`src/proxy.ts`; `NEXTJS_ROUTE_FLAGS=a,b` is
the local override the replays use). Server code reads no `nextjs_*` flag besides those in `routes.json` (the
only other literal, `nextjs_help`, appears in `proxy-gate.test.ts`). Other Flipper features the server reads
(`resend`, `force_resend_for_post_emails`, `buyer_local_currency`, `paypal_payout_fee`, `merchant_migration`,
`high_volume_seller_fee`, ...) are Rails' own features, not rewrite flags.

A flag is ready to enable when (a) the spec(s) in the second column have no missing fixtures, (b) the replay
in the third column is green with `PARITY_DATABASE_URL`, and (c) for flags marked $, the section 4 item for that
area is closed. Flags not listed under a replay are covered by the replay file in the group's row.

## 4. Unproven items, money and security first

Ordered by impact. "Unproven" means: ported, with unit tests or partial vectors, but without a Rails
fixture or golden that covers the behaviour, or with the covering check not run.

1. **Signed S3/CloudFront/Cloudflare URLs** (`nextjs/src/server/controllers/url-redirects/files.ts`,
   `streaming.ts`, `server/infra/signed-urls.ts`). Security: a wrong signature, expiry or key choice either
   breaks every download or hands out a longer-lived link. `signed-urls.golden.json` pins the algorithms with a
   fake RSA key and a frozen clock. The delivery replay (`url-redirects.replay.test.ts`) masks `X-Amz-Date`,
   `X-Amz-Signature`, `Expires` and `Signature`, so no check ever compares a real signature, the expiry
   window per file size, or the production key/bucket/distribution selection. 36 of the delivery examples
   have no fixture (1.1). To close: record `url_redirect_delivery_gate_spec`, `url_redirect_download_page_spec`
   and the kindle examples, replay `url-redirects` and `url-redirect-pages` against minio (the replay seeds
   `SPEC_OBJECTS` itself), then sign one URL per type in a staging environment and fetch it. Partly closed: `signed-urls-fixtures.test.ts` recomputes every S3 GET presign and CloudFront signature found in the committed Rails fixtures (test-environment minio credentials and RSA key from `.env.test`, the clock each URL carries) and matches them byte for byte. Still unproven: the Cloudflare `verify` token, the production bucket, key and distribution selection, and a fetch against a real bucket. Stamped PDFs,
   rich content, variants and preorders deliberately hand back (`files.ts` top comment).
2. **Chargeback-rate payout reserve** (`server/page-props/payouts/payouts.ts`: `chargebackRateReserveCents`,
   `payableCentsUnderReserve`, `reserveBreakdown`). Money: it decides how much a seller sees as payable.
   Vectors in `payout-reserve.test.ts` are copied from the Ruby specs, not recorded. The four `payouts_signed_in_spec`
   examples at :251-:283 (whole rows under 75 percent, leftover on the waiting line, below the minimum, reserve
   anchored on what was already paid) have no fixture. The `paidCentsUnderChargebackRateHold` SQL (payments in
   `creating/processing/unclaimed/completed`) has no Rails comparison at all. The page only reads; no payout
   execution is ported. Cached balance stats: a miss is computed live plus the refresh job, and a blob that exists is served as
   stored (its `next_payout_period_data` and `processing_payout_periods_data`, plus the routing flag), five recorded examples compared in
   `checkout-payouts.replay.test.ts`. A blob Rails would raise on (no processing list, a period that is not a hash) hands back and is unit-tested only.
3. **Sales tax rounding and validation** (`server/tax/sales-tax-calculation.ts`, `regional-vat-id-shape.ts`,
   `sales-tax-countries.ts`). Money: `lookupTaxCents` rounds half away from zero in BigInt arithmetic; its tests
   are `customer_surcharge_controller_spec` vectors "then derived half-up cases", i.e. the extra cases are
   reasoned, not recorded. The Bahrain, Kenya, Nigeria and Tanzania id validators and `regionalVatIdValidator` routing
   are "derived from the Ruby source" in `sales-tax.test.ts`. No tax golden or fixture exists for them. TaxJar,
   `ZipTaxRate`, GeoIP and vendor validators hand back. Close with a golden generator that calls
   `SalesTaxCalculator` and the validators on the same inputs (none exists in `nextjs/scripts/golden`).
   Recorded 2026-11-10 (lane 15): the empty 400 typed by a registered `format` param (`xml`, `text`, `js`). Disproved by recording:
   a Singapore rate with an empty `applicable_years` is not served as `[]` (`json_data_for_attr` reads a blank list as nil, so
   Rails raises `NoMethodError` on `include?`); the Node hand-back is right and stays. A missing or empty `products` renders
   the exceptions app, whose test-env body (debug page, `text/plain`) differs from production's, so a fixture cannot pin it.
4. **Billing and team writes** (`settings-mutations.ts`, `server/models/billing-detail.ts`,
   `policies/settings/billing.ts`). Security and compliance: they write billing details and change team roles,
   remove and restore members. The 13 examples in 1.1 (billing :255-:277, team :348-:401) have no fixture, so
   no check compares the rows written, the `versions`/jobs enqueued or the policy refusals ("refuses a member whose
   role cannot manage the team"). Replays ignore `db.versions` because specs run with PaperTrail off, so audit
   rows are unproven for every write in this list.
5. **Receipt mailer rental redirect** (`server/mail/mailers/receipt-mailer.ts:61`, `receipt-presenter.ts:318`).
   `RENTAL_REDIRECT_PROVEN = false` makes a rental with a rental url_redirect throw `MailViewNotPortedError`, so
   it hands back to Rails (safe default). To flip: regenerate the golden (section 2), confirm
   `receipt-presenter.test.ts` ("offers View content ... Watch ...") and the receipt replay pass, including the
   position of the `product_files` read (`receipt-mailer.ts:354`, "not recorded from Ruby"), then set the constant
   to `true` and delete the UNPROVEN comments and the README paragraph.
6. **License writes** (`server/models/license.ts`, `controllers/customer-actions.ts`, mobile
   `PUT|PATCH /mobile/licenses/:id`). Security and money-adjacent: disable, enable, set, adjust and reset uses.
   Fixtures exist (`seller_json_mutations_signed_in_spec` x11, `mobile_api_parity_spec` x4) but the replays have
   not been run; `ws12b.replay.test.ts` and `mobile-api.replay.test.ts` cover them. `Licenses` writes a
   PaperTrail version (`disabled_at`, `serial`) that the replay cannot compare (above). `License#rotate!`, license
   creation are not ported. The public API `licenses#verify|enable|disable|decrement_uses_count|rotate` run in
   Node for a plain purchase (`nextjs_api_v2_licenses`, 71 request-spec fixtures replayed by `api-v2-ws17c.replay`;
   the 53 `controllers/api/v2/licenses_controller_spec` fixtures, 61 exchanges, replay in `api-v2-ws17b.replay` and all pass).
   The N-Z v2 writes (`offer_codes`, `upsells`, `resource_subscriptions#create|test_event`, `thumbnails`, `product_builds#deploy|promote`,
   `tax_forms#download`) answer only their guards in Node; the writes hand back whole (`*_writes_parity_spec`, `thumbnails_parity_spec`,
   `tax_forms_download_parity_spec`), so the Rails side of those is proven only by the Rails specs.
7. **Everything above the line is replay-unverified.** Step 0 below replays all 1,759 existing fixtures. Until
   then treat even "proven by replay" in `workstreams.md` as unconfirmed here.

Smaller items noted in the code, none money or security: `models/README.md:380` (lock timeout retry path has
no golden), `models/README.md:487` (`page-props/signed-in/reads.ts` carries a looser copy of a flags read that no
golden pins), `discover.replay.test.ts` header says the replayer only rebuilds `products` and `purchases` while
`parity/README.md` and the recorder (`ES_ONLY_SORTS`) now cover `ProductPageView`; could not determine whether the
header is stale. Re-check before recording the Discover autocomplete and recently viewed examples.

## Recommended order

Bring-up, once: MySQL with `db/schema.rb` loaded into a disposable `parity_test`, Redis, Elasticsearch 7.17, minio
or an S3 test bucket, DynamoDB local, `bundle install`, `npm ci` in `nextjs/`, and a `.env.test` at the repo root.

0. **Baseline replay of what exists.** No recording yet.
   ```bash
   cd nextjs
   PARITY_DATABASE_URL=mysql://root:password@127.0.0.1:3306/parity_test npm run parity
   ```
   Fix or log every failure first: a red existing replay means a merged port is wrong, and it would hide
   failures in step 1. Do the same for `npm test` (unit tests) and `node scripts/typecheck.ts`.
1. **Money and security recordings** (these gate the largest-impact flags):
   ```bash
   PARITY_RECORD=1 bin/rspec spec/requests/payouts_signed_in_spec.rb
   PARITY_RECORD=1 bin/rspec spec/requests/url_redirect_delivery_gate_spec.rb spec/requests/url_redirect_download_page_spec.rb spec/requests/url_redirects_parity_spec.rb
   PARITY_RECORD=1 bin/rspec spec/requests/settings_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb
   cd nextjs && PARITY_DATABASE_URL=... npx vitest run --config parity/vitest.config.ts parity/__tests__/checkout-payouts.replay.test.ts parity/__tests__/url-redirect-pages.replay.test.ts parity/__tests__/url-redirects.replay.test.ts parity/__tests__/settings-mutations.replay.test.ts
   ```
2. **Receipt rental golden** (section 2 command), then flip `RENTAL_REDIRECT_PROVEN` and replay `src/server/mail`.
3. **Mobile API** (largest block; 0 without a fixture now):
   ```bash
   PARITY_RECORD=1 bin/rspec spec/requests/api/mobile/mobile_api_parity_spec.rb spec/requests/api/mobile/mobile_purchases_sales_parity_spec.rb spec/requests/api/mobile/mobile_products_subscriptions_parity_spec.rb
   cd nextjs && PARITY_DATABASE_URL=... npx vitest run --config parity/vitest.config.ts parity/__tests__/mobile-api.replay.test.ts
   ```
4. **Auth and audience**: `review_reminders_parity_spec.rb` (replay `auth-mutations`), `customers_paged_signed_in_spec.rb`
   (replay `customers-paged`).
5. **Pages**: `seller_products_edit_signed_in_spec.rb` (`seller-products`), `storefront_profile_products_parity_spec.rb`
   (`storefront-profile-products`), `discover_parity_spec.rb` (`discover`, needs Elasticsearch and the
   `ProductPageView` index).
6. **The gap outside the tagged specs** (1.2).
7. **Close-out**: `cd nextjs && node scripts/parity-debt.ts` must end with `0 without a fixture, 0 unresolved`;
   `git status` shows only new fixture files plus the receipt golden; `npm run parity` is green with
   `PARITY_DATABASE_URL`. Only then walk the flag table in section 3 and enable flags in staging, lowest
   blast radius first (reads, then JSON writes, then the $ flags last, each with the shadow period README.md
   requires).

Record fixtures on one machine in one sitting where possible: fixtures embed the recording app's
`secretKeyBase` and `recordedOn`, and a spec that records times far from `travel_to` re-records with a
different offset each day (see the Limits section of `nextjs/parity/README.md`).

Library `?purchase_id=` (WS-16, lane 16: `gumroad_test_16`, Redis 61-63, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c16`) runs in Node: `receipt_purchases` comes from `LibraryPresenter#receipt_purchases` (library-visible purchase, membership access, receipt/all `ThirdPartyAnalytic` on the product or its seller) and `purchase_analytics` is `{}` unless the seller has a pixel id (below). Three recorded examples in `buyer_pages_parity_spec.rb` (tracked purchase, receipt analytics flag, purchase not in the library) replay in `buyer-pages.replay` (39 tests pass). `PurchaseSellerAnalyticsPresenter#props` is now ported (`purchaseAnalyticsProps` in `library.ts`) for a USD purchase of a USD product with no `purchase_presentments` row; two more recorded examples (Google Analytics + Facebook ids; TikTok id with quantity 2, `tax_cents` 150 and `skip_free_sale_analytics`) replay green (41 tests pass). Still handing back: a pixel-id seller's purchase displayed or priced in another currency (`usd_cents_to_currency` rates), a purchase with a buyer-currency presentment row (`buyer_presentment_*` event fields), and a seller opted into buyer-local currency (`buyerCurrencyDisplayProps` throws `NotPortedError`). Only the first `purchase_id` is read (a flat query keeps the last one), not the controller's `MAX_TRACKED_PURCHASE_ANALYTICS` list. No debt line was open for it; the subscriptions tab and bundle filters with a download page are unchanged. The 'purchase_id' hand-back that `LIBRARY_OPTIONS` had for any value is gone. Library `?purchase_id[]=` lists (lane 16) run in Node: `queryParamStrings` reads `Array(params[:purchase_id]).select(String)` (a flat key keeps the last value, `purchase_id[]` keys each value), `trackedPurchaseIds` keeps the first `MAX_TRACKED_PURCHASE_ANALYTICS` (50) and drops ids that do not decrypt, and `purchaseAnalyticsProps` / `receiptPurchases` answer for every id the buyer owns, ordered by purchase id as `by_external_ids` returns them. `assertFlatQuery` takes `arrayParams` so only `purchase_id[]` escapes the bracketed-query hand-back (`creators[]` and the like still hand back). Still handing back: a flat `purchase_id` mixed with `purchase_id[]` (Rack raises ParameterTypeError), `purchase_id[key]` hashes and `;` separated queries. Non-USD purchases and products still hand back (the rates file / Redis cache port is the next piece). No debt line was open for it.
A product page whose product has a collaborator runs in Node: `collaboratorForDisplay` in `product-props.ts` is `Link#collaborator_for_display` (an undeleted `Collaborator` whose invitation was accepted, shown unless `Collaborator#show_as_co_creator_for_product?` is false: the affiliate's `dont_show_as_co_creator` flag when `apply_to_all_products`, the `affiliates_links` row's otherwise) and `collaborating_user` is that user's `author_byline_props`. The `assertNoCollaborator` hand-back is gone. Four examples in `product_page_parity_spec.rb` (credited collaborator, opt-out on every product, opt-out on this product, pending invitation), recorded on lane 15 (`gumroad_test_15`, Redis 56-58, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15`) and replayed in `product-page.replay` (60 tests pass; with the port reverted the four fail). The product edit page (`Products/Edit`) and the bundle editor (`Bundles/Product/Edit`, `Bundles/Share/Edit`) run in Node for a product or bundle with a collaborator, for the owner and for the collaborator. `collaborating_user` is `collaboratingUserProps` in `seller-products/edit.ts` (`collaboratorForDisplay`, then `author_byline_props`). `ProductPresenter#collaborator_editing?` (the viewer's seller is not the owner) changes three things: `existing_files` stops at the product's own files (already empty, since a `product_files` row is a blocker), `waitlist` is nil, and `current_seller_external_id` is the collaborator's account (already `context.currentSeller`). So `editBlockers` takes a `collaboratorEditing` flag that drops the `existing_files` and `waitlist` probes, and `assertEditSupported`/`assertBundleEditSupported` no longer hand back a viewer other than the owner. The `collaborator` row probe and the `is_collab` flag blocker are removed from both editors (`is_collab` does not enter `eligible_for_installment_plans?`). Seven examples in `seller_products_edit_signed_in_spec.rb` (credited collaborator, opt-out on every product, opt-out on this product, pending invitation, a collaborator editing the owner's product, the bundle editor for the owner and for the collaborator), recorded on lane 15 (`gumroad_test_15`, Redis 56-58, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15`) and replayed in `seller-products.replay` (90 tests pass). `seller-products.replay` now enables `nextjs_bundle_editor`: these are the first recorded bundle-editor visits, and they found one difference, fixed here: Rails puts the bundle's `title` meta tag after the application-level tags (`Bundles::BaseController` redeclares `set_default_page_title`, which Rails moves to run last), so `bundles/pages.ts` calls `meta.moveToEnd(["title"])`. The bundle examples pin the bundled products' `created_at`, which the editor lists. The other bundle-editor states (versions, SKUs, files, covers, an offer code) are still only unit-tested. Verified by reverting the source files: the new unit tests and the seven replays fail. No debt line was open for it.

`purge-old-deleted-asset-previews-worker.golden.json` was recorded on lane 22 (`gumroad_test_22`, Redis 36, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22`), 24 cases. `PurgeOldDeletedAssetPreviewsWorker` runs fully in Node: only `AssetPreview#destroy!` is ported (not the validations, creation callbacks or URL helpers). The golden script drops the scratch schema Active Storage foreign keys (TRUNCATE refuses them) and stubs `SecureRandom.uuid` for the ActiveJob `job_id`. `Links.touch` takes `deferred` for `touch_later`, which the golden shows leaves the version `object` in column order (a direct touch lists `updated_at` first); the cause in Rails was not traced. No debt line was open for it.

`backfill-price-checker-index-fields-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 22 (`gumroad_test_22`, Redis 36, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22`), 9 cases. `BackfillPriceCheckerIndexFieldsJob` runs fully in Node. `EsCapture` gained `es_bodies` (one answer per request) so the scroll can end, and `search/api.ts` gained `scrollById`, since this job's `EsClient.scroll(scroll_id:)` is a GET with the id in the path, unlike the `body:` form the product indexer uses. Its test needs a real Redis (`JOBS_REDIS_URL`) for the unique-jobs lock on `SendToElasticsearchWorker`, so it is skipped without one. No debt line was open for it.

`link-metrics.golden.json` (78 cases) and `cache-product-data-worker.golden.json` (34 cases) were recorded on lane 22 (`gumroad_test_22`, Redis 36, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22`) over a real purchases index filled from `Purchase#as_indexed_json` and reached through `es_recording_proxy.rb`. `CacheProductDataWorker` runs in Node, on the five `Link` metrics ported in `models/link-metrics.ts` (`successful_sales_count`, `remaining_for_sale_count`, `monthly_recurring_revenue`, `revenue_pending`, `total_usd_cents`) and `ProductCachedValue#assign_cached_values`. Still handing back: a tier closed to new purchases on a tiered membership (`BaseVariant#can_close_to_new_buyers?` reads a Flipper feature) and a bundle variant that is a Sku, both after the cache expiry ran, which Rails repeats harmlessly. The local MySQL accepts a `decimal(10, 2)` value past its range (the golden INSERTs for 6e9 or 44e9 succeed, clipped by the server); production's strict mode may raise `ActiveRecord::RangeError` instead, which the golden cannot show. Elasticsearch stores `monthly_recurring_revenue` as a `float` (32-bit), so a sum past 2^24 cents differs from the exact quotient; the recorded answers carry that.

The dashboard home (`GET /dashboard`, `nextjs_dashboard`) renders a product card's `thumbnail` for an Unsplash thumbnail, and `null` for a product with none or a soft-deleted one (`cardThumbnail` in `creator-home.ts`, over the existing `thumbnailUrl`); any alive thumbnail used to hand the whole page back. Four unit tests in `creator-home.test.ts`. Still handing back: a card whose thumbnail is an uploaded file (the recorder cannot serialize the binary blob upload, so that case is unit-tested only). The spec builds its purchases with `build(:purchase, ...).save!(validate: false)` because the factory charges a card. No debt line was open for it.
`DELETE /followers/:id` (WS-13b, `FollowersController#destroy`, `nextjs_followers_writes`; lane 16: `gumroad_test_16`, Redis 27/28/29/32 with `DISABLE_TEST_REDIS_ISOLATION=1`, replay Redis 33, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c16`) runs in Node (`followers-mutations.ts`) on new model files `follower.ts` (callbacks, `has_paper_trail`, the `ConfirmedFollowerEvent` indexing job, `with_opt_out_lock_wait`) and `audience-member-record.ts` (validations, derived columns, the Elasticsearch jobs). Twelve examples in `followers_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb` replay in `audience-mutations.replay` (60 tests pass); nine fail with the controller import removed. The policy only asks about the role, so a seller can remove another seller's follow (as in Rails). Hand-backs: a seller with a `FeatureAnnouncementNewsletterCutover` row, an audience member that is still a customer or affiliate (its columns are recomputed from details with absolute dates, which replay cannot pin), a `LockWaitTimeout` (Rails answers 503 with `Retry-After`) and a follow that fails validation. `GET /follow/:id/confirm` (`nextjs_followers_confirm`, same file) redirects the confirmed follower to the seller's profile with the notice: eight examples in `followers_confirm_link_spec.rb` replay in the same file (68 tests pass; five fail with the controller import removed). It hands back a seller with a cutover row, with follower or audience workflows to schedule (`schedule_workflow_jobs`), without a subdomain, and a follower whose audience member row already exists. The inserted audience member's `details` are asserted for shape only: they hold the follow's `created_at` as an absolute time and the replay seeds it from its own clock. `cancel` (its HTML page and JSON) and the comments actions stay in Ruby.

`POST /posts/:post_id/comments`, `PUT` and `DELETE /posts/:post_id/comments/:id` (WS-13b, `CommentsController`, `nextjs_comments_*`; lane 16) run in Node (`comments-mutations.ts`) behind `comment-context.ts`, a port of `CommentContextPolicy` that covers only the paths without a purchase: the seller, an admin or marketing member, and the author. Hand-backs: a reader (not the seller or a team member) creating a comment, since `User#visible_posts_for` and the seller notification mail are not ported; a request with a `purchase_id`; a blank or invalid comment (Rails answers 422); a delete of a comment that has replies (the descendants are marked deleted in one pass); a signed-out visitor; and a user whose team membership cannot be resolved. `GET /posts/:post_id/comments` (index) is not ported.

## 404 content negotiation

`ws-parity-accept-404-1` (lane c404: `gumroad_test_c404`/`gumroad_replay_c404`, container `gr-ruby-c404`, Redis 30-33 recording and 34 replay, `ES_TEST_INDEX_PREFIX=c404-`; WebMock and the test key only, no external call, no money). Question from `ws12-products-readonly-1`: a 404 whose `Accept` is only `application/json` is JSON in Node and the HTML debug page in the Rails test environment.

- **Test environment only.** `config/environments/test.rb` sets `consider_all_requests_local = true` and `show_exceptions = :rescuable`, so DebugExceptions answers a `RoutingError` (`e404`) before `ActionDispatch::PublicExceptions`. Over 35 requests (a route, an unrouted path, `.json`, `?format=`, XHR, HEAD) it answers `text/html` for every `Accept` (`application/json`, `application/json, text/html`, `*/*`, none), `text/plain` for an XHR, and, for HEAD only, the format the request names. That is the only thing the original observation showed, and it does not hold in production.
- **Production differs, and Node was wrong.** `production.rb` and `staging.rb` set `consider_all_requests_local = false` and there is no `exceptions_app`, so `PublicExceptions` renders `request.formats.first`. Reproduced in the Ruby container through the full Rack stack with `show_exceptions = :all` and `show_detailed_exceptions = false` (`nextjs/scripts/golden/public-exceptions.rb`, 347 cases, `__fixtures__/public-exceptions.golden.json`). Node only recognised an `Accept` without a comma, so the header the frontend really sends, `application/json, text/html`, got the HTML page where Rails answers `{"status":404,"error":"Not Found"}` (and `text/html, application/json` got HTML in both). Also differing: `Accept` with parameters (`application/json; charset=utf-8`), q-values sorted by weight, the `text/xml`/`+xml` reordering, `text/*` and `application/*` expansion, `?format=` and a path extension (`.json`, which beats `Accept`, as the route's `(.:format)` does), an XHR with a `Content-Type` and no `Accept`, and HEAD (empty body, content type of the first format, even `text/plain` or an empty type).
- **Ported.** `controllers/mime-negotiation.ts` is `Mime::Type.parse`, `AcceptList.sort!` and `formats` (actionpack 8.1.3.1); `publicException` in `application-controller.ts` uses it. `public-exceptions.test.ts` replays all 341 modelled golden cases (production and test) and fails 162 of them against the old helper. In `RAILS_ENV=test` a 404 now answers the debug page's content type (`text/html`, `text/plain` for an XHR) instead of varying with `Accept`, like the 422 rule that was already there; the page body is the public page because the replay ignores it. Production behaviour is the `PublicExceptions` one.
- **Replay coverage.** 4 examples in `mobile_tracking_parity_spec.rb` (Accept only `application/json`, `application/json, text/html`, `*/*`, XHR asking for JSON) and 1 in `product_editor_json_signed_in_spec.rb` (`.json` route, Accept only `application/json`) are recorded in the test environment. `ws12o` and `ws12c` replay them and now compare the `content-type` of a debug-page 404 (ws12o used to ignore it; the other 35 replay files that skip `response.headers.content-type` for debug-page fixtures could drop it the same way). Against the old helper the replays fail 3 of the 5. The other examples keep passing, so a test-env fixture stays comparable and nothing is hidden.
- **Not ported, because it cannot be proven from here.** (1) An `Accept` with an invalid MIME type (`garbage`, `text/html, garbage`) is an empty 406 on every Rails route, 200 pages included; Node has no such rule anywhere and the 404 helper treats it as no format. The golden keeps the cases (status 406) and the test skips them. (2) A route that sets `defaults: { format: :json }` (15 in `config/routes.rb`: guardians, beneficial owners, `price_check`, ...) answers its 404 as JSON whatever `Accept` says; the helper does not know the route. The path extension stands for `(.:format)` on every route, which is wrong only for the `format: false` routes (`/features.md`, `follow_from_embed_form`, `product_build_player_file`) and for a `format` taken from a body. (3) The v2 API's own 404s (`error_with_object`, `e404_json`) are `render json:` and do not depend on `Accept`. (4) A request with `Accept: */*` to `GET /products/:id/other_refund_policies.json` is handed back to Rails by Node today (the replay's stand-in answered 204); unexplored.

## Redirect-only routes

`spec/requests/route_redirects_parity_spec.rb` and `route_redirects_signed_in_spec.rb` (101 examples, all with fixtures) replay in `route-redirects.replay.test.ts` (lane 24: `gumroad_test_24`, Redis 24/25/28/29/32, replay Redis 32, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c24`). Recording showed that a plain `redirect("/path")` drops the query string, `redirect(path:)` rebuilds it with `to_query` (sorted, `CGI.escape`d), the block forms append `request.query_string`, and a request that carries a session cookie gets it written back. The router's redirect has an empty body: `rackRedirect` used to send the "You are being redirected" page and now sends none. Hand-backs, both proved with `hands a ...` examples: a nested query (`a[]=1`, `a[b]=1`) on the three `/subscriptions` links, and malformed percent-encoding in a path parameter. Open: `/animation` loses to a top-level Discover taxonomy named `animation` in Rails (`DiscoverTaxonomyConstraint` comes first); none is seeded, and the redirect is not path-gated against it.

No debt line was open for these.

## Rack::Attack-throttled routes

`config/initializers/rack_attack.rb` counts requests in Rails' Redis. Node counts what it answers itself (`src/server/controllers/rack-attack.ts`): the same Redis, keys (`rack::attack:<window>:<rule>:<discriminator>`), expiry (`period - now % period + 1`, reset on every hit), limits and backoff tiers, the 429 body, `Retry-After` and the Rack envelope of Rails' responder. There is one counter, called from two places: `processAction` (mutations, handlers, redirects, Inertia visits) and `pageEnvelope` (the full page load of an `inertia` page, which the proxy builds without `processAction`). Both count ahead of the action chain, and `forwardToRails` takes the count back (`DECR`) so a request Node hands back is counted once, by Rails; the proxy takes `pageEnvelope`'s count back before it sends a redirect or a bare 400 through `processAction`. `rack-attack-single-count.test.ts` fails on a second count (recorded INCRBYs, net zero after a hand-back, the two call sites and the importers of the module); breaking `processAction` or `pageEnvelope` to count twice fails it (checked by hand, 4 and 3 failures).

Every rule of the Ruby file is modelled, in its declaration order (production-only ones behind `RACK_ATTACK_PRODUCTION_RULES=1` or `RAILS_ENV=production`). `nextjs/scripts/golden/rack-attack-throttles.rb` loads the initializer a second time as production and dumps every throttle (389 names, limits and periods) and the keys and TTLs Rack::Attack writes for 47 requests; `rack-attack-rules.test.ts` pins both. `rack-attack-throttles.test.ts` mirrors the path and method of every rule (the path literals are read back from the Ruby file) and fails when a `routes.json` entry matches one that Node does not count (`countsInNode`: a mutation, handler, redirect or `inertia` page) or that is not justified in `ALLOWED`.

What the lane added to the counter (lane 25: `gumroad_test_25`, `gumroad_replay_25`, Redis 1-4 recording and 3/5 replay, container `gr-ruby-c25c`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c25c`):

- `invalid_params` (5 per minute, 7 tiers): a `%` that does not start a hex escape, more than 4096 pairs, or 32 or more nested brackets in a key. Rack leaves invalid UTF-8 alone (the golden counts nothing for it), and Node's parser cannot read it, so that request hands back. The rules that read a parameter skip an unparseable request, as the backoff wrapper's `rescue` does.
- The Warden-user rules (`/settings/totp/confirm`, `/settings/passkeys`, `/settings/passkeys/registration_options`, the marketing execute): the user comes from the session cookie (`resolveRequestAuth`). A request signed in by the remember cookie alone has no Warden user at that point in Rails (the cookie is read by a strategy in the controller), so it is not counted, and the proxy hands it to Rails anyway. The totp rule counts a signed-out request under the empty identifier, as `"#{nil}"` does.
- The product `PUT`/`PATCH` rules with the VIP buckets (`/vip/ip`, `/vip/params`). Node asks the database for the token's owner and sums `payments.completed`; Rails caches that answer for 10 minutes (`rack_attack:vip_token:<sha>` in `Rails.cache`), so a creator who just crossed $5,000 counts as a VIP in Node up to 10 minutes earlier. Node does not serve the product writes, so this only matters once they are ported.
- Brand accounts, `/users/auth/facebook`, `/`, `/discover_search`, `/purchases` (two rules), `/stripe/setup_intents`, `/settings/credit_card`, the help contact form, the OAuth device and token rules, passkey login, `/:username/affiliate_requests`, the AI product details generation and the ACME challenge (keyed on the path too, so every token has its own budget).

Pages and routes that came back to Node because the counter now sees them: `GET /signup`, `GET /discover`, the storefront root (`seller:/`) and product root (`product:/`), `GET /purchases` (the 301 to `/library`), the ACME challenge on seller and product hosts, `POST /settings/totp/confirm`, `POST /settings/passkeys` and `/registration_options`, `POST /products/:product_id/marketing_actions/:id/execute.json`. Their earlier fixtures replay natively again (`parity/src/rails-only.ts` is empty; `rack-attack-routes.replay.test.ts` is gone). `spec/requests/rack_attack_routes_parity_spec.rb` records 19 examples for them: "counts ..." reads the keys Rack::Attack wrote, "answers 429 ..." puts the first throttle at its limit (`Rack::Attack.cache.count` for the user rules) and expects the 429 and a `Retry-After`, "hands a registration without a pending challenge to Rails" leaves no count behind in Node; `rack-attack-routes.replay.test.ts` replays them against `next dev` with the counter on Rails' Redis db.

Still Rails whole, with the reason:

| Route | Rule | Why it is not Node's |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `POST /mobile/forgot_password` | `password_reset/ip`, `password_reset/email` | the API-host twin of `POST /users/forgot_password`; its `redirect_back` and `login_url` on the API host are not recorded |
| `POST /sellers/brand_accounts(.json)` | production `/ip:/sellers/brand_accounts*` | no Node handler (creates a user and sends a Devise confirmation mail) |
| `/users/auth/facebook` | production `/ip:/users/auth/facebook` | Rails has no such route (it answers 404); Node has no handler for it either, the rule only matters if one is added |
| `POST /v2/products`, `PUT`/`PATCH /v2/products/:id`, `GET /v2/products/comps`, `POST /v2/products/:id/preview_custom_html`, `POST /v2/media`, `POST /internal/ai_product_details_generations` | product, preview, media and VIP rules per IP and token; the hourly AI throttle | counted by the single counter and answered in Node up to the first write (see WS-17 rest 2); the writes, the comps search, the sanitizer and the media download still hand back whole |
| `/users/auth/facebook` | production `/ip:/users/auth/facebook` | Rails has no such route (it answers 404, pinned by `facebook_omniauth_route_spec.rb`: the provider list excludes it, a GET falls to `application#e404_page`, a POST raises a routing error); Node has no handler for it either, and no second Rack::Attack counter is added: the rule only matters if a route is added |
| `POST /v2/products`, `PUT`/`PATCH /v2/products/:id`, `GET /v2/products/comps`, `POST /v2/products/:id/preview_custom_html`, `POST /v2/media` | product, preview, media and VIP rules per IP and token | no product create/update port, no `PageSanitizer`, no media upload |
| `POST /login/passkey(/options)`, `/oauth/device*`, `POST /help/contact`, `/stripe/setup_intents`, `/settings/credit_card`, `GET /discover_search`, `GET /` on the Gumroad domain, mobile purchases index | their rules | no Node route yet |
| a page without `inertia: true` (an App Router page the proxy serves without `pageEnvelope`) | any | not counted by Node, so `rack-attack-throttles.test.ts` rejects such an entry when it matches a throttle; none does today |

WS-10 rest (lane 23: `gumroad_test_23`, `gumroad_replay_23`, Redis 45/48/49 recording, 57 Rack::Attack and 58 replay, container `gr-ruby-c23`). `nextjs_auth_confirmations` now also serves `POST /users/confirmation` (`confirmations-create.ts`, 7 examples in `confirmations_create_parity_spec.rb`; the flash is compared decrypted, the queued `ResendConfirmationEmailJob` and the `confirmation_sent_at` claim through the jobs and db channels; a blank, unknown or already confirmed address renders the ERB `new` view and hands back). `nextjs_custom_domain_verify` serves `POST /custom_domain/verify` (`custom-domain-verifications.ts`, `services/custom-domain-verification.ts` over `node:dns`, 8 examples in `custom_domain_verifications_parity_spec.rb`; the Rails examples stub `Resolv::DNS`, so the replay proves the paths no DNS record can change (a refused domain, a name nobody configured) and `custom-domain-verification.test.ts` proves the CNAME and A-record branches with an injected resolver. The "name nobody configured" example asks the real resolver for `unconfigured-shop.example.com`: it fails closed when offline. A `product_id` hands back, because the CustomDomain model validations are not in Node). The counter above flipped fourteen routes (two-factor, forgot password, `/products/search`, resend receipt, chat messages, comments, both landing products routes) from "Rails whole" to Node: their earlier fixtures replay natively again (`auth-mutations`, `audience-mutations`, `links-controller`, `purchases-controller`, `users-controller`, `content-delivery-mutations`) and `rack_attack_throttles_parity_spec.rb` trips the limit over HTTP for password reset by IP (4th request), by recipient across IPs (5th), receipt resend (3rd), two-factor (11th) and landing products (31st); `ws10-rest.replay` replays each sequence request by request against a Node counting in its own Redis database and compares the 429 (body, content type, the session cookie Rails re-issues, `Retry-After` in range). With `count > limit` loosened, all five fail. Left in Rails because the rule or the proof is missing: `POST /login`, `PUT /settings` and `POST /signup` (production-only rules, which the test environment does not load, so no recorded example can trip them), the per-user rules that read the Warden user (`totp/confirm`, passkeys, marketing execute), `/`, `/discover` and `/purchases` (pages), the passkey login, brand accounts, the device flow and the OAuth token endpoints (their actions are not ported), and Rack::Attack's path normalisation.

Kept, because the rule does not cover the request: `PATCH /settings` and `PUT`/`PATCH /users/forgot_password` (the rules compare the method exactly), `DELETE /api/v2/products/:id` (the product write rule is `PUT` and `PATCH`), `POST seller:/affiliate_requests` (the rule needs a `/:username` segment), `GET /login`, `GET /two-factor`, `POST /users/sign_in` (the login rule is the exact path `/login`), and `GET /v2/products/comps`, which the `/v2/products/:id` route hands back to Rails. `/products/:id` leaves `search` to Rails (`redirectExcept`), and the discover `/*taxonomy` glob only passes a taxonomy slug.

WS-11n (lane 24: `gumroad_test_24`, Redis 34/50/53/56, replay Redis 32, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c24`; db 54 held five stale keys, so the rack attack db is 56): `GET /blog/p` (3 examples added to `gumroad_blog_spec.rb`, replayed by `public-pages.replay`, 49 tests pass), `DELETE /discover_search_autocomplete` (12 examples in `discover_search_autocomplete_parity_spec.rb`, `discover-search-suggestions.replay`, 14 pass; the discover host only, matching `DiscoverDomainConstraint`) and `POST /test_pings` (12 examples in `test_pings_parity_spec.rb`, `test-pings.replay`, 14 pass). Removing the `/blog/p` and discover `routes.json` entries makes 13 of those replays fail. The recording showed a denied team member gets Rails' 200 generic failure, because `rescue Exception` swallows `Pundit::NotAuthorizedError`.

Profile save (WS-10q, lane 23: `gumroad_test_23`, Redis 45/48/49 recording and 57 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c23`) runs in Node for the JSON request the editor sends: `PUT|PATCH /profile` (`Settings::ProfileController#update`, `nextjs_settings_profile_writes`). It covers the name, bio and the `product_page_storefront_enabled` and `hide_follow_form` switches, the `seller_profile` theme (the model clears the custom style cache), the batched sections upsert (`SaveService#upsert!`) and the tabs, under the same user lock and `SellerProfile.lock` as `with_locked_seller_profile`, with the early and the locked `layout_version` check, tab references resolved to ids and the pruning of unlisted profile sections. Answers match Rails: `{ success: true }`, validation messages as a sentence, the stale-layout, unconfirmed-email, custom HTML and `Invalid section type` errors, and 401 JSON for support and accountant roles. Hands back whole, before any write: any `profile_picture_blob_id` key (Active Storage attach or purge), an Inertia-format request, a blank `custom_html` for a seller who has a page, a rich text section, a section or a layout whose data breaks its JSON schema (the transaction rolls back first), and parameters of a shape `permit` would reshape. 44 examples in `settings_profile_signed_in_spec.rb` replay in `settings-profile.replay` (47 tests; the layout version in a body is recomputed from the seeded section times, and its digest is checked against the one Rails recorded); `SellerProfile#validate_json_data` now accepts data that satisfies `seller_profile.json`. `GenerateSubscribePreviewJob` is not compared (its lock is held by the seller factory in the Rails run). `GET /profile/products/:id` stays in Rails: `ProductPresenter#product_props` for a viewer without a Pundit user needs the call, membership and wishlist paths `productProps` still hands back, so it is not a cheap reuse. The session-with-data branch of the OAuth completion has no recorded hand-back example, because a request spec cannot seed `session[:stripe_connect_data]`. `nextjs_checkout_write_guards` (`controllers/checkout-write-guards.ts`, 22 fixtures in `checkout-write-guards.replay`): discounts create/update/destroy, upsells create/update, `POST /purchases/:purchase_id/pings` and `PATCH /purchases/:purchase_id/variants/:variant_id` answer the 404 and the Pundit 401. `nextjs_discover_categories`: `GET /discover/categories` (an action Rails does not define, so a 404 before any filter) answers a visitor; a signed-in seller hands back because Node seeds the nav items first. WS-10 OmniAuth phases (lane 23: `gumroad_test_23`, Redis 45/48/49 recording, 57 Rack::Attack, 58 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c23`). `nextjs_omniauth_phases` runs the passthru 404 of the seven providers, the Google and Stripe Connect request phase, and the callback checks that end in the failure action in Node (28 examples in `omniauth_phases_parity_spec.rb`, 30 replay tests in `omniauth-phases.replay`; the request phase compares the state for shape and checks the session cookie holds it). Hand-backs, each pinned by a `hands ...` example: a callback with a code (Google and Stripe Connect; the exchange spends the code, so it goes to Rails before the provider is asked), and a request-phase POST with a wrong or missing authenticity token. Not registered: the POST callbacks of Google and Stripe Connect (YouTube, Instagram and TikTok: next paragraph; Twitter and Apple: the paragraph after). None of these paths is in `rack_attack.rb`, so no counter is skipped. The replay and request specs need `public/vite-test` and a no-op `npx` in the Ruby container (the Vite build in `spec_helper`).

WS-10 social connect phases (`nextjs_omniauth_phases`; lane 27: `gumroad_test_27`, `gumroad_replay_27`, Redis 11/12/13/15 recording and 17 replay, container `gr-ruby-c27`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c27`; the recording sets `INSTAGRAM_APP_ID` and `TIKTOK_CLIENT_KEY`, which the test environment lacks, and the replay passes the same values). YouTube, Instagram and TikTok now run in Node up to the token exchange: the POST request phase (provider redirect with the state in the session; Instagram sorted like Faraday, TikTok in the strategy's own order with Rack's escaping), the strategy's flag check (the Flipper actor is the impersonated seller or the signed-in user; TikTok is also off without a client key) answering `/settings/social_connections` or `/login` as a middleware redirect with the session cookie only (`Redirected.middleware`), the `attempted` funnel event of `before_request_phase` for a signed-in user, and the callback checks that end in `failure`: a `failed` event with the sanitized error as `referrer` (`recordSocialConnectEvent` gained `extra`), the "Couldn't connect ..." flash and the redirect to the verified `social_connect_return` destination (token, seller, owner policy and 15 minute window checked, session entry spent either way) or to `/login` for a visitor. 48 examples in `social_connect_omniauth_parity_spec.rb`, 50 replay tests in `social-connect-omniauth.replay` (with the new strategies removed from `omniauth-phases.ts`, 39 fail). Hand-backs, each pinned by a `hands ...` example: a callback with a code (the exchange, the channel/profile fetch, `SocialConnectVerification` and the identity write are not ported), a request phase of a disabled connect that carries `social_connect_return` (it ends in the failure action, a controller, and this route's chain is bare), and a wrong authenticity token; a remember-cookie sign-in and a session Devise ends are handed back without an example. The callback with a code now runs the token exchange in Node as well (30 examples in `social_connect_token_exchange_parity_spec.rb`, 32 replay tests in `social-connect-token-exchange.replay` against a stand-in that asserts the outbound calls, their order, the form body and the credentials; YouTube also checks tokeninfo and the id_token claims, Instagram wraps `data[0]`, TikTok adds `client_key`; `SocialConnectVerification.record_from_*` and the identity upserts are ported). The remaining `hands ...` examples are the ones decided before the code is spent: a YouTube callback with its own `redirect_uri` param, a blank code, an XHR callback. Shapes that can only be seen after the code is spent are thrown as `NotPorted` (a 500, not a hand-back, since the exchange cannot be repeated): a token-endpoint redirect, invalid JSON from tokeninfo or userinfo, network failures other than timeout and DNS, an expired token that has a refresh token, an Instagram `data` that is not an array, a JWT payload that is not an object, and non-integer handle types. Twitter (OAuth 1) and Apple stay handed back whole. Not done in lane 27: Zoom and Google Calendar `oauth_redirect`, Google Calendar `account_info`/`calendar_list`, Discord `server_info`, the Circle provider calls, SellerPlan, passkeys and GET /discover.

WS-10 Twitter (X) and Apple OmniAuth (`nextjs_omniauth_phases`; lane 25: `gumroad_test_25`, `gumroad_replay_25`, Redis 1-4 recording and 5 replay, container `gr-ruby-c25d`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c25d`; nothing touched a live service: the recordings use WebMock answers, a throwaway RSA key for the id_token and the committed test key `spec/support/fixtures/apple_web_test_key.pem` for the client secret). **Twitter** (OAuth 1.0a; `infra/oauth1.ts` signs HMAC-SHA1 like the `oauth` gem, pinned by Twitter's documented signature vector): the POST request phase (signed request-token call, session `oauth.twitter`, authenticate/authorize redirect) and the link callback of a signed-in seller (access-token call, `verify_credentials`, `User.query_twitter` writes, `SocialConnectVerification`, the reconnect-notice clear, `social_connect_return` destination) run in Node, as do the failure branches of a link callback (session expired, a refused or unavailable access token, a timeout). The recording fixes the nonce and timestamp (`OAuth::Client::Helper#generate_key`/`#generate_timestamp` stubbed in Ruby, `OAUTH1_PARITY_NONCE`/`OAUTH1_PARITY_TIMESTAMP` honoured by Node only when `RAILS_ENV=test`) and the replay stand-in compares the whole `Authorization` header with Rails'. 26 examples in `twitter_omniauth_parity_spec.rb`, 28 replay tests in `twitter-omniauth.replay`. **Apple**: the POST request phase (state, nonce, `_apple_oauth_nonce` message-verifier cookie with `SameSite=None`) and the form_post callback up to a sign-in of a user who already has an Apple identity run in Node: the id_token is verified against the JWKS (RS256, iss, aud, iat, exp, nonce) before the code is exchanged, the ES256 client secret is signed from `APPLE_WEB_PRIVATE_KEY` (the replay verifies it with the public half of the test key and compares its header and claims with the recorded ones), and every failure ends in Rails' null-session `302 /users/sign_in` with no cookies and no flash. 22 examples in `apple_omniauth_parity_spec.rb`, 24 replay tests in `apple-omniauth.replay` (replay clock pinned to the recording: the id_token and the cookie expire). With the two controllers removed from `gr-mutation/route.ts`, 19 Twitter and 16 Apple replay tests fail. Hand-backs (parity debt, each pinned by a `hands ...` example, all decided before the single-use verifier or code is spent): the async link page (an ERB view), a request phase answered with a 5xx, a timeout or a refusal (its failure action is a full controller chain; the request-token call is repeated by Rails), a request with a `callback_url` of its own, a wrong authenticity token; Apple: a callback without an id_token or code, GET callbacks, a visitor who is already signed in, and a wrong authenticity token on the request phase (the callback is exempt from CSRF protection, as in Rails); the account-writing cases of Apple moved to Node in the next paragraph. A hand-back after Apple's keys were fetched repeats that idempotent GET. Quirks reproduced on purpose: Apple's client secret has `sub: null` and the token form has no `client_id`, because the gem resolves no authorized client id once an id_token was verified. The picture download of a link callback is ported in the Active Storage paragraph below.

WS-10 Apple account creation and linking (`nextjs_omniauth_phases`, `models/social-apple.ts`, `controllers/omniauth-apple.ts`; lane 25: `gumroad_test_25`, `gumroad_replay_25`, Redis 1-4 recording and 5 replay, container `gr-ruby-c25e`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c25e`; WebMock answers and the committed test key only, nothing touched a live service). `User.find_or_create_for_apple_oauth` now runs in Node, split like the Ruby so every decision is made before Apple's code is spent: `planAppleSignIn` reads, `applyAppleSignIn` writes after the exchange. Ported: the first sign-in (a new `users` row with `provider = apple`, a random 20 character password hashed with bcrypt, the name from the posted `user` JSON with `:` removed, the verified address, the User callbacks shared with `POST /signup` such as `GlobalAffiliate`, the refund policy and `GenerateUsernameJob`, `confirm` and its `AttachPastPurchasesToUserWorker`, the `user_external_authentications` row, the Warden sign-in), a sign-in that matches an account by its address (a confirmed password account is adopted: new random password, `last_active_sessions_invalidated_at`, first-party mobile tokens revoked; an account that already has a provider only gains the identity), a user with a blank name given the one Apple sent (a name is never overwritten), a pending `unconfirmed_email` (signed in, not confirmed) and a user whose `confirmed_at` is nil (confirmed: `confirmed_at`, the stale reset token cleared, the attach job), an address Apple did not verify and a record the account rules refuse (disposable domain; `/signup` with "Sorry, something went wrong", reported unless a `blocked_signup` gate refused it), and a deleted account that matches by address (`/login` with the deleted-account error). 15 new examples in `apple_omniauth_parity_spec.rb` (they replace the four Apple hand-back examples that covered these cases; 33 in all, recorded in one session: the replay clock is pinned to the latest nonce cookie, so a partial re-record fails the older id_tokens), 35 replay tests in `apple-omniauth.replay`; with `planAppleSignIn` forced to hand back every sign-in without an Apple identity 9 fail. The replay checks the random password is a `$2a$04$` digest (and differs from the seeded one for an adopted account), the session's Warden key by bcrypt version and cost, and the devise-pwned lookup by the shape of its 5 character SHA-1 prefix against a stand-in. Still handed back, each pinned by a `hands ...` example: the adoption of an unconfirmed password account (`revoke_unproven_access!`: payout destinations, team seats, credentials); the first sign-in's guest purchases are ported in the next paragraph. Not ported and not recorded: an id_token with a non-string `email`, or a name part that is not a string (both hand back by code). Google, Twitter and Stripe Connect callbacks that create or link an account are unchanged and still handed back: Google (`find_or_create_for_google_oauth2` also downloads the avatar, resolves deleted-placeholder rows and retries on deadlocks, and its token exchange and userinfo call have no stand-in yet), Twitter (profile picture download and `$twitter` lookups), Stripe Connect (`find_or_create_for_stripe_connect_account` also writes a `UserComplianceInfo` and calls `Stripe::Account.retrieve`).

WS-10 Apple guest-purchase attach (`models/social-apple.ts`; lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, `gumroad_replay_22`, Redis 36-39 recording and 40 replay, container `gr-ruby-c22f`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22f`; WebMock answers and the committed test key only, nothing touched a live service).
