The `Purchase.where(email:, purchaser_id: nil).each { attach_to_user_and_card(user, nil, nil) }` loop of `find_or_create_for_apple_oauth` runs in Node after the identity row, as in Rails: `planAppleSignIn` reads the guest rows (`assertAttachable`) before Apple's code is spent, `applyAppleSignIn` calls `changePurchaser` for each (a `purchaser_id` write with the PaperTrail version and the delayed `ElasticsearchIndexerWorker` update, ported for `/r/:id/change_purchaser`). A reassignment-locked purchase (flag bit 32) is skipped like `return false`; the test-purchase and seller guards cannot fire for an account this call just created. A user with purchases lands on `/library` instead of `/dashboard`, which the fixture proves. 5 new examples in `apple_omniauth_parity_spec.rb` (two attach, one locked, three hand-backs; the old single hand-back example is gone; 37 in all, recorded in one session), 39 replay tests in `apple-omniauth.replay` (the replay's job drain now reports a delayed job's `at`; without the attach 2 fail, without the plan check 3). Hand-backs, decided before the code is spent, each pinned by a `hands ...` example: a guest purchase that is a bundle purchase (`attach_unclaimed_bundle_product_purchases` after save), has a `preorder_id` (`preorder.save!`) or has a subscription that is not a gift sender's (`subscription.save!`). Still handed back as before: Google (`find_or_create_for_google_oauth2` avatar download, deleted-placeholder rows, deadlock retry; its token exchange and userinfo call have no stand-in yet), Twitter login and signup (picture download), Stripe Connect (`UserComplianceInfo` write, `Stripe::Account.retrieve`) and the unconfirmed-account adoption above. Their writes call models that Node does not have (`query_google`/`query_twitter` for a new user, `revoke_all_oauth_access!`, webauthn, TOTP, devices, merchant and bank account retirement), so each needs its own recording before it can move.

WS-10 Apple unconfirmed-account adoption (`models/user-adoption.ts`, `models/bank-account.ts`; lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, `gumroad_replay_22`, Redis 36-39 recording and 40 replay, container `gr-ruby-c22g`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22g`; WebMock answers and the committed test key only, nothing touched a live service). A sign-in that brings the first Apple identity to a password account whose `confirmed_at` is nil no longer hands back: `User#revoke_unproven_access!` and `#revoke_all_oauth_access!` run in Node in Rails' two transactions. The first commits the sign-in access (`unconfirmed_email`, `confirmation_token` and the four Twitter columns cleared in one `users` UPDATE; approved device authorizations denied, grants and tokens revoked, resource subscriptions soft-deleted; passkeys, the TOTP credential and devices deleted one row at a time, so the `devices` DELETEs show per row), and its after-commit job is the `ReindexSubscriptionCurrentEmailWorker` of the `users` save. The second commits the payout and team access (`notification_endpoint`, `payment_address`, the invalidated PayPal address and the `check_merchant_account_is_linked` flag; each alive merchant account through `delete_charge_processor_account!(validate: false)`, which saves twice; every merchant account, alive or not, through `mark_retired_by_adoption!` with a `Time.current.iso8601` stamp in `json_data`; alive bank accounts through `mark_deleted!(validate: false)`; non-owner seller memberships, non-owner user memberships and unaccepted invitations soft-deleted with their PaperTrail versions). A merchant account whose `json_data` is not a Hash is replaced by one, as `clear_non_hash_json_data_for_disconnect!` does. New Node models: `BankAccounts` (only `mark_deleted!` and the `after_update_commit` that enqueues `RefreshUserProductsRecommendationEligibilityJob` unless another payout method exists) and the retirement helpers; no validation of those rows is ported, since both transactions save with `validate: false`. 3 new examples in `apple_omniauth_parity_spec.rb` (every way in at once, payout rows that no longer validate with a non-Hash `json_data`, and an account with nothing set up; they replace the hand-back example; 39 in all, re-recorded in one session because the replay clock is pinned to the latest nonce cookie), 41 replay tests in `apple-omniauth.replay`; with the `revokeUnprovenAccess` call removed 5 fail. The replay masks the `retired_by_adoption_at` stamp (checked to be the sign-in's own moment, the rest of `json_data` compared) and collapses the duplicate `RefreshMerchantAccountProductsRecommendationEligibilityJob` pushes of the recording, since that job holds an `until_executing` lock that the recording's test adapter does not enforce. Still handed back before the code is spent: an adoption where a merchant account's `json_data` starts with `{` but is not valid JSON. The Google login/signup adoption (`query_google`) calls the same `revokeUnprovenAccess`, so that prerequisite is paid.

WS-10 Active Storage attach and the Twitter picture (`models/active-storage-attach.ts`, `models/profile-picture.ts`, `infra/image-identify.ts`; lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, `gumroad_replay_22`, Redis 36-39 recording and 40 replay, container `gr-ruby-c22h`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22h`; the recording sets `TWITTER_APP_ID`/`TWITTER_APP_SECRET`; WebMock answers for Twitter and the picture host, MinIO as the bucket, nothing touched a live service). `user.avatar.attach(io:, filename:, content_type:)` + `avatar.blob.save!` + `avatar.analyze` run in Node: a blob row (28 base36 character key, MD5 checksum, size, the content type read from the magic bytes, `{"identified":true}`, the configured service name), the attachment row and the user's `touch_later` in one transaction, the `PUT` to the public bucket after the commit (`Content-MD5`, `Content-Type`, `x-amz-acl: public-read`), the second touch of `blob.save!`, then the inline analysis (`{"identified":true,"width":..,"height":..,"analyzed":true}` and a third touch) and the `ActiveStorage::AnalyzeJob` the attachment's create-commit hook enqueues (pushed to Sidekiq like `purge_later`, queue `default`; the recording runs ActiveJob on the test adapter, so the spec asserts the payload and the replay compares Node's push to it). `User#twitter_picture_url` is ported on top of it (a bearer token by the client credentials grant, `users/show`, `_normal.` -> `_400x400.`, `URI::DEFAULT_PARSER.escape`, the download), and the Twitter link callback of a seller with no avatar no longer hands back: 4 examples in `twitter_omniauth_parity_spec.rb` replace the hand-back one (29 in all), 31 replay tests in `twitter-omniauth.replay` against a stand-in bucket that answers the upload from memory and checks the blob row existed, the key, checksum, type, ACL and bytes (the replay's blob key is random and ignored in the row diff, compared to the upload path instead); without the attach 2 fail. The recorder gained `bodyBase64` for a body that is not text and the upload headers of calls to the storage endpoint. Deviations, on purpose: (1) Rails ends `twitter_picture_url` with `avatar_url`, which renders the 400x400 variant (a `active_storage_variant_records` row, a second blob, its attachment and a second upload of ImageMagick's re-encoding). The bytes cannot be reproduced, so Node leaves the variant to the first render that asks for it, and the recording stubs `avatar_url` to leave it out; (2) Rails reads the bucket before the upload (a 404 existence probe from the avatar validation) and downloads the blob for the analysis, Node does neither and analyses the bytes it holds; (3) the gem keeps its bearer token for the life of the process, Node asks for a new one on each lookup. Shapes that only show after the verifier is spent, so they cannot be handed back, are thrown as `NotPorted` (a 500): a picture that is not a PNG or JPEG, one over 10 MB, a JPEG whose EXIF orientation is not top left or a PNG with an eXIf chunk (ImageMagick swaps the dimensions for every orientation named Left or Right, 2 and 4 included), and a user that no longer validates (Rails then leaves an orphan blob). A picture that cannot be fetched (Twitter 404, a refused download) leaves the seller without one, as in Rails. Google's avatar does not go through `attachAvatar`: it is attached before the user is saved and never analysed (`assignAvatar`, below).

WS-10 Twitter login and signup (`models/social-twitter.ts` `findOrCreateForTwitterOauth`/`queryTwitter`, `controllers/omniauth-twitter.ts` `loginOrSignup`; same lane, container and recording as the paragraph above). `User.find_or_create_for_twitter_oauth!` and the login half of `OmniauthCallbacksController#twitter` run in Node: the user holding the Twitter id or a new one (`provider = twitter`, a random password, `skip_confirmation!`, the User creation callbacks and the password breach check), `query_twitter` without the funnel's `connected` event (profile fields, `SocialConnectVerification`, the picture of a user with none), the credentials saved on the user, then the branches: a team member (`/login`, "You're an admin..."), a deleted account, a user with an email (the sign-in or the two-factor preparation of `sign_in_or_prepare_for_two_factor_auth`, then `safe_redirect_to /two-factor?next=...`), a user with none (signed in, `/settings`, the `signup_events` row and "Please enter an email address!", or "Please confirm your email address" for a pending change). The refused and unavailable access token of a login now end in the sign-in page failure in Node as they do for a link (2 examples renamed from `hands ...`). 35 examples in `twitter_omniauth_parity_spec.rb`, 37 replay tests in `twitter-omniauth.replay`. Hand-backs, decided before the verifier is spent, each pinned by a `hands ...` example: a login callback of a visitor who is already signed in, and one that would merge a guest cart (`assertNoGuestCart`); the async link as before. Thrown as `NotPorted` after the spend: a record the account rules refuse, a payload with an `errors` entry (Rails raises NoMethodError on the Hash). Not done: a team member's or deleted account's `update!` of the tokens happens before the refusal, as in Rails.

WS-10 Google login and signup (`nextjs_omniauth_phases`; `models/social-google.ts`, `controllers/omniauth-google.ts`, the code-callback registry of `controllers/omniauth-phases.ts`; lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, `gumroad_replay_22`, Redis 36-39 recording and 40 replay, container `gr-ruby-c22j`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22j`; the recording runs OmniAuth for real with WebMock answers for the token exchange, tokeninfo, userinfo and the picture host, MinIO as the bucket, nothing touched a live service). The Google callback runs in Node from the code exchange to the sign-in: `social-connect-exchange.ts` `exchangeGoogleLogin` (token, tokeninfo and its `scope` read, the ID token check, userinfo, in the gem's order), `googleAuthData` (the auth hash, now strict), `User.find_or_create_for_google_oauth2` (live-before-deleted lookup by `google_uid`, the `@deleted.invalid` husk and the live account that holds its address, an unverified address, the lookup by address, adoption of a password account, name, address for new accounts only, `confirm`, the guest-purchase loop, the deadlock/lock-wait retry of two retries then nil, `RecordInvalid` as nil) and `sign_in_with_oauth` (signup page, closed-account refusal, `post_auth_redirect`). 46 examples in `spec/requests/google_omniauth_parity_spec.rb`, 48 replay tests in `google-omniauth.replay` (the 46 and two meta tests). **Why this could not be handed back and what replaced it.** Everything the method decides depends on the userinfo answer, which exists only after Google's single-use code is spent, so no case can be judged beforehand (Apple's id_token arrives with the callback; Google's comes in the token answer). The three shapes the earlier note listed are therefore ported and recorded instead: (a) *guest purchases of a new account's address*: `attach_to_user_and_card` saves the preorder (`models/preorder.ts`, `purchaser_id`, validated) and the subscription (`user_id`, through `Subscriptions.updateBang`) and then `changePurchaser`, whose `after_save` `attach_unclaimed_bundle_product_purchases` (`models/purchase-delivery-writes.ts`: the bundle's unclaimed members in a success state, each locked and reloaded, case-insensitive address match, reassignment-locked skipped, a failure reported per member) attaches the bundle's products; examples for a plain purchase, a locked one, a bundle in two orders, a preorder, a subscription and a gift sender's subscription (not attached). Apple's loop is not changed (it still hands these back before its spend). (b) *the picture*: `google_picture_url` runs before `save!`. `assignAvatar` writes the blob row at once (`{"identified":true}`: Google never analyses, the validation's analysis cannot find the file) and stages the attachment on the user (`ivars.pending_avatar`); a new or changed user gets the attachment row, the bucket upload and the `AnalyzeJob` from the `afterSave` of the caller's own `save!` (the new `avatar_is_valid` extension check runs there), a persisted unchanged user is saved by `attach` itself. Recorded for a new user, an account found by its address (which the sign-in changes), a returning user, a JPEG, a `sz` query kept apart from the others, a doubled scheme and a user who already has a picture (nothing downloaded), a refused download and a text type (no picture), and a GIF sent as a PNG (Marcel names the bytes' type, the extension check fails the save, so the sign-up is refused and the blob stays: `marcelContentType` knows PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP and PDF signatures, bytes that match none keep the declared type). The filename is stored as `attach` was given it (`File.basename` of the URL, a `?` included): ActiveStorage sanitizes on read only, which also corrects Twitter's attach (its names had no unsafe characters, so nothing recorded changes). (c) *a picture URL `URI.parse` rejects*: the gem's `image_url` runs inside the strategy, so a rejection is `fail!(e.message)` and ends in the failure action with the flash `Could not authenticate you from GoogleOauth2 because "Bad uri ..."`, not a 500. `infra/ruby-uri.ts` reproduces `URI.parse` (URI 1.1.1, RFC 3986 with the per-scheme rules for http, https, ws, wss, ftp, file, ldap(s) and mailto), `query=` and `to_s`, proven by `parity/golden/google-image-url.rb` (5610 cases through the real gem's `image_url`, 1721 of them raising) in 5611 vitest cases; for ftp, file, ldap(s) and mailto only acceptance is compared, not the printed form. A picture that is not a String ends the same way (`undefined method 'gsub' for ...`), and so do the other shapes of the answer the auth hash cannot read (`rubyIndex` carries Ruby 3.4's messages). The `Left to Rails before the code is spent` examples are the ones Node can see: a visitor who is signed in, an XHR callback, a `redirect_uri` parameter, the async Twitter link state and an `error_description` (5 `hands ...` examples; the old phases example that handed the callback whole is gone). **Never throw after the spend.** An error that is not one of the above (a database error that is no deadlock, a stand-in the bucket refuses, a JSON column Ruby would also reject) is reported and ends in the failure action (`Could not authenticate you from GoogleOauth2 because "Internal error"`) where Rails would answer 500; the code is gone either way, and the visitor can start again. Residual, unproven: Marcel signatures other than the five above (an unusual file sent as an image type is taken for the declared type), OpenURI's redirect rules (Node follows every redirect, OpenURI refuses `https` to `http`), invalid JSON in a provider answer and the exact wording of provider errors Node reports with its own message (a redirect from a provider endpoint, a token that has to be refreshed, a network error other than a timeout), a bucket upload that fails after the commit (Rails raises from the commit callback, Node reports), and `revoke_unproven_access!` on a merchant account whose `json_data` is not valid JSON (Apple hands this back from its id_token; here it is an internal error). Deviations on purpose, as for Twitter: the 400x400 variant `avatar_url` renders is left to the first render (the recording stubs `avatar_url`), and the existence probe Rails makes before the upload is not repeated. The replay stand-ins hold Node to the same provider calls in the same order (WebMock keeps only the last value of a repeated query name, so the picture URL is compared with that rule), the upload to the bucket (bytes, checksum, type, ACL, after the commit) and the analysis job; seeded rows are made two seconds older than the request because Rails saw a second pass between a factory and the request on second-precision columns. The generated "Routes that hand back" list in `remaining-work.md` still names the Google callback, as it does the YouTube one: `omniauth-phases.ts` builds the callback pattern from the strategy name, which the route scan does not read as a literal. **Stripe Connect** (`find_or_create_for_stripe_connect_account`): unchanged. The way out documented earlier (an extra idempotent `Stripe::Account.retrieve` before the code is spent) does not work: the account id (`auth.uid`, the `stripe_user_id` of the token answer) comes back from the token exchange, so there is nothing to retrieve before the spend, and the email, name and country it writes come from that call. The callback with a code stays handed back whole, as a justified decision and not an unproven one; it can only move the way Google's did, by porting every case it can reach after the spend.
WS-10 Stripe Connect callback of a signed-in seller (`nextjs_omniauth_phases`, `controllers/omniauth-stripe-connect.ts`, `exchangeStripeConnect` in `social-connect-exchange.ts`; recorded in `stripe_connect_omniauth_parity_spec.rb`, replayed by `stripe-connect-omniauth.replay`; WebMock answers only, lane `gumroad_test_o1`). The action writes only the session, so it runs after the code is spent: token exchange (platform key as Bearer, no client credentials in the body), the strategy's `/v1/account` call, the seller-already-connected refusal, `Stripe::Account.retrieve`, the unsupported-country refusal (the country name is empty for a code Gumroad has no name for) and `session[:stripe_connect_data]` with the redirect to `/oauth_completions/stripe`; a refused exchange ends in the failure action. **Still hands back whole, before the spend:** a visitor who is not signed in (sign in, `find_or_create_for_stripe_connect_account` and the `disable_stripe_signup` refusal all depend on the account id the exchange returns), an impersonating visitor, a Devise sign-in/sign-out on the way, an XHR callback, a callback without a code, the async Twitter state and an `error_description`. Unproven: a `Stripe::Account.retrieve` error (Rails answers 500; Node reports it and ends in the failure action), a token answer with no `stripe_user_id`. The old `omniauth_phases_parity_spec` example that handed a signed-in callback back is removed. The Google, YouTube, Instagram and TikTok callbacks already run in Node; the route scan lists them only because their patterns are built from the strategy name.
WS-10 form POST callbacks and the Apple GET callback (`ws10-omniauth-3`; `gumroad_test_o3`, Redis 6-9 recording and 43 replay, container `gr-ruby-o3`; WebMock answers only). Devise routes `/users/auth/:provider/callback` to the same action for GET and POST, and `protect_from_forgery` (`null_session`) covers the POST of every provider but Apple (`skip_before_action :verify_authenticity_token, only: [:apple]`): a POST without a valid authenticity token runs the action with a session Rails never writes, so none of that is reproduced and the framework's CSRF check hands it back whole. A POST with a valid token runs the GET handler of Twitter, Stripe Connect, Google, YouTube, Instagram and TikTok (`formCallback`: form or no body only, no XHR; a JSON body is a Rails parameter the middleware never reads). Recorded in `omniauth_phases`, `stripe_connect`, `google`, `twitter` and `social_connect` specs (failures, a signed-in Stripe Connect, a Google sign-in and sign-up, a Twitter link) and replayed by their existing replay files; with the POST registration removed the non-"hands" examples fail. `GET /users/auth/apple/callback` runs the POST handler with a session: the failure action then writes its flash (`error`/`error_description`, a claim the id_token fails (`Iss invalid` and so on) and a refused code (`Invalid credentials`)); a signature, key id or JWKS failure carries a library message and hands back. **ws10-omniauth-4 (stacked on ws10-omniauth-3; `gumroad_test_o4`, Redis 23-26 recording and 33 replay, container `gr-ruby-o4`):** the environment note above was not the environment. (1) The Apple replay pins its clock to the newest `_apple_oauth_nonce` expiry among all fixtures of the file, and the fixtures of the file had been recorded in two sessions a day apart, so the older id_tokens read as expired (`Exp invalid`) and every POST sign-in diverged; the whole file is re-recorded in one session (60 tests pass), and a spec file must be re-recorded whole, never one `describe`. (2) The Twitter `GenerateSubscribePreviewJob` was not a parity bug: it is `until_executed`, Rails' factory took the lock before the request so the recording holds no job, and Node (seeded without the lock) enqueues it as production does when the bio is filled; the Twitter replay now ignores that class as the Apple replay does, which also ends the failure of the 16 GET callbacks. The Twitter POST login and signup success paths are recorded and replay. (3) On the Apple GET callback, an `id_token` signed with another key (`Json::jws::verificationfailed`), an unknown key id (`Json::jwk::set::kidnotfound`) and a blank `error`/`error_reason` (truthy in Ruby, so it fails the callback; `""` or the reason) run in Node. Still handed back: a JWKS answer that is not 200 or cannot be fetched (the message embeds an object address or the HTTP library's text), a callback with no `id_token` or code (the gem reaches the token call), a blank `id_token`, and an `error` that is not a string.

`delete-unused-public-files-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16`, Redis 27), 36 cases. `DeleteUnusedPublicFilesJob` runs in Node; `models/public-file.ts` ports only the validations `mark_deleted!` runs. PublicFile's before_validation callbacks (blank display name with an attached file, blank public id, nil file type or group) are not ported, so a batch with such a row hands back after the preload and before any write, as does a resource type other than Link or User and a blob with preview or variant images. The scratch schema's Active Storage foreign keys are dropped by the script, as in the asset preview golden. No debt line was open for it.

`regenerate-sales-related-products-infos-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16`, Redis 27), 30 cases. `RegenerateSalesRelatedProductsInfosJob` runs fully in Node. Rails casts a string limit and treats a null limit as no limit; the port hands those back (and a float or string product id, which Ruby fails on later) rather than reproduce the casts. No debt line was open for it.

`update-sales-related-products-infos-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16`, Redis 27), 29 cases; its test needs a real Redis (`JOBS_REDIS_URL`, replayed on db 33) for the unique-jobs lock and is skipped without one. `UpdateSalesRelatedProductsInfosJob` runs in Node. `transaction()` gained an `isolation: "read_committed"` option (Prisma `isolationLevel`), and `sqlReplay` leaves the BEGIN out of an isolated transaction because Rails sends `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL` and `BEGIN` as one batch that the recorder drops with its other SET statements. The deadlock is injected in the golden script (a Mysql2 error 1213 recorded as MySQL would answer), so the retry and backoff are proven against Ruby's behaviour, not against a real MySQL deadlock. `models/sales-related-products-info.ts` ports only `update_sales_counts` and the purchase scope. No debt line was open for it.

`update-taxonomy-stats-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16`, Redis 27), 18 cases. `UpdateTaxonomyStatsJob` runs in Node. The scratch MySQL accepted a NULL write into a NOT NULL stat column (non-strict mode), so a null cardinality hands back instead of reproducing a write production MySQL would reject; the other answer shapes are replayed. `golden/support/rails_cases.rb` now orders a dump by its first column when the table has no `id` (`taxonomy_hierarchies`). No debt line was open for it.

Wishlist pages (WS-11/WS-12, `nextjs_wishlist_show` and `nextjs_wishlist_products_index`; lane 15: `gumroad_test_15`, Redis 56/57/59/60 recording and 63 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15`) run in Node on the owner's host. `GET seller:/wishlists/:wishlist_id/products` (`controllers/wishlist-products-index.ts`, `WishlistPresenter#public_items`): the 20-card page with Pagy's metadata (an overflowing page is empty with `prev` the last page), `option` through `variantToOption` (versions and skus), `giftable` and `purchasable`, the owner's global affiliate in each card URL. 12 examples in `wishlist_products_index_parity_spec.rb` replay in `wishlist-products-index.replay` (14 tests; 12 fail with the `routes.json` entry removed). `GET seller:/wishlists/:id` (`page-props/storefront/wishlist-pages.ts`, `presenters/wishlist.ts`): `Wishlists/Show` for a signed-out visitor with no `layout`, with `PageMeta::Wishlist` (title, description, canonical, `index,follow` only for a recommendable wishlist with three distinct products, the `ItemList` JSON-LD) and the favicon tags. 13 examples in `wishlist_show_page_parity_spec.rb` replay in `wishlist-show.replay` (15 tests; 11 fail with the entry removed). Follow-up (lane 15, ws11-12-port-2): the signed-in page (`following`, `can_edit`, `discover_opted_out`, the owner's `user: null`), `layout=profile` (`creator_profile`), `layout=discover` (`taxonomies_for_nav`), an unknown or blank `layout` and a blank `recommended_by` run in Node (`signedOutOnly` removed; 11 more examples in `wishlist_show_page_parity_spec.rb`). `wishlists/products#index` answers the 500 Rails does for an absent, zero, negative or non-numeric `page` (`fetch_as_production` records the production error page; the replay compares the status), reads a blank page as 1 and `1abc` as 1 (Pagy's `to_i`); 6 examples, which fail with the controller change reverted. Hands back: a discover layout for a visitor whose cart and purchases hold 4 or more distinct products (ws14-discover-3 ported fewer), a page above 2^31-1, a host with no seller, and any card the product presenter cannot prove (a seller on buyer local currency). `GET /wishlists/:id` on the Gumroad domain does not exist in Rails. The IP-detected `detected_buyer_currency` (`InertiaRendering#buyer_currency_footer_actions`) is now shared by `PageSpec.buyerCurrencyFooter` for `Users/Show`, `Products/Show`, `Discover/Index` and `Wishlists/Show` (`detectedBuyerCurrency` moved to `infra/buyer-currency-countries.ts`); before, those pages left the key out. A recorded example reads the GeoLite2 test database (`stub_const GEOIP`) for `81.2.69.142` and expects `gbp`; with the flag off it fails (`detected_buyer_currency  missing in next`). The storefront profile, product page, discover and profile products replays still pass. No debt line was open for these.

Stripe disconnect (WS-21/WS-23, `ws21-handbacks`, lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, Redis 36/37/38 recording and 43 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22`). `POST /settings/stripe/disconnect` runs in Node (`nextjs_payouts_guards`, `models/stripe-merchant-account-disconnect.ts`). `StripeMerchantAccountManager.disconnect` makes no Stripe call for a Connect account (`delete_charge_processor_account!` marks the local row), so it needed no cassette: the Connect row is soft-deleted and its processor columns cleared (two saves), the seller's `check_merchant_account_is_linked` flag is cleared, the managed account holding an unpaid balance, else the one named by the active bank account, is reactivated unless retired by adoption, and `MerchantAccount`'s `before_save` queues `RefreshMerchantAccountProductsRecommendationEligibilityJob` after commit when the seller has no other payout method. The audit note for a team admin is written as in Rails, only when a Connect account existed. Writes run in Rails' order, each save in its own transaction; the seller and the Connect account are validated first, so a record that no longer validates hands back before the first write. Hands back: a seller with no Connect account (Rails answers 500 on nil) and a Connect `json_data` that is not a Hash. 7 recorded examples in `payouts_guards_parity_spec.rb` replace the two stubbed "hands a disconnect" ones; with the controller reverted 6 replay tests fail. `MerchantAccounts` is now a writable model (presence and uniqueness validations, `JsonData` merge, the recommendability callback); the PayPal and Stripe account creation paths are not ported. The `updated_at` of a row the request touches is not compared (a seeded row is as old as the replay). No debt line was open for it. The payments settings saves (Stripe account create and update, bank accounts, PayPal connect), instant payouts, the checkout charge, refund and dispute paths and the five unported routes were not attempted in this lane.

`send-payment-reminder-worker.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16`, Redis 27/28), 25 cases. `SendPaymentReminderWorker` runs fully in Node: the grouped `find_each` over sellers holding more than 1000 unpaid cents (`findInBatches` gained a `groupBy` tail for the GROUP BY / HAVING that Rails keeps after the `id >` condition, 1,001-seller case proves the second batch), then the bank, Stripe Connect and PayPal lookups in the order and short-circuiting Ruby evaluates them, and one `ContactingCreatorMailer#remind` `MailDeliveryJob` push on the `low` queue per reminded seller. A `json_data` that is not a hash fails with the same `json_data must be a hash`. No hand-back; the mail is rendered by Ruby or Node per `mailRoute`, as for the other ported mail jobs. No debt line was open for it.

`radar-sync-value-lists-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16`, Redis 27/28), 41 cases. `Radar::SyncValueListsJob` runs fully in Node (`radar-sync-value-lists-job.ts`, sharing the list and item helpers of `radar-value-list-jobs.ts`): the three 25-hour selections per type (added, unblocked, expired; the card ones filtered with the same MySQL `REGEXP`), the per-row reload that skips a block cleared or deleted after the SELECT, and the Stripe calls through the capture server, including `already exists` races and a failure on the email list stopping the card sync. A write made while the first item is added is replayed from the recording. No hand-back. No debt line was open for it.

`alert-on-retired-managed-account-activity-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16`, Redis 27/28), 49 cases. `AlertOnRetiredManagedAccountActivityJob` runs in Node: the account read, the `active?` exit, the three legs (balance transactions, charges, the owner's balances) created at or after the retirement with the 25-row cap, and the `InternalNotificationWorker` push to `payouts`. `concerns/ruby-time-parse.ts` ports `Time.iso8601` (the date-only form is rejected by this Ruby, as the golden shows; month, day, minute, second and offset range errors raise ArgumentError with Ruby's message). Hand-backs, before any write: a non-Integer account id, a retirement time that is not a String, a year outside 1000..9999, and a time with more than one out-of-range component or an out-of-range offset together with another bad component (which check Ruby trips first is not pinned). The recorder ran with TZ=UTC, so a time without a zone is read as UTC. No debt line was open for it.
`remove_credit_card` and `set_country` (WS-21, `nextjs/ws21-money`, lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, Redis 36/37/38 recording and 43 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22`; `nextjs_settings_payments_guards`). `User#remove_credit_card` makes no processor call: it refuses while a preorder authorization or a paid, live subscription needs the card (400 with the empty error text), else clears `credit_card_id` with a full save (400 with the validation messages if the seller no longer validates) and notes the change for a team admin. 8 recorded examples (clears the card, no card, team admin note, preorder, live subscription, test-only subscription, ended subscription, invalid seller). `set_country` for a first country writes in Node: `dup_and_save!` on the compliance revision (an unsaved blank revision is inserted already deleted, as Rails does, then the copy), the seller's currency moves to `Country#default_currency` inside the same transaction (`country-currency.ts`), the two after-create-commit jobs are enqueued for each inserted revision (Detect... before Handle..., the latter 5 seconds out) and the non-owner audit note is written; 4 recorded examples. Still handed back: a country the ISO list does not know, Japan (kana validations), a revision with a legal guardian, a birthday under 13, a business country of Japan and any open compliance request (a new revision can `mark_provided!` it). The jobs replay compares the BullMQ and Sidekiq stores together and sorts by record, because the two stores have no common enqueue order; the order between the two jobs of one record is therefore not proven. The `updated_at` of a seeded compliance revision is not compared (a seeded row is as old as the replay). With the two actions reverted, 4 and 8 replay tests fail. The Stripe-account half of `PUT/PATCH /settings/payments`, `paypal_connect`, the remediation leftovers, instant payouts and the checkout charge, refund and dispute writes were not attempted in this lane yet.
Remaining call availabilities on a seller's or custom domain's host (WS-12, `nextjs_remaining_call_availabilities`; lane 15, ws11-12-port-2; `controllers/remaining-call-availabilities.ts`): routes.rb declares `resources :remaining_call_availabilities` inside `UserCustomDomainConstraint`, but the controller defines only `index`. `GET seller:/products/:product_id/remaining_call_availabilities.json` answers the slots as on the Gumroad domain; `new`, `edit`, `show`, `create`, `update` (PATCH and PUT) and `destroy` raise `AbstractController::ActionNotFound` before any filter and answer 404 in Node, with no CSRF check (`skipForgeryProtection`). A signed-in visitor hands back (Node's chain seeds dashboard nav items first). 10 examples in `seller_remaining_call_availabilities_parity_spec.rb` replay in `seller-remaining-call-availabilities.replay` (12 tests; 9 fail with the `routes.json` entries removed). The id segment is `[A-Za-z0-9_=-]+`, narrower than Rails' `[^./]+`; any other id stays with Rails.

Rack::Attack counter in Node (`controllers/rack-attack.ts`; lane 15, ws11-12-port-2). `GET /offer_codes/compute_discount` is back in routes.json (`nextjs_checkout_offer_codes`, handler `compute_discount`) and no longer Rails-only: processAction counts the request in Rack::Attack's own Redis (`RACK_ATTACK_REDIS_HOST`) before it answers, with the same keys (`rack::attack:<epoch / period>:<name>:<discriminator>`, name `/ip:/offer_codes/compute_discount`, tiers `/2` to `/5`), expiry (rest of the window plus one second), limits (60 in 30 seconds, then `rpm * level` in `8 ** level` seconds), discriminator normalizer (strip, downcase), `any?` short-circuit across tiers, `CF-Connecting-IP`-else-`X-Forwarded-For` client address, loopback safelist and 429 (`Retry later`, `Retry-After`, the SecureHeaders non-CSP headers). A request Node then hands back to Rails is uncounted first (`forwardToRails`), so Rails' own count is the only one. 6 new examples in `offer_codes_compute_discount_parity_spec.rb` (the sixtieth request answers, the sixty-first and a second-tier overflow answer 429, another address, the `X-Forwarded-For` client, a loopback client) put the earlier requests of the window in the counter directly and freeze the clock; the replay adds the same count to Node's Redis after its reset and checks `Retry-After` against the clock (the three 429 examples fail with the `throttled(...)` call removed). `rack-attack-throttles.test.ts` lists the route in ALLOWED with the check that rack_attack.rb's rule and the handler's tiers agree. Not verified: the Redis key bucket when a request lands across a window boundary between the two counts (a race the Ruby side has too), a request with no forwarding header (taken as loopback, because production traffic always carries one), and `X-Forwarded-For` spoofing checks beyond the rightmost-public-entry rule (`remoteIp`).

Follow-up: `/charge_data`, `/license_key_lookup_data` and `/paypal_charge_data` run in Node behind `nextjs_public_lookup_data` with `groupedReceiptThrottles` (`/ip:<path>`, 3 in 20 s, plus the shared `/params:grouped_receipt:email` budget, 6 in 60 s to level 6, keyed on the stripped, downcased `email`; a non-string `email` hands back). 6 new examples in `public_data_parity_spec.rb` replay in `ws11-public.replay` (4 fail with the `throttled(...)` call removed).

WS-10 rest 2 (lane 23, container `gr-ruby-c23b`). `rack_attack_throttles_parity_spec.rb` now also trips the counters that had no example (comments 6th, comment edit 6th, chat messages 61st, `/products/search` 301st; the last two freeze the clock because the recorded requests outlast the window, and the replay sends the 299 identical middle searches without seeding them). The production-only rules are recorded by loading `rack_attack.rb` again with `Rails.env` stubbed as production inside the example (`with_production_throttles`) and restoring the throttle table afterwards. Node counts them (`productionOnly` in `rack-attack.ts`: on when `RAILS_ENV=production`, or `RACK_ATTACK_PRODUCTION_RULES=1` for the replay) together with `logins/login`; `POST /login` and `PUT /settings` are registered in `routes.json` again (`nextjs_auth_login_writes`, `nextjs_settings_main_writes`), their fixtures replay natively (`auth-mutations`, `settings-main`: 115 tests), and a login and a settings sequence trip at the 4th request. `parity/golden/rack-attack-throttles.json` is dumped with the production rules loaded (stub `Rails.env.production?`, reload the initializer, print `Rack::Attack.throttles`). `GET|POST /signup` stay in Rails: a page is not counted by Node and account creation (bcrypt digest, random tokens, confirmation mail, `User` callbacks) is not ported, which also keeps `find_or_create_for_google_oauth2` and the OAuth callbacks that create a user in Rails. `GET /integrations/zoom/account_info` runs in Node (`integrations-zoom.ts`, `services/ratelimit.ts` on the `ratelimit` gem's keys, `nextjs_integrations_zoom`): 7 examples in `integrations_zoom_parity_spec.rb` with Zoom stubbed by WebMock, replayed against a stand-in that answers the recorded calls; the calls Node makes are compared with Rails'. A Zoom answer that is not a JSON object raises (500) as Rails does; not recorded. Not started: SellerPlan, Google Calendar, Discord `server_info`, Circle's provider calls, the OAuth device and token endpoints, passkeys, the Warden-user rules, `/`, `/discover`, `/purchases`, `invalid_params`.

WS-11/12 leftovers, third pass (lane 15: `gumroad_test_15b`/`gumroad_replay_15b`, Redis 56/57/59/60 recording and 63 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15b`, container `gr-ruby-c15b`). Three controllers run in Node as `GET` mutation handlers (`registerMutations`, `skipForgeryProtection`), each with a Rails `parity: true` request spec recorded with `PARITY_RECORD=1` and a replay file; each replay was also run with its flag off and fails (17 of 20, 28 of 31 and 9 of 12 examples), so the examples depend on the port:

- `ProductReviewVideos::{Streams,StreamingUrls,UploadContexts}Controller` (`nextjs_product_review_videos`; `controllers/product-review-videos.ts`; `product_review_videos_parity_spec.rb`, 18 examples, `product-review-videos.replay`, 20 tests): the SMIL (`<smil><body><switch><video src=...>` with the key signed as Ruby does, bug-for-bug: the url is the S3 key), the two urls of `streaming_urls` (the SMIL path carrying the digest, and the MinIO or CDN download link), the digest and Pundit gates (`ProductReviewVideoPolicy#stream?` for the purchaser and the seller of the current account; the SMIL denial is a bare 401 with the SMIL type, the JSON denial the usual `You are not allowed ...` or role message), the 404 of an unknown, removed or non-text id, and `upload_context` (404 JSON for a signed-out visitor, the access key, `s3_url` and the user's `external_id` column, which is a random number string and not an obfuscated id). Signed query strings are masked in the replay (`X-Amz-*`, `Expires`, `Signature`), as in `url-redirects.replay`. Hand-backs: a refused `streaming_urls` request that is not JSON (Rails redirects to the dashboard), a digest param that is not text, a purchase without an email, a video file whose url is not on S3. The approve and reject POSTs were already in `internal-api.ts`.
- `ProductFilesUtilityController` (`nextjs_product_files_utility`; `controllers/product-files-utility.ts`; `product_files_utility_parity_spec.rb`, 29 examples, `product-files-utility.replay`, 31 tests): sign-in (404 JSON, a redirect to the login page for HTML), `set_product` (404 for a missing or foreign product, the `edit?` refusal as 401 JSON or a dashboard redirect), `download_product_files` for one file (JSON `{files: [{url, filename}]}` or a 302 to the signed link; the 404 and the editor redirect with the "no longer available" warning when the object is missing from storage; the library query is `alive_product_files_preferred_for_product`), `download_folder_archive` (the null url, the route that redirects, the 302 to the signed archive, the 404 for HTML). `external_link_title` answers failure in Node for a missing url, a url that is not http or https, `localhost`, and a literal address in `SsrfFilter`'s IPv4 blacklist or `::1`. Hand-backs, each with a "hands a ..." example or a comment: a public url (needs the SSRF-filtered fetch and Nokogiri; no replay can intercept a server's outbound request), more than one file in a request (the library query is `group(:url)`, whose row order is MySQL's), a file whose url another row of the library shares, a folder archive of a version, a url that is not on S3, and IPv6 or hostnames that only resolve to a private address.
- `ProductBuildPlayerFilesController#show` (`nextjs_product_build_player_files`; `controllers/product-build-player-files.ts`, `auth/player-grant.ts`; `product_build_player_files_parity_spec.rb`, 10 examples, `product-build-player-files.replay`, 12 tests): the grant (`ProductBuild.verified_player_grant`: a `MessageEncryptor` message, AES-256-GCM under the `product_build_player_grant` key of `SECRET_KEY_BASE`, parts cut from the end because a url-safe part can end in `-`), the path rules, the 404s (wrong token, garbage, a save grant used for files, a missing object), the signed MinIO redirect with `max-age=300, private`, js and css streamed, svg and xml with the sandbox CSP and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`. The replay pins the server clock one second after the earliest grant was issued (a grant lives 12 hours). `mutation.ts` learned `*glob` segments and passes `rawTarget` (the forwarded path before `new URL` resolves `a/../b`). Hand-backs: a page of the build (html, htm: the save shim and the `product_build_player` kill switch; the Rails examples need `public/js/build-player-indexeddb-bundle.js` from `rake vite:build_widget`, which the lane did not have, so they are not recorded), the save POST (`hands a save to Rails`, not registered), and a storage that is not MinIO (the signed link is a Cloudflare URL). Not proved: a path with `..` segments. Rails answers 404; the replay client resolves the dot segments before sending, so the example cannot be recorded, and the Node check on `rawTarget` is untested.

Not started in this pass (still Rails, no change; the first two were done in ws11-12-port-4, see the paragraph after this one): `Products/{Iframe,Profile,Discover}/Show`, the cover and call-product `PageMeta` tags, the `home#*` ERB pages, `/mcp`, `/claude`, `/muse`, `/gumclaw`, `/js/gumroad*`, `POST /links` and commissions. There is no `oEmbed` route in `config/routes.rb`; the oEmbed cover is the `Product::AsJson`/`AssetPreview` HTML noted above. Any route covered by a Rack::Attack rule stays handed back, as before; none of the routes above is throttled, and `countRackAttack` still runs ahead of each Node answer.

`calculate-sale-numbers-worker.golden.json` (24 cases) and `calculate-payout-numbers-worker.golden.json` (22 cases) were recorded on lane 23 (`gumroad_test_23`, Redis 23). `CalculateSaleNumbersWorker` and `CalculatePayoutNumbersWorker` run in Node. A Float sum past 2**53 is not reproduced (Ruby's `BigDecimal` conversion of a Float goes through its shortest digits): it hands back before the key is written, as does a string sum for the sale numbers and a boolean or object cardinality. `purchase-search.ts` now sends a `ZonedInstant` (`rails-time-zone.ts`) as the offset-bearing `iso8601` string Rails writes for a `TimeWithZone` (`-07:00`), where a plain `Date` is still sent in UTC. No debt line was open for either.

`seller-update-worker.golden.json` was recorded on lane 23 (`gumroad_test_23`, Redis 23), 53 cases. `SellerUpdateWorker` runs in Node. `Date.today` in Ruby is the server's local date; Node uses the UTC date, which is the production server's zone (the recorder ran with TZ=UTC). No debt line was open for it.

`subtitle-file-size-worker.golden.json` was recorded on lane 23 (`gumroad_test_23`, Redis 23), 24 cases, with the S3 HEAD answered by WebMock (`capture_http!`; the recorder's `AWS_S3_ENDPOINT` is `http://localhost:9000`). `SubtitleFileSizeWorker` runs in Node; `models/subtitle-file.ts` is the new model (the `url` presence validator is ported but no job path reaches it, because a blank url raises `NoMethodError` first). The table has no `json_data` column, yet `serialize :json_data` makes Rails list a nil `json_data` in the version's `object`, right after the assigned attributes; `loadSubtitleFile` reproduces that. No debt line was open for it.

`alert-on-zero-buyer-currency-wallet-purchases-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16b`, Redis 28 for `REDIS_HOST` and `SIDEKIQ_REDIS_HOST`), 36 cases. `AlertOnZeroBuyerCurrencyWalletPurchasesJob` runs fully in Node: the two features read as `Flipper.enabled?` plus the 100 percent fallbacks, the recent-purchase and last-purchase queries, the `SET NX EX` throttle (reset when the lane is off or a purchase is recent, released when the push fails) and the `agent_reports` push. Hand-back, before any write: a feature on a percentage of time strictly between 0 and 100 (Flipper rolls `rand` per call, so Rails itself is not reproducible) or an expression gate. The generator guard in `rails_cases.rb` now also accepts a database named `_<N>b`. No debt line was open for it.

`order-review-reminder-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16b`, Redis 28 for `REDIS_HOST` and `SIDEKIQ_REDIS_HOST`), 39 cases. `OrderReviewReminderJob` runs in Node, reusing `eligibleForReviewReminder` and `deliverReminder` from `subscription-reminder-jobs.ts` (now exported). The `uniq.one?` rule counts truthy addresses, so a missing address next to one shared address still sends the single order email; an order with no address at all sends one per purchase. Hand-backs, before any write: an address with a non-ASCII character among the eligible purchases (Ruby's Unicode `downcase` is not ported) and an order id that is not an Integer or its digits. No debt line was open for it.

`record-order-charge-outcome-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16b`, Redis 28), 24 cases. `RecordOrderChargeOutcomeJob` runs fully in Node: the `Order.find_by(id:)` read (nil id included), the `partially_successful` exit, the checkout-succeeded and checkout-failed existence checks (one case per state in both lists) and the `UPDATE orders SET flags = flags | 2, updated_at = ...`. Hand-back, before any statement: an order id that is not an Integer, its digits or nil. The `connected_to(role: :writing)` wrapper that keeps the reads on the primary cannot be seen in the statement log, so the golden does not prove it. No debt line was open for it.

`sync-watched-users-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16b`, Redis 28, `ES_INDEX_SUFFIX` unset so the index paths read `purchases`), 21 cases. `SyncWatchedUsersJob` runs fully in Node (`models/watched-user.ts`: the required `user` and the revenue threshold being an integer above 0; the presence check is unreachable on a NOT NULL column). A watch whose user is gone, a threshold of 0 or less and an answer missing an aggregation are reported per watch while the sweep continues, with the messages Ruby produced. `ownAffiliateCreditCents` now fails with `undefined method 'total' for nil` when an affiliate aggregation is absent, as Ruby does. Not proven: a seller sum that Elasticsearch answers with `null` fails in Ruby with `nil can't be coerced into Integer`, where Node reports `undefined method 'value' for nil`; only the text of the report differs, and Elasticsearch answers a sum over no documents with 0. No debt line was open for it.

`retry-stripe-jp-address-resyncs-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16b`, Redis 28), 53 cases. `RetryStripeJpAddressResyncsJob` runs in Node: `Time.iso8601` (the port from `ruby-time-parse.ts`) decides due-ness with an ArgumentError counting as due, as `due_for_retry?` rescues it, while a non-String retry time or a `true`/Array/Hash count fails with the TypeError/NoMethodError text Ruby produced. The pushes of `RetryStripeJpAddressResyncForSellerJob` carry an `until_executed` lock, so the replay needs a real Redis (`JOBS_REDIS_URL=redis://<redis-ip>:6379/31`, flushed between cases) and is skipped without one. Hand-backs: `json_data` the parser rejects (the JSON::ParserError text is not ported), a retry time in year 10000 or later and time shapes whose Ruby error depends on which check trips first. These can happen after the pushes of an earlier batch; Ruby repeats them and the lock drops the duplicates. A non-finite Float count also hands back and is not in the golden. No debt line was open for it.

`process-payment-worker.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16b`, Redis 28), 19 cases, with `PayoutProcessorType.get` replaced by a recorder and outbound HTTP answered by WebMock, so no payout call was possible. `ProcessPaymentWorker` runs in Node for a missing payment (Ruby's RecordNotFound text, nil id included) and for every state but `processing`. Left in Ruby: `PayoutProcessorType.get(payment.processor).process_payments([payment])` for a processing payment (the job hands back after the SELECT, before any write or call), and a Float or word id that Rails casts. No debt line was open for it.

`refresh-custom-domain-routability-worker.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16b`, Redis 28), 20 cases, with `CustomDomainRoutabilityService.new` replaced by a recorder, so no DNS or TLS lookup left the machine. `RefreshCustomDomainRoutabilityWorker` runs in Node for every domain Rails leaves alone (the `alive` read, `active?` with its strict 90-day bound, `routability_refresh_due?` with its strict 6-hour bound; the boundary cases fall on whole seconds). Left in Ruby: `CustomDomainRoutabilityService#process` for a due domain (the job hands back after the SELECT, before any write), and a Float or word id that Rails casts. No debt line was open for it.

`PUT/PATCH /settings/payments` leftovers, `set_country` and `paypal_connect` (WS-21, `nextjs/ws21-money-2`, lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, Redis 36/37/38/43 recording and 44 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22b`; `nextjs_settings_payments_guards`, 36 new examples (three hand-back ones replaced) in `settings_payments_guards_parity_spec.rb`, 92 replay tests pass; with the set_country change, the preferences save and the PayPal call reverted, 4, 11 and 15 replay tests fail respectively). What now runs in Node:
- `set_country` no longer hands back a code the ISO list does not know, or a param that is not text (a string, array, hash, number, boolean or nothing at all): Rails saves a second revision whose country is nil, leaves the currency alone and answers 204, and so does Node. The code is read in any case (`ISO3166::Country["de"]` is Germany). Still handed back: Japan, a guardian, a birthday under 13, a business country of Japan and open compliance requests.
- A `PUT/PATCH /settings/payments` that carries only payout preferences (`payouts_paused_by_user`, `disable_buyer_local_currency` and `disable_buyer_currency_rounding` as JSON booleans, `payout_frequency` as text, `payout_threshold_cents` as an integer or a string of digits, optionally `user: null`) runs fully. Rails inserts a `tos_agreements` row with the request IP on every such save, even one that is then refused, so Node does too (`models/tos-agreement.ts`). Then it refuses a threshold under the minimum (a redirect with the base error; the row stays), saves the seller with the preferences (`json_data` keeps the writer's value uncast, so a text threshold is stored as text; a seller that does not validate gets its first message), notes the save for a team admin and answers 303 with the "all set" notice. Handed back before the first write: any other parameter (`user`, `payment_address`, `card`, `bank_account`), a value of another type or shape, a seller with an active bank account and no managed Stripe account (Rails would create one), and a stored military ZIP (Rails runs the save in a transaction there and the refusals `return` out of its block; what that does is not pinned).
- `paypal_connect` runs in Node past the parameter checks (`models/paypal-merchant-account-connect.ts`, `PaypalMerchantAccountManager#update_merchant_account`): the eligibility check, the row found or built for the merchant id (a deleted one is brought back), `meta` from `params.slice` in the order Rails asks for the names, the first save, the partner OAuth token (only when none is cached) and the merchant lookup, the terminal PayPal refusal check, the country and currency save, the verified, not receivable, missing permissions and unconfirmed outcomes, the retirement of the seller's other PayPal accounts, and the `MerchantRegistrationMailer#paypal_account_updated` job (queue `default`, a Ruby job). 15 examples with PayPal answers recorded by WebMock; the replay answers them from the stand-in, asserts method, URL and order, and asserts the mail job by name. The old example that stubbed `update_merchant_account` is gone. Not proven: a 2xx PayPal answer that is not a JSON object, a country or currency that is not text and a primary email that is not text raise a `NotPortedError` after the first save (Rails fails there too); the `updated_at` of a seeded merchant account is not compared.
- Still handed back, and why. The Stripe account half of the save (`StripeMerchantAccountManager.create_account` and `update_account`, `UpdateUserComplianceInfo`, `UpdatePayoutMethod`: some 4.8k lines of country-specific account, person and bank-account payloads plus idempotency-key bookkeeping, retries and reconciliation notes) was not ported; one hand-back example now records the first Stripe call of the creation (`POST /v1/accounts`, stubbed to fail), but no write or call order of the success path is proven. Instant payouts: even the refusals need the Stripe external-account read (`supports_instant_payouts?`) and the seasoning rules, and the success path creates `Payment` rows and Stripe payouts in several steps. Checkout refunds and disputes were not attempted. Nothing ran against live Stripe or PayPal.
WS-17 rest (lane 26, container `gr-ruby-c26c`, `gumroad_test_26c` and `gumroad_replay_26c` created for the run because `gumroad_*_26` was in use by another agent, Redis 7-9 recording and 8 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c26c`). `nextjs_internal_agent` also serves the store agent's custom HTML preview (`internal-agent-previews.ts`, 31 examples in `agent_custom_html_previews_parity_spec.rb`, 33 replay tests in `agent-custom-html-previews.replay`; with the CSP header, the snippet occurrence check and the length limit altered, 8 fail). `GET /internal/agent/custom_html_previews/:token` runs whole, including the custom page CSP header (built from the same domain and CDN config as `RendersCustomHtmlPages::CUSTOM_HTML_CSP`) and the expired page. `POST /internal/agent/custom_html_preview` runs the gates and every refusal before the sanitizer. Handed back, each pinned by a `hands a` example: a rendering preview (needs `Ai::PageSanitizer`, `Pages::Interpolator`, `Pages::ProfileData`, `Pages::DefaultProfileDocument`), a proposal that clears the page and a guarded undo; an array `custom_html` also hands back, with no example (Rails' parameter munging is not modelled). Not proven: a Redis read that stalls (Rails serves the expired page; Node catches any error the same way), and a page with invalid UTF-8. The other six unported WS-17 routes (`Api::V2::LinksController#create|update|comps|preview_custom_html`, `MediaController#create`, `POST /internal/ai_product_details_generations`) are Rack::Attack-throttled and stay in Rails; the AI generation route additionally needs a WebMock-recorded model exchange, which was not started.

WS-20 checkout rest 2 (`ws20-checkout-rest-2`, lane 24: `gumroad_test_24`, `gumroad_replay_24`, Redis 50/53/57/58 recording and 59 replay, container `gr-ruby-c24b`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c24b`). Four of the five `Checkout::Form` hand-backs left by lane 24's predecessor now run in Node; every one has recorded examples replayed in `checkout-form.replay` and was revert-checked. (1) `paypal_connect` for a seller with merchant accounts (`checkout-form/paypal-connect.ts`): `PaypalIntegrationRestApi` fetches the connected account's email after `partnerAuthToken` (or with only the lookup when Redis holds the partner token), a PayPal refusal, a list, a body that does not parse or any failure below HTTP leaves the email out (Rails rescues all of them and reports to `ErrorNotifier`; the replay compares no Sentry event), a text body answers `"primary_email"` when it contains the word (`String#[]`), and only an admin of a seller whose country PayPal Connect serves (or China with an account) makes the call. `User#paypal_disconnect_allowed?` reuses `activeSubscribers`/`activePreorders` from the Stripe disconnect, now parameterised by processor and optional merchant account. 17 examples in `checkout_form_paypal_connect_parity_spec.rb`; the PayPal answers come from the cassette bodies Rails recorded against api.sandbox.paypal.com (a 200 token and merchant-integrations pair, and the 401 pair), served by a stand-in (`OUTBOUND_HTTP_STAND_IN`) whose received method, URL and body the replay compares with the fixture's `http`, in order. With the email read disabled 14 of the 17 replay tests fail. Not covered: a network failure below HTTP (WebMock's `to_raise` would sleep through the token retries; unit-tested only through `primaryEmail`). (2) The habtm order of `CustomField#products`: Rails has no ORDER BY, and the recording answered in the linked products' id order where the old Node read (join row order) disagreed; the product-side read (`Link#product_checkout_custom_fields`) answered in join order in one recording and in field id order in the next for the same example. The order is the optimizer's choice, so Node serves only the case where both orders coincide (links made in ascending id order, `assertFormSupported` and `customFieldDescriptors`) and hands back the rest; the product-side read had been served in join order before, which is therefore tightened. 9 examples in `checkout_form_custom_fields_parity_spec.rb`, 3 of them "hands a ...". (3) An uploaded thumbnail (`buyer/products.ts` `uploadedThumbnailUrl`): the processed 600px variant's URL through the CDN rewrite, the file's own URL for a gif or a non-transformable file; the unprocessed-variant case stays handed back and is only unit-tested because Rails processes the variant during the request and uploads a PNG the recorder cannot serialise (`JSON::GeneratorError`). Every caller of `thumbnailUrl` (product page, profile props, dashboard home, customers, discover, upsell products) is served the same way; their replays still pass, and the two upsell-product "hands a ..." examples with a text thumbnail were re-recorded as served. The uploaded avatar needed no code (served since `help_center_signed_in_gaps_spec.rb`) and is now recorded for the checkout form; its S3 existence check goes through the AWS client, so the replay does not count calls to the object store. 6 examples in `checkout_form_uploaded_images_parity_spec.rb`. (4) `restart_intent` (`services/checkout-restart-intent-token.ts`): the token is the `checkout_restart_intent` message verifier with an hour expiry and `{product_id, buyer_id, issued_at}`; the replay ignores the clock-bearing value and instead decodes the token Rails recorded and the one Node issued with the replay secret and compares the payloads and that Node's was issued just now, and `restart_intent_token_node_spec.rb` has Rails's own `restart_intended?` accept tokens Node issued (golden `spec/support/fixtures/restart_intent_token_node.json`, pinned by the Node test). Whole-second `issued_at` values serialise as `n` in Node and `n.0` in Ruby; Rails reads both. 5 examples in `checkout_form_restart_intent_parity_spec.rb`. Not done, with why: the `accepted_offer`, wishlist and gift arrivals of `checkout_props` have no Node route (`/checkout` hands back `?product=`, `?wishlist=`, `?gift_wishlist_product=` and any cart with lines) and need the visitor's IP, a buyer and the request params threaded through `checkout_product`; the charge, refund and dispute write paths have no recorded example that proves DB-write / Stripe-call / idempotency-key ordering (the fixtures' `http` channel records method, URL, body and response but not headers, so no `Idempotency-Key`, and `db` is a before/after diff with no timeline against the calls; the API v2 refund fixtures show only the call order GET charge, POST refund, GET refund, GET charge), so those stay handed back until the recorder captures headers and an event order. No debt line was open for these.

WS-11/12 product page layouts and head tags (lane 15c: `gumroad_test_15c`/`gumroad_replay_15c`, Redis 32/33/34/36 recording and 37 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15c`, container `gr-ruby-c15c`; replay run with `RAILS_ROOT` pointing at the main checkout, which holds `public/vite-test`). `LinksController#show` now picks `Products/{Show,Iframe/Show,Profile/Show,Discover/Show}` in Node: `PageSpec.component` may be a function of the action, `productPageKind` follows Rails' order (`layout=profile`, `layout=discover`, then any `embed` or `overlay`, even blank), and `ProductsPage.tsx` mounts the client page that matches the rendered component. The iframe page is `product_props` alone, profile is the standard page props, discover adds `taxonomy_path` (closure_tree ancestry from `taxonomy_hierarchies`) and `taxonomies_for_nav`; `layout` reaches a bundle's product links. The discover search box's `X-Inertia-Partial-Data: autocomplete_results` request answers the suggestions with the product page's meta tags. `PageMeta::Product` image tags (`og:image` and alt, `twitter:card` summary_large_image, `twitter:image`, the structured data `image`) are set for Unsplash covers, and a call product preloads `components/ui/Calendar.tsx` through `infra/vite.ts` (`viteAssetPath`: the manifest of `public/vite-test|vite|vite-dev`, asset host outside test and development). `product_page_parity_spec.rb` gained 13 examples (77 replay tests pass; with `productPageKind`, the cover image and the calendar preload reverted 14 fail). Hands back, still Rails: a storefront-enabled seller's page for a visitor (the catalogue sections), a discover layout for a visitor whose cart and purchases hold 4 or more distinct products (ws14-discover-3 ported fewer; examples "hands a visitor with four products ..."), `recommended_by=search|discover` (writes a `DiscoverSearch`), a non-text `layout`, uploaded and oEmbed covers and their `preview_image_path?` preloads (signed S3/CDN URLs and the retina variant cache need Rails.cache; not proved), `wanted`, `purchase_id`.

Not started in ws11-12-port-4: signed cover and thumbnail URLs, oEmbed covers, the `home#*`/`/mcp`/`/claude`/`/muse`/`/gumclaw` ERB pages (they render `layouts/application` with design settings, ERB meta tags, flash and Vite tags, so each needs byte-exact HTML from a Node ERB renderer), `/js/gumroad*`, `POST /links` and commissions. None of these routes is throttled by Rack::Attack code touched here; `rack-attack.ts` was not edited.
`reindex-seller-offer-codes-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16c`, Redis 33), 27 cases, with `ProductOfferCodeIndexingService.new` replaced by a recorder, so nothing reached Elasticsearch. The Node jobs reproduce the lock (`SET NX EX 600`), the cooldown (`String#to_f` of the stored value, compared with `Time#to_f` so a cooldown written in the same millisecond does not defer) and the five-second re-queue with its unique-jobs lock. Node never runs the indexing service: a seller with targeted products or a catalogue pass pending has the lock released and is handed back with Redis as it found it, so the `last_batch` and cursor bookkeeping stays in Ruby. A cooldown starting with a dot and a seller id that is not an Integer, String or nil hand back.

`custom-domains-verification-worker.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16c`, Redis 33), 12 cases; the job only reads `custom_domains` and schedules `CustomDomainVerificationWorker`, so nothing was replaced. `enqueueWith` (`perform_in`/`perform_at`) now omits `at` for a time that is not after now, as Sidekiq does, so the first domain's job goes to the queue rather than the schedule; no earlier port enqueued with a zero or past delay.

`record-product-file-missing-from-storage-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16c`, Redis 33), 22 cases, with `S3KeyUnicodeNormalization.existing_variant` and `ProductFile#s3_object` replaced by recorders, so nothing reached S3; the golden holds the `S3_BASE_URL` it was recorded under and the replay mocks `storageConfig` with it. The clock is a whole second there so the grace-period boundary is exact. Node never decides the storage outcome: every eligible file hands back after the single SELECT.
WS-21/WS-23 money, third pass (`nextjs/ws21-money-3`, lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, `gumroad_replay_22`, Redis 36/37/38/44 recording and 43 replay, container `gr-ruby-c22c`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22c`). The recorder now keeps outbound call headers (`Idempotency-Key`, `Stripe-Account`, `PayPal-Request-Id`, an `Authorization` digest) and, for a request that made a call, an ordered `timeline` of SQL writes, calls and jobs; `src/stand-in.ts` asserts them in a replay (see parity/README.md). Old fixtures replay unchanged (re-recording `checkout_form_paypal_connect_parity_spec` and replaying `checkout-form` passed, 94 tests; the rewritten fixtures were reverted). Stripe answers come from existing VCR cassettes served by WebMock; nothing live ran. Ported: the refusals of `PUT /purchases/:id/refund` that Rails makes before the processor (`controllers/purchase-refund.ts`: comma amount, 404 for missing, refunded, free or foreign sale, nothing to refund, active dispute, refunds disabled, insufficient balance); 8 examples in `purchases_refund_parity_spec.rb`, with the controller reverted 8 replay tests fail. Handed back, each with a "hands a ..." example proving Node makes no Stripe call and no write: a full refund, a refund with an amount, a team member's refund, an instant payout, and the settings save that creates a US individual's Stripe account. The recordings prove Rails' order. Refund: `GET charge`, `POST /v1/refunds` (random key), `GET refund`, `GET charge`, then every ledger write (refund, balance transactions, credit, balance, purchase) and eight jobs. Instant payout: external-account read, balance read, balance update and `payments` insert, `POST /v1/payouts` (random key, `Stripe-Account`), `payments` update. Account creation: `tos_agreements`, `users`, `merchant_accounts` insert and a comment before `POST /v1/accounts` (random key), the account, bank account and comment updates after. Not ported: the refund ledger (FlowOfFunds from the Stripe balance transactions, balance selection, fee-retention credit, ES and analytics jobs), the instant payout and the account creation themselves. Dispute evidence submission is `FightDisputeJob`, a Sidekiq job with S3 and file uploads to Stripe, not a request, so the request recorder cannot drive it; it was not attempted. `Stripe-Account` connect refunds, PayPal and partial refunds are not proven either.
WS-21/WS-23 money, fourth pass (`nextjs/ws21-money-4`, lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, `gumroad_replay_22`, Redis 36/37/38/44 recording and 43 replay, container `gr-ruby-c22d`, `ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22d`). `FightDisputeJob` submits evidence from Node (`worker/jobs/fight-dispute-job.ts`, `server/stripe/charge-processor-errors.ts`) for a dispute of one Stripe-charged purchase on Gumroad's own account (no merchant account, or one without an owner) whose evidence has no file attached: `GET /v1/charges/:id`, then `POST /v1/disputes/:dispute` with the evidence fields in Rails' order (nil fields dropped, `service_date`/`shipping_date` as `%B %-d, %Y`, the statement and context joined with a blank line) and the `Idempotency-Key` `dispute_evidence_<external id>`, then `update_as_resolved!` (`submitted`; `rejected` for "This dispute is already closed"; `submitted` with the message truncated to 255 for "maximum number of evidence submissions"). A purchase with no transaction id is rejected without a call. Stripe failures become the `ChargeProcessor*Error` classes with Rails' messages, so the retry sees the same class. `fight-dispute-job.golden.json` (25 cases, `scripts/golden/fight-dispute-job.rb`) records the real job against a scratch database and the capture server; the replay compares every statement (including the paper_trail version row and the `has_one_attached` reads, in Rails' order, interleaved with the Stripe calls), the Stripe requests with their idempotency key, the raised error and the hand-backs. Mutation checks (idempotency key, the owner key's place in the version, truncation, the Connect guard, the closed-dispute branch, the receipt read, the context join, the subscription guard) each fail 1 to 13 of the 26 tests. Still handed back before the first POST or write, each a golden case: a Connect charge, a charge of several purchases, a service charge, a processor other than Stripe, a purchase with a subscription (its policy image is the cancellation policy and the save reads the subscription), and evidence with any attached file (`customer_communication_file`, `receipt_image`, `cancellation_policy_image`, `refund_policy_image`: each is downloaded from storage and uploaded to Stripe as a file whose id keys the submission, and an oversized customer communication file is omitted and mailed about). The charge read may already have happened when a file turns up; it is repeatable. The old `refund-payout-guard-jobs.golden.json` lost its two closed-window FightDisputeJob cases (they ran to the hand-back marker; the new golden covers that path). Not ported in this pass, with nothing written for them: the full refund (`PUT /purchases/:id/refund`), the instant payout and the Stripe account creation for a US individual. They stay behind the hand-backs of the third pass, whose recordings already prove the order. The refund needs `Purchase#refund_purchase!` as a whole (the Refund callbacks `sync_fee_retention_recoverable`, `assign_product`, `assign_seller`, the retained-fee credit, `decrement_balance_for_refund_or_chargeback!` with its affiliate and collaborator branches, `FlowOfFunds` built from the Stripe balance transactions, the purchase and audience member callbacks that enqueue the other six jobs and the refund and partial-refund mails); the instant payout needs `User#instant_payouts_supported?`, `Payouts.create_payments` and `StripePayoutProcessor.process_payments` (1,278 lines, with `Payment`'s state machine and failure handling); the account creation needs `StripeMerchantAccountManager.create_account` and the country payloads. None of the three has a smaller slice that is both reachable from the recordings and safe to leave half done, and a port that stops after the first Stripe call must not hand back, so they were left whole.
WS-10 rest 3 (lane 23, container `gr-ruby-c23d`, `gumroad_test_23`/`gumroad_replay_23`, Redis 30/31/33/34 recording and 59/60 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c23d`). `POST /signup` (`nextjs_auth_signup_writes`, `controllers/signup-create.ts`; 17 examples in `signup_create_parity_spec.rb`) runs in Node: `handle_existing_users` (sign-in of the owner of the password, refusals), the account (bcrypt `$2a$` digest, random confirmation token and OTP secret, the User callbacks, `GlobalAffiliate`, `SellerRefundPolicy` and `tos_agreements` rows, the confirmation mail job, `GenerateUsernameJob`), sign-in, the `signup_events` row and the two-factor cookie. The replay checks the bcrypt hash verifies, the mail carries the stored token and the two-factor cookie decrypts to the new user id (its name carries a hash of the random external id, so `parity/src/compare.ts` ignores segments by `prefix*`). Hands back: a referral, a `purchase_id`, a card token, `remember_me`, a Canadian address (GeoIP turns announcements off), an unconfirmed existing account, a guest cart, a signed-in visitor, non-text parameters, `POST /users`. Not proven: a Canadian signup and a pwned password flash on the existing-account path (the recording's range is empty). `POST /mobile/forgot_password` (`nextjs_mobile_forgot_password`, API host; 7 examples in `mobile_forgot_password_parity_spec.rb`) runs in Node with a null session: no flash, no cookie; a request with a session cookie or CSRF token hands back. Not ported, still hands back whole: OAuth token issuance and the device flow (recorded in this lane then dropped: the format is `SecureRandom.urlsafe_base64(32)` for tokens and refresh tokens, `expires_in` nil, `use_refresh_token` per grant, `resource` copied from the code, a refresh revokes the old row; a Rails spec proving Node-written tokens authenticate was not written), and login passkey (WebAuthn assertion check against a stored challenge). `OAuth` social callbacks that create a user (`find_or_create_for_google_oauth2`, `save_to_library` with a user hash) are unblocked by the user creation above but not ported.
WS-20 checkout rest 3 (`nextjs/ws20-checkout-rest-3`, lane 24: `gumroad_test_24`, `gumroad_replay_24`, Redis 50/53/57/58 recording and 59 replay, container `gr-ruby-c24c`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c24c`). `/checkout` now renders what the URL puts on the page, for a cart that holds nothing: `?product=` (with `username`, `option`, `recurrence`, `quantity`, `price`, `rent`, `call_start_time`, `pay_in_installments`, `force_new_subscription`, `affiliate_id`, `recommended_by`, `recommender_model_name`), `?wishlist=`, `?gift_wishlist_product=` and `accepted_offer_id` (`page-props/checkout/arrivals.ts`; `checkoutProduct` takes the buyer, the visitor IP and the request params). It also computes `checkout_payment` (the Stripe lane selection when the sellers are not on the client-confirm element) and `checkout_style`. The filter builds the arrival once and hands the request back on a `NotPortedError`. 68 examples in `checkout_arrivals_parity_spec.rb` replay in `checkout-arrivals.replay` (69 tests); with the filter forced to hand back, 61 fail. `Link#ppp_details` with a factor below 1 is now served for every caller (the product page too; only the checkout recording proves it). Hand-backs left, each recorded as a "hands a ..." example or unit-tested only: a seller with no `username` column (Ruby answers the external id), a seller ramped into `stripe_payment_element_client_confirm` or `india_card_mandate_reliability` (`Checkout::PaymentMethodResolver`, `StripeIntentChargeRouting`), a seller on buyer-local currency whose visitor's currency differs, a buyer with an existing-customers or tiered offer code on an accepted offer or cross-sell, a purchase with several variants, a partial reload of `recommended_products`, and a cart with lines or stored codes. Not proven: a visitor from India buying alone (`recurring_upi_registration_shape?`) hands back by code, with no recorded example because no Indian address was found in the test GeoIP database; a wishlist is served in `wishlist_products.id` order although Rails sets no ORDER BY; `Rack` and `permit` treatment of repeated or bracketed keys is left to the existing flat-query guard. Tasks 2 to 4 of this lane (`POST /links`, commissions' Active Storage signed ids, `Purchase#purchase_info` and `content_url` tokens) were not started: they stay open.
WS-10 integrations 3 (lane 27: `gumroad_test_27`, `gumroad_replay_27`, Redis 11/12/13/15 recording and 17 replay, container `gr-ruby-c27c`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c27c`; the recording sets `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`, `DISCORD_CLIENT_ID`, `DISCORD_CLIENT_SECRET` and `SELLER_PLAN_PRICE_5`, which the test environment lacks, and the replays pass the same values). Four more provider-backed routes run in Node, every provider answer a WebMock stub in the recording and a stand-in server in the replay that asserts the outbound calls, their order and their bodies:
- Google Calendar `account_info` and `calendar_list` (`nextjs_integrations_google_calendar`, `integrations-google-calendar.ts`): 19 examples in `integrations_google_calendar_parity_spec.rb`. A nil `code` or refresh token is a bare key in the form body (`URI.encode_www_form`), the userinfo query is encoded like HTTParty's `query:` (`ERB::Util.url_encode`), the calls go through the `GOOGLE_CALENDAR_API_RATE_LIMIT` keys, a 401 triggers one refresh and one retry. Hand-back, pinned by a `hands ...` example: a parameter sent as a list or hash.
- Discord `server_info` (`nextjs_integrations_discord`, `integrations-discord-server-info.ts`): 14 examples in `integrations_discord_server_info_parity_spec.rb`. The profile request mirrors Discordrb (a Discord error body is a `CodeError` and fails, a 403 raises as Rails does, a 429 is waited out and retried, a 502 is retried without a limit, as the gem does).
- Circle provider calls (`integrations-circle.ts`): 13 examples in `integrations_circle_calls_parity_spec.rb` replace the three "hands a request with an API key" examples; both calls of `communities_and_space_groups` are made before either answer is read, as Rails does.
- `GET /seller_plan/checkout` (`nextjs_seller_plan`, `seller-plan.ts`, `stripe/resources.ts#CheckoutSession`): 12 examples in `seller_plan_checkout_parity_spec.rb` (new session, canceled offer, open and expired stored session, unknown stored session, active plan, Stripe refusal, missing price, no offer, no product, admin and marketing team members). The offer is read under `FOR UPDATE` around the Stripe calls, as `with_lock` does.
