The action rescues `StandardError` around `authorize`, so a Pundit refusal is the "We couldn't start your plan checkout" redirect to `/`, not the dashboard redirect; the replay compares the flash from the decrypted session cookie. Not recorded: a Stripe connection or rate limit error (the redirect to the editor with the "couldn't reach" alert is in the code but no fixture covers it) and an `ErrorNotifier` call (Node reports, the replay does not observe it).
The replay is `integrations-providers.replay` (47 tests) and `seller-plan-checkout.replay` (14 tests); with the controllers removed 46 and 12 of them fail. Known gaps shared by the new HTTParty ports: redirects are followed by `fetch` rather than HTTParty (a POST redirect is not converted to a GET), no timeout is set (Net::HTTP waits 60 seconds), and an answer HTTParty cannot parse raises (a 500) in both.

Left handed back: the `oauth_redirect` page of Zoom and Google Calendar (and the page Discord's action falls through to). `render inline: "", layout: "application"` embeds the Vite entry tags (`vite_entrypoint_stylesheet_tag "design"`, the `Link` preload header, `vite_typescript_tag "base"`) from Rails' build manifest, and nothing in Node serves those assets; the empty body is a Rails ERB layout, not an Inertia page, so a Node port would invent an asset pipeline the recording cannot check (the recording stubs the Vite helpers). It was recorded once to see the markup (200 and 400, signed in and out) and the fixtures were dropped because no replay can prove them. Passkey login (`POST /login/passkey/options` and `/login/passkey`) was not started: the assertion verification (COSE key, signature, counter) could be proven by replaying a recorded valid assertion, but the success path also signs the user in (Warden session, remember cookie), merges the guest cart and refreshes the passkey setup prompt, and the route carries a Rack::Attack rule, so it stays whole in Rails.
WS-17 rest 2 (lane 26d: `gumroad_test_26` and `gumroad_replay_26`, Redis 6-9 recording and 10 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c26d`, container `gr-ruby-c26d`). The six routes whose Rack::Attack throttle had kept them in Rails are now counted by the single counter in `rack-attack.ts` (no second counter) and answer in Node up to their first write; everything after hands back whole, un-counted. `nextjs_api_v2_products` serves `POST /v2/products`, `PUT`/`PATCH /v2/products/:id`, `GET /v2/products/comps` (registered ahead of `/v2/products/:id`) and `POST /v2/products/:id/preview_custom_html`, `nextjs_api_v2_media` serves `POST /v2/media`, and `nextjs_internal_ai_product_details` serves `POST /internal/ai_product_details_generations` (`api-v2/product-writes.ts`, `product-comps.ts`, `product-custom-html.ts`, `media.ts`, `controllers/internal-ai-product-details.ts`, `ai/openai-chat.ts`). Answered in Node: the scopes, the product and category lookups, the unsupported-upload-field, price, currency, taxonomy, tags, custom HTML (feature gate, type, length) and refund-policy refusals, the media `account` checks, and the whole AI route: sign-in, the hourly Redis throttle (the Ruby Lua script; the 429 carries `Retry-After`), the Pundit check, the prompt filter (Ruby's ASCII `\w`/`\s` against Unicode `\b`), the model call with its retry, and the response rendered with Ruby's `Float#to_s` and integer/float distinction. Handed back, each pinned by a "hands a ..." example: a create or update that passes the guards (callbacks, tags, rich content, files, price rows), a comps request with a body or one that reaches the Elasticsearch search, a custom HTML preview that reaches `Ai::PageSanitizer`, and a media create that reaches the download, moderation and file creation. 77 examples in `api/v2/products_throttled_parity_spec.rb` replay in `api-v2-throttled.replay` (79 tests) and 25 in `api/internal/ai_product_details_generations_parity_spec.rb` replay in `ai-product-details-generations.replay` (27 tests); the model exchange is a WebMock stub when recording and a stand-in server that asserts the request (path, content type, JSON body, bearer token) when replaying, so no run reached a live model. Each route has a "counts ..." example (the Rack::Attack keys Rails wrote), an "answers 429 ..." example and its hand-backs, which must leave every counter at zero (a hand-back un-counts, so the keys stay with value 0). With the `routes.json` entries removed the replays fail. Unit tests: `internal-ai-product-details.test.ts` (`sanitizePrompt`, `rubyFloat`). Not ported and not proven: the create/update writes, the comps search, the `PageSanitizer` preview, the media download and the agent `custom_html_previews` renders (those need `Ai::PageSanitizer`, `Pages::Interpolator` and related classes with byte-equal examples, which were not recorded).

`update-purchase-email-to-match-account-worker.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16d`, Redis 33), 11 cases; a user with a purchase runs `Purchase#update!` in Ruby (it failed on the bare seeded rows), so Node stops at the first `find_each` batch and hands back before any write.

`release-chargeback-rate-payout-pause-for-seller-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16d`, Redis 33), 48 cases, with nothing replaced. A releasable seller within the rate limit ran the lock, `update!` and the audit comment in Ruby (the validation failed on the bare seeded user); Node hands those back at the lock's BEGIN. `format("%.1f%%")` ties (x.25, x.75) round differently in `toFixed`, but never across the 1.5 limit.

`retry-stripe-jp-address-resync-for-seller-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16d`, Redis 33), 49 cases, with `StripeMerchantAccountManager.handle_new_user_compliance_info` replaced by a recorder that raises, so nothing reached Stripe; the `merchant_migration` feature was activated in Redis for one case. Rails answers a repeated identical SELECT (`alive_user_compliance_info`, the Stripe accounts) from its query cache and Node reads each once.

`generate-username-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16d`, Redis 33), 16 cases, with every outbound request answered by WebMock (`capture_http!`); a user needing a completion issued one request in Ruby and Node hands back before it.

`cancel-subscriptions-for-product-worker.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16d`, Redis 33), 18 cases. A deleted product with an active subscription ran `cancel_effective_immediately!` in Ruby (failing on bare seeded rows); Node hands back after the subscriptions query.

`record-product-file-missing-from-storage-job.golden.json` was re-recorded with `AWS_S3_ENDPOINT=http://localhost:9010` (Spring must not be running, or the old environment sticks) and grew to 28 cases: accented and spaced keys, an empty key and a fractional-second clock. The recorders still replace both lookups; Node answers an ASCII key with its own HEAD, mapped from whether the case wrote. The case recorded with a variant found for an ASCII key is unreachable in Ruby and passes in Node because the HEAD finds the object.
Five default-queue jobs ported on lane 26 (`gumroad_test_26e`, Redis 9, Elasticsearch answered by a local socket, outbound HTTP by WebMock, so no Stripe or storage call was possible). `charge-successful-preorders-worker.golden.json` (16 cases): `ChargeSuccessfulPreordersWorker` runs fully in Node (the link, the authorized preorders, one `ChargePreorderWorker` push per preorder and the 20-minute `SendPreorderSellerSummaryWorker` carrying the argument as given); a Float or word id hands back before any statement. `rename-product-file-worker.golden.json` (34 cases, `MultipartTransfer.transfer_to_s3` replaced by a recorder): a missing file, one deleted from the CDN and one whose display name equals its S3 name finish in Node, a file outside S3 fails with Ruby's `TypeError` / `NoMethodError`, and a namespaced file without a display name fails with `ends_with?` on nil; handed back after the read: a file that reaches the copy, an S3 url that is not namespaced (Ruby reads `user` through the owner, which raises `DelegationError` when the file has none) and a url ending in a dot (Ruby's `File.extname` answers `"."`, `rubyExtname` answers `""`). `delete-product-files-worker.golden.json` (24 cases): an undone deletion, a product with sales (one sales search with the default total hits) and a deleted product with no files finish in Node, with Ruby's errors for an answer without hits, without a total or with a null, string or boolean value; a product with a file to delete hands back after its files are read, and so does an answer whose total is a bare number, an array or an object. `cancel-preorder-worker.golden.json` (13 cases): a missing or not authorized preorder finishes in Node; an authorized one hands back after the read (`mark_cancelled!`). `cancel-subscriptions-for-product-worker.golden.json` (17 cases): an undone deletion and a deleted product with no active subscription (the seller mail pushed on the critical queue with the id as given) finish in Node; a product with an active subscription hands back after the subscriptions are read. No debt line was open for any of them. Not attempted: the rest of the default queue (reports, Stripe and PayPal processing, mail slices) needs Stripe, PayPal or S3 calls or money movement, which stay in Ruby.

WS-20 checkout rest 4 (`nextjs/ws20-checkout-rest-4`, lane 24: `gumroad_test_24`, `gumroad_replay_24`, Redis 50/53/57/58 recording and 59 replay, container `gr-ruby-c24d`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c24d`). `POST /links` (`controllers/links-create.ts`, `models/link-create.ts`, flag `nextjs_links_create`) runs in Node for a USD digital, course, ebook or audiobook: price parsing (`clean_price`, euro-style comma, half-away-from-zero rounding, a plus suffix or a free price becomes pay-what-you-want), the price range and adult-keyword validations, `errors.to_hash`/`to_sentence` in the 302 back to `/products/new`, the product and `prices` rows as a draft, the `add_product` event under the acting team member, the search index job. 39 examples in `links_create_parity_spec.rb` replay in `links-create.replay` (41 tests; 28 fail with the controller reverted). Hands back before the first write: a physical product, membership, coffee, bundle, another currency, a custom summary, an AI prompt, a missing price, the mobile app web view, a seller whose profile sections add new products, and a seller near the daily creation limit. Unproven: PaperTrail versions of the create and touch (disabled in the test env, so `db.versions` is ignored). The seller with no `username` column now gets the external id in the checkout arrival (`effectiveUsername`), and an existing-customers or tiered offer code on an accepted offer or cross-sell is evaluated for a signed-in buyer (`OfferCode#evaluate_for_buyer`; 75 arrivals tests, 5 fail with the evaluation reverted). Still handed back: a seller on buyer-local currency whose visitor's currency differs, and a purchase with several variants. Not started or not provable here: `Purchase#purchase_info` and `content_url` (the url_redirect tokens are stored random values, not signed; the blocker is the large unported `purchase_info` hash) and commissions (Active Storage signed ids). No debt line was open for any of it.
WS-20 purchase path, first slice (`nextjs/ws21-checkout-purchase`, lane 27: `gumroad_test_27`, `gumroad_replay_27`, Redis 11/12/13/15 recording and 17 replay, container `gr-ruby-c27d`, `ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c27d`). `POST /orders` (`nextjs_orders_validations`, `controllers/orders-validations.ts`) answers the requests Rails refuses before it writes a row or calls a processor, 31 examples in `orders_validations_parity_spec.rb` replayed by `orders-validations.replay` (33 tests; with the handler's constants broken 7 of them fail): a bot (`{ success: true }`, halted before the after_actions, so no XSRF-TOKEN cookie), a cart above 50 lines, a request without line items (`can_buyer_sign_up` reads `users`), a blank or repeated uid, a permalink that matches no product, and a signed-out buyer's missing or invalid email on a plain one-time USD product (`valid email required`; the purchase fails its first `save` on validation, so the recording shows no `db` rows, no jobs and no outbound call; `updated_product` is `checkoutProduct`). The email regexp is pinned by `order-email-validity.golden.json` (`EmailFormatValidator.valid?`). Handed back before any write, each with a `hands a ...` example: every line that would be saved (valid email; a sold out, unpublished or deleted product and a price mismatch are all persisted as failed purchases with an order, an event and two `ElasticsearchIndexerWorker` jobs), a signed-in buyer, a gift, discount code, variants or tiers, membership, pay-what-you-want or free product (the factory makes a $0 product customizable), a product in another currency, a custom field, a quantity other than 1, a physical product (it writes a `shipments` row before the validation), and any request that reaches reCAPTCHA verification (Rails' test environment has no key, so a Google call is unrecorded; only development and test with no key configured are answered). Not proven: the `_gumroad_guid` cookie sent present but empty (Rails refuses a paid order; the request-spec cookie jar cannot send one, so Node hands it back), and the product-inventory Redis semaphore `Purchase::CreateService` takes before the email check (a contended lock answers "Sorry, something went wrong", unrecorded).
Slice 2 (free purchase, `nextjs/ws21-checkout-purchase-2`, lane 24, container `gr-ruby-c24e`) recorded, not ported: `orders_free_purchase_parity_spec.rb` holds 21 Rails examples (13 served shapes: signed-out, essentials only, email normalisation, confirmed-account email, signed-in own and other email, product with a file, product and no refund policy, same email twice, custom receipt with ping endpoint, browser details, referrer and url parameters; 8 hand-backs: workflow, purchase limit, cart, affiliate, two products, repeated free download, discount code, free membership) with `parity_timeline: true`, so each fixture's `timeline` interleaves statements and job enqueues (the recorder now emits it for tagged examples). Recorded order for the plain case: INSERT purchases, purchase_refund_policies, UPDATE links (`sales_count_for_inventory_cache`), `ElasticsearchIndexerWorker`, `RefreshAudienceMemberJob`, INSERT purchase_sales_tax_infos, orders, order_purchases, charges, charge_purchases, UPDATE purchases (`succeeded_at`, then `purchase_state`), INSERT url_redirects, then the after-commit jobs (`InvalidateProductCacheWorker`, `SendToElasticsearchWorker`, `RecordOrderChargeOutcomeJob`, `UpdateSalesRelatedProductsInfosJob`, two more `ElasticsearchIndexerWorker`, `RefreshAudienceMemberJob`, the `ContactingCreatorMailer.notify` `MailDeliveryJob`, `PostToPingEndpointsWorker`, `ActivateIntegrationsWorker`), INSERT events, `SendChargeReceiptJob`. `audience_members` is not written during the request in the test environment (the refresh job is stubbed inline). No Node handler and no replay test exist yet: `POST /orders` with an acceptable email still hands back whole. Unrecorded invisible effects to settle first: `Rails.cache` deletes and the Redis streak-key script. `parity-debt.ts` once listed `orders_free_purchase_parity_spec.rb:25` as an example without a fixture: its scanner reads any line starting with `example` as an example, and the `around` hook's `example.run` matched (the block variable is now `free_example`; the 21 recorded examples are unchanged). Port notes for the next slice: the first `save` of the purchase commits on its own (so the audience and Elasticsearch jobs enqueue before `purchase_sales_tax_infos`), `Cart.fetch_by` and `UtmLinkVisit` (by `_gumroad_guid`) and `Affiliate.by_cookies` must be checked before serving, and `defineModel` in `server/models/runtime` already gives the callback and after-commit order the timeline needs. Slice 3 (`nextjs/ws21-purchase-model-1`) ported the pieces that need no database: the create side of `PurchaseRefundPolicy`, `PurchaseSalesTaxInfo`, `Order` (its `after_save :schedule_review_reminder!` only looks for a cart and hands back when it finds one), `OrderPurchase`, `ChargePurchase` and `Charge` (`models/purchase-refund-policy.ts`, `purchase-sales-tax-info.ts`, `order.ts`, `order-purchase.ts`, `charge-purchase.ts`, `charge-record.ts`), each saved through `defineModel` and compared with the row the recording inserted (`free-purchase-records.test.ts`, which also checks `Event` and `UrlRedirect` and pins the recorded write order), and `purchaseResponse` (`models/purchase-response.ts`: the ~70-field line item, purchase_info plus the ping payload, rendered from rows and replayed against all 13 served fixtures; it refuses a priced purchase, another currency, a discount code, a membership, a pre-order, a physical or licensed product, a bundle, a gift, an affiliate, tax, shipping, variants, a purchase limit, custom fields, a test purchase and a missing url redirect, so the handler can hand back before writing). Not ported, and why it is the next slice rather than a small one: `Purchase`'s own `defineModel` (the 15 `before_create` guards each read other tables: sanctioned-location and fraud checks, `not_double_charged`'s two windows, currency minimums, offer-code and inventory reads, `mirror_buyer_opt_out`, the inventory-counter callbacks), the `process` to `successful` state machine hooks that enqueue the 13 jobs in the recorded order, `Order::CreateService` and `Purchase::CreateService` (about 1,300 lines of Ruby), the reads that gate serving (`Cart.fetch_by`, `UtmLinkVisit`, `Affiliate.by_cookies`), the loader that fills `PurchaseResponseFacts` (`has_files?`, `is_following?`, third-party analytics, `Integration.enabled_integrations_for`) and `controllers/orders-free-purchase.ts` with its replay. The recorded fixtures hold writes and enqueues, not SELECTs, so the statement-level goldens for `Purchase` need a Ruby recording in the lane's `gr-ruby` container first. No route changed: `POST /orders` with an acceptable email still hands back whole.

Not started (the port itself), with the measured size (probe recording of one free product): the free ($0) purchase writes `audience_members`, `charge_purchases`, `charges`, `events`, `links` (update), `order_purchases`, `orders`, `purchase_refund_policies`, `purchase_sales_tax_infos`, `purchases` and `url_redirects`, enqueues eleven jobs (`ElasticsearchIndexerWorker` x4, `InvalidateProductCacheWorker`, `SendToElasticsearchWorker`, `RecordOrderChargeOutcomeJob`, `UpdateSalesRelatedProductsInfosJob`, `PostToPingEndpointsWorker`, `ActivateIntegrationsWorker`, `SendChargeReceiptJob`) and answers a 40-field `purchase_response`. The plain card purchase (slice 3) and subscription, preorder, PayPal and 3DS (slice 4) are untouched: `Order::ChargeService` and `Purchase#charge!` are handed back whole, and no Stripe call, idempotency key or write ordering is ported.
WS-30 jobs 9 (lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, Redis 36-40, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22e`): ten jobs ported against goldens recorded from the real classes run as Sidekiq jobs. Nothing leaves the machine: the Rails calls that stay with Ruby were replaced by recorders (or an error named `GoldenReached`), Elasticsearch was answered by the local capture socket and outbound HTTP by WebMock. The replays share `__fixtures__/guard-golden.ts` (cases Rails stops at, cases it reaches, ids Rails casts). Each job runs everything up to the Ruby-only call in Node and hands the run back after that read, before any write, so Rails re-running it is safe:

- `ProcessAssetPreviewRetinaWorker` (9 cases): a missing or deleted preview finishes in Node; a live one hands back before `AssetPreview#generate_retina_variant!` (ImageMagick and S3). A float or word id hands back before any statement.
- `RefreshMerchantAccountPaymentMethodAvailabilityWorker` (22 cases): `RecordNotFound`, a deleted account, a processor that is not alive and a non-Connect account finish in Node; a Connect account, or a `json_data` Ruby cannot dig into, hands back before `StripeConnectPaymentMethodAvailabilityService` (Stripe). The job's `rescue Stripe::*` clauses stay Ruby's, since nothing in Node reaches Stripe.
- `HandleGrmcCallbackJob` (19 cases) and `HandleSnsMediaconvertEventWorker` (28 cases): a notification no processing `TranscodedVideo` carries, and an SNS message that is not a `Notification`, finish in Node; one that matches hands back before the transcode sweep that updates videos and streamables. A notification or message Ruby reads loosely (non-hash, non-string job id, JSON that does not parse, no `detail` hash) hands back before any statement. Node parses the SNS `Message` with `JSON.parse`, so a body Ruby's `JSON.parse` rejects but JavaScript accepts would finish in Node where Rails raises; none is in the golden.
- `HandleNewUserComplianceInfoWorker` (8 cases), `RegenerateCreatorAnalyticsCacheWorker` (10 cases) and `ReindexUserElasticsearchDataWorker` (10 cases): `RecordNotFound` is raised in Node with Rails' message and statements; the existing record hands back before `StripeMerchantAccountManager`, `CreatorAnalytics::CachingProxy` or `DevTools.reindex_all_for_user` (and the admin note). A date Ruby cannot parse hands back with the rest.
- `ModerateProductsJob` (17 cases): no ids, and ids no product has, finish in Node with the batch read Rails sends (`1=0` is no statement; one id is `= x`, several `IN (...)`); a product that exists hands back before `ContentModeration::ModerateRecordService`. Ids that are not all Integers (digit strings, floats, nil, past 2**53) hand back before any statement.
- `RecordSocialScoreShadowEvaluationsJob` (17 cases): the cron tick pushes its dated re-enqueue under the unique-jobs lock (those two cases need `JOBS_REDIS_URL`), a dated run reads the verified user ids and the 500-id user slices with a replica lag check before each, and the first slice holding a user hands back before `SocialScoreShadowEvaluationService`. A date other than `YYYY-MM-DD` hands back before any statement.
- `MillionDollarMilestoneCheckWorker` (23 cases): the seller query (flag, purchase window, success states, chargeback rule) and one purchase search per seller run in Node; the first seller at or over $1M, and a sum that is not a number, hand back before the compliance info read, the Slack message, the flag update and the `InternalNotificationWorker` push. The sellers before it were all under the threshold and are checked again by Rails.

Left whole: `RefreshAudienceMemberJob` (`AudienceMember#refresh!` needs `Purchase#audience_member_details`, `should_be_audience_member?` and the follower and affiliate details, none of which Node has), `RefreshAffiliateEarningsJob` (the `AffiliateEarningsCache` claims live in `Rails.cache` and its retries-exhausted hook must stay paired with it), `HandleResendEventJob` and `HandleSendgridEventJob` (`HandleEmailEventInfo` writes mail events and unsubscribes), `RefreshSitemap*Worker` (S3), `RemoveDeletedFilesFromS3Job` (S3), `GoogleCalendarInviteJob` and `DetectFailingPaypalRailsJob` (outbound calls).
WS-17 rest 2 (lane 26d: `gumroad_test_26` and `gumroad_replay_26`, Redis 6-9 recording and 10 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c26d`, container `gr-ruby-c26d`). The six routes whose Rack::Attack throttle had kept them in Rails are now counted by the single counter in `rack-attack.ts` (no second counter) and answer in Node up to their first write; everything after hands back whole, un-counted. `nextjs_api_v2_products` serves `POST /v2/products`, `PUT`/`PATCH /v2/products/:id`, `GET /v2/products/comps` (registered ahead of `/v2/products/:id`) and `POST /v2/products/:id/preview_custom_html`, `nextjs_api_v2_media` serves `POST /v2/media`, and `nextjs_internal_ai_product_details` serves `POST /internal/ai_product_details_generations` (`api-v2/product-writes.ts`, `product-comps.ts`, `product-custom-html.ts`, `media.ts`, `controllers/internal-ai-product-details.ts`, `ai/openai-chat.ts`). Answered in Node: the scopes, the product and category lookups, the unsupported-upload-field, price, currency, taxonomy, tags, custom HTML (feature gate, type, length) and refund-policy refusals, the media `account` checks, and the whole AI route: sign-in, the hourly Redis throttle (the Ruby Lua script; the 429 carries `Retry-After`), the Pundit check, the prompt filter (Ruby's ASCII `\w`/`\s` against Unicode `\b`), the model call with its retry, and the response rendered with Ruby's `Float#to_s` and integer/float distinction. Handed back, each pinned by a "hands a ..." example: a create or update that passes the guards (callbacks, tags, rich content, files, price rows), a comps request with a body or one that reaches the Elasticsearch search, a custom HTML preview that reaches `Ai::PageSanitizer`, and a media create that reaches the download, moderation and file creation. 77 examples in `api/v2/products_throttled_parity_spec.rb` replay in `api-v2-throttled.replay` (79 tests) and 25 in `api/internal/ai_product_details_generations_parity_spec.rb` replay in `ai-product-details-generations.replay` (27 tests); the model exchange is a WebMock stub when recording and a stand-in server that asserts the request (path, content type, JSON body, bearer token) when replaying, so no run reached a live model. Each route has a "counts ..." example (the Rack::Attack keys Rails wrote), an "answers 429 ..." example and its hand-backs, which must leave every counter at zero (a hand-back un-counts, so the keys stay with value 0). With the `routes.json` entries removed the replays fail. Unit tests: `internal-ai-product-details.test.ts` (`sanitizePrompt`, `rubyFloat`). Not ported and not proven (the simple update has since moved to Node, see WS-17 rest 3): the create and the rest of the update writes, the comps search, the `PageSanitizer` preview, the media download and the agent `custom_html_previews` renders (those need `Ai::PageSanitizer`, `Pages::Interpolator` and related classes with byte-equal examples, which were not recorded).

WS-17 rest 3 (lane 28: `gumroad_test_28` and `gumroad_replay_28`, Redis 45-48 recording and 49 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c28a`, container `gr-ruby-c28a`). `PUT`/`PATCH /v2/products/:id` now writes in Node for one shape: `name`, `is_adult`, `display_product_reviews` and `should_show_sales_count` and nothing else (`api-v2/product-writes.ts`, `updateSimpleAttributes`), through `Links.updateBang`. `models/link.ts` gained what that save needs and nothing more: `name` as a ported change, the bits of those three flags, the `name`, `display_product_reviews` and `is_adult` entries of `ATTRIBUTE_TO_SEARCH_FIELDS_MAP` (in the Ruby map's order, which is the order of the fields in the update), the `content_has_no_adult_keywords` validation, and a `content_moderation_check` guard that raises `NotPortedError` for a rename of a published product. Handed back before the first statement, so a hand-back never follows a write: any other parameter the action reads (price, currency, description, tags, taxonomy, permalink, files, rich content, custom HTML, refund policy, covers), a name that is not text, a flag that is not a JSON `true`/`false` (FlagShihTzu's `TRUE_VALUES` and ActiveModel's casts differ), the rename of a published product (the moderation service is Ruby's), and a product with any applicable offer code (`check_offer_code_validity` runs after the save). Answered in Node, after the existing guards: the save, the `Name can't be blank`, `Name is too long (maximum is 255 characters)` and `Adult keywords are not allowed` refusals, and the product JSON of the response. 12 new examples and one renamed in `api/v2/products_throttled_parity_spec.rb` (seven that write, three refusals, two "hands a ..."), replayed by `api-v2-throttled.replay` (91 tests pass; before this every update example that passed the guards was a "hands a ..."). The replay now also drains the BullMQ jobs and points Node's Elasticsearch client at a recording server, and for every answered example it compares the jobs and the Elasticsearch requests Rails recorded (`POST /link-test/_update/1` with `{"doc":{"name":"Pen","rated_as_adult":false}}`; the index name is dropped because Rails' test index is `link-test`). Not proven: PaperTrail rows (off in Rails specs; the Link model's unit tests pin them), the order of the SQL statements inside the save beyond the row diff (the `link-writes` golden covers the shared chain), and a seller whose profile has adult keywords (no example). Unit tests: `api-v2/product-writes.test.ts` (12), `models/user-link.test.ts` (the refusal now uses `description`).

WS-17 mobile library writes (lane 27g: `gumroad_test_27` and `gumroad_replay_27`, Redis 11-14 recording and 17 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c27g`, container `gr-ruby-c27g`, `DISABLE_TEST_REDIS_ISOLATION=1`). `POST /mobile/purchases/:id/archive`, `.../unarchive` and `DELETE /mobile/purchases/:id` (`nextjs_mobile_purchases`) run in Node for the purchase `purchase_attributes` already serves (plain: no url redirect, preorder, subscription, refund or review, seller and product without posts): `fetch_purchase`, then the library's flag write (`setLibraryFlag`, the same `update!` the web library makes: `flags`, PaperTrail, the delayed Elasticsearch update and the analytics cache job) and the purchase's `json_data_for_mobile` as the row stands after the write. `mobile-purchases.ts` loads and checks everything the JSON needs (`plainPurchaseJson`) before the write, so a purchase Node cannot serialize hands back unchanged. Validations are not run, as in the web library port: a purchase that fails `save!` is a 422 in Rails and a write in Node; the recorded rows are valid paid purchases, because `purchase_row` rows (no processor details) fail `financial_transaction_validation`. 7 examples in `api/mobile/mobile_library_writes_parity_spec.rb` (`parity_timeline`; archive, unarchive, archive of an archived purchase, delete, an older purchase of a Tokyo seller, the 404 of a refunded purchase, and "hands an archive of a purchase whose seller has a post to Rails") replay in `mobile-api.replay` (8 with the existing library hand-back; with the controller reverted 6 fail). Unit tests: `mobile-purchases.test.ts` (45). Still handed back: a library or search page with rows (`Purchase.for_mobile_listing`, `preload_product_updates_data!`), a purchase with a url redirect (`UrlRedirect#product_json_data`), a preorder (`Preorder#mobile_json_data`), a membership (`subscription_data`), a refund or a review, and sales show. No debt line was open for it.

WS-17 mobile `UrlRedirect#product_json_data` (lane 27g, same resources as the library writes above). `purchase_attributes`, archive, unarchive and delete now also serve a purchase with a url redirect (`presenters/mobile-url-redirect.ts`): the product JSON plus `url_redirect_external_id`, `url_redirect_token` and `file_data` (`ProductFile#mobile_json_data`, the mobile `download_url` when `is_file_downloadable?`, `streaming_url`, `external_link_url`, `content_length`; a file outside S3 is named `.<filetype>` as in Rails). The files come from `url-redirects/files.ts#aliveProductFiles`, so a variant purchase, a product with variant categories or rich content, a preorder and a stamped PDF still hand back, as do a redirect of a post, a membership, several redirects for one purchase and any purchase with a `media_locations` row (`latest_media_location` and `latest_epub_location` are always null here). 4 examples in `mobile_library_writes_parity_spec.rb` ("with a url redirect": read with a video, a PDF and a link file; archive; a stream-only video; "hands a read of a purchase that has a media location to Rails") replay in `mobile-api.replay` (204 tests pass). "hands an archive of the buyer's own purchase to Rails" in `mobile_purchases_sales_parity_spec.rb` became "hands an archive of a purchase of a product with rich content to Rails": its free purchase has no url redirect row, so Node serves that one. Still handed back: the library and search pages with rows (`for_mobile_listing` and its filters), preorders, memberships, refund and review serialization, sales show, the media location branches, the MCP tools in the note below and the remaining mobile writes. Not started in this lane: `get_product`, `list_files`, `get_analytics`, `get_subscriptions`, MCP writes, OAuth client registration, `ProductFileIngest` (the first four and publish/unpublish moved in lane 27h, below).

WS-19 MCP read tools (lane 27: `gumroad_test_27` and `gumroad_replay_27`, Redis 11-13 and 15 recording and 17 replay, rack-attack 16, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c27f`, container `gr-ruby-c27f`, `DISABLE_TEST_REDIS_ISOLATION=1`). `mcp/tool-bodies.ts` now answers `get_sales_totals` for every breakdown, `list_sales` and `list_products`. The day, week and month breakdowns bucket by the seller's local date: the range is cut at each change of the zone's UTC offset (`offsetSegments`, found by scanning 6 hours at a time and bisecting to the second, on top of the new `utcOffsetAt` in `rails-time-zone.ts`) and each segment is grouped on `DATE(DATE_ADD(created_at, INTERVAL offset SECOND))`, as `Mcp::Server#sales_total_buckets` does. The country breakdown names each group with `Compliance::Countries.find_by_name` and merges spellings; it hands back a spelling the list does not know (MySQL's collation would pick which spelling a group carries) and two countries with equal revenue (Ruby's `sort_by` is not stable). `list_sales` is one join query (product, offer code, subscription flags, UTM link) with `paginate`'s keyset cursor; it reads `after`/`before` only as `YYYY-MM-DD` or whole-second ISO 8601 and hands back any other spelling, and hands back a sale driven by several UTM links (`has_one :utm_link, through:` picks one by no order). `list_products` serves buy-only, non-recurring, non-tiered products whose default price is a single alive buy `Price` in the product's currency (`Product::Prices#price_cents` reads that row, not the column); tiered memberships, rentals, recurring products and competing buy prices hand the whole listing back. 30 new examples in `mcp_parity_spec.rb` (the old "hands back a sales totals breakdown by country" example and its fixture are gone) replay in `api-v2-ws17c` (`WS17C_SPEC=mcp_parity`, 58 tests pass). Hour-granular seeds: the fixtures that print a sale or product time put every row on the hour and pin the seed clock to the recording hour in `PINNED_CLOCKS` (`05:00` for the totals and sales, `06:00` for the products), so a new example of the same names must be recorded within 30 minutes of that hour. Unit tests in `tool-bodies.test.ts` (day/week/month segments across the 2026-03-08 US change, country merge, ties and unknown names, sales filters and cursor, products). Not done: the other MCP tools (`get_product`, `list_files`, `get_analytics`, `get_subscriptions` and the writes), OAuth client registration, `ProductFileIngest`, and every WS-17 mobile hand-back (purchases index/search/show, sales show, the mobile writes): those need `Purchase#json_data_for_mobile`, `UrlRedirect#product_json_data` and the Pundit-backed write chains, none of which has a Node port or a byte-equal recording yet. The older branch `nextjs/ws-lane21e-mcp-whole-days` had nothing unmerged (its tip is an ancestor of `nextjs-rewrite`).

WS-19 MCP product, analytics, subscription and publication tools (lane 27h: `gumroad_test_27` and `gumroad_replay_27`, Redis 11-14 recording and 17 replay, rack-attack 16, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c27h` and `ES_TEST_INDEX_PREFIX=c27h-`, container `gr-ruby-c27h`, `DISABLE_TEST_REDIS_ISOLATION=1`). `mcp/tool-bodies.ts` now answers `get_product`, `list_files`, `get_analytics`, `get_subscriptions` (breakdown `subscriptions`), `publish_product` and `unpublish_product`; all share `find_product!` (blank id is the -32602 `id is required`, a missing, other-seller's or deleted product is the tool error `The product was not found.`) and `productPayload`, which `list_products` now uses too (tiered memberships, rentals, recurring products and competing buy prices still hand back, in every tool). `get_product` adds the description, covers that are Unsplash photos (an uploaded or oembed cover hands back) and, for a token with `view_sales` or `account`, `sales_count` and `sales_usd_cents` from the ported `successfulSalesCount` and `totalUsdCents` (Elasticsearch); a bundle hands back (its members are not read). `sales_usd_cents` is a Ruby Float, so tool results now print it through `FloatValue` and `toolText` (`JSON.pretty_generate` spells `1500.0`; `structuredContent` carries the plain number). `list_files` hands back files that share a position (`ORDER BY position` leaves their order open). `get_analytics` reads the ported `CreatorAnalyticsCachingProxy` and sums per product or referrer; Ruby's `sort_by { -views }` is unstable, so any two rows with equal views, and permalinks or referrers a JavaScript object would reorder (integer-like names), hand back whole; the conversion rate is `FloatValue(rubyRoundHalfUp(sales / views, 4))`; a date that is not `YYYY-MM-DD` hands back, a range wholly before the account's creation day answers `rows: []` without reading analytics. `get_subscriptions` is one join query (non-test subscriptions of the seller's products, `Subscription.active` and its complement for `status`) plus the purchases of the page; the status is `customers.ts#subscriptionStatus`, the cursor is in microseconds (`TIMESTAMPDIFF`). Handed back: `breakdown: churn` (`CreatorAnalytics::Churn` rates are Ruby Floats the cached payload does not keep apart from integers), a subscription whose renewals a card mandate disabled, a gifted subscription, several original purchases and tied newest purchases (`max_by`). `publish_product`/`unpublish_product` reuse `publishBang`, `assertPlainPublish` and `Links.updateBang` (the API v2 enable and disable ports), refuse everything Node cannot follow before the first write (offer codes on the product, physical, sales, transcoding tier, direct affiliates for all products, seller plan gate, unverified seller with content moderation) and answer a `LinkInvalid` message as a tool error. Recorded from `spec/requests/api/v2/mcp_products_parity_spec.rb` (25 + 16 + 17 examples) and `mcp_publication_parity_spec.rb` (26, `parity_timeline`), replayed by `api-v2-ws17c.replay` (`WS17C_SPEC=mcp_products` 59 tests and `mcp_publication` 27 pass; with the `toolBody` cases renamed away 12 to 18 of each tool's examples fail). The replay rebuilds the `purchases` and `product_page_views` indices of an example whose name has `-reports-the-sales-figures-` or `-get-analytics-` from the fixture (`seededDocuments`), and pins the seed clock to the hour after the recording for `-get-analytics-` and `-get-subscriptions-` (the `<now-Nh>` tokens round to the nearest hour, so a recording at 10:46 pins to 11:00). Unit tests: `tool-bodies.test.ts` (159 with the older ones). Not proven: `structuredContent` prints `1500` where Rails prints `1500.0` (JSON-equal), PaperTrail rows (off in specs), the Elasticsearch `Link` reindex a publish queues beyond what the timeline records, and a seller on a time zone Node does not know (hands back). Still handed back: the other MCP write tools (`create_draft_product`, `update_product`, `add_cover`, `add_file`, bundles, discount codes), `get_subscriptions` churn, OAuth client registration and `ProductFileIngest` (not started); the WS-17 mobile hand-backs listed under "WS-17 mobile `UrlRedirect#product_json_data`" above (library and search pages with rows, sales show, preorder, membership, refund and review serialization, the media location branches) were not started in this lane either.

WS-19 MCP write tools (lane 27i: `gumroad_test_27` and `gumroad_replay_27`, Redis 11-14 recording and 17 replay, rack-attack 16, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c27i` and `ES_TEST_INDEX_PREFIX=c27i-`, container `gr-ruby-c27i`, `DISABLE_TEST_REDIS_ISOLATION=1`). `mcp/tool-bodies.ts` now answers `create_draft_product`, `update_product`, `create_discount_code` and `update_discount_code`. `create_draft_product` reuses `createApiLink` (the API v2 create port) for a digital draft in USD with a price above zero and a description with no `<`, `>` or `&`; the tier argument errors of a membership (`validated_tiers!`) are answered in Node, a valid membership, a free product (`set_customizable_price` runs after the save), a seller pricing in another currency, markup in the description and a token without the scope hand back before any write. `update_product` serves name, description and `price_cents` on a product with one buy price (`planBuyPriceWrite`/`writeBuyPrice`, `plannedDescription` and `Links.updateBang` from the API v2 update port, `lock!` first); a `permalink` argument (seller lock, outgoing slug redirect), the rename of a published product (content moderation), a description over upsell cards, a product beside discount codes (the warning lists the codes the new price breaks) and a tiered membership hand back. `create_discount_code` and `update_discount_code` reuse the OfferCode port (`buildOfferCode`, `addProduct`, `assertPlainCode`, `assertPlainProducts`) for plain codes, with the seller row locked first as in Ruby; times are served only as `YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm:ss` with `Z` or an offset (a bare date, a fraction or a non-time hands back), an update keeps the kind of amount and the currency (a switch between percentage and fixed amount re-derives which products the code covers and hands back), and `expire_discount_code` hands back (`save!(context: :expiring)` skips the price validation, which the port has no context for). Recorded from `spec/requests/api/v2/mcp_product_writes_parity_spec.rb` (25 + 23 examples) and `mcp_discount_codes_parity_spec.rb` (49), all `parity_timeline`, replayed by `api-v2-ws17c.replay` (`WS17C_SPEC=mcp_product_writes` 49 tests, `mcp_discount_codes` 49 pass; with `writeBuyPrice`, the name strip or the `max_purchase_count` assignment broken, 3, 1 and 5 of them fail). The replay now also swaps the permalink Rails drew inside an MCP result's JSON text and ignores the session cookie value when it re-compares after the swap. Not proven: PaperTrail rows (off in specs; the tools run outside the `withPaperTrailRequest` wrapper of the REST controllers, so production `versions` rows would lack the request ip, path and uuid), the exact `updated_at` microseconds of rows the replay seeds at its own clock. Still handed back, not started in this lane: `add_cover` (fetches the URL and runs Active Storage analysis), `add_file` (`Mcp::ProductFileIngest`, a download from the network; `ProductFile` creation from ws-rest-sweep-5 has not landed on `nextjs-rewrite`), `create_bundle`/`update_bundle`, `expire_discount_code`, memberships in `create_draft_product`, permalink changes in `update_product`, OAuth client registration, `get_subscriptions` churn (Ruby Floats the cached payload keeps apart from integers), equal-views ordering in `get_analytics`, and the WS-17 mobile hand-backs listed above (library and search pages with rows, sales show, preorder/membership/refund/review serialization, media location branches).

WS-19 MCP write tools, second pass (lane 27j: `gumroad_test_27` and `gumroad_replay_27`, Redis 11-14 recording and 17 replay, rack-attack 16, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c27j` and `ES_TEST_INDEX_PREFIX=c27j-`, container `gr-ruby-c27j`, `DISABLE_TEST_REDIS_ISOLATION=1`). `expire_discount_code` runs in Node for a plain code: `defineModel` takes `context` on a save (`record.validationContext` while the validations run, and `on: create/update` callbacks do not match a custom context, as in Rails), and the OfferCode port declares `price_validation` with `unless: expiring`. 15 examples in `mcp_discount_codes_parity_spec.rb` (`parity_timeline`) replay in `api-v2-ws17c.replay`, among them a code whose product price now fails the check, a membership code, an already-expired code and the lookup strip with a newline; tiered codes and a token without the scope hand back. Mutations: with the `unless` removed the skipping-the-price-check example fails; with the lookup strip removed in `findDiscountCode` the update and expire strip examples fail (the strip in `create_discount_code` cannot be shown by any example, because the model's own `stripped_fields` strips the code again before it is validated or saved). `get_subscriptions` with `breakdown: churn` runs in Node on `churnData` (the dashboard port) with `churn_rate_percent` as a `FloatValue`; 13 examples (the purchases index filled by the replay) pass and 6 fail with the wrapper removed; a date not written YYYY-MM-DD still hands back, and the Faraday timeout message ("Churn took too long") is not ported, since the replay cannot make Elasticsearch time out. `get_analytics` no longer hands back rows with equal views: `models/concerns/ruby-sort-by.ts` ports the uniform introsort of `Enumerable#sort_by` for Integer keys (insertion sort up to 16 rows, quicksort with a median pivot, heapsort past the depth limit) and matches Ruby 3.4.12 on the 363 arrays of `parity/golden/ruby-sort-by-integer.json`; two examples with 18 products and 18 referrers (past the insertion sort) fail when a stable sort replaces it. The port assumes the production Ruby sorts like the recording one (Ruby 3.4, not a build that routes `sort_by` through a libc `qsort_r`). Every `tools/call` runs inside `withPaperTrailRequest` (`mcpPaperTrailRequest`: remote ip from the forwarded chain, request path, request id or a drawn uuid, no whodunnit for a token); PaperTrail is off in specs, so the replay cannot show it and `ws17c-mcp.test.ts` pins the request a tool body runs under (it fails without the wrapper). Still handed back, each with a recorded `hands a ... back` example (`mcp_product_writes_parity_spec.rb`, "tools that stay in Rails"): `add_cover` and `add_file` (a URL fetch plus Active Storage analysis; no recording proves the fetch and the blob writes in order, and WebMock stand-ins only cover the failing fetch), `create_bundle` and `update_bundle` (a product, its taxonomy and `Bundle::UpdateProductsService` across several tables, order not recorded) and a valid OAuth client registration (random client id and secret). Not started in this lane: the WS-17 mobile hand-backs (library and search rows, sales show, preorder/membership/refund/review serialization, media-location branches), memberships in `create_draft_product` and permalink changes in `update_product`. The 2099 validity-instant examples of `mcp_discount_codes` were re-recorded because their `<now+Nh>` tokens had drifted by an hour from the earlier recording.

WS-17 rest 4 (lane 28: `gumroad_test_28` and `gumroad_replay_28`, Redis 45-48 recording and 49 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c28b`, container `gr-ruby-c28b`). The v2 offer code writes now run in Node for a plain code: `POST /v2/products/:link_id/offer_codes`, `PUT`/`PATCH /v2/offer_codes/:id`, `PUT`/`PATCH /v2/products/:link_id/offer_codes/:id` and `DELETE /v2/products/:link_id/offer_codes/:id` (`controllers/api-v2/offer-codes.ts`). `models/offer-code-writes.ts` is the write side of `OfferCode` (`defineModel`: the `before_validation` chain with `stripped_fields`, the validations that can fire for a plain code in Ruby's order and with Ruby's messages, the HABTM join insert, PaperTrail callbacks, `invalidate_product_cache`, `reindex_associated_products` after commit, `repair_detached_default_discounts`), `models/offer-code-index.ts` ports `ReindexSellerOfferCodesJob.enqueue` and `.enqueue_products` (the INCR and the Lua script of the main Redis, then `perform_async`; a Redis error is logged and skipped as in Ruby). `link-data.generated.json` gained each currency's `symbol` (the price message prints it). Plain means: no automatic application, cancellation or upsell role, customer limits, duration tiers, duration in billing cycles, excluded products, option scope or ownership products on the code (`assertPlainCode`), and applicable products that are neither memberships nor rentals nor have options, in a currency Node holds a minimum for (`assertPlainProducts`). Both run before the first statement and before `offer_code.products << product` (which in Ruby queues the reindex at once, so a refused non-universal create still enqueues one job), so a hand-back never follows a write. Also handed back: `auto_apply: true`, a non-text name, an amount that is a list, hash, fraction or outside the 32-bit column, a validity period with a fraction of a second. Strings follow ActiveModel's integer cast (`"many"` is 0, `"1e3"` is 1, `"5.5"` is 5, blank is nil; each recorded). Recorded quirks reproduced: a refused deletion answers "The offer_code was not found." (the action renders the error without the record); a refused non-universal create has already enqueued its job; an update that changes nothing still runs the callbacks and enqueues. 82 examples in `api/v2/offer_codes_writes_parity_spec.rb` replay in `api-v2-ws17c.replay` (the row diffs, the response and the jobs; 82 pass with the `routes.json` entries unchanged). The replay collapses identical `ReindexSellerOfferCodesJob` pushes (`lock: :until_executing`: Rails' test queue records each push, the lock drops the repeat), ignores `updated_at` on the update diff (seed-clock noise, as for users) and compares the 2099 validity instants by hours with a tolerance of one. Unit tests: `offer-code-index.test.ts`. Not proven: the order of the statements inside the save (the recorder keeps row diffs), the Redis writes of the enqueue (the fixture holds Redis as it was before the request; the Lua script is copied and its arguments pinned by the unit test), PaperTrail rows of `OfferCode` (off in specs, `db.versions` is ignored; the generic port is golden-tested) and the whodunnit of a request that carries a session (a token request is a null session).

WS-17 rest 5 (lane 28, container `gr-ruby-c28c`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c28c`). Version (variant) create, update and destroy of a plain version run in Node (`models/variant-writes.ts`, `api-v2/product-resource-writes.ts`): position, max_purchase_count clamp, validations in Ruby's order, the after-commit product touch, cache invalidation and search reindex, and the destroy audit row (`recordVariantDeletion`). Handed back before the first write: tiered membership, call and coffee products, a price of 0 default, price-change flags, own prices, tier update/delete, `rich_content`, and non-integer params outside ActiveModel's cast. 43 examples in `variants_writes_parity_spec.rb` (`parity_timeline: true`; replaces `products_api_variants_parity_spec.rb`); statement order is pinned by `variant-writes.test.ts`. `PUT /v2/products/:id/enable` (`Link#publish!`) runs in Node for a plain product (`models/link-publish.ts`; 22 examples in `product_enable_parity_spec.rb`, order pinned by `link-publish.test.ts`); handed back: deleted or physical product, unverified seller while content moderation is on, seller plan gate, no payment address, tier 3 seller, a product with sales, a direct affiliate for all products, a product with discount codes. Still not done when this pass began (price, description and tags moved in WS-17 rest 6; files and the offer code states did not): the `PUT /v2/products/:id` price, description, tags and files params (kept as hand-backs; `save_tags!` takes counter-cache locks in tag-id order and the price/file saves write across several tables, not yet proven by a timeline spec) and the offer-code leftovers (auto_apply, cancellation/upsell, customer limits, duration tiers, excluded products), which stay behind `assertPlainCode`.

WS-17 rest 6 (lane 28: `gumroad_test_28` and `gumroad_replay_28`, Redis 45-48 recording and 49 replay, 52 for Rack::Attack, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c28d`, container `gr-ruby-c28d`). `PUT`/`PATCH /v2/products/:id` now also writes `price`, `description` and `tags` in Node, alone or with `name` and the three flags, in the action's transaction (`product-writes.ts`: the Price row is saved first, as `price_cents=` does while the attributes are assigned, then the product, then the tags, so a refusal anywhere rolls all three back). `models/price-writes.ts` is `BasePrice::Shared#create_or_update_new_price!` for the one live USD buy price of a product that is not a membership, tier, rental, preorder, physical, call, coffee, commission or bundle product (a JSON integer or a text of digits; `suggested_price_cents` of the row is reset to NULL as Ruby does; the price range, unverified-seller maximum and `set_customizable_price` of a free price are the `Link` validations and callbacks already ported). `Price`'s `belongs_to :link, touch: true` is `touch_later`: the `UPDATE links` runs just before the commit, after the tags, which the recorded `timeline` shows (a price-only save writes `prices` then `links` once). `models/product-tags.ts` ports `save_tags!`, `tag!` and `untag!` with `Tag` and `ProductTagging` (counter cache `taggings_count` by a relative `UPDATE`, one `["tags"]` search update per tagging after commit): the work is sorted by the tag row's id with removals first, which is the counter-cache lock order, and the recorded example with a removal whose tag sorts after an addition's pins it (`link-update-writes.test.ts` replays the statement order of that example and of the price save against the fixtures' `timeline`). `description` goes through `nokogiriRoundtrip` (a well-formed fragment of the few tags in `html-fragment.ts`, no `upsell-card` in the old or the new text) and `Link` now treats a changed description like a changed name (adult keywords, search fields `description` and `rated_as_adult`, IndexNow, and the content moderation guard). Handed back before the first write, each with a "hands a ..." example: a price that is not whole cents or digits (`"3.50"`, `""`, a fraction, a sign), a product outside the shape above, one with any other live price row, a description that is not text, that Nokogiri would rewrite, that replaces or holds upsell cards, or that changes on a published product (moderation is Ruby's), a tag that is not 2 to 20 characters of lowercase ASCII (capitals, commas, hashes, doubled spaces), a repeated tag, a tag that the collation matches to a tag spelled differently or two names that resolve to one tag. Answered in Node: the "too large" refusal of `price_cents=` (before any write), the minimum, the unverified-seller maximum and the adult-keyword refusals of the save (the Price update is rolled back; the recorded `timeline` for the refused ones shows the `update prices` that Rails also rolled back). 35 examples added to `api/v2/products_throttled_parity_spec.rb` (21 served, 14 "hands a ..."; the two old price hand-backs became served ones), recorded on lane 28 and replayed by `api-v2-throttled.replay` (124 tests pass; with `price`, `tags` and `description` handed back again, 21 fail). The replay now ignores `db.prices.updated.*.changes.updated_at` (the price row is made in the second of its request, so the seed clock can equal the write's). Unit tests: `link-update-writes.test.ts` (14), `api-v2/product-writes.test.ts` (16). Not proven: PaperTrail rows of `Price` (off in specs; the item type is assumed to be the base class `BasePrice`), the runtime runs the deferred link touch as a before-commit hook after the Prisma transaction has closed (same statement order, but that one `UPDATE` is not in the transaction), non-ASCII tag names and tags with several rows of the same spelling (handed back, not recorded), prices in another currency (the `Link` price validation needs a rate from Redis or HTTP), and the order of the Elasticsearch partial updates of a save that changes a price and a tag (the replay compares the requests in order, which match). Still in Rails for the update after rest 6 (rest 7 below serves `taxonomy_id`, `customizable_price`, `suggested_price_cents`, `max_purchase_count`, `quantity_enabled`, `custom_receipt` and `custom_summary`): `files` (S3 checks, `SaveFilesService`, archives), `rich_content`, `custom_html`, the refund policy params, covers, `price_currency_type`, `custom_permalink`, `has_same_rich_content_for_all_variants`, and the price of a membership, rental, recurring or non-USD product.

`POST /v2/products` was still handed back whole after its refusals when rest 6 ended; WS-17 rest 7 below serves it for a plain digital product.

The v2 offer code writes now also run in Node for a code applied automatically (`auto_apply`, `sale_label`): `POST /v2/products/:link_id/offer_codes`, `PUT`/`PATCH` on a code and the delete of an auto-applied code (`models/offer-code-writes.ts`: `lock_seller_for_auto_apply` as a locking read of the seller, `validate_auto_apply` with its "only a discount that applies to all products" and "another discount (CODE) is already applied automatically" messages, the `FOR UPDATE` read of the seller's other codes of a saved code, `validate_sale_label`, and `normalize_sale_label` stripping the label). `assertPlainCode` no longer refuses `auto_apply`; it still hands back before the first write: a cancellation or upsell discount, a code limited to existing or new customers, a duration in billing cycles or duration tiers, excluded products, option scope and ownership products (each recorded as a "hands a ..." example), and a sale label that is not text. 16 examples added to `offer_codes_writes_parity_spec.rb` (the two auto-apply hand-backs became served ones) and replayed by `api-v2-ws17c.replay` (97 offer code tests pass; with `validate_auto_apply` disabled 4 fail). Not proven: the order of the statements inside the save (the seller lock is a read, which the `timeline` does not record) and `Current.auto_apply_offer_codes`, which lives for one Rails request. Still open for offer codes: the cancellation, upsell, customer-limit, duration-tier and excluded-product states (`validate_*` for each, `ownership_products` joins, `renewal_eligible` reads, and `invalidate_excluded_product_cache`/`reindex_removed_product` callbacks on the HABTM) and the creation of a code on a membership or a product with options.


WS-17 rest 7 (lane 28: `gumroad_test_28`, `gumroad_replay_28`, Redis 45-47 recording, 49 replay, 52 for Rack::Attack, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c28e`, container `gr-ruby-c28e`). **Replay hygiene.** The `links` fixture "hands back the comps collection to Rails" was stale (comps moved to Node in WS-17 rest 4): the example is now "answers a comps request without a taxonomy or a query" and is recorded as the served refusal. The offer-code "strips the name of the code" flake was the first request to a route: `next dev` compiled it (10 to 15 s), so the seeded timestamps aged to `<now+10s>` against `<now>` whenever that fixture ran first (a filtered run, or the first of its route). `api-v2-ws17c.replay` now replays each route once, unchecked, ahead of its first fixture (`warmRoute`), so a filtered run is as steady as a full one (777 tests pass). **`POST /v2/products` runs in Node** (`api-v2/product-writes.ts` `createDigitalProduct`, `models/link-create.ts` `createApiLink`): a plain `digital` product (the default or an explicit `native_type: "digital"`) with `name`, `price` (raw `price_cents=`: a positive JSON integer or a text of digits, never `clean_price`; over `MAX_PRICE_CENTS` is refused before any write), `description` (the Nokogiri round trip of `html-fragment.ts`, so the second `save!` is the no-op Rails also makes), `custom_summary`, `tags`, `taxonomy_id` (blank means the `other` taxonomy) and `draft`/`published` (ActiveModel's boolean cast, `castBoolean`). The first save reuses the `LinkCreations` callback chain of the web form (`set_unique_permalink`, daily creation limit, validations, `autosave_prices`, `enqueue_search_index!`, profile sections); then the description save, `save_tags!` (planned before the first write with `planTagChanges(0n, ...)`) and the commit; `publishBang` runs after the commit, outside the transaction, and its refusals answer the `Saved as a draft: ...` warning (an unconfirmed seller is recorded). Handed back before any write, each with a "hands a ... back to Rails" example: a missing, null, zero or non-whole price (a free product sets `customizable_price` in an after-save), `custom_permalink`, `max_purchase_count`, `customizable_price`, `suggested_price_cents`, `refund_period`/`refund_fine_print`, `rich_content`, `files` (S3), a native type other than digital, a currency other than USD, a request from the command line tool (`created_via_cli`), a description Nokogiri rewrites or with upsell cards, a tag outside the plain shape, a seller with a discount code that applies (`validate_product_price_against_all_offer_codes?` then decides the warning) or whose publication `assertPlainPublish` does not prove (checked with a synthetic draft before the first statement). 40 examples in `api/v2/product_create_parity_spec.rb` (`parity_timeline: true`), replayed by `api-v2-ws17c.replay` with the random one-letter permalink swapped as `links-create.replay` does (`reconcilePermalink`); with the create handed back again 22 of them fail. The product JSON now serves a product with a taxonomy (`taxonomy_id`, `category`, `category_label` from the table `GET /v2/categories` reads), which every created product has through `other`; the old "hands back a product with a taxonomy category" example became "shows the category of a product with a taxonomy". Not proven: the `Link::CreationLimit` message (the specs lift the limit, so the message is only pinned by the model port), the order of statements of the create (the `timeline` is recorded but only the db diff, jobs and response are compared; there is no `link-create` timeline test for the API path), PaperTrail rows of the create (off in specs), and a seller with the `seller_plan_gate` or content moderation on (handed back by `assertPlainPublish`, as for `enable`).

**`PUT`/`PATCH /v2/products/:id` takes eight more params in Node**, alone or together with name, flags, price, description and tags, in the action's transaction: `customizable_price` and `quantity_enabled` (JSON booleans; the flag bit 14 joins `PORTED_FLAG_BITS`), `suggested_price_cents` and `max_purchase_count` (a JSON integer, a text of digits, `null` or `""`; beyond the 32-bit range goes to Rails, which answers `One or more numeric values are out of range.`), `taxonomy_id` (already validated by the before action; `null` and `""` clear it), `custom_receipt` (text, at most 10,000 characters, or `null`) and `custom_summary` (a `json_data` key, text or `null`, now in `PORTED_JSON_DATA_KEYS`). The `Link` model port gained the missing `max_purchase_count_is_greater_than_or_equal_to_inventory_sold` validation (`sales_count_for_inventory_cache` read fresh) and the search fields of `max_purchase_count`, `taxonomy_id` and `customizable_price` from `ATTRIBUTE_TO_SEARCH_FIELDS_MAP`; the clamp of `max_purchase_count` (below 0 to 0, above 10,000,000 to 10,000,000) and the suggested-price validations were already there. 39 examples added to `products_throttled_parity_spec.rb` (pay-what-you-want and suggested price 10, purchase limit 10, quantity 3, taxonomy 6, receipt and summary 9, custom permalink 1; the "hands a ..." ones are a flag as text, a fraction, a value past the integer range, a summary or receipt that is not text and the custom permalink), replayed by `api-v2-throttled.replay` (161 tests pass; with the eight params handed back again 31 fail, and with the inventory validation removed the "refuses a limit under the number sold" example fails). One recorded example had to be re-recorded: Elasticsearch answered `document_missing_exception` to the reindex Rails sent, so Rails queued its `SendToElasticsearchWorker` fallback, which Node (whose stand-in answers the update) cannot reproduce. **Still handed back for the update**: `custom_permalink` (the permalink claim: `lock_for_permalink_claim!`, `release_custom_permalink_if_possible`, `stage_renamed_custom_permalink` and the redirect rows are not in the model port, and the example exists only as a hand-back), `price_currency_type`, `has_same_rich_content_for_all_variants`, `refund_period`/`refund_fine_print`, `custom_html`, `files`, `rich_content`, `cover_ids`, and a text, number or list in place of the values above. Not proven: a `max_purchase_count` or `customizable_price` change on a tiered membership, a bundle or a product with sales rows (the JSON of a tiered membership or bundle is still handed back before the write), the order of statements of these saves (recorded `timeline` on one example per group, not replayed against a unit test), and `quantity_enabled` on a membership or call product (JSON hand-back precedes it).

Earlier text, kept for history (variant category writes and disable were already native): variant category and variant writes and product publish/unpublish (`LinksController#enable`) were not started: they need a ported `VariantCategory`/`Variant` save and `Link#publish!` with Rails-recorded SQL order, job enqueues and responses, and no example was recorded for them in this lane. They stay in Rails whole, so nothing in them changed.

Still handed back, with the reason it cannot be proven yet: `POST /v2/products` (a new `Link` row: `associate_price`, permalink generation, `set_template_properties_if_needed`, `SaveContentUpsellsService`, tags and, with `files`, S3 `head_object` calls; the model guard `Link#save on a new record` is still in place), every other `PUT` parameter (price rows, `SaveFilesService`, rich content, tags, custom HTML through `Ai::PageSanitizer`, refund policy rows, `lock_for_permalink_claim!`), `LinksController#enable` (`publish!`), the offer code writes (`Api::V2::OfferCodesController#create`, `#update`, `#destroy`: `OfferCode` is read-only in Node, with no ported validations, join rows or product reindex), the variant writes, and the S3 file endpoints (presign, complete, abort, direct uploads: no MinIO or WebMock exchange was recorded). `GET /integrations/zoom/oauth_redirect` and `GET /integrations/google_calendar/oauth_redirect` stay in Rails and are not registered in `routes.json`: both do `render inline: "", layout: "application", status: params.key?(:code) ? :ok : :bad_request`, and the layout holds the session's CSRF token, the Vite tags and the shared meta tags, none of which a recorded fixture can compare (the recorder keeps no body for the 400, and the 200 differs per session). Discord's `oauth_redirect` keeps its guards in Node and hands this same page back (see its paragraph above); Zoom and Google Calendar have nothing to run before it, so no "hands a ..." example was added.
WS-11/12 leftovers, fifth pass (lane 15: `gumroad_test_15d`/`gumroad_replay_15d`, Redis 56/57/59/60 recording and 63 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15d`, container `gr-ruby-c15d`; the replays also need a `purchases-c15d` index, which the product-search sorts read and no replay file creates). Each item has recorded `parity: true` examples and was checked by reverting the source change (the failing counts are given).

- Profile `tags[]=`, `tags[key]=` and `filetypes[]=` visitor filters (`visitorSearchParams`, `requireHtmlPage` with `nestedKeys`): Rack's list and hash shapes of the two filters run in Node; a flat key mixed with a nested one, `tags[1]=` (JavaScript would reorder integer-like keys), a deeper nesting, a valueless nested key, `filetypes[key]=` (Elasticsearch gets the hash in Rails) and any other nested visitor param hand back, as does a repeated nested key (`tags[a]=x&tags[a]=y`: `rack-attack.ts`'s parser refuses it before the page). 5 new examples in `storefront_profile_products_parity_spec.rb`; the old "nested param" hand-back example is gone.
- Tiered-membership cards on a profile: the `NotPortedError` guard is removed; Rails' page for a profile with a tiered membership is recorded and replays. 3 of the new profile examples fail with the source changes reverted.
- Profile sections whose `type` is no section class: `ActiveRecord::SubclassNotFound` is a 500 in Rails, so the page and the Inertia visit answer the 500 page (2 examples; the replay ignores the unrecorded debug-page body, as `wishlist-products-index.replay` does). A `NULL` type cannot exist (`seller_profile_sections.type` is NOT NULL); the hand-back for it was dead code and is gone.
- Wishlist owners without a username: `username` falls back to the external id, so the page renders on `<external_id>.<root domain>` (the wishlist URL and `profile_url`). 3 examples (`wishlist_show_page_parity_spec.rb`, `wishlist_products_index_parity_spec.rb`); the show page 500s with the presenter change reverted.
- `recommended_by=search|discover` on a product page: the click is written to `discover_searches` (`clicked_resource` the product, the product's own `taxonomy_id`, `query`, `autocomplete`) and a suggestion row for a present non-autocomplete query, after the page is proven renderable and before the noindex header; bots and the `store_discover_searches` flag decide as on the Discover page. 9 examples (`product_page_parity_spec.rb`, "arriving from Discover"); 7 fail with the presenter change reverted.
- A storefront seller's product page (`product_page_storefront_enabled`, visitor who is not the seller): the page is `Products/Profile/Show` with the seller's products sections (or the virtual default section headed "More from <name>") below the product, minus the viewed product (`exclude_ids`, the total shrunk whether the product is on the fetched page or beyond it), the visitor's search filters applied. Discover pages and embeds stay catalogue-free, the seller's own view stays standalone. 15 examples (`product_page_parity_spec.rb`, "of a storefront seller"); 12 fail with the filter, kind and presenter changes reverted. Still hands back: a product with sections of its own.
- Not done, with the reasons: (1) custom HTML profile pages and the custom HTML store pages need Node ports of `Pages::Interpolator`, `Pages::ProfileData`, `Pages::ProductPrices` and the page sanitizer. (2) The ERB layout pages (`home#*`, `/mcp`, `/claude`, `/muse`, `/gumclaw`, `/js/gumroad*`, `POST /custom_domain/verify`): a recording of `GET /gumclaw` (cookie-less, GitHub star count seeded in the Rails cache) shows what a byte-equal port needs: the `home` and `application` layouts, `erb_meta_tags` (the `ApplicationController` meta tags as `<meta ... inertia="head_key">` lines, plus the masked CSRF token), the design settings JSON, the nonce'd `javascript_tag` and Lottie script, `Link: <vite entrypoint>; rel=preload` from `infra/vite.ts`, the XSRF, session and guid cookies of a non-bare handler, and hand-written ports of `home/shared/{nav,header,button,how_we_build,footer}` (nav and footer read `GithubStarsController.cached_count`, `signed_in?` and a seller external id). The replay would have to mask the CSRF token and the CSP nonce in the body. No part of it is built; the recording was not kept because nothing replays it. (3) Uploaded and oEmbed cover URLs: not started, so the signed CDN/S3 URL proof (`signed-urls-fixtures.test.ts`) was not extended to them.

`update-tax-rates-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16e`, Redis 33), 82 cases, with TaxJar's `/v2/summary_rates` answered by WebMock (`capture_http!`) and nothing replaced; the generator rewrites the gem's user agent (it names the machine's uname, Ruby and OpenSSL builds) to `(<platform>)`. Node sends no machine description and no timeout is set beyond 60 seconds (the gem's `timeout` option is nil, so Ruby waits indefinitely). Rails reads `average_rate.rate` lazily and fails with a NoMethodError or ArgumentError in the middle of the writes for a qualifying entry whose rate is missing, null or an unparsable string; Node reads every qualifying entry first and hands back before the first write, as it does for a numeric string (the gem coerces `"0.05"` to a Float), a rate of 10 or more (the `decimal(8, 7)` column rejects it) and a rate with more than 15 significant digits (`BigDecimal == Float` compares through a rounding). `find_or_create_by(country:)` fails validation without a combined rate and writes nothing, the row is inserted by the following `update`; the golden pins the two selects, the single INSERT and the second transaction of `mark_undeleted!`. Quoting a whole-number `Decimal` now writes `1.0`, as `BigDecimal#to_s("F")` does.

`delete-product-files-worker.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16e`, Redis 33), 15 cases, with Elasticsearch answered by a local socket and `ProductFile#delete!` replaced by a recorder that raises; a deleted product with no sales and a file row (live or already deleted: `product.product_files` has no scope) reaches `delete!` in Ruby and Node hands back after the files query. The delete soft-deletes the row through PaperTrail, invalidates the product cache, retires the HLS renditions and deletes the subtitle files; none of that is ported. A Float or word id is cast by the column in ways Node does not reproduce and hands back before any statement.

`compile-gumroad-daily-analytics-job.golden.json` was recorded on lane 16 (`gumroad_test_16e`, Redis 33), 28 cases of 414 statements each (46 days). `Date.today` and `date.all_day` are read as UTC dates, which is what the workers run in (`config.time_zone` is unset). The day's bounds are cast to whole seconds because `purchases.created_at` and `service_charges.created_at` are `datetime(0)`. A sum past the 4-byte integer column raises ActiveModel::RangeError when the row is written, after all four aggregates ran and before the transaction opens; the golden pins that.

`RefreshAffiliateEarningsJob` was looked at on lane 16 and not ported: `AffiliateEarningsCache` stores `{ cents:, computed_at: Time.current }` in `Rails.cache`, an ActiveSupport::TimeWithZone, and `server/cache/marshal.ts` refuses Time (it pins only nil, booleans, numbers, strings, symbols, arrays and hashes against `Marshal.dump`). Reading the stale-check value or writing the refreshed one needs a Marshal vector for TimeWithZone first (`[utc, zone name, time]` as a `U` object holding `Time` `_dump` bytes with its offset, zone and nano ivars), recorded in `rails-cache.golden.json`; the claim locks (`write unless_exist`), the `MAX_EXECUTION_TIME` hinted sum and the retries-exhausted release follow from it. `DeleteProductFilesWorker` stays handed back at `ProductFile#delete!` for the same reason as the other soft deletes (PaperTrail, `invalidate_cache`, HLS renditions, subtitle files).

WS-10 rest 4 (lane 23, container `gr-ruby-c23e`, `gumroad_test_23`/`gumroad_replay_23`, Redis 45/48/49 recording, 57 Rack::Attack and 58 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c23e`). Four ports; the Rack::Attack counters were not touched (the single counter stays in `controllers/rack-attack.ts` and counts before the action chain).

- `POST /oauth/token` (`nextjs_oauth_tokens`, the default-host path only) issues tokens: `authorization_code` (PKCE included), `client_credentials`, `refresh_token` and the device-code grant. Client authentication, the scope rules and the redirect URI matching live in `oauth-client-authentication.ts` and `oauth-redirect-uri.ts`. Proof that the token is the one Doorkeeper would issue: the replays compare the rows, and a Rails spec uses what Node wrote. Still handed back: brand-token refresh, IP-literal and special-scheme redirect URIs, and a request with a session cookie. The API-host `/oauth/token` and the `muse`, `claude` and `chatgpt` aliases stay in Rails. The 429 for `oauth_token/ip` is unit-tested, not recorded.
- `POST /oauth/device/code`, `GET /oauth/device` (signed-out redirect with the session handoff) and `POST /oauth/device` (signed-out refusal) run in Node, with the device-code grant (`models/oauth-device-authorization.ts`: SHA256 digests, `GRD-XXXX-XXXX` user codes, 600 s lifetime, 30 s poll interval and +5 s slow-down under the application and device row locks). `oauth_device_flow_parity_spec.rb` has 48 examples; `oauth_node_device_flow_spec.rb` feeds the codes and token Node wrote to Rails. The ERB approval page a signed-in user sees stays in Rails, as does a tie in `created_at`. The revoked-after-poll race has no recording.
- `POST /login/passkey/options` and `POST /login/passkey` (`nextjs_login_passkeys`) verify the WebAuthn assertion in `services/webauthn-assertion.ts` the way webauthn-ruby 3.4.3 does and sign the user in with the Warden session and the remember cookie. `login_passkeys_node_session_spec.rb` sends the cookies Node set to Rails. Handed back whole, before a write: a signed-in visitor, a guest cart, an unconfirmed account, a non-ES256 key, authenticator data with attested-credential or extension flags, client data that is not a JSON object with a text challenge, and a signature that is not DER (OpenSSL raises in Rails, so there is no response to copy).
- `POST /save_to_library` with a `user` hash (`nextjs_signup_save_to_library`) builds the account with the same builder as `POST /signup` (`buildSignupUser`) and answers `{success: true}` without signing in, or the first validation message. Handed back: `purchase_id`, `remember_me`, an empty hash and a hash with no permitted key. The replay is in `auth-mutations.replay` (121 tests); with the handler forced to hand back, 4 fail.

The Google OAuth callback (`User.find_or_create_for_google_oauth2`) no longer hands back: see "WS-10 Google login and signup" above. The Stripe Connect callback stays in Rails whole.
WS-11/12 leftovers, sixth pass (lane 15: `gumroad_test_15`/`gumroad_replay_15`, Redis 56-59 recording and 63 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15e`, container `gr-ruby-c15e`). ERB layout pages, first slice: `GET /gumclaw` (`nextjs_gumclaw`), `GET /mcp`, `/claude` and `/muse` (`nextjs_mcp_connectors`) now render in Node, byte for byte against Rails (`spec/requests/erb_layout_pages_parity_spec.rb`, 11 examples; `erb-layout-pages.replay` 10 tests and `erb-layout-pages-cold-cache.replay` 3 tests, sharing `parity/src/erb-layout.ts`). The replay masks the two per-request values of the body (the `csrf-token` meta tag and the `<script nonce>`) and ignores the random `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie value; the session and guid cookies, the `Link: ...; rel=preload` header (`infra/vite.ts` `viteEntrypointStylesheet`) and every other header compare. New code: `server/erb/html.ts` (tag helpers), `erb/template.ts` (renders the real `.erb` file; every `<%= %>` expression, `if`/`unless` condition, `each` loop and assignment must be listed in a table of closures on its exact Ruby text, anything else throws `NotPortedError` and hands back; Erubi's line trimming), `erb/home-layout.ts` (the `home` and `application` layouts, head, nav, header, button, how_we_build, scripts, footer), `MetaTag#toTag` and `MetaTagBuilder#toErb` (`erb_meta_tags`), `Rendered.cspNonce` (the CSP header now carries the nonce the body embeds), and `RAILS_CACHE_TEST_SEED` (test env only: seeds the in-memory `Rails.cache` of the replay's server, since a spec's `Rails.cache.write` cannot reach it). Handed back: a signed-in visitor (Dashboard nav, seller analytics flags; example "hands a signed-in seller"), a cold or nil star-count cache (Rails would call the GitHub API, or print the "GitHub" nav; two "hands a ..." examples), a flash message (toast partial, no example), `as_embed`, a page that keeps the base entrypoint, the Vite dev server. Not proven: the nav for a signed-in user, the flash toast. Two `next dev` servers cannot share a directory, so the cold-cache examples are a second replay file.
