Still not done: the other `home#*` pages (`about` needs `RedisKey.prev_week_payout_usd`; `terms`, `privacy`, `prohibited`, `dpa`, `pricing`, `features`, `saas`, `small-bets`, `hackathon` need the edge-cacheable rules of `HomeController`: a cookie-less GET writes no session, guid or XSRF cookie and answers `public, s-maxage` caching, while a cookie or `utm_*` takes the normal path; the static ones fit the `renderErb` table approach), the `/js/gumroad*` embeds and `POST /custom_domain/verify`. Signed uploaded and oEmbed cover URLs and the custom HTML page ports (`Pages::Interpolator`, `ProfileData`, `ProductPrices`, the page sanitizer) were not started: the signed-URL proof needs recordings with a fixed key and clock, and the interpolator and sanitizer need byte-equal Ruby examples that do not exist yet.

WS-30 jobs 11 (lane 26: `gumroad_test_26`, Redis 3-10, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c26f`, container `gr-ruby-c26f`): nine job classes ported against goldens recorded from the real classes run as Sidekiq jobs. No Google, PayPal, S3 or mail service was reached: Google Calendar was answered by WebMock (`capture_http!`) and the Ruby-only tails were replaced by marker exceptions. Each replay compares the rendered SQL statement by statement and the Redis snapshot, and was checked with the code deliberately broken (the replay failed, then the code was restored).

- `RefreshAudienceMemberJob` (30 cases): the seller, follower, customer and affiliate reads, the `valid?`/`save!`/`destroy!` sequence and the `audience_members` row run in Node. Hands back before the first write: a purchase that could qualify (`Purchase#should_be_audience_member?` and `audience_member_details` are not ported) and any direct affiliate row.
- `HandleResendEventJob` (30 cases) and `HandleSendgridEventJob` (36 cases): events Rails drops (untracked type, blank `mailer_args`, bad timestamp, old shape without identifiers) finish in Node. A valid, tracked event hands back before the email-info update; a non-hash payload hands back untouched.
- `DetectFailingPaypalRailsJob` (37 cases): the connected-account batches, the grouped failed count, the completed-purchase exclusion, the user row lock, the seven-day dedup and the admin note with its version row run in Node, including the per-account error report when a note cannot be saved.
- `GoogleCalendarInviteJob` (43 cases): runs fully in Node, through the shared rate limit (`services/google-calendar-api.ts`, now also used by the integration controller), one token refresh on a 401 and the event id saved on the call. After the Google request Rails cannot be re-run without a second event, so an event id or token Google answers in an exotic shape fails loudly (`NotPortedError`) instead of handing back. A calendar id or token that Ruby's URI and header handling might treat differently is handed back before any request.
- `RefreshSitemapDailyWorker`, `RefreshWishlistSitemapDailyWorker` and the categories step of `RefreshSitemapMonthlyWorker` (16 cases): a run that finds the generation lock held re-enqueues itself after 15 minutes (the monthly retry gives up at attempt 24) in Node. A run that would take the lock hands back before it, since `SitemapGenerator` and S3 are not ported; the monthly worker's first step (it enqueues the product sitemaps) is handed back whole. The lock is read with GET rather than SET NX, so a lock released between the read and the hand-back costs one run Rails would have started at once.
- `EnforceRefundPolicyForSellerJob` (23 cases): the purchase, the seller, the idempotence guard and `User#dispute_rate_stats` (three counts) run in Node, and every gate that returns does so. A seller past the allowed dispute rate hands back before `seller.transaction`, because the `refund_policy_enforced` update, the refund policy bump and the admin comment need the unported user callbacks.

Not examined: S3 jobs such as `RemoveDeletedFilesFromS3Job` and the rest of `SitemapGenerator`. `mergeJsonDataWithCurrentRow` gained an optional `type` so an STI integration row is read with its type condition, as `unscoped` does in Rails. The `integrations-google-calendar.ts` controller only had its rate limit and token refresh moved into the shared service; it has no test file of its own, so typecheck is its only check.
WS-30 jobs 13 (lane 26: `gumroad_test_26`, `gumroad_replay_26`, Redis 3-6, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c26g`, container `gr-ruby-c26g`): goldens recorded from the real classes run as Sidekiq jobs; S3 was answered by WebMock and no real service or credential was reached. Each replay compares the rendered SQL statement by statement and was checked with the code deliberately broken.

- `RefreshAudienceMemberJob` (85 cases): `Purchase#should_be_audience_member?` and the purchase details (variants, subscription, license) are ported, as are direct affiliates (with `affiliates_links` expansion). Only a purchase with a NULL `created_at` still hands back.
- `RemoveDeletedFilesFromS3Job` (47 cases): runs fully in Node over `infra/s3-deleter.ts` (alive-duplicate checks, HLS listing, 1000-key slices, the `update_column` stamp, per-file rescue). Unproven: the S3 wire format is compared as parsed keys (list prefix, delete keys), not the raw XML or signature; non-integer window arguments hand back.
- `HandleResendEventJob` (80 cases) and `HandleSendgridEventJob` (101 cases): the receipt path (`CustomerEmailInfo` find-or-initialize and the bounced/delivered/opened transitions) and events no handler claims run in Node. Hands back, before any statement of the batch: installment, abandoned cart and piracy events, receipts of a charge, a Sendgrid complaint on a receipt, a preorder receipt of the old Sendgrid shape, a purchase id that is not a plain integer.
- `EnforceRefundPolicyForSellerJob`: unchanged, enforcement still hands back before `seller.transaction`. The real transaction was recorded (user update with `json_data` lock and versions, refund policy bump, comment, `seller_profiles` read, `refund_policy_enforced_notification` mail) but not ported: the Node user save path was not reproduced statement for statement, and the golden grew past 270k lines because of the `versions` rows.
- Not examined: the `SitemapGenerator` pieces of the sitemap workers.

WS-20 subscription cancellation (`ws-rest-sweep-1`, lane 25: `gumroad_test_25`, `gumroad_replay_25`, Redis 1-4 recording and 5 replay, container `gr-ruby-c25f`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c25f`; no external service is involved). `Subscription#cancel!` runs in Node (`cancelBang` in `models/subscription-writes.ts`, over the existing `with_lock`, `end_time_of_subscription` and save callbacks) and serves `POST /subscriptions/:id/unsubscribe_by_seller` (customers page; `SubscriptionPolicy` in `policies/subscription.ts`; 204), `POST /subscriptions/:id/unsubscribe_by_user` (manage page; `check_can_manage` is shared with the manage page through `manageDecision`; flash notice and redirect, magic-link redirect, 404), both under the new flag `nextjs_subscription_cancellations`, and `POST /mobile/subscriptions/:id/cancel` (`nextjs_mobile_subscriptions`). 17 examples in `subscription_cancellations_parity_spec.rb` (recorded with `defer_audience_refresh`, so `RefreshAudienceMemberJob` is a recorded job) and 2 in `mobile_products_subscriptions_parity_spec.rb` (replacing the "hands a cancel to Rails" example). 19 replay tests in `subscription-cancellations.replay` compare the `subscriptions` update, the jobs (`PostToPingEndpointsWorker` five seconds out, the `ElasticsearchIndexerWorker` update, `RefreshAudienceMemberJob`) and the mail jobs (asserted apart, because the request spec's adapter records none); with the webhook enqueue removed 7 fail. Not recorded: cancelling an installment plan (the factories charge a card, so no row could be built), a charged-back last purchase, and a subscription with no purchases (Node throws where Rails raises NoMethodError). A signed-out `unsubscribe_by_seller` request hands back (`hands a ...` example). The replay does not compare `Vary: Accept` on the JSON answers. Still Rails, not started: `POST /products/:id/builds/:id/promote`, `PATCH /commissions/:id` and `/complete`, `POST /d/:id/play/:grant/save`, `GET|POST /purchases/export` and `GET /affiliates/export` (the synchronous CSV needs `purchase_data` and the affiliate totals; only the enqueue half exists in `exports/enqueue.ts`), `POST /sellers/brand_accounts`, `GET /global_affiliates/product_eligibility/:url` (an SsrfFilter fetch), `POST /help/contact`. The three `GET /products/:id/{other_refund_policies,available_offer_codes,builds}` rows listed under "hands back" in remaining-work.md are served (`products/[id]/*.json` route folders); the script matches them by the extensionless path.

WS-20 checkout rest 5 (`nextjs/ws20-checkout-rest-5`, lane 27: `gumroad_test_27`, `gumroad_replay_27`, Redis 11-14 recording and 15 replay, container `gr-ruby-c27e`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c27e`). `POST /links` now also runs in Node for a membership (the "Tier" category, its "Untitled" tier, the tier price and the zero price the product keeps, every recurrence, `subscription_duration` left NULL when the form sent none), coffee (the "Suggested Amounts" category and variant, the Price row rewritten to 0, `customizable_price` set; a free coffee product rolls back with no alert, as Rails does), a bundle, a custom summary (the Rails action saves the product once before it marks it tiered and sets the shipping flag, so the recorded quirks are reproduced: a membership with a summary keeps a plain recurring Price and no tier, a physical product with a summary gets two identical Price rows and no IndexNow job, every other type enqueues `SubmitToIndexnowJob`), a missing price (`New products should be created with a price`, or `default_price_cents can't be blank` for a membership), any currency in `config/currencies.json` (single-unit yen/dong parse without cents; a seller who is not verified needs the rate in Redis under `currencies:<CODE>`, else the request hands back), a physical product (403 for an unconfirmed or suspended seller or an account under 90 days; allowed with the deprecated flag or an existing physical product), sellers whose products sections add new products (profile row lock, `shown_products` appended), the daily creation limit (`Sorry, you can only create N products per day.`; the limit is 10 unless the seller is compliant (100) or has `daily_product_creation_limit`; team members are exempt), and `one_coffee_per_user`. 79 examples in `links_create_parity_spec.rb` replay in `links-create.replay` (83 tests pass). Still hands back, each with a "hands a ..." example: an AI prompt, the mobile app web view, and a physical product for an account past 90 days that has no flag and no physical product (`sales_cents_total` is an Elasticsearch aggregation). Hands back by code without a recorded example (Rails raises a 500 in a request spec): an unknown subscription duration or currency. Unproven: the Elasticsearch partial updates and cache invalidations the variant, category and price callbacks queue after commit (the full index document written after commit covers them), PaperTrail versions, and a membership priced in yen. `POST /v2/products` is not ported: `Api::V2::LinksController#create` assigns `price_cents` raw (no `clean_price`), takes tags, files, rich content, refund policy and publishes (`publish!`) unless asked for a draft, and answers with the product JSON, so it shares only the final `save!` with this code; it still hands back whole after its refusals.
`RefreshAffiliateEarningsJob` was looked at on lane 16 and not ported (ported since, see WS-30 jobs 12 below): `AffiliateEarningsCache` stores `{ cents:, computed_at: Time.current }` in `Rails.cache`, an ActiveSupport::TimeWithZone, and `server/cache/marshal.ts` refuses Time (it pins only nil, booleans, numbers, strings, symbols, arrays and hashes against `Marshal.dump`). Reading the stale-check value or writing the refreshed one needs a Marshal vector for TimeWithZone first (`[utc, zone name, time]` as a `U` object holding `Time` `_dump` bytes with its offset, zone and nano ivars), recorded in `rails-cache.golden.json`; the claim locks (`write unless_exist`), the `MAX_EXECUTION_TIME` hinted sum and the retries-exhausted release follow from it. `DeleteProductFilesWorker` stays handed back at `ProductFile#delete!` for the same reason as the other soft deletes (PaperTrail, `invalidate_cache`, HLS renditions, subtitle files).

WS-30 jobs 12 (lane 16: `gumroad_test_16`, `gumroad_replay_16`, Redis 33, container `gr-ruby-c16h`, `ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c16h`; nothing left the machine). Three jobs and the Marshal piece the first one waited on.

- `server/cache/marshal.ts` dumps and loads `ActiveSupport::TimeWithZone` in UTC (`RubyTimeWithZone`): the `Time#_dump` words, the `nano_num` / `nano_den` / `submicro` ivars (the packed digits lose their zero byte when the last digit is 0), the `zone` ivar and the two `"UTC"` strings every TimeWithZone in a payload shares (the second one links to the first's), and the `@` link from the third element back to the Time. `rails-cache.golden.json` gained 24 Marshal vectors (whole seconds, microseconds, nanoseconds, the epoch, 1900, year 9999, two times in one array, the `{ cents:, computed_at: }` hash), 2 entry-coder cases and 6 store steps (a write with `expires_in` 1 day, `unless_exist` while the claim is held, a delete), all from real Rails (`scripts/golden/rails-cache.rb`, which now also needs `sqlite3` on the gem path). Refused on both sides: a zone other than UTC, a Time with sub-nanosecond precision, a year outside 1900..9999, a Time that is not UTC, a TimeWithZone whose local time is not its UTC time.
- `RefreshAffiliateEarningsJob` and its `sidekiq_retries_exhausted` hook run fully in Node (`server/services/affiliate-earnings-cache.ts`, `worker/jobs/refresh-affiliate-earnings-job.ts`; golden 39 cases, with `Rails.cache` spied on: every read, write with its TTL and `unless_exist`, and delete, in order; the hinted `MAX_EXECUTION_TIME` sum with purchases that must be kept and dropped, a sum past 2**30, the stale boundary at nanosecond precision, a claim held by a request or by another worker, a sum failing with a timeout or a lost connection, which re-arms the claim and releases the run lock). Affiliate rows are read with the explicit column list ActiveRecord sends (the model ignores `archived_at`). Handed back before any write: a float or word id, an affiliate whose `type` is not a model, a cached `computed_at` that is not a time, a cached value Node's Marshal subset cannot read. The hook cannot hand back: a float or word id fails there and the worker logs it. Not proven: the request half of the cache (`fetch`, which enqueues this job) and a real memcached.
- `AlertOnStuckPiracyReportsJob` (golden 29 cases): the six reads (blocked, review, delivery failed or unconfirmed at the 24-hour boundary, signed and unsent while `piracy_reports_sending` is on, never screened at six hours, repeated counter-notices at the 90-day date boundary) run in Node and a run where nothing waits finishes; a run with something to report builds its message from `PiracyReports::SendService` reasons and hands back after the reads, before any write (the job writes only through the Slack push). A sending feature on a partial percentage of time (Flipper rolls per call) or with an expression gate hands back too.
- `RecoverStrandedBuyersJob` (golden 28 cases, the recovery service replaced by a stub that raises): the candidate emails (blocked-checkout failures of the last 30 days, 20,001-row limit), then per slice of 500 the settled-history counts (three paid purchases older than 60 days, emails downcased) and the dispute filter run in Node; a run where no buyer survives them finishes, the first survivor hands back before the latest-failure reads, `DecliningPlatformBlocks` and the recovery service. Not run: a truncated scan (20,001 candidates).
- Not ported, with the reason: `DeleteProductFilesWorker`'s delete path is `ProductFile#delete!`, which is `mark_deleted!` (a validated save: ISBN, thumbnail, belongs-to-product-or-installment validations, `set_filegroup`, PaperTrail) with `invalidate_product_cache`, `delete_transcoded_videos` (HLS), the commit callbacks (`classify_product_taxonomy_attributes` through `Discover::TaxonomyAttributeClassifier`, `stamp_existing_pdfs_if_needed`) and `delete_all_subtitle_files!`. Node has no `ProductFile` write model, so none of that order is provable yet; the job still hands back after the files are read. `AlertOnStalledPostEmailBlastsJob` writes (`PostEmailBlast.expire`) before it scans Sidekiq sets, and `RefreshAudienceMemberJob`, `HandleResendEventJob`, `HandleSendgridEventJob`, `RefreshSitemap*Worker`, `RemoveDeletedFilesFromS3Job`, `GoogleCalendarInviteJob` and `DetectFailingPaypalRailsJob` keep the blockers listed under jobs 9.
- Lane note: `rr.sh`-style helper scripts in the shared scratchpad are overwritten by other lanes; a golden run that lands in the wrong container writes into that lane's worktree. Keep a private directory.

WS-10 rest 5 (lane 23, container `gr-ruby-c23f`, `gumroad_test_23`/`gumroad_replay_23`, Redis 45/48/49/52 recording, 50 replay and 51 Rack::Attack, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c23f`). Narrows the hand-backs that rest 4 left; nothing touched a live service.

- Requests with a session cookie: `revoke`, `introspect` and `GET /oauth/token/info` now take the same path as `/oauth/token` (`assertPlainSession`: a cookie whose session holds only `session_id` is answered like the Rails session middleware does, anything more stays in Rails). Proof: `a request that sends back the session cookie of an earlier response` in `oauth_tokens_parity_spec.rb` (5 examples) and `oauth_token_issuance_parity_spec.rb` (2), whose last exchange carries the cookie of the first.
- API host: `POST api:/oauth/token`, `POST api:/oauth/revoke`, `POST api:/oauth/introspect` and `api:/oauth/token/info` (`use_doorkeeper` has no host constraint). `on the API host` examples in both specs (4 and 2). The muse, claude and chatgpt aliases were already served on the Gumroad host (`routes.json`), contrary to the rest 4 note.
- IP-literal and special-scheme redirect URIs (`models/oauth-redirect-uri.ts`): IPv6 literals follow the RFC 3986 grammar Ruby's parser uses (zone ids and malformed literals raise `URI::InvalidURIError`, an IPvFuture literal parses and is not loopback), IPv4 loopback rejects zero-filled octets as `IPAddr` does, an IPv6 loopback is `::1` or `::ffff:127.x`, `ws`/`wss` compare without the fragment, and a loopback comparison drops the userinfo too (`URI::Generic#port=`). The golden grew to 1,782 cases (`scripts/golden/oauth-redirect-uri.rb`). Still handed back: a registered `ftp`, `file`, `ldap(s)` or `mailto` URI (their classes compare other components); a requested URI of those schemes is refused when no registered URI of the same scheme exists.
- The `oauth_token/ip` 429: the counter is brought to 3000 with `Rack::Attack.cache.count` and only the 3001st request is recorded (`rack-attack-throttles-answers-the-3001st-token-request...`); the example asserts the Redis key, and `ws10-rest.replay.test.ts` writes that key into Node's counter database before replaying.
- Passkey login: RS256 and PS256 stored keys are verified (`services/webauthn-assertion.ts`: PKCS#1 v1.5, and PSS with `salt_length: :auto`). `with an RSA passkey` in `login_passkeys_parity_spec.rb`: 6 examples (valid, tampered, wrong-length signature, and a PKCS#1 signature for a PS256 key). Still handed back, before a write: a signed-in visitor, a guest cart (the merge reads and writes carts), an unconfirmed account (Devise's failure app answers after `stored_credential.save!`, not recorded), any other algorithm, attested-credential and extension flags (webauthn-ruby re-encodes the COSE key and decodes the extensions with the cbor gem, whose errors are not `VerificationError`), and a non-DER signature (OpenSSL raises, Rails answers 500).
- `POST /save_to_library`: a `remember_me` key (no sign-in, nothing is remembered) and a `purchase_id` that names no purchase (unknown, or an id that decrypts to no row) are served; 6 new examples. A `purchase_id` of an existing purchase, or one that is not text, is still handed back (`attach_to_user_and_card`).

Not done, with the reasons: brand-token refresh (`MobileAccountToken.valid?` reads the team membership, its member and the session). The device flow tie in `created_at` depends on how `sort_by` orders equal keys on the platform's `qsort_r`, which differs between glibc versions and cannot be fixed by a recording. The revoked-after-poll race needs a revoke interleaved between `poll!` and the second lock, which a replay cannot inject; `issuedTokenRevoked` stays as the unit-tested guard.
WS-30 jobs 14 (lane 16: `gumroad_test_16`, `gumroad_replay_16`, Redis 33, container `gr-ruby-c16i`; nothing left the machine). Three jobs.

- `DeleteProductFilesWorker` now deletes in Node: `server/models/product-file-writes.ts` is the `ProductFile` write model (`filegroup` derivation, ISBN and thumbnail validations, belongs-to-product-or-installment, PaperTrail with a `normalizers` option, `invalidate_product_cache`, HLS `delete_transcoded_videos` with the nested flag update, `delete_all_subtitle_files!`, the filetypes index and `PdfUnstampableNotifierJob` commit callbacks). Golden 78 cases. Handed back after the files are read and before the first write: a product with a taxonomy or inferred taxonomy values (the commit callback would run `Discover::TaxonomyAttributeClassifier`, not ported). Commit callbacks a delete cannot reach throw `NotPortedError`. This supersedes the "stays handed back at `ProductFile#delete!`" notes above.
- `AlertOnStalledPostEmailBlastsJob` (golden 28 cases): the abandoned-blast expiry (ids read, re-check, update) and the candidate read run in Node, and a run with no candidate finishes. A run with candidates hands back after the read, since the Sidekiq dead, retry and busy set scans and the alert push stay in Ruby. The expiry has already been written by then, which Rails' re-run finds expired. A failed expiry hands back.
- `CreateLicensesForExistingCustomersWorker` (golden 34 cases) with `Purchase#variant_content_permits_license_key?` in Rails' statement order and through the query cache (a write clears it): the alive-variants probe, then for a product sharing content across variants the product page and alive variant page reads, then the purchase's variants and their pages. `License.where(link:, purchase:).first_or_create!` with `generate_serial` and the PaperTrail create row. A first batch of 1000 sales, and a float or word id, hand back before any write. The customers page's own copy of the permit check (`page-props/customers`) keeps its own statement order and was not changed.

WS-10 rest 6 (lane 23, container `gr-ruby-c23g`, `gumroad_test_23`/`gumroad_replay_23`, Redis 45-52, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c23g`). Shrinks what rest 5 handed back; nothing touched a live service.

- Guest cart on sign-in (`services/merge-carts-service.ts`): login, the 2FA token, the signup of an existing account and passkey login plan the merge before `sign_in` and run it after the 2FA session reset (copied lines, target cart, then the guest cart's soft delete, in one transaction). - 2FA recovery codes: the code is normalized, matched against the bcrypt list under `FOR UPDATE` and removed with an `updated_at` bump (4 examples). The hand-backs (confirmation, cart plan) run before the check because a burnt code cannot be replayed in Rails; a recovery list that is not bcrypt hashes hands back.

Not done, with the reasons: the device flow tie in `created_at` depends on how `sort_by` orders equal keys on the platform's `qsort_r`, which differs between glibc versions and cannot be fixed by a recording. The revoked-after-poll race needs a revoke interleaved between `poll!` and the second lock, which a replay cannot inject; `issuedTokenRevoked` stays as the unit-tested guard.


WS-30 jobs 15 (lane 26: `gumroad_test_26`, `gumroad_replay_26`, Redis 5, container `gr-ruby-c26h`; goldens recorded from the real classes run as Sidekiq jobs, no service or credential reached). Each replay compares the rendered SQL statement by statement, the Sidekiq pushes and (where a user is saved) the cache and Elasticsearch calls, and was checked with the code deliberately broken.

- `EnforceRefundPolicyForSellerJob` (golden 37 cases) now runs the enforcement in Node: `seller.transaction` with the `Users.saveBang` of the `refund_policy_enforced` flag (user callbacks, `json_data` lock and versions row as the user-writes golden proves), the seller-level policy bump (a 0-day policy becomes 30 days and its fine print is cleared; longer policies and a seller without one are left alone), the `on_probation` admin comment, then the `ContactingCreatorMailer#refund_policy_enforced_notification` push after the commit. `format("%.1f")` rounds an exact tie to the even digit (`2.25` is `2.2`, `3.75` is `3.8`), which `toFixed` does not; a policy row with a NULL period raises Ruby's NoMethodError and rolls the user update back. The golden keeps only statements, Redis, cache and Elasticsearch: the purchases seeded to reach the volume gates stay out of the fixture. Replaying it showed two older Node errors: `RefundPolicies`' enforcement-minimum validation read `users.flags` where Rails sends `SELECT 1 ... flags & mask != 0 ... LIMIT 1`, and a has_one load reads the foreign key first (PaperTrail's `object` lists `seller_id` first).
- `UtmLinkSaleAttributionJob` (29 cases) runs fully in Node: the order's successful purchases grouped by creation second, the latest visit per UTM link in the seven-day window (the raw inner query with `quote_string`, the preload of the links, the `||=` claim in newest-visit order, product page links filtered by product), the visit country update (PaperTrail version, one instance per visit so a visit claiming several purchases updates once) and `first_or_create!` of `utm_link_driven_sales`. New models `UtmLinkVisits` and `UtmLinkDrivenSales`. A visit whose link row is gone raises Ruby's NoMethodError; a non-String `browser_guid` hands back.
- `StripePayoutsPausedEmailJob` (24 cases) runs fully in Node: both loads, the seller lock with the account reload, the claim-token check, the pause-source decision, the at-stake reads (unpaid balance sum, any successful sale), the claim release through `MerchantAccounts` and the mail pushed after the lock. Replaying it showed two more older errors: `MerchantAccount`'s merchant-id presence validation loads `user` (a SELECT) before it looks at the id, and ActiveRecord 8.1 compares a case-sensitive uniqueness with `CAST(? AS BINARY)`, not `BINARY ?` (`validatesUniquenessOf` now sends the former; the users `username` check uses it too).
- `AlertOnBlockedEstablishedBuyersJob` (28 cases plus a synthetic truncated scan) shares the stranded-buyer candidate, settled-history and dispute reads with `RecoverStrandedBuyersJob` (`worker/jobs/stranded-buyer-scan.ts`) and finishes when nobody survives them. A surviving buyer (latest failures, `DecliningPlatformBlocks`, the notification) and a scan truncated at 20,000 candidates hand back; nothing has been written by then.
- Sitemaps, not ported: `RefreshSitemapDailyWorker`, `RefreshWishlistSitemapDailyWorker` and the categories step of `RefreshSitemapMonthlyWorker` still hand back when they win the generation lock. A port needs `SitemapGenerator`'s XML (`urlset`, the image extension, `lastmod` as W3C datetime) and its gzip, `AwsSdkAdapter#write` (`upload_file` with `acl`, `cache_control` and `content_type`, signed with the dedicated uploader credentials, local `public/` file outside production), `delete_object` for an empty wishlist run, `Wishlist.seo_indexable` (a grouped `HAVING COUNT(DISTINCT product_id) >= 3` that `find_each` cannot batch, so an id subquery) with `url_slug`, `RobotsService#expire_sitemap_configs_cache`, and for products `preview_url` (an ActiveStorage retina variant that writes on first use). The gzip header carries the wall clock and Node's zlib build may differ from Ruby's, so the proof would compare inflated XML, which no golden has recorded yet. (Superseded for the wishlist worker by the jobs 17 notes below.)
- Looked at and not ported: `CreateLicensesForExistingCustomersWorker` (needs `Link#has_product_level_rich_content?`, per-variant rich content and the `License` create with its serial), `ResendConfirmationEmailJob` and `StaleTransientSuppressionSweepJob` (SendGrid suppression API through the sendgrid gem), `PdfUnstampableNotifierJob` (waits for the ProductFile write model of jobs-14), `ScheduleMembershipPriceUpdatesJob` and `UpdateBundlePurchasesContentJob` (plan-change and bundle services). (Superseded by the jobs 17 notes below: only the two product-side sitemaps remain handed back; `CreateLicensesForExistingCustomersWorker` was already ported, its thousand-sales leftover is gone.)
- Lane note: the shared scratchpad lost a helper script once; keep a private directory per lane. A golden run can capture a stray Flipper seeding write in one case's Redis snapshot: regenerate and check that only `queue:*` keys appear.
