WS-20 third REST sweep (`ws-rest-sweep-3`, lane 25: `gumroad_test_25`, `gumroad_replay_25`, container `gr-ruby-c25h`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c25h`; payments only against rows saved with `save!(validate: false)`, no live service). Served by Node: `GET|POST /purchases/export` (`controllers/purchases-export.ts`, `exports/purchase-export-data.ts`; flag `nextjs_purchases_export`; `purchases_export_parity_spec`, 18 examples replayed by `rest-sweep-3.replay`): the ES count against `SYNCHRONOUS_EXPORT_THRESHOLD`, the CSV of up to 2,000 plain sales (buyer, discount code, zip padding, refund, dispute, revoked access, discover and PPP flags, custom field columns), the date and product filters, and the asynchronous path (`SalesExport` row, `Exports::Sales::CreateAndEnqueueChunksWorker`, the 303 to `/customers` or `{ success: true }` for JSON). Handed back whole (a read, so nothing is written): signed-out requests, the mobile `access_token` form, `in_seller_time_zone`, `variant_ids`, dates that are not `YYYY-MM-DD`, and any result holding a sale with variants or SKUs, tax, a gift, pre-order, subscription charge, license, review, tip, affiliate, UTM link, upsell, cart, buyer-currency or PayPal charge, a seller's own Stripe Connect account, a state or country taken from the IP address (GeoIP), a non-ASCII address (ActiveSupport transliteration) or a product with several custom fields (unspecified column order). The CSV is compared with the clock time masked, because replay seeds are hour-granular. Installment plan cancellation by the seller and by the buyer needed no new Node code: `cancelBang` already carries the `installment_plans_cannot_be_cancelled_by_buyer` validation, it was only unrecorded. `subscription_cancellations_parity_spec` now builds the plan with `save!(validate: false)` and records the seller cancel (and the already-cancelled no-op), the buyer's refusal (`Installment plans cannot be cancelled by the customer` in the manage page flash) and the 404 for a plan with every installment paid; the cancellation replay passes 23 tests. Not attempted: the build promote itself, the commission file change and balance charge of `complete`, and brand account creation stay handed back (their `hands a ...` examples are unchanged); no Rails example was added that proves their write order. `GET /products/:id/{other_refund_policies,available_offer_codes,builds}.json` are App Router routes, so the remaining-work "hands back" rows for them are a static-check false positive. Still Rails: the handed-back cases above, and the small unregistered routes (`/healthcheck`, `/profile/:username` third-party analytics, `/bundles/create_from_email`, `/users/confirmation/new`, `/_/test/outgoing_traffic`) that this sweep did not reach.

WS-20 free purchase, the handler (`nextjs/ws21-purchase-model-4`, lane 24: `gumroad_test_24`, `gumroad_replay_24`, Redis 59 replay; no external service is involved, the free path makes no Stripe call). `POST /orders` (`nextjs_orders_validations`) now writes the purchase for one free line of a plain one-time USD product (`controllers/orders-free-purchase.ts`, `services/free-order-create.ts`, `services/purchase-response-facts.ts`): 13 of the 21 examples of `orders_free_purchase_parity_spec.rb` are served and replay green against the recording (rows, response, session cookie, jobs); the other 8 shapes (seller workflow, purchase limit, buyer with a cart, affiliate, discount code, repeated download from one address, membership, two lines) are handed back before the first write. Known gaps that hand back: a referrer that carries url parameters while the line has none, any seller-side effect a purchase can have beyond the plain row set (presentments, shipments, integrations, affiliate credits, custom fields), a non-USD or tiered product, a buyer currency quote, a gift, a tip, variants. `plugins` and `friend` sent at the top level are not stored on the event, as in the recording. Difference accepted in the replay: `RefreshAudienceMemberJob` is recorded twice by Rails (no unique-jobs lock in the test environment) and once by Node (real `until_executing` lock). The SEO `from_seo` visit flag and a repeated download count are modelled only as hand-backs.

WS-10 rest 7 (lane 23, container `gr-ruby-c23h`, `gumroad_test_23`/`gumroad_replay_23`, Redis 41-45, `ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c23h`). Nothing touched a live service.

- Team invitation accept joins the team in Node (`GET /settings/team/invitations/:id/accept`, `nextjs_settings_team_invitations_writes`): under the `User.transaction` locks (both users ordered by id, then the invitee) the invitation gets `accepted_at` and `deleted_at`, the invitee gets an owner membership if it lacks exactly one, the `TeamMembership` is created with the invitation's role, `TeamMailer.invitation_accepted` is enqueued after commit, `last_accessed_at` is stamped and the encrypted `current_seller_id` cookie is written (`switch-seller-account.ts`, shared with `POST /sellers/switch`), then the welcome notice. A seller suspended between the checks and the locks gets the invalid-link alert with no write. 3 new parity examples (the settings-mutations replay reads the cookie back and checks the mail job's membership). Still handed back before any write: joining the Gumroad account's team (`from_gumroad_account?` also flags the invitee `is_team_member`; any `hi@` seller hands back).
- TOTP setup, confirmation and recovery codes run in Node (`Settings::TotpController`, `nextjs_settings_totp_writes`). Setup replaces a pending credential and returns the secret, `provisioning_uri` and `qr_svg`; `lib/qr-svg.ts` ports rqrcode_core (level :h, byte mode, `as_svg(use_path: true)`) and matches 12 Ruby vectors (provisioning URIs and longer inputs, more than six QR versions) in `qr-svg.test.ts`. Confirmation checks the code under the row lock with `TotpCredential::DRIFT` (30 seconds behind, none ahead) and generates ten bcrypt codes (cost 12, `$2a$`, stored as a JSON list); regeneration replaces them. Randomness cannot match a recording, so the replay ignores the secret, URI, QR code and recovery codes, asserts their shape (and, for the codes, that each bcrypt hash matches), and `node_output_parity_spec.rb` feeds Node's rows back to Rails: the URI and SVG drawn from Node's secret are identical to Rails', and Rails redeems all ten of Node's codes. The code a confirmation sends depends on the clock, so the replay rewrites it to the code of its own clock (previous interval accepted, next interval refused). A code that is not text hands back before any write.
- Passkey login judges attested credential data and extension data (`webauthn-assertion.ts`): an ES256 key and nothing left over, or one CBOR item that is not null or false; 8 recorded examples (200s, refusals) on a passkey the spec signs for. Handed back, because Rails raises something nothing rescues (EOFError of empty or truncated extension data, a truncated AAGUID/id, a COSE key the gem has to judge) or because the encoding is not the shortest. Non-DER signatures stay handed back for the same reason: OpenSSL raises and Rails answers 500, an exception a replay cannot compare.
- Still in Rails after rest 7: joining the Gumroad account's team (`is_team_member`), `registration_options`/`create` of a new passkey (random challenge in the session, attestation verification), and the unprovable passkey shapes above.

WS-30 jobs 16 (lane 16: `gumroad_test_16`, `gumroad_replay_16`, Redis 33, container `gr-ruby-c16j`; nothing left the machine). Three pieces.

- The taxonomy attribute classifier is ported (`server/models/taxonomy-attribute-classifier.ts`: `Discover::TaxonomyAttributeClassifier`, `FontsAttributeClassifier`, `Link#save_inferred_taxonomy_attribute_values`, `TaxonomyAttribute#normalize_value`). It calls no LLM or other service, only the product's own rows, so nothing needed a stand-in. The `ProductFile` `classify_product_taxonomy_attributes` commit callback runs it on the Link instance the file was loaded through (a `WeakMap` keeps one `Links` record per product row, so the taxonomy, ancestry path, attribute set, user and installment-plan associations stay loaded across files as in Ruby). `DeleteProductFilesWorker` no longer hands back for a product with taxonomy or inferred values; golden 97 cases (19 new: fonts with one to three files and mixed types, variable-font names that need the owner's external id, stored inferred and seller values, no attributes defined, a missing taxonomy, taxonomies with no classifier that clear stored values). `Link`'s `validates_associated :installment_plan` now keeps the has_one loaded on the instance (a second save of one instance asks nothing, as Rails). Mutation checks: the one-extension rule, the slug path test and the ancestry order each fail the replay. The enum/boolean/number normalizer throws `NotPortedError` for a numeric text, which the classifier never submits.
- `CreateLicensesForExistingCustomersWorker` no longer hands back at 1000 sales: `find_each` continues with `id >` batches after the first batch's writes (golden 36 cases, 2 new at 1000 and 1001 sales; the fixture is 11 MB because purchases rows are 100 columns wide). Batch-size mutants (999, 1001) fail. The permit check moved to `server/models/purchase-license-key.ts` and the customers page (`page-props/customers`) now calls the same function, so its statement order is Rails' (product-level content first, then the purchase's variants) instead of a second copy; the page's unit tests pass but no parity replay of the customers page was run in this lane.
- `AlertOnStalledPostEmailBlastsJob` runs fully except one write path (golden 90 cases, from 28): the dead, retry, queued (`queue:default`) and busy (`processes`, `*:work`) Sidekiq set scans, the dispositions, `resolve_action` (fully delivered, non-opener hold, past-window hold, NX markers with the 4 hour TTL, a sender that reappears between the scans and the decision), `SendPostBlastEmailsJob` pushes and the `InternalNotificationWorker` alert with `message_for` (held rows first, the 25-line cap, hours rounding and `Float#to_s`). The Redis a case starts from is its `redis_before`; the job takes its commands through `StalledBlastRedis` (main database and Sidekiq's). Hand-backs before the first resume write: a live run where a blast is `dead` (taking the entry out of the dead set unlocks through sidekiq-unique-jobs, whose `delete.lua` writes a `uniquejobs:changelog` entry Node cannot produce), `auto_resume_stalled_post_blasts` on a partial percentage of time or an expression gate, a quota deferral time that is not `iso8601` UTC, and a Sidekiq entry that is not JSON. Not proven by mutation: the backward pass of `sender_visible_now?` (it only differs when a job moves between sets during the scans, which a golden cannot interleave) and the `round(1)` half-up correction at the float edge.
- Still not ported: `RefreshAudienceMemberJob` qualifying paths, `HandleResendEventJob` / `HandleSendgridEventJob` valid events, `RefreshSitemap*Worker`, `RemoveDeletedFilesFromS3Job`, `EnforceRefundPolicy` enforcement (jobs-15 owns the sitemap and refund-policy ones), the dead-entry removal above, and `ProcessPaymentWorker`'s payout processors (not started in this lane).


WS-21/WS-23 payments hand-backs, fifth pass (`nextjs/ws-payments-1`, lane 23). Nothing was ported: the refund success path, the instant payout and the Stripe account creation stay handed back before any write or Stripe call, and no code, flag or fixture changed. The third-pass recordings (`purchases_refund_parity_spec.rb`, `instant_payouts_parity_spec.rb`, `settings_payments_stripe_account_parity_spec.rb`; timelines under `nextjs/parity/fixtures/requests/`) prove the order of the success path in each case. They do not prove a failure path, and a port cannot stop at the first Stripe call and hand back, so every failure branch that follows a call must be ported and recorded before the success path can be. What blocks each one:
- Instant payout (`InstantPayoutsService`). The recorded request is `external-account GET`, `balance GET`, then `balances` update, `payments` insert, `payments_balances` insert, `payments` update, `POST /v1/payouts` (random key, `Stripe-Account`), `payments` update. Success needs `Payouts.create_payments` (`is_user_payable`, `select_and_claim_payable_balances`, the chargeback-reserve branches), `StripePayoutProcessor.payout_groups`, `get_payout_details`, `prepare_payment_and_set_amount` (retired-account drain, currency mismatch, balance drift, the internal `Transfer.create` for Gumroad-held balances with a 3-attempt `Charge.retrieve` and `sleep(2)`, the instant fee division) and `perform_payment`. The part that cannot be left out is the `ensure`/`rescue` of `perform_payment` after `Payout.create`: `PAYOUT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` on a dropped connection, `mark_failed!` with `Payment::FailureReason`, `BankAccount#mark_deleted!` for a bank Stripe cannot find, `reverse_internal_transfer_or_hold_payouts!` (a second Stripe call plus a payout hold and credit) and `ErrorNotifier`. The recording has one Stripe answer (success); each failure reason would need a WebMock-stubbed Rails example (the `InvalidRequestError` variants of `stripe_invalid_request_error_failure_reason`, 429, connection reset) with its own timeline, and `Payment`'s state machine side effects (mails, comments, `Balance` unpaid/processing/paid transitions) are not visible in the table-level timeline. Needed first: those failure recordings, a `Payment` model service with its callbacks, and the `Payouts` selection port with golden vectors.
- Full refund (`PUT /purchases/:id/refund`). The recorded request is `GET charge`, `POST /v1/refunds` (random key), `GET refund`, `GET charge`, then the ledger and eight jobs. Success needs `Purchase#refund_and_save!` with `StripeChargeProcessor#refund!` (including the destination-charge and application-fee paths of `destination_payment_refund_for`, the `REFUND_FEE_REVERSAL_CAPACITY_ERROR` retries, `NoRefundFeeTransferError`, `UnmatchedApplicationFeeRefundError`), `FlowOfFunds` built from Stripe's balance transactions, the `Refund` callbacks, balance selection and `decrement_balance_for_refund_or_chargeback!` (affiliate and collaborator branches), the retained-fee credit, and the purchase and audience-member callbacks. A refund Stripe rejects after the POST, an already-refunded charge and a partial refund each take a different Ruby path; none is recorded, and a Connect (`Stripe-Account`) refund and PayPal/Braintree are not proven either. Needed first: the refund ledger as a model service with goldens for the FlowOfFunds shapes, plus request examples for the rejected, already-refunded, partial, Connect and team-member refunds.
- Stripe account creation (settings payments save of a US individual). The recorded request is `tos_agreements`, `users`, `merchant_accounts` insert and a comment before `POST /v1/accounts` (random key), then the account, bank-account and comment updates. `StripeMerchantAccountManager.create_account` and `update_account`, `UpdateUserComplianceInfo` and `UpdatePayoutMethod` carry country-specific account, person and bank-account payloads (4.8k lines); a Stripe error after the POST leaves a `merchant_accounts` row that `handle_new_user_compliance_info` reconciles, and only the US individual shape is recorded. Needed first: one Rails example per country family and entity type that Node would serve, and the error and reconciliation recordings.
No hand-back was weakened, and the lane resources (DBs, containers, Redis, Elasticsearch) were not created because no recording was added.

WS-30 jobs 17 (lane 26: `gumroad_test_26`, `gumroad_replay_26`, Redis 5, container `gr-ruby-c26i`; goldens recorded from the real classes run as Sidekiq jobs, no service or credential reached). Each replay compares the rendered SQL statement by statement (or the recorded outbound requests) and was checked with the code deliberately broken.

- `RefreshWishlistSitemapDailyWorker` (14 cases, `sitemap-generation.golden.json`) generates in Node. The golden forces `SitemapService` onto its production path (`upload_sitemap_to_s3?` and `ping_search_engines?` true, fake uploader keys) and answers every outbound request from WebMock, so the S3 `PUT` (key, `x-amz-acl`, `cache-control`, `content-type`), the `DELETE` of an emptied sitemap and the search engine ping are recorded as requests. Node takes the lock with `SET NX EX` and releases it with Ruby's Lua script, reads `Wishlist.seo_indexable` as an id subquery in `find_each` batches with the owner preload (`IN (2, 1)` in first-appearance order, `= 2` for one owner), builds the one-file `urlset` (the header string, `lastmod` as W3C datetime in UTC, `priority` 0.7), gzips it, uploads through `PutObjectCommand` with the dedicated `S3_SITEMAP_UPLOADER_*` identity, expires `robots_redis_namespace:sitemap_configs` and pings Google with the CGI-escaped sitemap URL in production only. The gzip header carries the wall clock and Node's zlib is not Ruby's, so the replay compares the inflated XML and the call's arguments, not bytes. Hand-backs (the lock is taken and released first, nothing is written): development and test (Rails writes `public/`), a missing uploader key, a sitemap past 50,000 links or 10 MB, a non-ASCII name (`parameterize` stays strict here; I18n transliteration is not ported), and a URL that would need XML escaping (no golden covers it). A wishlist whose owner row is gone raises Ruby's NoMethodError after the lock is released. The 1,001-wishlist case proves the second `find_each` batch.
- Sitemaps still handed back when they win the lock: `RefreshSitemapDailyWorker` (products: `preview_url` is an ActiveStorage retina variant that writes on first use, plus `FORCE INDEX (PRIMARY)` batching) and the categories step of `RefreshSitemapMonthlyWorker` (`taxonomies_for_nav` is read through `Rails.cache.fetch` for an hour, so the statements depend on cache state and Node would have to write a Marshal entry Rails reads). The lock-held paths of both are unchanged.
- `ScheduleMembershipPriceUpdatesJob` (16 cases) runs `Variant.find`, the price-change flag, the category's product and the tiered-membership flag, then the subscriptions' first `find_each` batch; it finishes a tier that changes nothing, a product that is not a tiered membership and a product with no subscription. A subscription (plan changes, `Purchase#set_price_and_rate`) hands back after the same statements. A tier whose category or product row is gone raises Ruby's DelegationError and NoMethodError.
- `UpdateBundlePurchasesContentJob` (19 cases) runs `Link.is_bundle.find` (its not-found message carries the `WHERE` fragment), `MAX(updated_at)` of the alive bundle products (a bundle without one reads sales with `1=1`) and the first `find_in_batches` batch of qualifying sales (`created_at <=` the newest change); a sale to update hands back.
- `StaleTransientSuppressionSweepJob` (16 cases, outbound requests recorded through WebMock) skips blank subuser keys, pages through each subuser's bounce and block lists (`start_time`/`end_time` from the 60- and 3-day window, `limit=500`, `offset`, `Authorization: Bearer`, at most 20 pages, a short page ends the list) and finishes when no list holds an entry with an email. A usable entry (reason classification, owner sign-in, `DELETE`) hands back, and so does a non-2xx status, a body that is not JSON or not a list (Rails notifies `ErrorNotifier` and moves on).
- `ResendConfirmationEmailJob` (11 cases) runs `User.alive.find_by` and the "nothing left to confirm" check; a user with something to confirm hands back (suppression clearing, `send_confirmation_instructions`).
- `PdfUnstampableNotifierJob` (16 cases; it had not landed in jobs-14) runs `Link.find` and the `find_each` over alive PDFs with stamping enabled and `stampable_pdf` NULL, and finishes a product with none; a PDF to check hands back (`PdfStampingService` reads S3).
- `CreateLicensesForExistingCustomersWorker`: the "first batch of 1,000 sales hands back" leftover is gone. Two golden cases (exactly 1,000 sales all licensed, 1,001 sales with one missing licence in each batch; the product is physical so no per-sale content reads inflate the file, 6.7 MB) prove the `id >` walk after the batch's licence writes and the empty read after an exact multiple. The parity-debt note that the worker needed `has_product_level_rich_content?` and the `License` create was out of date: both landed earlier.
- Test helper: `__fixtures__/replay-job-golden.ts` replays a model-job golden (statements, pushes, error message; a marker case must send a prefix of Rails' statements and push nothing; named cases hand back before any statement).
- `jobs.integration.test.ts` ("until_executed, duplicate is dropped") fails on `nextjs-rewrite` as well in this lane: the `uniquejobs:changelog` zset members come back as `Locked, Limit exceeded` where the golden has `Limit exceeded, Locked`. Not touched here.


WS-30 jobs 22 (`nextjs/ws30-jobs-22`, lane 26: `gumroad_test_26`, `gumroad_replay_26`, Redis 3-10, container `gr-ruby-c26n`; goldens recorded from the real classes run as Sidekiq jobs, with Stripe, image generation and mail stubbed by marker exceptions; nothing left the machine, no credential or service involved). Every port and the worker change were checked with the code deliberately broken (about 40 mutations; all fail a test).

Open design question from jobs 21, answered: the worker has an "exhausted, hand back" outcome. A `sidekiq_retries_exhausted` hook may throw `HandBackToRuby`; `handleFailure` then returns an `ExhaustedHandBack` carrying the msg with `retry_count` one below the maximum (`Math.max(retry - 1, 0)`), and the processor pushes it to Ruby's queue and completes the BullMQ job. Ruby runs one last attempt and, if that fails, its own hook, so the money block (`reverse_internal_transfer_or_hold_payouts!`) and the image block (`perform(..., require_avatar: false)`) never run in Node. Proved in `retry.test.ts` (the outcome, the `kill` path, `retry: 0`) and `hand-back.test.ts` (the processor pushes the item with `retry_count` 4 for `retry: 5`, pushes nothing while retries remain, and still fails the job when the hook finishes in Node or raises something else). Limit: Ruby's final attempt can now succeed where the Ruby hook would have run at once; and since these ports hand back before any failing work, Node reaches the hook only after a Node-side failure of the read (a database error), which is rare.

Ported (8 classes plus the pieces they needed):
- `ReverseDeferredInternalTransferJob` (28 cases, 10 of them the hook): `Payment.find`, the no-internal-transfer and state returns run in Node; a failed, returned or cancelled payment hands back before `Stripe::Transfer.retrieve`. The hook reads `Payment.find_by(id:)` (a nil id reads `id IS NULL`) and returns for a missing payment or one without an internal transfer; otherwise it hands back (exhausted, hand back).
- `GenerateSubscribePreviewJob` (22 cases): `User.find`, then hand back before image generation; the hook is the same read followed by a hand back.
- `Exports::AffiliateExportWorker` and `Exports::AudienceExportWorker` (20 and 35 cases): `User.find(seller_id, recipient_id)` as Rails runs it (nil and repeated ids dropped, one id a plain find, two `id IN (a, b)` in the order given with Rails' "Couldn't find all Users" message). The audience worker also runs the option validation (`ArgumentError` when no audience type is truthy). An id Rails casts, the same id as Integer and string, and options that are not a Hash hand back; the export and the mail hand back.
- `ProductBuildUnpackJob` (12): `ProductBuild.find_by(id:)`; a missing build returns, a build hands back before the unpack.
- `StampPdfForPurchaseJob` (12): `Purchase.find`, then hand back before the stamping.
- `DeliverFilesReadyNotificationJob` (16): the request check (`COALESCE(flags, 0) & 16`, a blank id returns) and `Purchase.find`; a requested notification hands back before `stamp_pending?`.
- `CustomDomainVerificationWorker` (36): `CustomDomain.find`, `deleted?` and `valid?` (uniqueness, owner lookups, format, forbidden suffixes); a valid domain hands back before the DNS lookups. `valid?` runs through the new `validWithoutTransaction` of `defineModel` because the existing `valid` opens a transaction Rails does not.
- Test helper `replayHookGolden` in `__fixtures__/replay-job-golden.ts` replays a `sidekiq_retries_exhausted` golden.
- `ExhaustedHandBack` is a class with a plain field, not a parameter property: `loads-under-node.test.ts` rejects the latter.

Not ported: `SyncGuardianToStripeJob` (`pays_user_via_stripe_connect?` and `User#stripe_account` are not ported yet), `UnstickStuckInProgressPurchasesJob` and `BackfillMembershipRenewalRemindersJob` (a Redis claim and cursor before the first read), `DuplicateProductWorker` (a write before any read), `GenerateMerchantCenterFeedWorker`, `AnnualTaxSummaryExportWorker`.

The `uniquejobs:changelog` ordering failures: re-checked with Redis db 4; only `jobs.integration` "until_executed, duplicate is dropped" was run (`Locked, Limit exceeded` against the golden's `Limit exceeded, Locked`). The entries are zset members of one score, so their order is the lexicographic order of the JSON `cjson.encode` writes inside the unique-jobs Lua. My reading is that it follows the Lua build of the Redis that served the golden (Valkey 8.1.10 here), not the Node code; I did not confirm it by comparing `cjson.encode` output on both servers. Not changed; the other three files were not re-run.

WS-30 jobs 21 (`nextjs/ws30-jobs-21`, lane 26: `gumroad_test_26`, `gumroad_replay_26`, Redis 3-10, container `gr-ruby-c26m`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c26m`; goldens recorded from the real classes run as Sidekiq jobs, nothing left the machine, no credential or service involved). Each port was checked with the code deliberately broken (5 mutations each, every one failed the replay; one that survived at first got a golden case of its own).

Step 0, the 11 failing test files jobs 20 reported with `JOBS_REDIS_URL` set (checked on a clean `nextjs-rewrite` tip, own Redis dbs 7-10):
- No real regression. `flush-delivered-email-infos`, `publish-scheduled-post`, `schedule-workflow-installment`, `send-feature-announcement-campaign`, `send-workflow-installment-worker`, `update-sales-related-products-infos` and `workflow-writes` all pass when the files run one at a time. They fail only when vitest runs files in parallel: every one of them flushes the one database `JOBS_REDIS_URL` names (and `REDIS_HOST`/`SIDEKIQ_REDIS_HOST` point at), so they wipe each other's keys. Same environment, parallel files: 114 failed tests; serially: 16. Fixed in `vitest.config.ts` (file parallelism is off whenever `JOBS_REDIS_URL` is set; commit "Run the Redis-backed job tests one file at a time"). Without `REDIS_HOST` set a few more files try `127.0.0.1:6379`; that is the environment, not the code.
- `product-search-jobs` (3 tests of ReindexRecommendableProductsWorker) and the `jobs.integration` / `push-bulk.integration` files are the same pre-existing failure: the `uniquejobs:changelog` zset members come back in a different order from the golden (`Locked, Locked, Locked, Queued...` against `Locked, Queued, Locked...`; members of one score tie). They fail serially on a clean branch with and without this lane's changes; not touched. Setting `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX`/`ES_INDEX_SUFFIX` in the unit-test environment makes `product-search-jobs` fail much more (its goldens carry unsuffixed index names); the suffix belongs to the parity replays only.

Ported (9 classes plus the pieces they needed):
- `EmailOutstandingBalancesCsvWorker`, `SendStripeCurrencyBalancesReportJob`, `SendDeferredRefundsReportWorker`, `SendFinancesReportWorker`, `SendStripeBalanceSummariesReportJob`, `SendDailyFinanceLedgerReportJob` (`finance-report-jobs.ts`, 162 golden cases). They all `return unless Rails.env.production?`; what is left outside production is the bookkeeping of the modules they include, which now runs in Node: `LongRunningJobTracking` (the `deploy:long_running_jobs_in_flight` token taken with the gem's ZADD/EXPIRE Lua and removed in an `ensure`) and `FinanceReportCompletionTracking` (`finance_report_last_completed_at:<class>:<period args joined by ,>`, 120 day expiry; the period resolved through `default_alert_args`: last month as `[month, year]`, the previous UTC day for the ledger). A wrong argument count raises after the token is taken, as in Ruby. In production the job hands back before the token; so does an argument whose `to_s` is not plain (a Float, a Hash). `FinanceReportFailureAlert`'s `sidekiq_retries_exhausted` is in Node for all six: `AccountingMailer.finance_report_job_failed(...).deliver_later` pushed as ActiveJob does, the period pinned to the job's `created_at || enqueued_at` (a `created_at` of 0 counts, so does an empty class name); a Float or Hash argument fails in the hook, which the worker logs (hooks cannot hand back).
- `TaxFormTransactionReportJob` (36): `User.find`, the seller's `us_1099_k` tax form of the year and the live Stripe merchant account check run in Node; the report build (Stripe) and a tax form without a usable `stripe_account_id` (the fallback to the seller's own Stripe account) hand back. A user id or year that Rails casts (float, word, nil) hands back before any statement. Its exhausted block (`ErrorNotifier.notify(exception, context: {user_id:, year:})`) is in Node.
- `HandleNewBankAccountWorker` and `DeleteGuardianStripePersonJob` (37): their performs are one Stripe call, so they hand back (after `BankAccount.find`; before anything is read). What moves is the exhausted block: a seller-invisible payout note (`User#add_payout_note`: Comment insert and PaperTrail version in one transaction, now `addPayoutNoteTo` in `payment.ts`) written before the Sentry notification, with Ruby's rescue around the note (log, notify, then the plain notification still goes out); nil, digit string, integral float and word ids are looked up as Rails casts them. Limit: a perform that always hands back never retries in Node, so these hooks run only for a Node-side failure of the read before the hand-back; the gain is that the worker accepts the classes.
- `GenerateCommunityChatRecapJob` (36): `CommunityChatRecap.find`, then hand back before the generator (a model API call). `FailureHandler` runs in Node: the recap is marked failed with the error message and `CommunityChatRecapRun#check_if_finished!` (new in `community-chat-recap-run.ts`, with the weekly-run and notification pushes of the model callbacks) runs. Its exhausted cases need a Redis (unique-locked pushes), like `flush-delivered-email-infos`.

The Node retries-exhausted mechanism needed no change (`JOB_RETRIES_EXHAUSTED`; `handleFailure` already runs the hook once `retry_count` reaches the maximum). What was missing was a golden of the Rails blocks themselves, which the generators now record by calling `sidekiq_retries_exhausted_block`.

Not ported, with the reason found reading the Ruby:
- `ReverseDeferredInternalTransferJob`: its exhausted block is `StripePayoutProcessor.reverse_internal_transfer_or_hold_payouts!` (a Stripe reversal, or the payout pause when that fails): money, and a hook cannot hand a run back to Ruby. Stays in Ruby with its hook.
- `GenerateSubscribePreviewJob`: its exhausted block runs `perform(..., require_avatar: false)` (image generation and an Active Storage attach); same problem.
- Open design question for the next lane: either the worker learns an "exhausted, hand back" outcome that re-queues the item to Ruby with `retry_count` one below the maximum (Ruby runs one last attempt and its own hook on failure), or such classes stay Ruby-owned. Neither was built here.

WS-30 jobs 20 (lane 26: `gumroad_test_26`, `gumroad_replay_26`, Redis 3, container `gr-ruby-c26k`; goldens recorded from the real classes run as Sidekiq jobs, no service or credential reached). Each replay compares the rendered SQL statement by statement and was checked with the code deliberately broken. Twelve more classes run in Node up to their first write or outbound call, then hand the whole job back to Rails.

- `ProcessEarlyFraudWarningJob` (18 cases): `EarlyFraudWarning.find` (RecordNotFound for a missing row) and `resolved?`; a resolved warning finishes, an unresolved one hands back before `update_from_stripe!`. `ProcessPurchaseEarlyFraudWarningJob` is a constant alias; the registry keeps aliases as Ruby, so payloads already carrying the old name still run there.
- `CreateStripeMerchantAccountWorker` (10) and `StripeRestraintNoticeEmailJob` (12): `User.find` only; they hand back before `StripeMerchantAccountManager.create_account` / before `with_lock`'s BEGIN.
- `AnalyzeFileWorker` (32, recorded in each Rails environment): `Rails.env.test?` finishes; any other environment hands back before the class name is constantized.
- `TransferDropboxFileToS3Worker` (23): `DropboxFile.find` and the `transfer_to_s3` guard (`state != in_progress` or a `deleted_at` finishes, including a missing or unknown state); a file in progress hands back before `mark_failed!` or the download. A file without `expires_at` (Ruby raises on `Time.current > nil`) hands back.
- `GenerateVideoPosterWorker` and `ResizeOversizedAssetPreviewWorker` (11 each): `AssetPreview.find_by(id:)` and `deleted?`; a live preview hands back before the Active Storage work.
- `MassRefundForFraudJob` (26): `Purchase.find_by_external_id` for each id (an id that does not decrypt reads `id IS NULL`, as `find_by(id: nil)` does) and the `link_id == product_id` check; ids that match nothing only fill a hash the job logs, so such a job finishes in Node. The first purchase of the product hands back before `refund_for_fraud_and_block_buyer!`. A product id that is not an Integer (`link_id == 1.0` is true in Ruby) and external ids that are not a list of strings hand back before any read. Needs `OBFUSCATE_IDS_CIPHER_KEY` in the replay (the test sets Rails' test value).
- `ChargePreorderWorker` (16): `Preorder.find_by(id:)`; a missing preorder raises Ruby's NoMethodError on nil, a preorder hands back before `charge!`.
- `PushNotificationWorker` (25): `User.find` and the receiver's `devices` of the app type; a receiver with no iOS or Android device finishes (other device types are skipped), the first push hands back. A push to the team admins (`to_team_admins` truthy, its own member query) and an app type that is not a string hand back before any read.
- `FinalizeBuyerPresentmentPurchaseJob` (25, one case per purchase state): `Purchase.find`; anything but `in_progress` finishes (`pending_processor_settlement?` starts with `in_progress?`), an in-progress purchase hands back before the settlement reads.
- `AttachPastPurchasesToUserWorker` (34): `User.find`, `email ||= user.email if user.confirmed?`, Ruby's `blank?` (`[[:space:]]`: U+0085 counts, U+FEFF does not; cases for both) and the `find_each` over `purchaser_id IS NULL` purchases of the email; the first batch with a row hands back before `attach_to_user_and_card`. An email that is not a string hands back.
- Not done: `EmailOutstandingBalancesCsvWorker` (its `LongRunningJobTracking` wrapper writes a Redis in-flight entry and `FinanceReportFailureAlert` needs a retries-exhausted handler), `TaxFormTransactionReportJob`, `HandleNewBankAccountWorker`, `ReverseDeferredInternalTransferJob`, `DeleteGuardianStripePersonJob`, `GenerateSubscribePreviewJob` and `GenerateCommunityChatRecapJob` (each defines `sidekiq_retries_exhausted`, which the worker refuses to start without a Node handler for), `HandlePaypalEventWorker` and `DuplicateProductWorker` (first step is already an outbound call or a write), `FinalizeBuyerPresentmentChargeJob` (`Charge#settlement_deferrable?` reads far more than the golden covers), `BackfillPdfStampsJob`, `DeliverFilesReadyNotificationJob`, `RefundPurchaseForFraudWorker` (its rescue notifies Sentry before re-raising a missing purchase).


WS-30 jobs 19 (lane 26: `gumroad_test_26`, `gumroad_replay_26`, Redis 3, container `gr-ruby-c26j`; goldens recorded from the real classes run as Sidekiq jobs, no service or credential reached). Each replay compares the rendered SQL statement by statement and was checked with the code deliberately broken. Eight more classes run in Node up to their first write or outbound call, then hand the whole job back to Rails.

- `GenerateProductFilesArchivesJob` (11 cases): `Link.find_by(id:)`, finishes a missing or deleted product; a live product hands back before `with_lock`'s BEGIN.
- `ActivateIntegrationsWorker` (21 cases), `DeactivateIntegrationsWorker` (27) and `UpdateIntegrationsOnTierChangeWorker` (26) share `models/purchase-integrations.ts` (`Purchase#find_enabled_integration`: the first option's integration, or the product's for a physical product or no option; `keep_inactive_members`; `DiscordIntegration.discord_user_id_for`) and `worker/jobs/support/integration-services.ts`. They finish when nothing is added or removed (Discord's `activate` has no block in Ruby, so it only reads); a Circle member to add or remove and a Discord member to remove hand back before the API call. The query cache hides the repeated lookups Rails makes, as it does in Node. Deactivate also reads the subscription's other purchases with a live purchase integration; tier change reads `purchases.is_original_subscription_purchase.order(:id)[-2]` and the unordered `has_one :original_purchase` (a missing one raises Ruby's NoMethodError). A purchase with options whose product row is gone hands back (no recording of Ruby's message).
- `SendPaypalTopupNotificationJob` (20 cases) and `RenewCustomDomainSslCertificates` (12): goldens recorded in each Rails environment (`Rails.env` switched in the generator). Outside production (for the renewal: production, or staging outside a branch deployment; an empty `BRANCH_DEPLOYMENT` still counts as one) they return; otherwise they hand back before `PaypalBalanceCheckService` / `SslCertificates::Renew` is built.
- `SendSignedPiracyNoticesJob` (21 cases): reads the `piracy_reports_sending` Flipper feature (boolean, time 100 and 0; actor and actor-percentage gates are closed without an actor) and scans `state = 'signed'`; finishes when off or nothing is signed. A signed report, a partial percentage of time or an expression gate hands back.
- `GenerateLargeSellersAnalyticsCacheWorker` (6 cases): the `users INNER JOIN large_sellers` scan; a large seller hands back before `CachingProxy#generate_cache`.
- Equivalent mutants: dropping the id de-duplication in `DeactivateIntegrationsWorker`, or processing the single purchase twice, is invisible in the statements because the query cache serves the repeats.


WS-11/12 leftovers, eighth pass (lane 15: `gumroad_test_15`/`gumroad_replay_15`, Redis 56-63, container `gr-ruby-c15g`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15g`). The seventh pass's hand-backs are paid down except as listed below.
- Covers. `coverProps` reads oEmbed documents (`presenters/asset-preview.ts`: the YAML column, `oembed_url` from the first `<iframe src>`, `oembed_thumbnail_url`, `display_width`/`display_height`, `to_i` of the reported size) and uploaded images (`asset-preview-upload.ts`: the blob row, `Rails.cache` entries `attachment_<id>_original_url`/`_retina_url`, the retina variant's blob key by `resizeToLimitDigest(.., [width, nil])`, `cdn_url_for`, and `ProcessAssetPreviewRetinaWorker` enqueued on every read while no variant record exists, as `url_from_file` does). The blob key is not random on read: it is a stored column, so the replay needs no Rails-side blob fixture; the recorded examples (`product_page_parity_spec.rb`: YouTube, Vimeo with a protocol-relative player and a scaled size, a size that is not a number, an uploaded image with and without a processed variant, an uploaded GIF; `profile_product_props_parity_spec.rb`: YouTube, uploaded) replay green. The product page's meta tags follow `social_share_image`: `og:image` is the Unsplash or uploaded URL, else the oEmbed thumbnail through `Addressable::URI.escape` (identity for a URL of unreserved characters only; a space, `%`, brackets or non-ASCII hands back), the twitter player card, and the `rel=preload as=image` link per uploaded image. Rails enqueues the retina job once per URL it reads (five on a product page) because the test adapter takes no unique lock; Node's real lock keeps one, so that fixture ignores `jobs` and asserts the one BullMQ job. Each upload example burns attachment ids (`burn_attachment_ids`) because the replay's Next process keeps `Rails.cache` across fixtures while the database restarts at id 1. Still handed back: an uploaded video (poster generation and the `preview_image` attachment), an upload without analysed `width`/`height` metadata, a blob on another service, an oEmbed document with comments, raw-text elements, character references or an iframe without `src`, an oEmbed cover with no thumbnail or iframe on the product page (twitter player card with a nil content), and `/l/` pages for products whose cover is both a photo and an upload. `mobile-serializers.ts` (`hasAlivePreviews`) and the admin product serializer still hand back any cover.
- Home pages (`erb-pages.ts`, `home-pages.ts`, `erb/home-layout.ts`). The signed-in nav (Dashboard button, `dashboard_url(host: DOMAIN)`) and the analytics flags of a signed-in seller (`signedInPresenters.thirdPartyAnalyticsDisabled`) render from a recorded Warden session; an impersonated session still hands back. The flash toast (`layouts/shared/_flash.html.erb`: first of alert, warning, info, notice) renders; only a warning (a failed login's flash) is recorded, the alert, info and notice branches are the same template with another class string and are not recorded. `as_embed` makes the body transparent (a flat `as_embed=` is an empty string and truthy, a bare `as_embed` is nil, a list or hash is truthy). The cold star count calls GitHub in Node (`GithubStarsController.fetch_stars`, `Rails.cache.fetch` with a one hour expiry and a ten second race condition TTL; any failure caches nil and the nav prints "GitHub" without a count); the replay stands in for api.github.com with `GITHUB_API_ORIGIN`, the way `TAXJAR_ENDPOINT` does for TaxJar. HEAD takes the normal (not edge cacheable) path and drops the body. `rackTopLevelParams` (`rails-params.ts`) reads the top-level keys Rack builds from a query string, so a list or hash param no longer hands the home pages back; `a[b][c]`, a name used flat and nested (a 400 in Rack) and an invalid percent escape still do. Recorded in `home_static_pages_parity_spec.rb` and `erb_layout_pages_parity_spec.rb`; the replays are `home-static-pages`, `home-static-pages-cold-cache`, `erb-layout-pages`, `erb-layout-pages-cold-cache` and the new `erb-layout-pages-nil-stars`. Not pinned: HEAD of a cacheable request (Rails answers it on the normal path too, which is what the fixture holds).
- `Pages::Interpolator.interpolate_profile` is ported (`services/pages-interpolator.ts`, 23 byte-equal cases from `__fixtures__/generate-pages-interpolator-golden.rb`): profile `name`/`bio`, product-scoped `price`/`original-price`/`currency` with `data-gumroad-option`, valueless markers. A marker on a void or raw-text element, a table or list element, whitespace-only text or control characters hands back. Not ported and still Rails': `Pages::Interpolator.interpolate` (product half: `price_formatted_verbose`, review stats, `BuyButtonParams`), `Pages::ProfileData`, `Pages::ProductPrices` and `Ai::PageSanitizer`. No endpoint calls the TypeScript interpolator yet, because every caller sits behind a custom HTML page that renders the sanitizer first.
- The typecheck errors in `purchase-refund.ts`, `purchases-product.ts`, `ProductsIframeShow.tsx` and the api-v2 `ActionResult` tests are fixed (`npm run typecheck` is clean; `tsc -p parity` still reports errors in `*.replay.test.ts` files that this pass did not touch).

WS-20 paid purchase, recording and pre-charge steps (`nextjs/ws21-purchase-model-5`, lane 24: `gumroad_test_24`, `gumroad_replay_24`, Redis 50-53 recording and 59 replay, container `gr-ruby-c24k`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c24k`). Nothing left the machine: Stripe is answered by copies of existing `OrdersController` VCR cassettes under `vcr_cassettes/Orders_paid_purchase/` with `record: :none`, so a call a cassette does not hold raises. Served in Node: nothing new for the request; a paid `POST /orders` still hands back whole before its first write. What exists:

- `orders_paid_purchase_parity_spec.rb` (3 examples, `parity_timeline: true`): a $5 USD product bought with a tokenized card as a signed-out buyer, as a signed-in buyer, and with a declined card. `orders-paid-purchase.replay.test.ts` replays them as hand-backs (the request reaches the Rails stand-in once and the database is untouched) and `orders-paid-purchase.timeline.test.ts` pins the recorded order.
- The recorded order of the successful card purchase: INSERT `purchases`, `purchase_refund_policies`, UPDATE `links` (`sales_count_for_inventory_cache`), `ElasticsearchIndexerWorker`, `RefreshAudienceMemberJob`, INSERT `purchase_sales_tax_infos`, `orders`, `order_purchases`, `charges`, `charge_purchases`; then Stripe call 1 `GET /v1/payment_methods/:pm`; UPDATE `charges`, `purchases`, `ElasticsearchIndexerWorker`; call 2 `POST /v1/payment_intents` (`amount`, `currency`, `description` "Gumroad Charge <charge external id>", `metadata[purchases{0}]`, `transfer_group` "CH-<charge id>", `payment_method_types[0]=card`, `off_session=false`, `payment_method`, `statement_descriptor_suffix`); call 3 `POST /v1/payment_intents/:pi/confirm`; call 4 `GET /v1/charges/:ch?expand[]=application_fee.balance_transaction&expand[]=balance_transaction`; then UPDATE `charges`, INSERT `processor_payment_intents`, UPDATE `purchases`, INSERT `balance_transactions` and `balances`, UPDATE both, UPDATE `purchases` twice, INSERT `url_redirects`, the after-commit jobs, INSERT `events`, `SendChargeReceiptJob`. A declined card stops after call 3 (UPDATE `purchases`, `links`, `RecordOrderChargeOutcomeJob`, INSERT `events`, no balance, no receipt).
- Idempotency key: the two POSTs carry the stripe gem's per-attempt UUID (recorded as `<uuid>`); `Charge::CreateService` passes an explicit key only for a buyer-currency presentment, so for a plain USD charge the key is random and what is proven is its shape and its presence on writes only. The GETs carry none. A Node client has to send its own random key and reuse it on a retry of the same call.
- Ported with a golden: `models/purchase-fees.ts`, `Purchase#calculate_fees` for a one-time purchase (flat and processor rates, waived and high-volume seller rates, a custom rate from the purchase or seller, the discover surcharge, Pix IOF, a Brazilian Connect account), 2,233 cases recorded by `scripts/golden/purchase-fees.rb` from the real method (145 cents for the recorded $5). Nothing calls it. The predicates it takes as inputs are not ported: `seller.high_volume_seller_fee?` (a Flipper feature), `charged_using_gumroad_merchant_account?`, `charge_discover_fee?`, and where the Pix `card_type` comes from.
- What blocks serving the shape, in order. (1) The pre-charge writes (purchase, refund policy, tax info, order, charge) are mostly the free path's, but a paid purchase also needs the processor-specific fields (merchant account selection, chargeable resolution from `pm_...` through the first Stripe read, `stripe_fingerprint`, `card_type`, `card_expiry_*`, `card_visual`), the `charges` row stamped with merchant account, amount and gumroad amount (the UPDATE `charges` between calls 1 and 2), and `Order::ChargeService` (930 lines: sales tax through TaxJar and the zip tax rate table), `Charge::CreateService` (491 lines) and `StripeChargeProcessor#create_payment_intent_or_charge!` with its Indian-mandate, UPI, FX-quote and direct-charge branches. (2) The post-charge half is unported: success handling after a charge (`stripe_transaction_id`, `processor_fee_cents` from the balance transaction, `Balance` and `BalanceTransaction` creation per merchant account and day, `purchase_success_balance_id`), `ProcessorPaymentIntent`, affiliate and collaborator credits, and the post-success jobs of a non-free purchase. (3) Ambiguous outcomes (a timeout after the PaymentIntent create, 3DS `requires_action`, client-confirmed late success, `Purchase::SyncStatusWithChargeProcessorService`) have no recording, and a Node failure between the intent create and the purchase update would leave a charged card with an in-progress purchase, so no partial serving is safe. (4) `statement_descriptor_suffix` is built from the seller's name plus a hex suffix; its source was not traced.
- Revert-check: with the waived-fee branch of `calculateFees` removed, `purchase-fees.test.ts` fails on the golden. The replay hands back by construction (`orders-free-purchase.ts` serves only a `$0` line), so there is no served path to revert.
- Not recorded, so still unknown: a membership or preorder first charge, a discount code, a tipped or taxed order, a buyer-currency quote, a connected-account direct charge, PayPal and Braintree, and a card needing 3DS.

WS-20 paid purchase, building blocks (`nextjs/ws21-purchase-model-6`, lane 24: `gumroad_test_24`, Redis 50-53, container `gr-ruby-c24l`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c24l`; Stripe only through WebMock or recorded JSON). Done, each with a golden from the real Ruby and mutation checks: (2) the fee inputs, `purchase-charge-facts.ts` (1,059 cases): `User#high_volume_seller_fee?`, `User#merchant_account` + `Purchase#prepare_merchant_account` (UPI Autopay stays on Gumroad's Stripe account; Brazilian Connect with an affiliate is an error), `charged_using_gumroad_merchant_account?`, `charge_discover_fee?`, and the Pix `card_type`, which comes from the Stripe payment method's `type` when it has no card brand. `statement_descriptor_suffix` is the seller's `name_or_username` (`Charge#statement_description`) stripped to `[A-Z0-9./\s]`, trimmed and cut to 22 characters. (3) `stripe-payment-intent-request.ts` (72 cases through WebMock plus the three recorded $5 requests): future usage, off-session `confirm`, Indian mandates (`mandateLookups` says which Stripe lookups the caller must make first), UPI Autopay refusals, FX quote (`Stripe-Version` 2025-07-30.preview), direct (`Stripe-Account`, `application_fee_amount`) and destination (`transfer_data`) charges, seller-proceeds refusals; every POST needs a fresh random idempotency key (the create's is caller-chosen only for a buyer-currency presentment). (1, partly) `stripe-charge-facts.ts` (34 cases: fee from the `stripe_fee` detail, flow of funds for platform, direct, destination and never-credited destination payments, card details) and `balance-transaction-amounts.ts` (261 cases). Not done: the `balances`/`balance_transactions` writes for a purchase (the existing model refuses a purchase; `increment_sellers_balance!` ends in a full Purchase save), `processor_payment_intents`, affiliate credits, the non-free success jobs, (4) `Charge::CreateService` and the TaxJar step of `Order::ChargeService`. Still blocking served paid orders: all of that, the chargeable resolution from the first Stripe read, and the ambiguous outcomes (timeout after the intent create, 3DS `requires_action`, late client confirmation), which have no recording; a Node failure after the intent create would leave a charged card with an in-progress purchase, so no partial serving is safe.

WS-20 paid purchase, ledger writes (`nextjs/ws21-purchase-model-7`, lane 24: `gumroad_test_24`, `gumroad_replay_24`, Redis 50-53, container `gr-ruby-c24m`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c24m`; Stripe and PayPal untouched, nothing in these paths calls them). Done, one commit per slice, each with a golden recorded from the real Ruby classes under `rails runner` (`scripts/golden/purchase-seller-balance.rb`, `processor-payment-intent.rb`, `purchase-affiliate-balance.rb`, sharing `support/paid_sale.rb`), a Node test replaying every statement, Flipper read and job in order, and a mutation check:
- `Purchase#increment_sellers_balance!` (`models/purchase-seller-balance.ts`): the seller's `BalanceTransaction` and the day's `Balance` it lands on, then the purchase's `purchase_success_balance_id` (14 cases). The balance date is `succeeded_at.to_date`; an open (unpaid) balance of the same day and merchant account is reused, a paid one or another day's is not.
- `ProcessorPaymentIntent` (`models/processor-payment-intent.ts`): the insert of a charged purchase; datetime(6) columns keep microseconds.
- Affiliate credits (`models/purchase-affiliate-credit.ts`, 9 cases): `increment_affiliates_balance!` for a non-collaborator affiliate: the explicit-column `SELECT affiliates`, the `sellers_bear_affiliate_fees` Flipper read, the fee cut, an existing-credit check, the affiliate's own `BalanceTransaction` (or the balance of one already booked), `AffiliateCredit.create!` and its `ElasticsearchIndexerWorker` after-commit (2 seconds). `affiliates_links` is read twice by Rails (fee cut, then the credit's basis points); the recording ran without a query cache, so the replay wraps the call in `uncached`.
- Quirks the goldens pinned down: when the sale already has an applied seller transaction, Ruby assigns `purchase_success_balance` and never saves (the `save!` is conditioned on an id that has just been assigned), so the link stays in memory until a later save; PaperTrail's `object` key order depends on whether the purchase was `find`-loaded (lazy attributes) or built in process, and is proven here only for a `find`-loaded purchase, not for the integrated order flow; Node reads the purchase's presentment before it credits an affiliate (Ruby reads it afterwards, in the seller's share) so a presentment is refused before the first write, and the affiliate replay moves that one SELECT to the front.
- Handed back with `NotPortedError` before any write: a collaborator's credit, a direct affiliate (`direct_affiliate_id` in `json_data`), a buyer-currency presentment, a purchase with no merchant account, fee or `succeeded_at`, a Connect seller with an affiliate, a missing Gumroad merchant account or affiliate user.
- Not done: the non-free success jobs (receipt and Elasticsearch/analytics jobs after a paid purchase), `Charge::CreateService`, the 3DS/SCA and timeout paths, and the TaxJar step. Paid `POST /orders` still hands back whole: no recorded example proves the whole order, so none of these pieces is called from the handler.

WS-20 paid purchase, success transition and collaborator credit (`nextjs/ws21-purchase-model-8`, lane 24: `gumroad_test_24`, `gumroad_replay_24`, Redis 50-53, container `gr-ruby-c24n`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c24n`; Stripe and PayPal untouched, nothing in these paths calls them). Two slices, each with a golden from the real Ruby classes under `rails runner`, a Node test replaying every statement, Redis command, Flipper read and job in order, a mutation check and one commit:
- The success jobs (1): `Purchase::MarkSuccessfulService#perform` for a charged purchase (`scripts/golden/purchase-paid-success.rb`, 7 cases, `purchase-paid-success.golden.json`; `markPaidPurchaseSuccessful` in `models/purchase-success.ts`). The finding that settles "the exact set and order of the non-free jobs": the transition itself enqueues the same ten jobs, in the same order, as a free purchase (`InvalidateProductCacheWorker`, `SendToElasticsearchWorker`, `RecordOrderChargeOutcomeJob`, `UpdateSalesRelatedProductsInfosJob`, two `ElasticsearchIndexerWorker`, `RefreshAudienceMemberJob`, the `ContactingCreatorMailer.notify` `MailDeliveryJob`, `PostToPingEndpointsWorker`, `ActivateIntegrationsWorker`, plus `ScheduleWorkflowEmailsWorker` for a seller with a workflow); what the paid timeline has beyond the free one is not a success job but the hooks of the saves around the charge (timeline jobs 2, 3 and 4 of `orders_paid_purchase_parity_spec.rb`: the Elasticsearch `fee_cents`/`paypal_email` update at +5s after the merchant-account save, then the `stripe_refunded` update and a `RefreshAudienceMemberJob` after `save_charge_data`), which `purchase-create.ts` already models as update hooks. `SendChargeReceiptJob` is pushed by the order service after the events insert, as in the free path. Between the `succeeded_at` save and the state save the transition runs `increment_sellers_balance!` (`purchase-seller-balance.ts`, now taking the merchant account the charged instance already holds so the replay sends no `SELECT merchant_accounts` there). `financial_transaction_validation` now accepts a charged purchase (price above zero, transaction id, merchant account, fingerprint or PayPal order, processor) and rejects a priced one without them, as Ruby does. `assertPaidSuccessServable` asks with reads only for everything the transition could still refuse after its first write (a cart on the order, a Gumroad Day date, the waived fee, the high-volume fee), so a caller can hand back before the processor is reached; an affiliate, a membership charge, a recommended purchase and a purchase without a charge receipt are refused by the row alone. Mutation checks: removing the `increment_sellers_balance!` call fails 5 replays; forcing the charged branch of `financial_transaction_validation` off fails 6.
- Collaborator credit (2): `Purchase#increment_affiliates_balance!` for a `Collaborator`. Two differences from a direct affiliate, both proven by new golden cases: the collaborator always shares the fee (the seller's `bears_affiliate_fee` flag and the `sellers_bear_affiliate_fees` feature apply only to other affiliates, so no Flipper read happens) and `Collaborator#basis_points(product_id:)` reads the product's `affiliates_links` rate whatever the `apply_to_all_products` flag says. Four new cases (all products, a product rate, a seller who bears the fee, a credit already recorded) plus the existing collaborator case replay green; the earlier cases of the golden are byte-identical. Recorded but NOT ported: a direct affiliate credited beside the collaborator (`direct_affiliate_id` in `json_data`, `direct_affiliate_credit_cents`, a second `affiliate_credits` row with `is_direct_affiliate_credit`, the collaborator's fee taken from what the direct affiliate's cut leaves); two cases pin the statements and Node still refuses it before the first write. Mutation checks: treating a collaborator as any other affiliate fails 5 replays; applying the all-products shortcut to collaborators fails 5.
- Assumptions the success golden makes that the integrated flow must still prove: the recorded instance is a `find`-loaded purchase whose `variant_attributes`, `merchant_account`, `order_purchase`, `charge.order` and `purchase_url_parameter` associations were primed before the first marker, because in the real flow the create and charge services leave them loaded (the free golden, recorded through the real app, shows no read for them); PaperTrail's `object` key order for the integrated order flow is still unproven (see the ledger-writes pass); the golden seeds `Order`/`Charge` with minimal columns.
- Not done, with what each needs. (3) `Charge::CreateService` and the TaxJar step: no golden. They need a recording through WebMock of `Order::ChargeService#perform` for a taxed order (TaxJar `POST /v2/taxes` stubbed, `PurchaseTaxjarInfo`, the `charges` stamp between the payment-method read and the intent create) and for a tipped, discounted and multi-line order. (4) The PaymentIntent response handling and the chargeable resolution: needs the real `Order::ChargeService` driven end to end against the three recorded Stripe answers with a statement-level (not write-only) log, so the associations loaded at each point, the `GET payment_methods` read and the `stripe_fingerprint`/`card_*` assignment are on record. Not served and why: paid `POST /orders` still hands back whole. The missing recordings, exactly: (a) an integrated paid `POST /orders` golden with every SELECT, Redis command and Flipper read (the request-spec fixtures hold writes and jobs only), for the succeeded case; (b) a timeout or dropped connection on `POST /v1/payment_intents` and on `.../confirm` (what Rails writes, whether the purchase fails or stays in progress, the idempotency-key retries of the gem, the `FailAbandonedPurchaseWorker`/`SyncStuckPurchasesJob` hand-off); (c) a `requires_action` intent (3DS: the `requires_card_action` response with `client_secret`, `intent_id` and the confirm-scoped order token, the `processor_payment_intents` insert, `FailAbandonedPurchaseWorker.perform_in(ChargeProcessor::TIME_TO_COMPLETE_SCA)`); (d) a late client confirmation through `Order::ConfirmService`/`FinalizeConfirmedChargeService` after the page returns; (e) a `processing` intent (`client_confirmed`, `stripe_status`); (f) the decline paths with their failure side effects beyond the recorded plain decline. A Node failure after the intent create would leave a charged card with an in-progress purchase, so no partial serving is safe and none was added.

WS-20 paid purchase, recordings (`nextjs/ws21-purchase-model-9`, lane 24: `gumroad_test_24`, `gumroad_replay_24`, Redis 50-53, container `gr-ruby-c24p`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c24p`; Stripe and TaxJar only through WebMock, no live service or credential). Three commits:
- Recordings: `scripts/golden/purchase-paid-order.rb` drives the real `POST /orders` through an integration session, with `StripePaidOrder` (`support/stripe_paid_order.rb`, fixtures extracted from the `Orders_paid_purchase` cassettes) as the WebMock responder, and writes `purchase-paid-order.golden.json` (18 cases, every SELECT, Redis command, Flipper read, job and HTTP call in order). Cases: a $5 sale; four declines (plain, incorrect zip, fraudulent, insufficient funds); the intent create losing its first / every answer; the confirm losing its first / every answer; `requires_action`; `processing`; `requires_action` confirmed late (succeeds / the card fails); taxed (TaxJar answers / TaxJar down); tipped; discounted; multi-line taxed, tipped and discounted. `purchase-paid-order.golden.test.ts` (17 tests) pins the outcomes. Findings a port must reproduce: the Stripe gem makes 4 attempts under one idempotency key per call; when every create answer is lost the purchase fails with `stripe_unavailable`, no PI row; when every confirm answer is lost the purchase also fails `stripe_unavailable` although Stripe may have charged the card, and neither `charges.stripe_payment_intent_id` nor `processor_payment_intents` holds the intent id, so nothing in Rails can find it again (a real hole, kept as is); `requires_action` leaves the purchase `in_progress`, stores the PI row and schedules `FailAbandonedPurchaseWorker` 900 seconds out; `processing` stores `stripe_status=processing`, sets `charges.flags` bit 2 and schedules nothing; the late confirmation reads the intent then the charge and never re-confirms (a failed card ends `failed`); a fraudulent decline alone enqueues `Radar::AddValueListItemJob`, no decline writes a balance; TaxJar is asked before Stripe, a 503 from it means no tax and a normal sale; a multi-line order of one seller is one charge, one intent, one TaxJar call per line (1452 cents, tax included).
- Port: `payment-intent-response.ts` (12 tests): the intent outcome classification (only `use_stripe_sdk` counts as 3DS), the `requires_card_action` and `processing` line item bodies, the confirm-scoped order token (reproduced byte for byte from the recorded IV) and the abandonment time. Mutation checks, each failing the tests: a 10 minute SCA window, a 2 hour token, dropping `stripe_connect_account_id`, ignoring `next_action.type`. The direct-affiliate-beside-collaborator credit was committed earlier (2bccaa4ef) with 17 golden replays and two mutation checks.
- Recorder notes: a full run of the script takes about 50 minutes (it truncates about 230 tables twice per case); re-record one case with `ONLY=<regexp>`, which merges into the existing JSON. The lane has no GeoIP database, so the taxed cases stub `GeoIp.lookup` for 203.0.113.7 (Seattle, WA); TaxJar results are cached in Redis (`taxjar_calculations`), so the script purges that namespace before each case or the down case is hidden.
- Not done, so paid `POST /orders` stays handed back whole: `Charge::CreateService` pieces and the TaxJar step have a recording but no Node replay; there is no Node Stripe client with the retry and idempotency behaviour above; `save_charge_data`, `confirm_charge_intent!` and the chargeable resolution from the first Stripe read are unported; the late confirmation (`Order::ConfirmService`, `FinalizeConfirmedChargeService`) has no Node replay. Not recorded: the `/finalize` and client-confirm `prepare` lane, two-seller (off-session) carts, and request-spec `parity_timeline` fixtures for the ambiguous outcomes (the golden is a superset, but it is not served through the parity harness). A Node failure after the intent create would leave a charged card with an in-progress purchase, so no partial serving is safe.

WS-21/WS-23 payments hand-backs, sixth pass (`nextjs/ws-payments-2`, lane 23: `gumroad_test_23`/`gumroad_replay_23`, Redis 41-45, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c23j`, container `gr-ruby-c23j`). The instant payout's failure recordings now exist; the port itself is still not done, and nothing was weakened (the success and every failure example stay `hands an ...`, so the replay asserts no Stripe call and no write from Node).
- Recorded: `spec/requests/instant_payouts_failures_parity_spec.rb`, 17 examples with `parity_timeline: true`, Stripe only through WebMock (the account setup replays the `InstantPayoutsService` cassette, the failing answers are stubbed over it; nothing leaves the machine). Fixtures are under `nextjs/parity/fixtures/requests/instant_payouts_failures_parity_spec/`. Stripe-held balance: `balance_insufficient` (code), `Cannot create payouts` (also writes the seller pause: `users` update plus a comment), the debit card limit, `Amount must be no less than`, `requires further intervention`, a currency-mismatch destination, a bank Stripe cannot find (`bank_accounts` soft-deleted after the payment is failed), a 429, an unclassified 400 (no reason), a 500, and a dropped connection (`PAYOUT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`: `payments`, `balances`, `users` and a hold comment). Gumroad-held balance: `POST /v1/transfers` and the destination `GET /v1/charges/py_...` before the payout, then after a failed payout the reversal (`GET` transfer, `POST .../reversals`, `GET` charge with refunds, `GET` balance transaction), the same with a currency difference (`credits`, `balances`, `balance_transactions` inserts and updates), a dropped connection that is reversed and held, a reversal that itself fails (`UNREVERSED_INTERNAL_TRANSFER` stamped on a requeueable reason, seller held), the internal transfer refused, and the internal transfer's connection dropped.
- What the recordings show a port must do, none of it in the earlier success-only fixture: the Stripe gem retries a dropped connection and a 5xx (`Stripe.max_network_retries = 3`) under one idempotency key, so Rails made four identical `POST /v1/payouts` (or `/v1/transfers`) before it gave up, while a 429 is not retried (the suite's own 12-retry wrapper in `spec/support/stripe_rate_limit_retries.rb` is stubbed out in the spec, since production does not have it); `mark_failed!` runs before the reversal and before the hold, so the order of writes after the last Stripe call differs per reason (see `timelineSegments` in `money-hand-backs.replay.test.ts`); `failure_reason`-specific comments (`add_payment_failure_reason_comment`) are inserted for every reason except insufficient funds, a 429, a 5xx and an unclassified error; a reversal that succeeds with no currency difference writes nothing after the last call. The cannot-pay and debit-card-limit mails go through the test mail adapter in a request spec (parity-local-stack.md), so no mail job is in the fixtures and a port cannot be checked against them yet.
- Recorder and stand-in: `Parity::Recorder#record_dropped_http` records a call WebMock dropped (WebMock's `to_raise` never fires `after_request`) as `response.dropped`; `startStandIn` destroys the socket for such an answer (`stand-in.test.ts`, which fails with the line removed). `money-hand-backs.replay.test.ts` replays all 17 as hand-backs and asserts the call shapes and the post-call writes of four of them.
- Still blocking the Node port of `POST /instant_payouts`, in order: (1) `Payouts.create_payments` and `Payouts.mark_balances_processing` (negative-ledger hold note, debt reserve, per-group claim) have no Node implementation or golden vectors; (2) `Payment`'s state machine (`mark_processing!`, `mark_failed!` with its `before_transition`/`after_transition` callbacks, the pause after repeated failures, the failure-reason comment texts, the cannot-pay and debit-card mails) is not a Node model; (3) `BankAccount#mark_deleted!`, `Credit.create_for_returned_payment_difference!` (balance plus balance transaction writes) and the `User` payout-hold fields are write paths Node does not have; (4) a Node Stripe client with the gem's retry and idempotency-key behaviour; (5) unrecorded and therefore to be refused before the first Stripe call: retired or adopted accounts, foreign-currency and KRW/HUF/TWD payouts, balances split into several payout groups, a debt reserve, the Stripe Connect (standard) seller who is completed without a payout, a second instant payout batch over the $9,999 cap, the cross-border deferred reversal (`ReverseDeferredInternalTransferJob`), and the internal transfer's three-attempt `Charge.retrieve` with `sleep(2)`. The refund and Stripe-account-creation items above are unchanged.


WS-20 sixth REST sweep (`ws-rest-sweep-6`, lane 25: `gumroad_test_25`, `gumroad_replay_25`, container `gr-ruby-c25k`, Redis 1-5, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c25k`, `ES_TEST_INDEX_PREFIX=c25k-`, `DISABLE_TEST_REDIS_ISOLATION=1`; Stripe, PayPal and every other live service untouched). Done: (1) `RichContent` description updates in Node. `rich-content-writes.ts` no longer throws for a changed description or a live page: `embedded_files_belong_to_product` and `link_hrefs_use_permitted_schemes` are checked (a description Rails would reject, or one the port cannot read, stays `NotPortedError` because no recording proves the message Rails builds) and `enqueue_license_backfill` queues `CreateLicensesForExistingCustomersWorker.perform_in(5.seconds, product.id)` after the commit when the save starts offering a license key (settled in `after_save` so nothing can throw after the commit; a version's page hands back, as it needs the version's category). The pure half lives in `rich-content-description.ts` (embedded ids in order, `reject_file_embeds`, `has_license_key_in?`, blocked href schemes, the browser's URL cleanup) with a Ruby golden (`scripts/golden/rich-content-description.rb`, 56 descriptions and 14 hrefs from the real model, `rich-content-description.golden.json`, replayed by `rich-content-description.test.ts`). Mutation check: `reject_file_embeds` recursing into groups only fails five golden cases. (2) `ProductBuild#promote!` in Node for a product with one set of pages (`models/product-build-promote.ts`, `models/product-build.ts`; `POST /v2/products/:id/builds/:id/promote` and `POST /products/:product_id/builds/:id/promote.json`): the product row lock, the superseded build's file `mark_deleted!` and its `superseded` status, the new zip as a `ProductFile` (url from the stored key, `display_name` truncated to 200 characters), the build made live, and `swap_file_embed` (the old embed leaves every page, container nodes included, an emptied group is dropped, the new embed lands on the page with the smallest position; a page is rewritten only when it changes), then `ProductBuildUnpackJob` after the commit. Rails-recorded with `parity_timeline: true`: 7 examples in `api/v2/product_builds_writes_parity_spec.rb` (first build, through the `/api` prefix, supersede with a page, two pages where only the first gets the zip, the embed inside a list, re-promoting a superseded build; replayed by `api-v2-ws17c.replay`, 29 tests) and 2 in `product_builds_promote_parity_spec.rb` (replayed by `rest-sweep-2.replay`, 11 tests selected). The order Rails took is `update product_files, update product_builds, insert product_files, update product_builds, update rich_contents..., job AnalyzeFileWorker, job ProductBuildUnpackJob`; `product-build-promote.test.ts` pins it against both fixtures. The embed's `SecureRandom.uuid` is masked on both sides by `parity/src/generated-uids.ts`; the spec's own embeds use `00000000-...` uids so they stay compared. Mutation checks: making the v2 handler hand back again fails 6 replays; creating the file before superseding the previous build fails the timeline test. Every shape the port cannot prove is a `NotPortedError` inside the lock, before the first write (rolled back, then forwarded to Rails whole): a build that is already live (the restore branch), a product with versions (per-version pages, `base_variants_product_files` joins and the page a version without one gets), a page with a `fileEmbedGroup` or a product with an alive folder archive (`generate_product_files_archives!` would create or drop zip archives and queue `UpdateProductFilesArchiveWorker`), a description the port does not read, one Rails would reject (a file of another product, a blocked link scheme), a stored build that no longer validates. Each has a "hands a ..." example (live build, versions and a folder in the v2 spec, the live build in the internal one) and a unit test asserting no write. Not recorded or ported: the variant branch of `swap_file_embed` and the archive generation (the two recordings with a folder and with versions show their writes, `product_files_archives`, `product_files_files_archives`, `base_variants_product_files` and a rich content insert, but without a timeline, so their order is unproven). The unpack job itself stays in Ruby.

Not done, with the reasons: (3) brand account creation (`User::CreateBrandAccountService`) was not started. Node does create users for sign-up (`signup-create.ts`, `Users.save`), so the user row is not the blocker; what has no recorded order is everything around it: a random Devise password, the owner membership `create_owner_membership_if_needed!` and the admin `TeamMembership`, `switch_seller_account` (the session and the flash that names the new account), and, with `use_existing_payout_setup`, the compliance info and bank account copies and `CreateStripeMerchantAccountWorker`. A recording of the order needs `parity_timeline: true` (the request makes no outbound call), and the existing `brand_accounts_parity_spec` examples only cover the refusals. It stays Rails whole. The commission file change and the balance charge in `complete` were not started: the charge needs a Stripe call recorded against WebMock stubs for the commission amount, and the order of the ledger writes around it is not in any recording. Both stay Rails whole. (4) `files` on `PUT /v2/products/:id`: read again, it runs `WithProductFiles#save_files!` for a product that already has files (reuse by url or by size and fingerprint, `modified`, deleting the files left out, subtitle files, thumbnails, the PDF stamp backfill) inside the same transaction as the rest of the update, followed by `generate_product_files_archives!` and the rich content id mappings; the update handler's order for those writes next to the product's own attributes is not recorded. Even a product with no file yet shares the update's transaction and `content_updated_at` write with the other params, so no narrower shape was split off. Still handed back whole.

The failing test sweep-5 reported (`user-link.test.ts` "refuses an update that changes a column the port does not cover") was not caused by sweep-5: it failed on `nextjs-rewrite` before it, since `85b574687` ported the v2 `description` write and put `description` in `PORTED_CHANGES`, so the update passed the guard and reached the user lookup (`undefined method 'links' for nil`). The test now changes `price_cents`, which is still outside the port.

Environment note for the next lane: the session scratchpad is shared between sibling lanes. An `env.sh` written there by this lane was overwritten by another lane's within a minute, so the first recording ran against that lane's database and Redis before it was noticed (`ALTER TABLE ... AUTO_INCREMENT = 1` on its test tables and a `flushdb` of its Redis databases; no row was written outside a rolled-back example). Keep the env file in a per-lane subdirectory and print `DATABASE_NAME` before recording. `public/vite-test` has to be copied into a fresh worktree (it is git-ignored and the request specs render the Vite manifest); `node_modules` stays a symlink.

WS-20 fifth REST sweep (`ws-rest-sweep-5`, lane 25: `gumroad_test_25`, `gumroad_replay_25`, container `gr-ruby-c25j`, Redis 1-5, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c25j`; Stripe, PayPal and every other live service untouched). Not done, with the reason found when reading the code again: (2) `ProductBuild#promote!` needs a ProductBuild model, `RichContent#update!(description:)` (the two extra description validations are still NotPortedError in `rich-content-writes.ts`), `swap_file_embed` over product and variant pages, variant `product_files` joins, archive generation and the unpack job; every one of those writes needs its own recorded order. WS-20 fourth REST sweep (`ws-rest-sweep-4`, lane 25: `gumroad_test_25`, `gumroad_replay_25`, container `gr-ruby-c25i`, Redis 1-5, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c25i`; no live service, no payment code touched). Served by Node: `GET /healthcheck` (`nextjs_healthcheck_index`, its own flag so the blue/green readiness gate can stay on Rails when `nextjs_healthcheck` is on; the handler and its three controller-spec fixtures already existed, only the `routes.json` entry was missing). `GET /profile/:username` on the third-party analytics host (`nextjs_third_party_analytics_profile`, `controllers/third-party-analytics.ts`; `third_party_analytics_profile_parity_spec`, 7 examples): the seller's alive universal snippets scoped to "all" inside the bare page, CSP off, the session skipped (`skipSession`: no session cookie, XSRF-TOKEN and the guid still written) and `cookies.clear` (a delete line for each cookie the visitor sent and for the guid this request minted, after the response's own cookies). `routes.json` gained the `analytics:` host scope (`HostScope` `analytics`, `host.thirdPartyAnalytics`). The recorder keeps one line per cookie name after sorting, so a minted guid that is written and then erased shows as whichever line sorts last, which depends on the first character of the random UUID: the replay ignores that one cookie and `third-party-analytics.test.ts` pins the pair.
