Needs `BundleProduct` (nine validations, paper trail) and the `price_cents` save path of `Link` before it can move.

Purchases export, hand-back families moved to Node one at a time, each with a recorded example (`purchases_export_parity_spec`, replayed in `rest-sweep-3.replay`): one variant or SKU per sale (the variant name unless it is "Untitled" or the default SKU, the variants price, the item price without it, the SKU's custom name or external id; the old "hands a sale with a variant" example is replaced by three); tax (the amount Gumroad or the seller collects, the label of the rate's country through the same tables as `purchase-json.ts`, "Tax Included in Price?"); affiliates (the affiliate user's `form_email`, unconfirmed address first); tips (`value_usd_cents / 100.0`, unrounded); the sale's own review (rating and message); license keys (serial of a licensed product, activation count, enabled); UTM links (the five columns) and the upsold flag. Still handed back whole: several variants on one sale (the HABTM read has no order), a variant of a tiered membership or of a licensed product (variant rich content decides the key), gifts, pre-orders, subscription charges, carts, buyer-currency and PayPal charges, a seller's own Stripe Connect account, a state or country from the IP address, a non-ASCII address (ActiveSupport transliteration), several tips, licenses or UTM links on one sale, and products with several custom fields.

Handed back after reading the Rails code, not attempted (no Rails example proves their write order, and the payment or file side cannot be recorded here): `ProductBuild#promote!` (product row lock, `ProductFile` creation with its callbacks, `swap_file_embed` over every rich content page and variant, `generate_product_files_archives!`, `ProductBuildUnpackJob` after commit; Node has no `ProductFile` create), the commission file change and the balance charge inside `complete`, and `User::CreateBrandAccountService` (a `User` with a Devise random password and confirmation mail, `TosAgreement`, owner and admin `TeamMembership`s, the copy of compliance info and bank account, the Stripe account job, then `switch_seller_account`). `parity_timeline` only writes a timeline for a request that made an outbound call, so a brand account request without a payout setup would not record the order of its inserts either. `GET /users/confirmation/new` renders Devise's gem view in the application layout (vite tags, CSP nonce), the same missing HTML shell as the `home#*` pages, and `GET /_/test/outgoing_traffic` reads one S3 object (`bucket("gumroad").object("outgoing-traffic-healthcheck.txt")`) so Pingdom can tell the Rails process still has outbound traffic: it has no recordable behaviour beyond that call and is meant to be answered by whichever stack serves production, so it stays in Rails until the cutover plan names who monitors it.

- `AlertOnStalledPostEmailBlastsJob` now removes the dead-set entry of a resumed blast: `ZREM`, then (when it removed the entry) the gem's own `delete.lua` (vendored into `unique-jobs-lua.generated.ts`, `deleteLockBang` in `unique-lock.ts`) over the digest `Job.add_digest` derives from the entry. WS-21/WS-23 payments hand-backs, seventh pass (`nextjs/ws-payments-3`, lane 23: `gumroad_test_23`/`gumroad_replay_23`, Redis 41-45, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c23k`, container `gr-ruby-c23k`). Items (2), (3 in part) and (4) of the sixth pass are paid; the instant payout is still handed back whole, and nothing was weakened.
- Done, with Ruby goldens and mutation checks. (a) The Stripe client already was a port of stripe-ruby 12.5.0's `StripeClient` (`stripe/client.ts`, `stripe-requests.golden.json`); its golden lacked the cases the instant payout needs. `generate-stripe-requests-golden.rb` now drives `Payout.create` and `Transfer.create_reversal` through four 500s, four hang-ups (the new `[:drop]` answer of `capture_server.rb`), two hang-ups then success and a 429, and `client.test.ts` replays them: four attempts under one `Idempotency-Key`, a GET carries none, a 429 is not retried, and a server that closes the socket before answering is the gem's `end of file reached`, not `Connection reset by peer` (that was a real difference, fixed in `nodeTransport`). Not pinned: Net::HTTP's own transparent retry of an idempotent request after an EOF (a GET is sent twice per Stripe attempt by the real gem; WebMock's `to_raise`, which is what the 17 recordings use, bypasses it, so the stand-in replay matches either way). (b) `models/payment.ts`: `markProcessingBang` and `markFailedBang` for a STRIPE payment with the callbacks the recordings use (`mark_balances_as_unpaid`, the cannot-pay and debit-card mail jobs on the critical queue, `add_payment_failure_reason_comment` from the generated `payment-failure-reasons.generated.json`, `pause_payouts_after_repeated_failures` with its exact `COUNT`/`MAX` statements, by bank account and by Stripe payout destination); `balance.ts` gained `fireBalanceEventBang` (`mark_processing`, `mark_paid`, `mark_unpaid`, `mark_forfeited`); `credit-creation.ts` gained `createForReturnedPaymentDifferenceBang` (the `returned_payment` branch of `Credit`'s validation and comment is no longer refused). `nextjs/scripts/golden/payment-state-machine.rb` records 27 cases from the real models (statements and their answers, Sidekiq pushes, rows); `payment-state-machine.test.ts` replays them, and fails with the 3-failure limit changed to 4 or with the callbacks moved to the wrong state. (c) `BankAccount#mark_deleted!(validate: false)`, already ported for account adoption, had no golden; recording it showed the Node `user_has_another_payout_method?` sent different statements from Rails (a column select instead of loading the user, `SELECT 1 AS present` for `SELECT 1 AS one`, a `LIMIT 1` Rails does not send on the PayPal merchant accounts read); fixed.
- Refused by `markProcessingBang`/`markFailedBang` before anything is written (`NotPortedError`/`InvalidTransition`): any processor but STRIPE (PayPal validations, `invalidate_paypal_payout_address`, the PayPal mails), every other event (`mark_completed`, `mark_cancelled`, `mark_reversed`, `mark_returned`, `mark_unclaimed`) and the generic `Payment` validations of those states. `Payment#sync_with_stripe`/`sync_with_paypal` are not ported.
- Still blocking the Node port of `POST /instant_payouts`, in order: (1) `Payouts.create_payments` and `mark_balances_processing` (seller row lock, `unpaid_balances_up_to_date`, `select_and_claim_payable_balances`, the negative-ledger hold note, the debt reserve, `StripePayoutProcessor.payout_groups`/`get_payout_details`/`destination_merchant_account`, `is_balance_payable`, the per-group `Payment.create!` with its `payments_balances` rows and `created_at` rule): no Node implementation, no golden. (2) The `user` side of the hold is only as far as `pause_payouts_after_repeated_failures`: `StripePayoutProcessor.hold_payouts_for_unaccounted_money!` and `reverse_internal_transfer_or_hold_payouts!` (user lock, `payouts_paused_internally`, the hold comment) are not ported. (3) `StripePayoutProcessor.prepare_payment_and_set_amount` (about 200 lines: the destination balance drift check, the internal transfer and its three-attempt `Charge.retrieve` with `sleep(2)`, the currency checks), `perform_payment` (the payout request, the `stripe_invalid_request_error_failure_reason` classification, the unknown-outcome path) and the reversal (`reverse_internal_transfer!`, `create_credit_for_difference_from_reversed_internal_transfer`): not ported. (4) `InstantPayoutsService` and `User#instant_payouts_supported?`/`instantly_payable_unpaid_balances` (the latter calls `Stripe::Account.retrieve_external_account`) as a write path. (5) The refuse-before-the-first-Stripe-call list of the sixth pass is unchanged and still has to be implemented with the handler: retired or adopted accounts, foreign-currency and KRW/HUF/TWD payouts, balances split into several groups, a debt reserve, the Stripe Connect (standard) seller, a second batch over the $9,999 cap, the cross-border deferred reversal, and the internal transfer's `sleep(2)` retry. (6) The mail jobs of the cannot-pay and debit-card limit failures are pinned against Ruby here (`payment-state-machine.golden.json`), but the 17 request recordings still hold no mail job (test adapter), so the request-level replay cannot check them. Because (1) to (3) are missing, `money-hand-backs.replay.test.ts` keeps all 17 failure examples and the success example as hand-backs; none was replaced.
WS-11/12 leftovers, ninth pass (lane 15: `gumroad_test_15`/`gumroad_replay_15`, Redis 56-63, container `gr-ruby-c15h`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15h`).
- Home pages: an impersonated session renders (the admin's Warden session is their own, so the page is the signed-in one; recorded after a real `/admin/impersonate`). `rackTopLevelParams` models Rack's per-key tree, so `a[b][c]` and deeper are read as the key they hang from; a path used as two kinds of value (Rack's 400), a bracket that does not close, a `[]` before the last segment and an invalid percent escape still hand back. The alert (stale confirmation link) and notice (unsubscribe) toasts are recorded. The info toast is not recordable: no controller sets `flash[:info]`. Vary stays unpinned in these fixtures because the replay ignores `response.headers.vary` (Next.js owns it); Rails records only `Origin`.
- Covers: an uploaded image whose blob has no (or only a width) analysed dimension renders (display size nil, retina variation limited to `[nil, nil]`); a dimension that is not a positive integer still hands back. oEmbed documents with well-formed comments (no `--` inside, none inside a tag) and with the four named or ASCII numeric character references in the iframe `src` render; a comment inside a tag, an unterminated one, other references and an iframe without `src` (NoMethodError in Rails) hand back. A twitter player card with a nil thumbnail or player prints the tag without `content`. Still handed back: an uploaded video, because `AssetPreview#video_poster_url` only yields a poster when ffmpeg exists on the Rails host and a recording cannot pin that.
- Mobile serializers: `Link#as_json(mobile: true)` now serves Unsplash and oEmbed covers (`presenters/mobile-preview.ts`; recorded on purchase, subscription and preorder reads). A product with an uploaded cover, or a cover that is both a photo and a document, hands back. The admin product serializer still hands back any cover.
- Not started this pass (nothing written): the TypeScript `Pages::Interpolator.interpolate_profile` still has no endpoint because every caller renders `Ai::PageSanitizer` first, which needs a byte-exact port of Loofah's libxml2 HTML4 fragment serialization; `Pages::ProfileData`, `Pages::ProductPrices` and `Interpolator.interpolate` (product half) are likewise unported.
WS-21/WS-23 payments, seventh pass: the full refund (`nextjs/ws-payments-4`, lane 28: `gumroad_test_28`/`gumroad_replay_28`, Redis 45-48 recording and 49 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c28f`, container `gr-ruby-c28f`; WebMock only, no live service, credential or money). The success path stays handed back in Rails, before any write or Stripe call, and no hand-back was weakened. What this pass added:
- Recordings (`purchases_refund_failures_parity_spec.rb`, `parity_timeline`, 25 examples, replayed in `money-hand-backs.replay`): every Stripe answer after the first call of `PUT /purchases/:id/refund`. A charge already refunded (400), insufficient balance (`balance_insufficient`), a charge Stripe cannot find (404 on the first read), a rejected request (400), a 500 (the gem sends it 4 times under one idempotency key), a dropped connection on the charge read and on the refund POST (4 attempts, one key), a 429 on the POST and on the refund read, and every read that follows the POST (refund 500, dropped, 429; charge 500; transfer 500 on a destination charge). A partial refund, the refund of the rest of a partly refunded sale, a typed amount equal to the price, a refund of an affiliate sale (full and partial, with the `AffiliatePartialRefund`), a destination charge with and without the application fee refund (transfer and destination payment reads), a sale on the seller's own Stripe Connect account (no balance write, every call with `Stripe-Account`, and the fallback to Gumroad's account when the connected one answers 403), and two refusals (amount above the price, which Node now answers itself; a disconnected Connect account, which hands back).
- What the recordings show Rails doing, which a port is held to. A refusal writes nothing. A 429 is not rescued by `refund_and_save!` (`RateLimitError`, `ChargeProcessorErrorRateLimit`), so Rails answers 500; when it comes on the refund read, Stripe has already refunded and Rails books nothing (no `Refund`, no balance, `stripe_refunded` unchanged), as it does for a 500 or a dropped connection on that read: the money has moved with no local record, and only the webhook path can reconcile it. A port that matches this must not throw after the POST; it has to produce the same 500 or the same message without a ledger write, which is a design call rather than a copy.
- Ported with Ruby goldens and mutation checks (pure, nothing writes): `stripe/stripe-charge-refund.ts` (`StripeChargeRefund#build_flow_of_funds`: plain, application-fee-account, destination with and without fee legs, `on_behalf_of`, the uncredited destination payment, the currency-mismatch `RuntimeError`, the unexpanded application fee `NoMethodError`; 20 cases from `scripts/golden/flow-of-funds.rb`) and `models/refund-ledger.ts` (`Purchase#refund!` dispatch, `build_refund`'s three builders, the refunded flags, the effective-refund sums, and the seller/affiliate split of `decrement_balance_for_refund_or_chargeback!` for a sale without a collaborator; 13 cases x 10 gross amounts x 12 typed amounts from `scripts/golden/refund-ledger.rb`). 15 mutations of the two modules (rounding, comparisons, the sums, each branch of the flow of funds) are each caught by the goldens; the one that first survived, the clamp of the Gumroad tax to what is refundable, got its own case. `purchase-refund.ts` uses `chooseRefund` for the one new refusal it serves, the amount above the price, for a USD sale with no refunds and a plain decimal; anything else with an amount still hands back.
- Why the success path is still Rails'. Not ported: (1) the writes `refund_purchase!` makes in one transaction: the `Refund` insert (`assign_product`/`assign_seller`, `sync_fee_retention_recoverable`, JSON data), `BalanceTransaction.create!` for a refund (`balance-transaction.ts` refuses it: it needs `create_issued_amount_for_seller`/`create_holding_amount_for_seller` and the `Balance` selection of the seller's merchant account and holding currency), the affiliate balance and `AffiliatePartialRefund` rows, `purchase_refund_balance`, `mark_product_purchases_as_refunded!`, the gift and subscription side effects, the `Event`, `Credit.create_for_vat_exclusive_refund!` and `create_for_refund_fee_retention!`, the audience member delete, the webhook, the license disable, and the jobs (`CustomerMailer.refund`/`partial_refund`, `LowBalanceFraudCheckWorker`, the analytics cache, Elasticsearch, `ContactingCreatorMailer.purchase_refunded`). (2) The Stripe calls Rails makes after the ledger and still unrecorded: `Refund#recover_pending_fee_retention!` after commit (`debit_stripe_account_for_refund_fee`: `Transfer` reversals with `REFUND_FEE_REVERSAL_CAPACITY_ERROR` retries, `NoRefundFeeTransferError` and the write-off, the grouped account debit for US accounts) and `reverse_excess_amount_from_stripe_transfer`/`reverse_the_transfer_made_for_dispute_win!`. The destination-charge recordings have a retained fee of zero, so none of these calls happens in them; each needs its own example with a non-zero retained fee. (3) Never recorded: PayPal and Braintree refunds, a team member refunding on a seller's behalf (the controller passes no reason, so it always answers "A reason is required"; only a team member's own sale goes through), buyer-currency (presentment) purchases and any sale not in USD, combined charges (`Charge#refund_and_save!`, `cap_to_unrefunded_amount`), a collaborator that also credits a direct affiliate, a refund after a chargeback win, subscriptions, gifts and licenses, and a refund of a sale that already has refunds (Node hands any of those back before the amount check).
- Overlap with `ws-payments-3`: nothing in the instant payout or `PayoutProcessor` was touched. The Stripe client in `server/stripe/` (`client.ts`, `resources.ts`, `charge-processor-errors.ts`) already has `Charge`, `Refund`, `Transfer` and the `with_stripe_error_handler` mapping, so no client was built here; the refund needs its retry and idempotency behaviour (4 attempts on a 500 or a dropped connection under one key, none on a 429, `Stripe-Account` on every call of a Connect refund) exactly as `ws-payments-3` leaves it, and the recordings above are the check. The only shared file is `money-hand-backs.replay.test.ts` (one entry added to `SPECS` and one test).

WS-21/WS-23 payments, eighth pass: the Stripe calls after the refund ledger (`nextjs/ws-payments-7`, lane 28: `gumroad_test_28`/`gumroad_replay_28`, container `gr-ruby-c28g`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c28g`; WebMock only, no live service, credential or money). The refund is still handed back in Rails before any write or Stripe call. What this pass added:

- Recordings (`purchases_refund_fee_retention_parity_spec.rb`, 13 examples with `parity_timeline`, replayed in `money-hand-backs.replay`): the order, headers and idempotency keys of the calls Rails makes after the ledger (the retained-fee transfer or reversal, `reverse_excess_amount_from_stripe_transfer`, the dispute-win reversal).
- Ported with a Ruby golden (`scripts/golden/refund-fee-retention.rb`, 70 cases, real classes in a rolled-back transaction with scripted Stripe) and mutation checks, none of it served: `stripe/refund-fee-collection.ts` (`debit_stripe_account_for_refund_fee`: US grouped transfer, other-country reversals with the 3-refusal cap and the capacity-error regex, source-transfer pinning, adoption of an earlier collection, concurrent writes under the row lock; a reversal list with `has_more` throws `NotPortedError`), `stripe/refund-transfer-reversals.ts` (`reverse_excess_amount_from_stripe_transfer`, `reverse_the_transfer_made_for_dispute_win!`) and `models/refund-fee-retention.ts` (the retained-fee arithmetic). Mutation results: collection 25 mutations, 0 survivors (one pattern did not match and was skipped); reversals 29 mutations, 10 survivors; retention 12 mutations, 3 survivors. The survivors are guards the golden never reaches (zero-amount and null early returns, an empty-metadata check, `every` against `some` over purchases that differ in `stripe_refunded`, the Math.abs of an existing reversal, the half-up rounding of an exact quotient, a fee currency other than USD, the Gumroad merchant-account side), so those inputs are not proven and need cases before the code is served.
- Still not ported, so the refund stays Rails': the `Refund` insert and callbacks, the refund `BalanceTransaction` and `Balance` selection, affiliate rows and the retained-fee `Credit` write, the purchase, gift, subscription and event side effects, jobs and mails, and the PayPal, Braintree and combined-charge branches. Node must never throw after the Stripe POST, so none of this is served until the whole path, including the Rails 500 after a 429, is proven.
- Overlap with `ws21-purchase-model-7`: `models/balance-transaction.ts` still refuses a refund balance transaction and was not touched; the refund code lives in separate files so the two do not conflict.


## E99 long tail: triage (`nextjs/ws-tail-1`, lane 16)

The backlog's unassigned bucket (204 sites in 69 files, 162 recorded "hands ..." examples in 28 specs) is now empty: `nextjs/scripts/backlog.ts` gained a second pattern pass (`fallback`, consulted only for a path no epic's `files` claims, so no file that already had an epic moved). Where each group went, and why it is still handed back:

- E10 (public API): every `spec/requests/api/v2/*` spec in the bucket (throttled products, links, product enable, licenses, upsells, users, files, thumbnails, sales, media destroy, marketing actions: about 100 examples). Blocked by the v2 product families already listed under E10 (files, rich content, covers); the throttled-products examples are Rack::Attack counters.
- E19 (editor, products): `links-create`, `links-controller` (publish, price check, landing embed, products search, `POST /links/:id`), `models/link.ts`, `products-mutations`, `rich-content-writes`, `product-tags`, `thumbnails`, upsells, `link-metrics`, call availabilities. Blocked by the `Link` save path (`price_cents`, `ProductFile`, rich content) and by Elasticsearch-backed search.
- E08 (integrations and S3): Discord (`join_server`/`leave_server`/`oauth_redirect`), Circle, Zoom, Google Calendar, `s3-utility`. These call the vendor and spend a single-use code; the guards before the call (blank seller id, state not JSON, no code) hand back because Rails answers them with its debug page, which has no recorded body.
- E12 (audience): marketing actions and abandoned carts (the "Available after your first payout" gate reads payout state), post send-for-purchase and post redirect, imported customers, events, test pings (calls the seller's endpoint).
- E14 (settings, users): `users-controller` (add purchase to library, deactivate, coffee page, custom HTML embed), `models/user.ts`, seller JSON guards, custom domains (a `product_id` runs `CustomDomain` validations), help-center contact (screenshot upload), feature announcements, piracy reports.
- E17 (purchases): purchase custom fields, purchase product updates, shipments, customer details, buyer products, `models/purchase.ts`.
- E01 (checkout): `purchase-pricing`, `merge-carts-service`, `models/{charge,order,credit}.ts`, the cart items count frame.
- E18 (fidelity): `number-with-delimiter`, `friendly-id`, `html-fragment`, `row-reads`, `with-filtering`, `public-handlers`, `public-data`, `secure-encrypt`, route redirects whose query Rails rebuilds with `to_query`. These are deliberate generic refusals.
- E09, E07, E16, E02: recommended products (the recommender's choice is random-seeded), widgets and embedded JavaScripts; the Instagram signed request; communities; `models/payment.ts`.

Examples flipped to served in this pass, each with a revert-check (the replay fails with the Node change reverted):

- `test_pings_parity_spec`: "answers the generic failure to a URL that is a list". A non-empty list of strings reaches `Regexp#match?` as an Array, raises `TypeError` into the action's `rescue Exception`, and answers 200 `success: false` with the generic message. Numbers and objects take the same road but have no recording, so they stay handed back.
- `custom_domain_verifications_parity_spec`: "answers that a domain sent as a list is not configured". `PublicSuffix.parse` raises on a list, `process` rescues it before any DNS lookup, and the message interpolates `Array#to_s`. Only strings whose `String#inspect` is the plain quoted text are rendered; anything else stays handed back.

Not attempted, with the reason: every other example in the bucket either needs a live vendor or the seller's endpoint (Discord, Circle, Zoom, Google Calendar, test pings with a valid URL), an Elasticsearch result order (products search), a `Link` or `ProductFile` write path Node does not have (publish, save, create), or has no recorded body to compare (Rails debug page for a missing parameter). No new Rails recording was made in this pass.


WS-21/WS-23 payments hand-backs, eighth pass (`nextjs/ws-payments-5`, lane 23: `gumroad_test_23`/`gumroad_replay_23`, Redis 41-45, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c23m`, container `gr-ruby-c23m`; Stripe only through WebMock, nothing left the machine). Slice (1) is half done and slices (2) and (3) are not started; `POST /instant_payouts` and every hand-back in `money-hand-backs.replay.test.ts` are unchanged, nothing was weakened.
- Done, with a Ruby golden and a mutation check. `nextjs/scripts/golden/payouts-create-payments.rb` records 17 cases from the real models into `models/__fixtures__/payouts-create-payments.golden.json`: `Payouts.create_payments`/`create_payment` (one and several balances, a date cut-off, a paused seller, an instant payout's `payout_type`, Gumroad-held balances joining the seller's group, a negative ledger withheld with its note and the repeat that adds none, non-positive groups released, a processor error failing the payment as `processor_unavailable`, a payment the processor already failed) with `prepare_payment_and_set_amount` stubbed to record its arguments, and the user hold (`reverse_internal_transfer_or_hold_payouts!` for a payment with no internal transfer, `hold_payouts_for_unaccounted_money!` on a paused and an unpaused seller, and the dedupe). `models/payment-hold.ts` ports the hold: `holdPayoutsForUnaccountedMoneyBang` and `reverseInternalTransferOrHoldPayoutsBang`; `payment-hold.test.ts` replays the four hold cases statement for statement (user lock, the flag and `payouts_paused_by` save with its version row, the marker dedupe query, `payment.reload`, the comment and its version row). Removing the dedupe `return` fails the repeat case.
- Not ported in the hold, refused with `NotPortedError` before any write: a payment with a `stripe_internal_transfer_id` (needs the reversal's Stripe calls), and the Ruby `rescue` of `reverse_internal_transfer_or_hold_payouts!` (re-stamping `UNREVERSED_INTERNAL_TRANSFER`, a second hold attempt, the one `ErrorNotifier` event, `reraise:`): an error in the hold propagates unchanged.
- Still blocking, in order. (1) The Node `Payouts.create_payments` and `mark_balances_processing` have goldens (above) but no port. The 26 to 39 statements per case are deterministic but include reads in an order Node has no model for: `User#unpaid_balances_up_to_date` (`balances.unpaid.where("date <= ?")`), a `merchant_accounts` row and then the `users` row per balance for `holder_of_funds`, `User#stripe_account` twice (`destination_merchant_account` and `filter_aggregate_payable_balances`), `alive_user_compliance_info` twice, `minimum_payout_amount_cents` (`stripe_rejected_payout_floor_applies?`), `active_bank_account`, the balance `lock!` plus `mark_processing!` per balance, then `Payment.create!` with the `payments_balances` inserts, a commit, and `mark_processing!` in its own transaction. Shapes to refuse before the first write: a foreign-currency balance on a Stripe-held account (`pay_out_currency?` probes `Stripe::Account.list_external_accounts` and `Stripe::Balance.retrieve`, unrecorded), a retired or adopted account (`split_retired_account_groups`), the chargeback-rate reserve (`under_reserve`), a debt reserve (`orphan_debt_reserve`), `retrying` (`RequeueTransientlyFailedPayoutsJob`), PayPal, and `MultiplePayoutGroupsError`. (2) `prepare_payment_and_set_amount`, `perform_payment`, the reversal and `InstantPayoutsService`/`instant_payouts_supported?` are untouched; the internal transfer's `sleep(2)` retry and the cross-border deferred reversal stay on the refuse list. (3) The `POST /instant_payouts` handler stays handed back whole; the 17 failure and 1 success recordings still replay as hand-backs.

WS-21/WS-23 payments hand-backs, ninth pass (`nextjs/ws-payments-6`, lane 23: `gumroad_test_23`/`gumroad_replay_23`, Redis 41-45, container `gr-ruby-c23n`; Stripe only through WebMock in the Ruby recordings, nothing left the machine; no Stripe call exists in the new code). `Payouts.create_payments` and `mark_balances_processing` are ported for the Stripe processor, in four commits, each replayed against `payouts-create-payments.golden.json` statement for statement (the golden regenerated byte-identical, so the recording is deterministic). All 17 cases are replayed: the 4 hold cases by the earlier `payment-hold.test.ts`, the other 13 by `models/payouts-create-payments.test.ts` (each in full, or up to its last claim for the claim-only tests; the file also holds 8 refusal tests).
- Done. (1) `models/payout-reads.ts`: `User#unpaid_balances_up_to_date`, `holder_of_funds` per balance (a Gumroad-held account has no user to read), `is_balance_payable`, `filter_aggregate_payable_balances`, `get_payout_details`/`destination_merchant_account`, `User#minimum_payout_amount_cents` (the `payout_threshold_cents` default is evaluated even when a value is stored, so the compliance info is read twice; `stripe_rejected_payout_floor_applies?` is memoized per instance). (2) `models/payouts.ts`: `mark_balances_processing` (seller lock, pause, per-balance lock and `mark_processing!`), `payout_groups`, the ledger check, the withheld-ledger hold (`BalanceTransaction` latest debits, the hashed incident key, one payout note per incident, the after-commit report that marks the note `notified`, the claim undone) and `Payment.create!` with its `payments_balances` rows (the payment's user is the caller's instance, so the failure path does not read it again). `createPaymentsBang`/`createPaymentBangFor` take `prepare` (the Ruby `prepare_payment_and_set_amount`) from the caller, so nothing in a request path can reach the claim until that is ported. (3) `Comment` can change a stored `json_data` (the merge under lock that Rails runs replaces a refusal), needed by the hold note.
- Refused with `NotPortedError`, from rows already read and before the first claim (the tests assert no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE was sent and `prepare` was never called): a Stripe-held balance in a currency other than its account's (`pay_out_currency?` would probe Stripe, unrecorded); a Stripe-held balance on an account retired by adoption or no longer active (`split_retired_account_groups`); an unpaid balance the processor does not claim (Connect-held money would add reads in the ledger step); a seller with Connect routing (`check_merchant_account_is_linked` or the `merchant_migration` feature); a seller with no Gumroad-managed Stripe account; a destination account not in USD (cross-border); a rejected Stripe account (the $1 floor); a legal entity outside the United States (`min_cross_border_payout_amount_usd_cents`); a non-instant payout for a seller paused by the system (may be the chargeback-rate reserve, which reads a comment); `retrying`; a payout that would split in several groups when a single one is asked for (`MultiplePayoutGroupsError`); a group that sums to nothing in a positive ledger (the release branch). The debt reserve is not reachable once foreign-currency balances are refused (`unpayable_currency_group?` is then always false), so it needs no separate refusal. Two refusals happen inside the claim transaction, after reads and claims and before any commit or Stripe call, so they roll back: an unpaid balance that appears after the claim (a concurrent write) and a hold note that is `failed` or `claimed` for over 15 minutes (the renewal is not recorded).
- Mutation checks, each failing the replay: dropping `mark_processing` fails the 5 claim and 6 payment cases; shortening the hold key hash fails the 2 note cases; reverting `Comment`'s json_data merge fails the same 2; dropping the threshold's eager default read fails the no-balance case; dropping `mark_processing!` of the payment fails 6 payment cases; the failure reason fails the processor-error case.
- Not done, unchanged. `StripePayoutProcessor.prepare_payment_and_set_amount` (destination balance drift, the internal transfer with its three-attempt `Charge.retrieve` and `sleep(2)`, currency checks, instant fee division), `perform_payment` (the payout request, `stripe_invalid_request_error_failure_reason`, the unknown-outcome path), the reversal, `InstantPayoutsService`/`User#instant_payouts_supported?`/`instantly_payable_unpaid_balances` and the `POST /instant_payouts` handler: not attempted. They need the 18 request recordings of `instant_payouts*_parity_spec.rb` replayed through new ports of about 700 lines of Ruby whose Stripe-call order is proven only for the recorded answers, and a port cannot stop after its first Stripe call; there was no room in this pass to do that safely, so the handler and every hand-back in `money-hand-backs.replay.test.ts` are unchanged. The request-level mail jobs of the failure paths are still not visible to the replays (test adapter). The balances/payment reads of a seller whose balances are split over several accounts were not recorded; extending the golden with a second account and a foreign-currency probe would let those refusals become ports.


WS-16 content delivery (`nextjs/ws16-delivery-1`, lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, `gumroad_replay_22`, container `gr-ruby-c22k`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22k`; MinIO only, no real credentials). Served by Node past the access gate, all in `url_redirect_player_pages_spec.rb` (replayed in `url-redirects.replay`): `GET /d/:id/subtitle_files/:id.vtt` (`subtitleToVtt`, a port of `SubtitleToVttService`), the `UrlRedirects/Stream` (10 examples), `UrlRedirects/Read` (8) and `UrlRedirects/Play` (7) Inertia pages, and the files of the `/d/:id` content page (9; `download-files.ts`: folders, the Kindle size flag, last media location). `GET /d/:id/play` issues the player grant itself (`issuePlayerGrant`, the inverse of `verifiedPlayerGrant`; unit-tested by round trip) and writes the visit count, seen flag and rental stamp after the props are built. The grant is random (IV) and clock-bound, so the replay masks the grant segment of `frame_url`; its decryption is not compared against a Rails-issued one beyond the shared key derivation already proved by `product-build-player-files.replay`. Real bug found: the Kindle size limit was 200 MiB in Node, Rails uses 15,500,000 bytes. Revert-checks (feature disabled) fail the new replays. Presigned S3 URLs are proved against the test MinIO with signature and date masked. Handed back before any write: Cloudflare-signed URLs (production key and distribution unprovable), a download page whose content has `product_builds` or ActiveStorage thumbnails, a commission's files, a mobile app token (`access_token`) on play. Not done: `Checkout/Returns/Pending`, `Purchases/Invoices/New`, `Purchases/Product/Show`, `Users/Coffee` props, and `Followers/Cancel` and `Followers/FromEmbedForm` (a mutation that renders an Inertia page is not supported by the harness yet). `GET /product_review_videos/:id/stream` was already served and replayed (18 examples).

WS-18 purchases 1 (lane 29: `gumroad_test_29`, `gumroad_replay_29`, container `gr-ruby-c29a`, Redis 17-20 recording and 20 replay, `ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c29a`): `POST /purchases/:id/resend_receipt` and `POST /mobile/sales/:id/resend_receipt` run in Node for a plain purchase (`purchase-resend-receipt.ts`, shared): one `SendPurchaseReceiptJob.perform_async(id, true)` and nothing else is written; a purchase with a blank email answers 404 (`Could not find receipt` on mobile), and a failed enqueue answers 503 on the web action (Rails rescues the Redis transport error; the mobile action has no rescue, so its error propagates) and is covered by `purchase-resend-receipt.test.ts` only, since a Redis outage cannot be recorded. A receipt of an order's charge (`uses_charge_receipt?`), a preorder authorization (`CustomerMailer.preorder_receipt` through ActiveJob) and a gift sender (also sends the giftee's receipt) hand back before the enqueue. Recorded in `purchases_controller_parity_spec.rb` (plain, no email, preorder authorization and gift sender, replacing the old `hands the resend to Rails` example) and `mobile_sales_actions_parity_spec.rb` (plain, no email, gift sender), replayed by `purchases-controller.replay` and `mobile-api.replay`; the mutation check (route back to the old hand-back) fails the plain and no-email examples. The `rack_attack_throttles_parity_spec` receipt example in `ws10-rest.replay` still passes. Replays that count Rack::Attack need `PARITY_RACK_ATTACK_REDIS_HOST`.

WS-18 purchases 2 (lane 29): `POST /purchases/:id/change_can_contact` and `POST /mobile/sales/:id/change_can_contact` save the purchase in Node (`models/purchase-contact-writes.ts`, `ContactPurchases`) when Node can prove what `save!` does: a successful one-off purchase with no review, no gift, no preorder and no installment plan, a product that is the seller's and not a call, an email, its three amounts, no recommender or custom fee in `json_data`, and a processor trace that passes `financial_transaction_validation` (a free purchase with none, or a priced one with a transaction id, a merchant account row, a fingerprint or PayPal order and a processor id), for a seller no newsletter cutover covers (`FeatureAnnouncementNewsletterCutover.covers?` hands back, since `fence_lifted_buyer_opt_out` and `mirror_buyer_opt_out` lock follows and write a user's preference). The value is `ActiveModel::Type::Boolean#cast` of the parameter; a blank or missing value (Rails stores NULL), a list or a hash hands back. A save that changes nothing issues no UPDATE and enqueues nothing, as in Rails. A change writes `can_contact` and `updated_at`, then enqueues `ElasticsearchIndexerWorker(update, fields: [can_contact])` and `RefreshAudienceMemberJob(email, seller_id)` (the examples are tagged `defer_audience_refresh: true`, because the spec suite runs that job inline by default and the recording would then hold its audience_members writes). A purchase that fails validation, which Rails answers with 422, hands back instead of being reproduced. Recorded in `purchases_controller_parity_spec.rb` (8 new examples: stop, resume, unchanged, `off`, priced with merchant account, and the missing value, cutover and review hand-backs) and `mobile_sales_actions_parity_spec.rb` (2 new), replayed by `purchases-controller.replay` and `mobile-api.replay`; with the Node change reverted the 5 native web examples and the 2 mobile ones fail. `PUT|PATCH /purchases/:id` stays in Rails: its response is the default `Purchase#as_json(pundit_user:)`, which is not ported, and an email change also re-indexes the subscription's current email and rebuilds two audience rows.

WS-18 purchases 1, left in Rails on purpose (lane 29): `DELETE /affiliates/:id` (E16) is the next candidate, not ported. The write is `mark_deleted!` of a `DirectAffiliate` plus `AffiliateMailer.direct_affiliate_removal(id).deliver_later`; Node could take it for an affiliate that is alive, has a basis-point value in 100..7500, no destination URL, a seller with a username and no `audience_members` row for the affiliate's address (`remove_from_audience_member_details` rewrites that row inline), and hand every other shape back. It stays handed back because the mail enqueue is the one side effect no recording proves: the request-spec test adapter never queues `MailDeliveryJob`, so the fixtures hold no mail job and the replays drop `MailDeliveryJob` from the comparison; it needs a replay that asserts the mail item itself. `PUT|PATCH /purchases/:id` (E17) stays handed back for the reason above; `cancel_preorder_by_seller` and `POST /purchases/:id/confirm` send mail or read the processor. The Rack::Attack-counting replays (`ws10-rest`, `rack-attack-routes`) need `PARITY_RACK_ATTACK_REDIS_HOST`; without it the receipt-resend throttle example fails with a 404 instead of a 429.


**Mail and export hand-backs (`nextjs/ws13-mail-1`):** The gift receiver receipt (`ReceiptPresenter` state `gift_receiver_purchase_successful`, free gifts only), `CustomerMailer#preorder_receipt` and the purchases export for gift senders and pre-orders are ported and proven against Rails recordings (receipt and customer mailer goldens, two export parity fixtures). Still handed back: (1) renewal receipts of a membership (see "E12 membership and free-trial receipts" below for the first receipt, now served); (2) E13 subscription charges and plan changes, since not every DB write, mail and job in order is covered by a recording; (3) priced, physical, rented and hidden-recipient gift receivers, a gift receiver inside an export, and seller zones whose `%Z` abbreviation comes from tzdata.

E12 membership and free-trial receipts (`nextjs/ws13-subscriptions-1`, lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, container `gr-ruby-c22o`, Redis 36 for the generator, `RECEIPT_GOLDEN_ES_INDEX=ws85-link-c22o`; no Stripe, PayPal or money call is possible in a mail render). `CustomerMailer#receipt` renders the first receipt of a membership (Purchase `is_original_subscription_purchase`, subject "You've subscribed to ...", the manage link and cadence in the footer instead of the unsubscribe link, the manage note on the item, the cadence on the product price, an upcoming-payment section) and of its free trial (`not_charged`, "Your free trial has begun!", "Today's payment" at $0, the first charge on `free_trial_ends_at`), for a dollar product whose next charge equals this one (`Subscription#current_subscription_price_cents`, so a permanent discount is served). Proven by 22 cases and 5 refusals added to `receipt-mailer.golden.json` (`generate-receipt-mailer-golden.rb`, run in production mode against the lane DB; the statements, in order, the rendered HTML and the SMTP bytes are compared; the old `receipt_membership_first_charge` refusal is now a served case). Handed back before any statement is sent: fixed-length memberships, installment plans, test subscriptions, gifted memberships, upgrades, a renewal (`is_recurring_subscription_charge`), a purchase with no price row, a product or purchase in another currency, and a next charge that differs from this one (Rails then recomputes sales tax with `SalesTaxCalculator`, which was not recorded). Not done for E13: `PUT /subscriptions/:id` (`Subscription::UpdaterService`, 836 lines: plan changes, card replacement, mandates, restarts and upgrade charges share one transaction and a processor call, and no row-only shape was recorded), subscription charges and free-trial conversions (processor calls). The golden was merged, not regenerated whole: re-running the generator rewrites auto-increment ids and Message-IDs in every older case.

WS-10 YouTube, Instagram and TikTok connect callbacks and passkey registration (`ws10-omniauth-7`, lane 15: `gumroad_test_15`, `gumroad_replay_15`, Redis 56-63, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15i`). The three social-connect callbacks run in Node whatever code, redirect URI or XHR header they carry (`omniauth-phases.ts`), recorded in `social_connect_token_exchange_parity_spec.rb` with the provider HTTP answered by WebMock and the stand-in server; the token exchange is the single-use spend, so every shape that cannot be compared (an empty `redirect_uri` is truthy in Ruby and replaces `postmessage`) was recorded first. `POST /settings/passkeys/registration_options` and `POST /settings/passkeys` run in Node for a `none` attestation with an ES256 key (`webauthn-attestation.ts`, `settings_passkeys_signed_in_spec.rb`, 55 examples replayed in `settings-mutations.replay`): the challenge lives in the Rails session cookie under `webauthn_registration_challenge`, Rails decodes Node's cookie and accepts an attestation for that challenge (`node_output_parity_spec.rb`, `WRITE_NODE_PASSKEY_REGISTRATION`), and the insert runs under the user's `FOR UPDATE` lock with the model's normalisation, default nickname, id and limit validations. Hand-backs before any write: signed attestation formats (`packed`, `tpm`, `fido-u2f`, `apple`, `android-key`, `android-safetynet`), keys that are not ES256, an attestation object that is not base64url or is truncated, authenticator data sent as CBOR text, and every shape where webauthn-ruby raises `EOFError`, which nothing rescues so Rails answers an uncomparable 500. The Stripe Connect callback stays in Rails: the code is spent in the middleware, an unrescued `Stripe::Account.retrieve` failure becomes that same uncomparable 500, and user creation and purchase attachment follow, so no recording proves the write order. The POST form of the three callbacks stays in Rails (null-session semantics, never sent by a real provider), and the existing `NotPorted` throws in `social-connect-exchange.ts` were not touched.

WS-10 float settings (ws10-settings-8; lane 30: `gumroad_test_30`, `gumroad_replay_30`, Redis 21-24 recording and 23 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c30a`, container `gr-ruby-c30a`). A JSON number written with a point or an exponent no longer hands a settings form back to Rails. `parseBody(..., floats)` reads the number literal off the source text (`JSON.parse` reviver `context.source`) and keeps it as a `RubyFloat`, which `paramsWithFloats` gives the main, third-party analytics, advanced and billing handlers; every other handler still sees plain numbers. What Rails does with a Float, each pinned by a recorded example: a string column stores `Float#to_s` (`12.5` -> `"12.5"`, `7.0` -> `"7.0"`, so a whole-number float Facebook pixel id `12345.0` fails the format check where the integer `12345` saves); an integer column truncates (`purchasing_power_parity_limit` and `max_refund_period_in_days`, `12.5` -> 12; a float past a 4-byte column hands back); `ActiveModel::Type::Boolean` has a Set of false values that holds only the Integer 0, so `0.0` casts to true (the feature announcements switch and the billing invoice switch); the feature announcements version is `Integer(value.to_s, exception: false) || -1`, so any float is -1 and the save reports the subscription as changed elsewhere; `FlagShihTzu::TRUE_VALUES.include?(1.0)` is true (Array `==`) and `0.5` is false; the JSON-data `tiktok_pixel_id` keeps a Float and `#strip` raises, the same generic failure as an Integer. A float snippet field still hands back before the first write (the permalink lookup of a float is unrecorded). The fractional-limit hand-back example became a served one; 20 new examples across `settings_main_signed_in_spec.rb` (9) and `settings_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb` (11) replay in `settings-main` (38 tests) and `settings-mutations` (154 tests). Mutation check: with `paramsWithFloats` reading plain numbers, 14 of the 20 fail (the others agree on both readings: `5.0` and `5` give the same unknown time zone, `1.0` the same set switch, a whole-number float TikTok id the same failure, and the snippet example is a hand-back either way).

WS-10 custom domains and integers past 2**53 on the settings forms (ws10-settings-8; same lane 30 as above). (1) `PUT|PATCH /settings/advanced` with a `domain` runs in Node when DNS does not point the name at Gumroad: the lookup (`customDomainPointsToGumroad`, a read) runs before the first write, and a name that does point at Gumroad hands back whole, because Rails marks it verified and Node does not port that path. The branch builds or loads the seller's alive `custom_domains` row, sets the domain, flips a verified row to unverified (`verify` with the attempts counter off), and saves once; the model gained `validate_domain_format` (the hostname regex, empty and hyphen-edged labels, PublicSuffix, `IPAddr`) and its `before_save` reset and `after_commit` `GenerateSslCertificate` push (+2 s) now apply to a new or renamed row. Rails saves in the state event and again after it; the second save writes nothing, so one save is the same net write, and the job is pushed once. A refused domain redirects with the sentence of its errors (`The custom domain is already in use.`, `x is not a valid domain name.`, the same text twice when both the format and the Gumroad-domain checks fail, which is what a test `DOMAIN` with a port does), after the ping endpoint was saved. 17 examples in `settings_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb` replay in `settings-mutations`; the old "hands a new custom domain" example is gone (it stubbed `verify`). The recording stubs `Resolv::DNS#getresources` so only Gumroad's own targets resolve; the replay's Node asks real DNS for `*.example.com` names and the offline sandbox answers no records, which is the same "not configured". Not proven: the verified outcome (a name that does point at Gumroad) is only a unit-tested hand-back (`settings-advanced-domain.test.ts`), because a replay cannot make DNS say yes; a `RecordNotUnique` rescue is not ported (no unique index on `custom_domains.domain`). (2) `parseBody(..., floats)` also keeps an integer past 2**53 as a bigint read off the source text, so the digits survive: a string column stores them digit for digit (`facebook_pixel_id` `12345678901234567890`, the ping endpoint and billing fields), the JSON-data `tiktok_pixel_id` raises on `#strip` like any Integer, a boolean flag is false, and the feature announcements flag reads as subscribed. A snippet field, a purchasing power parity limit and a feature announcements version with such an integer still hand back (Rails' `Integer(...)` and 4-byte column range are unrecorded). 8 examples (3 in `settings_main_signed_in_spec.rb`, 5 in `settings_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb`, 2 of them "hands a ..."). Mutation check: with the custom domain branch handing back every non-blank domain and the bigint read reverted, 20 of the 23 selected examples fail (the removal of a domain is unchanged code, the snippet example is a hand-back either way, and "ignores a domain made of spaces" failed on a MySQL "needs to be re-prepared" error, not on the mutation); the one failure left in a full `settings-mutations` run, `confirms with the code of the previous interval` (TOTP), is clock-phase dependent and does not touch these files. Not done in this workstream: Flipper UI and Sidekiq web (Rack engines mounted in `config/routes.rb`, no recordable request shape beyond their auth constraint) and Devise confirmation pages (no HTML shell in Node), brand account creation (ws-rest-sweep-6) and the OAuth callbacks (ws10-omniauth-7).

WS-21/WS-23 payments, ninth pass: refund mutation gaps and the seller's refund balance transaction (`nextjs/ws-payments-9`, lane 28: `gumroad_test_28`/`gumroad_replay_28`, container `gr-ruby-c28h`, Redis 45-49, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c28h`; no Stripe call exists in the new code, the Ruby goldens script Stripe). `PUT /purchases/:id/refund` is still handed back in Rails before any write or Stripe call; nothing was served.
- Mutation gaps closed. `refund-fee-retention.golden.json` grew from 70 to 90 cases. Retention now covers a fee in another currency on a partial refund, a USD fee currency with no fee amount, and Gumroad's own merchant account; reversals cover a booked reversal with a negative amount or larger than the excess, empty metadata with a blank refund id, exact-half rounding, no Gumroad tax, VAT refunded beyond the charge, a failure that only reads like a full reversal, a charge of several purchases (`every` against `some`), a reconciliation refund, a taxed purchase, refunds above the price and a purchase that is all tax. Re-run: retention 21 mutations, 0 survivors; reversals 46 mutations then the 14 earlier survivors re-run, 6 left, all equivalent mutants (a zero or null amount returns through the already-reversed comparison anyway, `<` against `<=` there for the same reason, the full-amount branch of the canonical figure computes the same number, a null refund id never equals a string in JS).
- Real divergence found and fixed: `Stripe::StripeObject` metadata is never `blank?`, even empty, so Ruby matches an empty metadata against a blank refund id; `externalRefundReversalFor` treated empty metadata as no match.
- New: `models/refund-seller-balance.ts` (`BalanceTransaction.create!(refund:)`, `update_balance!`, the refund branch of `find_or_create_balance`, `purchase_refund_balance_id` and the purchase save), 13-case golden `scripts/golden/refund-seller-balance.rb` replayed statement for statement and job for job (`refund-seller-balance.test.ts`); a buyer-currency presentment is refused before the first write. 17 mutations, 1 survivor (the immutability guard, unreachable on this path). Overlap with `ws21-purchase-model-8`: `models/balance-transaction.ts` was NOT edited; the refund file defines a second model over `balance_transactions` and copies `update_balance!`, so the two should be folded together once both settle.
- Still Rails', so the refund stays handed back: the `Refund` insert and callbacks (`Refunds` refuses every save), affiliate balance and `AffiliatePartialRefund` rows, the collaborator path, the retained-fee `Credit` write and its balance selection (`findOrCreateBalance` still refuses a fee-retention credit; the Ruby branch is just `unpaid_balances.first`), the purchase/gift/subscription/event side effects, `Credit.create_for_vat_exclusive_refund!`, the webhook, jobs and mails, the Rails 500 after a 429, PayPal, Braintree and combined charges.

WS-21/WS-23 payments hand-backs, tenth pass (`nextjs/ws-payments-8`, lane 23: `gumroad_test_23`/`gumroad_replay_23`, Redis 41-45, container `gr-ruby-c23p`; Stripe only through WebMock in the Ruby recordings, the Node replays use a scripted transport; nothing left the machine). Three slices, each replayed against a golden from the real classes (`scripts/golden/payouts-reversal.rb`, `payouts-prepare.rb`, `payouts-perform.rb`), statement for statement and with the number of statements before each Stripe call asserted.
- Done. (1) `models/payout-reversal.ts`: `reverse_internal_transfer_or_defer!`, `reverse_internal_transfer!` (an already-reversed transfer still owes its difference credit), the sibling check and the difference credit. (2) `models/payout-prepare.ts`: `prepare_payment_and_set_amount` (destination balance drift, the internal transfer, the three-attempt `Charge.retrieve` with its injectable `sleep(2)`, instant fee `floor(amount*100/103)`, HUF/TWD and KRW rounding, `build_metadata_large_list`). (3) `models/payout-perform.ts`: `process_payments` and `perform_payment` for a bank payout on a Gumroad-managed account: the payout request, `arrival_date`, the instant `gumroad_fee_cents`, every `rescue` branch with `stripe_invalid_request_error_failure_reason`, the unknown-outcome path after the request, `mark_deleted!` of a bank account Stripe cannot find before the reversal, and the reversal re-raised. Supporting ports: `BankAccount` validates its user (the hook reuses the loaded association), `BalanceTransaction#find_or_create_balance` dates a credit for a returned payment from that payment oldest balance, `payment-hold.ts` reloads a changed user as Rails does.
- Refused with `NotPortedError` before any write or Stripe call: a retired or inactive source account, a Stripe Connect account, a payout in a currency other than its account, a payment naming no bank account, a payable-currency probe, a cross-border sibling payout.
- Mutation checks, each failing the replay: dropping `mark_deleted!` (7 cases), swapping the unknown-outcome and unavailable reasons (2), dropping `reraise` (1), dropping the instant fee (1), ordering the returned payment balances the other way (1).
- Not done, unchanged. `InstantPayoutsService`, `User#instant_payouts_supported?` (Stripe reads), `instantly_payable_unpaid_balances` and the `POST /instant_payouts` handler: `createInstantPayout` keeps handing the whole request back to Rails before any write or Stripe call, because the 18 request recordings are not yet replayed through the Node handler and a partial flip could throw after a Stripe POST. Pre-existing failure, not touched: `user-link.test.ts` "refuses an update that changes a column the port does not cover" fails because `Links.update` now runs validations first, which read the user, and the test mock returns none (`undefined method links for nil`); ws-rest-sweep-6 owns it.

WS-18 affiliates 1 (`nextjs/ws18-affiliates-1`, lane 29: `gumroad_test_29`, `gumroad_replay_29`, container `gr-ruby-c29b`, Redis 17-20 recording and 19 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c29b`). The mail-enqueue gap that ws18-purchases-1 reported is closed: an example tagged `parity_mail_jobs: true` runs with the Sidekiq ActiveJob adapter, so `deliver_later` reaches the recorder's push hook and the fixture holds the `MailDeliveryJob` (mailer, method, `deliver_now`, arguments, queue), and `parity/src/active-job.ts` drains Node's BullMQ items, `queue:*` lists and `schedule`, unwraps the JobWrapper and sorts, so a replay compares the mail like any job instead of dropping it. `DELETE /affiliates/:id` runs in Node (`models/direct-affiliate-removal.ts`, `affiliates-mutations.ts`; flag `nextjs_affiliates_guards`): `mark_deleted!` of the DirectAffiliate, the removal of its entries from the seller's audience member (the row is destroyed and its Elasticsearch `delete` job queued four seconds out), then `AffiliateMailer.direct_affiliate_removal(id).deliver_later` on the `default` queue, then the flash redirect to `/affiliates` (303). Nine examples in `affiliates_destroy_parity_spec.rb` (`parity_timeline`, `parity_mail_jobs`) replay in `affiliates-destroy.replay` (11 tests); with the mailer method changed in Node four fail. Handed back before any write (`hands a ...` examples): an affiliate that is already deleted (Rails moves `deleted_at` and mails again), a fee outside 1%..75% (Rails answers 422), a destination URL, a seller with no username, and an audience member that stays valid after the affiliate leaves it (an affiliate who is also a customer). The old "hands the removal to Rails" guard example is replaced by these. Not started in this lane: `#create`/`#update` that save, the posts-subscription actions, and the rest of the E16 shapes (collaborators, wishlists, comments, reviews); the replay does not compare the statement/job order of `timeline`.

WS-18 affiliates 1, second slice: `DELETE /products/affiliated/:id` (an affiliate ending their own direct affiliation, flag `nextjs_products_affiliated_destroy_guards`) runs in Node: `assertAffiliatedSupported` first (the cached earnings sum hands back before any write), then the conditional `UPDATE affiliates SET deleted_at, updated_at, flags = flags | 8 WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` (no callbacks or validations, so the audience member is untouched, as in Rails), `AffiliateMailer.direct_affiliate_removal_by_affiliate_user(id).deliver_later` on the `default` queue, and the page props. Five examples in `products_affiliated_removal_parity_spec.rb` (the affiliate, an admin team member, one of two affiliations, a row that no longer validates, an account enrolled as a global affiliate) replay in `products-affiliated-removal.replay` (7 tests); with the removed_at_request bit dropped from the UPDATE five fail. The two former "hands a removal" examples of `products_affiliated_destroy_parity_spec` are gone (`ws12o.replay` 47 tests pass). Not recorded: a lost race (the UPDATE touching zero rows 404s in Node, unreproduced because it needs two concurrent requests) and an uncached global-affiliate earnings sum, which `assertAffiliatedSupported` still hands back whole.

WS-21/WS-22 processors, first pass (`nextjs/ws22-processors-1`, lane 16: `gumroad_test_16`, `gumroad_replay_16`, container `gr-ruby-c16p`, Redis 31-34 recording, replay and jobs tests on 33, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c16p`; Stripe only through WebMock, the golden's own key or the stand-in, nothing reached a live service). Slice 1 (E04): `spec/requests/settings_payments_stripe_account_failures_parity_spec.rb` records six `hands a save ...` examples with `parity_timeline: true` over the cassette of the success example: a 400 rejection, a rejection naming the postal code, a 429, a 500 and a dropped connection on every attempt, and a dropped first attempt followed by a successful retry. What they pin for a port: the `tos_agreements`, `users`, `merchant_accounts` insert and pending-creation `comments` insert all precede the first `POST /v1/accounts`; the gem makes four attempts under one idempotency key for a 5xx or a dropped connection (the spec asserts the keys are equal; the fixture normalizes them to `<uuid>`) and one attempt for a 429; after the last attempt Rails soft-deletes the merchant account row, updates the note (which carries `stripe_account_idempotency_key`) and enqueues `RetryStripeRejectedPayoutSetupForSellerJob` on `low` for a no-verdict failure, and writes two notes and no job for a rejection; a retry that succeeds writes the account, the bank account and a `CheckPaymentAddressWorker` job. The replay (`money-hand-backs.replay.test.ts`) asserts each example reaches Rails whole with no Stripe call and no write from Node, and a recorded-order test holds the call counts, key reuse and write order (mutation: changing the 429 count from 1 to 4 fails it). Not done and why: no payload builder (`account_hash`, `person_hash`, `bank_account_hash`, the passphrase-decrypted bank number), `UpdateUserComplianceInfo` or `UpdatePayoutMethod` was ported, so Node still does not call Stripe for the creation; a failure after the account was created (`cleanup_failed_merchant_account`, `Stripe::Account.delete`) could not be recorded because the only reachable trigger (a created account without an external account) raises a `NoMethodError` that escapes the request and leaves no response; the person-creation, guardian, Singapore alias and update_account branches have no recording at all.
Slice 2 (E05): `HandleStripeEventWorker` serves an `account.application.deauthorized` event of a connected account (`models/stripe-account-deauthorized.ts`, golden `stripe-account-deauthorized.golden.json` from `scripts/golden/stripe-account-deauthorized.rb`, 15 cases, replayed in `stripe-account-deauthorized.test.ts`; the cases that enqueue the recommendability refresh need `JOBS_REDIS_URL`). It is the one Stripe event whose handler makes no Stripe call: the last alive account with the Stripe id is found, `delete_charge_processor_account!` runs as its two saves (a Gumroad-managed account loses its `meta`), the seller is read for `merchant_migration_enabled?` and a `MerchantRegistrationMailer#account_deauthorized_to_user` job goes to `critical` (args `[user_id, "stripe"]`). `user_id` wins over `account` unless it is blank. It also fixed `MerchantAccount#user_has_another_payout_method?`, whose Node statements (a `payment_address` column read, `SELECT 1 AS present`) had never been compared to Rails: Rails loads the user row, then `active_bank_account` (`ORDER BY id ASC LIMIT 1`) only when the payment address is blank. Handed back before the first write: an account or user id that is not a String, a type that only starts with the deauthorization, an account whose `json_data` is NULL or not a Hash (Rails rewrites it to `{}` in the first save), a seller on the `merchant_migration` Flipper feature (its rules read the compliance country after the writes) and an account that fails validation. An account whose seller row is missing fails after the delete with Rails' `undefined method 'merchant_migration_enabled?' for nil`. Mutations: sending the mail on `default` fails 8 of 18 tests, resetting `meta` on Connect accounts instead of the managed ones fails 11. Every other event (`account.updated`, `capability.updated`, `payout.*`, `charge.*`, refunds, payment intents, seller plan checkout and subscriptions) and `HandlePaypalEventWorker` (IPN verification posts to PayPal, then the charge and payout processors) still hand back before any write; the signature checks and enqueues of the receivers were already recorded in `foreign_webhooks_parity_spec`. E06 (TaxJar and VAT validators) was not started.

WS-13 audience 1 (`ws13-audience-1`, lane 29: `gumroad_test_29`, `gumroad_replay_29`, container `gr-ruby-c29c`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c29c`). Served: `GET|POST /purchases/export` with `in_seller_time_zone` (`nextjs_purchases_export`). Rails reads `start_time` as `Date.parse(s).in_time_zone(zone).beginning_of_day` and `end_time` as the next day's local midnight (`created_before` is exclusive), and `PurchaseSearchService` writes both with `iso8601`, offset included, so the same strings land in the `SalesExport.query` YAML of an asynchronous export; Node reuses `startOfDayIso8601` (`rails-time-zone.ts`) and `ZonedInstant`. Proof: `scripts/golden/purchase-export-time-zone.rb` (every `ActiveSupport::TimeZone`, 21 dates, including midnights a DST change skips) against `sales-export.test.ts`, and 6 recordings in `purchases_export_parity_spec.rb` (Pacific days, one-sided range, London summer time, Brasilia 2018-11-04, the asynchronous YAML query, and the whitespace-only flag handed back). Mutation check: ignoring the seller's zone in `salesExportSearchOptions` fails 5 replay examples. Still handed back: a seller whose `timezone` is not an `ActiveSupport::TimeZone::MAPPING` name (Rails reads it as UTC, but a user with such a zone breaks the Rails app itself, so the shape cannot be recorded and only the guard exists) and an `in_seller_time_zone` that is not a plain word (whitespace is blank in Rails and stays UTC days; recorded as `hands a whitespace-only in_seller_time_zone`). Hand-backs of the same spec that stay, by design: several variants and a licensed product's variant (`variant_attributes` has no order of its own), a non-ASCII address (`pseudo_transliterate`), a membership charge. Posts and workflow emails whose files have a thumbnail variant Rails has not processed stay handed back (`thumbnails.ts` already serves processed variants): producing the variant is ImageMagick work with an S3 write, which a recording cannot prove. Not done: the rest of E12; memberships, free trials and subscription charges were excluded by the brief. Environment note: lane 29's databases were dropped by another session mid-run and recreated (`db:schema:load`; replay schema copied from `gumroad_replay_30`).

WS-16 pages (`nextjs/ws16-pages-2`, lane 22: `gumroad_test_22`, `gumroad_replay_22`, container `gr-ruby-c22m`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c22m`). `Followers/Cancel` is served: `GET /follow/:id/cancel` is now `"inertia": true, "jsonMutation": true`, so a JSON request still reaches the mutation handler and an HTML or X-Inertia one reaches the page, whose filters (`page-props/audience/follower-cancel.ts`) run `fetch_follower` (404) and the opt-out write before the render, like `UrlRedirects/Play` counts its visit. This is how a GET that renders an Inertia page after a write is served without teaching the harness anything new; 6 examples in `followers_cancel_and_new_spec.rb` (the old "hands a cancel page request" example became the served one; a newsletter cutover hands back before the write) replay in `audience-next.replay` (44 tests), and with the route put back to `"mutation": true` 5 of them fail. A lock wait timeout, an invalid follow and a cutover hand back before the write. Still open: `Followers/FromEmbedForm`, which is a POST (`/follow_from_embed_form`, throttled by Rack::Attack): App Router pages answer GET only, so a mutation that renders an Inertia page has no path in the proxy yet.

`Users/Coffee` is served (`seller:/coffee`, `"inertia": true`, flag `nextjs_users_profile_routes`, which used to route `#coffee`'s receipt redirect and 404 through the mutation handler; that handler is gone and the page filters answer both). The filters (`coffeeFilters` in `page-props/storefront/profile-pages.ts`) run the receipt-notice redirect, the 404 for a missing or archived coffee product, build the props (`productProps` plus `creatorProfile`, with the seller-subdomain `seller_custom_domain_url` that `UserCustomDomainRequestService.valid?` yields, a trailing-slash `profile_url` the first replay caught) and turn a `NotPortedError` into a hand-back before the render. 6 coffee examples in `users_controller_parity_spec.rb` replay in `users-controller.replay` (45 tests); with the route put back to `"mutation": true` 5 fail. Handed back: a signed-in visitor, a coffee product with custom HTML (the landing page document), and any of `wanted`, `native`, `layout`, `quantity`, `code`, `purchase_id`, `purchase_email_digest`, `offer_code`, `debug`, `currency` in the query.

`Purchases/Product/Show` is served for a narrow shape (`page-props/buyer/purchase-product.ts`; `GET /purchases/:purchase_id/product` is now `"inertia": true`, flag `nextjs_purchase_product_guards`, and the old mutation handler that only answered the 404 is gone): a signed-out visitor, a product with no `versions` row at or after the purchase's `created_at` (so PaperTrail's `version_at` returns the live row), no asset previews, variant categories or SKUs, and a purchase without a `purchase_refund_policies` row. The props are `productProps` plus the presenter's overrides (`is_compliance_blocked`, `has_third_party_analytics`, `ppp_details`, `can_edit`, `refund_policy`, `discount_code`, `purchase`) and the seller's `custom_styles`; the page renders client-only because `Product` reads `document` while rendering. Real finding: rspec disables PaperTrail unless an example says `versioning: true`, so the first "edited after the purchase" recording showed the renamed product; it is tagged now and Rails answers with the name from the version. 4 examples in `purchases_product_parity_spec.rb` replay in `purchase-pages-guards.replay` (19 tests); with the route put back to `"mutation"` 3 fail. Handed back before the page renders: a signed-in visitor, a product changed after the purchase, covers, variants, SKUs or a refund policy to rebuild as of the purchase, and the query parameters the product presenter reads.

`Purchases/Invoices/New` is served for a narrow shape (`page-props/buyer/invoice-form.ts`; `GET /purchases/:purchase_id/invoice/new` is now `"inertia": true`, flag `nextjs_purchase_invoices`, and the old mutation handler that answered the refusals is gone; the 404, the noindex header and the email confirmation now run as page filters): a signed-out visitor and a successful USD purchase with no tax, shipping, gumroad tax, refund, presentment, sales tax info, order, subscription or preorder row, no flag outside was_product_recommended, was_zipcode_check_performed and was_discover_fee_charged, a product that is not physical, a buyer address with a state, and a buyer in a country where `SupplierInfo` prints no Gumroad tax registration. The props are `form_data`, `form_metadata` (supplier, creator and invoice blocks, `Compliance::Countries.for_select`, `BusinessIdLabels`), `payment_id` and an empty `payments`. 3 rendering and 4 hand-back examples were added to `purchase_invoice_form_parity_spec.rb` (the two old "hands the form ... to Rails" examples became rendering ones) and replay in `purchase-pages-guards.replay` (24 tests); with the route put back to `"mutation"` 9 fail. Handed back before the page renders: a signed-in visitor (the form is prefilled from the buyer's saved billing details), tax, a refund, a buyer in the EU, the UK, Canada or another country with a Gumroad registration, a business VAT id, a charge or subscription, a gift, and a purchase priced in another currency. The `POST` that creates the PDF is unchanged and still hands back after its guards.

`Checkout/Returns/Pending` stays in Rails: it finalizes a Stripe payment intent, which cannot be replayed without Stripe. `Followers/FromEmbedForm` stays in Rails: it is a `POST` that renders an Inertia page, and the App Router only answers `GET`, so no route kind carries it; the harness change needed is a mutation result that names a page to render.

Re-checked on `nextjs/ws11-pages-2`: both still stay in Rails. `Checkout/Returns/Pending` renders only after `finalize_client_confirmed_order` reads a Stripe intent, and `Followers/FromEmbedForm` is the HTML answer of a `POST` (a plain form submit gets a full document, which the mutation harness has no way to render). The `routes.json` entries that matched no Rails route were matcher misses, not stale: `.smil` is a route format, `(/*path)` is two entries, and a param constrained to `/.*/` is a `*glob` in routes.json. `remaining-work.ts` now handles all three (two routes move to native); `/api/health` is the Node process probe with no Rails twin and is exempt.

WS-21/WS-23 payments, eleventh pass: the Refund row, the retained-fee and VAT credits, and the folded balance transaction (`nextjs/ws-payments-11`, lane 28: `gumroad_test_28`/`gumroad_replay_28`, container `gr-ruby-c28j`, Redis 45-49, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c28j`; no Stripe call exists in any new code, the Ruby goldens script Stripe or never reach it). `PUT /purchases/:id/refund` is still handed back in Rails before any write or Stripe call; nothing was served, no hand-back was weakened, and `money-hand-backs.replay` (76 examples, the 25 failure and 13 fee-retention refund recordings among them) passes unchanged.
- Done, each with a Ruby golden from the real classes replayed statement for statement and job for job, and mutation checks that fail when reverted. (1) `models/refund.ts`: `Refunds` no longer refuses saves. `assign_product`/`assign_seller` (on create), the three required belongs_to, `validates_uniqueness_of :processor_refund_id, scope: :link_id, allow_blank: true`, JsonData's `merge_json_data_with_current_row` (first, because the concern is included first) and `sync_fee_retention_recoverable`; `assignRefundAttribute` for the columns, the `is_for_fraud` flag and the `attr_json_data_accessor` keys; `appendRefund` is `purchase.refunds << refund` (an invalid refund rolls the transaction back, swallowed, and answers nil: nothing is raised). `scripts/golden/refund-write.rb`, 14 cases (plain, fraud/note/funded, a buyer-currency snapshot keeping its json_data key order, a taken processor refund id on the same product and on another, blank ids, the retained fee then the pending flag on the refund just inserted, the attempts cap at 168, a written-off refund, a save that changes nothing, and another request's json_data keys surviving a stale save); 7 mutations of `refund.ts`, 0 survivors. (2) The fold: `balance-transaction.ts` now books a refund (the `purchase ^ refund ^ credit` parity check in Ruby's order, the refund branch of `find_or_create_balance`, a new balance dated the day of the refund) and `refund-seller-balance.ts` lost its second model and its copy of `update_balance!`; it calls `createBalanceTransactionBang({refund})`. Both behaviours are proven by the goldens that already existed: `purchase-seller-balance` (14), `refund-seller-balance` (13), `money-jobs`, the three payout goldens and `payment-state-machine`, all green after the move. 6 mutations of the refund branch: 5 killed; the survivor is the `RecordInvalid`/`RecordNotUnique` race rescue after `Balance.create!`, which a single-connection golden cannot reach (it was equally unproven in the purchase branch). (3) `Credit` accepts a `refund` (with the PayPal Connect VAT refund comment author) and a `fee_retention_refund` (add_comment returns early) as the entity that justifies it, and the fee-retention credit's balance is `unpaid_balances.first` (`findOrCreateBalance` no longer refuses it). (4) `models/refund-fee-retention-credit.ts`: `Credit.create_for_refund_fee_retention!` for both branches (a processor-fee debit on the sale's own account with the retained fee saved on the refund and `fee_retention_pending` for an account managed for the seller, including a holding amount in CAD; the application-fee credit of a Stripe Connect seller on Gumroad's account, from the tax and from the affiliate share of a partial refund). `scripts/golden/refund-fee-retention-credit.rb`, 13 cases; 8 mutations, 0 survivors. The Stripe collection stays `Refund#recover_pending_fee_retention!`, after commit, and is not here. (5) `models/refund-vat-exclusive-credit.ts`: `Credit.create_for_vat_exclusive_refund!` for a Stripe sale (the figure with Ruby's float arithmetic and half-away rounding, the comment, the balance transaction). `scripts/golden/refund-vat-exclusive-credit.rb`, 7 cases; 8 mutations, 0 survivors. A Braintree sale throws `NotPortedError` before any write.
- Two traps the goldens found. The purchase factories' save recomputes `fee_cents` and resets `affiliate_credit_cents`, so a first draft of the retention golden never exercised the affiliate share until the figures were written with `update_columns` after the sale; a golden that does not show the amount it claims to test is not a golden. And the Ruby cases run with the query cache off (a model method outside an executor), so a Node replay must wrap the call in `uncached(...)`: with the executor's cache on, a repeated `SELECT` after a no-op `Balance` save is answered from the cache and one statement goes missing.
- Still not ported, so the refund stays handed back. The `refund_purchase!` body itself (`reload.lock!`, the flags and `build_refund` call order, the presentment derivation, `refunds << refund` with its attributes from the Stripe refund), the affiliate balance and `AffiliatePartialRefund` rows and the collaborator path, `mark_giftee_purchase_as_refunded`, `subscription.cancel_immediately_if_pending_cancellation!`, `mark_product_purchases_as_refunded!`, `debit_processor_fee_from_merchant_account!`, the dispute-win reversal call, the `Event` and the audience member delete, `send_refunded_notification_webhook`, `CustomerMailer.refund`/`partial_refund` (critical queue), `update_creator_analytics_cache`, `send_to_elasticsearch("index")`, `enqueue_update_sales_related_products_infos_job`, `LowBalanceFraudCheckWorker`, `disable_attached_license_if_fully_refunded!`, `ContactingCreatorMailer.purchase_refunded`, the PayPal and Braintree refunds, combined charges, and the Rails 500 after a Stripe 429 (the handler would have to answer the same 500 without a ledger write, a design call recorded above in the seventh pass). The three credit and row writers above are not called from any route, job or the handler; they are proven in isolation only, so a route served today would still need every item in this list before it could be flipped.

WS-11/12 custom HTML pages (`nextjs/ws11-12-port-10`, lane 25: `gumroad_test_25`/`gumroad_replay_25`, Redis 1-4 recording and 5 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c25m`, container `gr-ruby-c25m`). This corrects the earlier claim that these pages "need a byte-exact port" of the sanitizer: `Ai::PageSanitizer` is ported on the libxml2 fragment port (`ai/page-sanitizer.ts`, 1,129 goldens from the real class through `nextjs/scripts/golden/ai-page-sanitizer.rb`) and refuses, handing back before any write, what it cannot prove: nesting the parser refuses, attributes outside the allowlist and `xml:` namespaced attributes. `Pages::Interpolator` (`interpolateProduct` without prices), `Pages::ProfileData` for a seller who lists nothing (`pages-profile-data.ts`) and the sandboxed document (`controllers/custom-html-document.ts`, constants evaluated from Rails into `custom-html-document.generated.json`, goldens from `nextjs/scripts/golden/custom-html-document.rb`) are ported too. `POST /internal/agent/custom_html_preview` now stages the preview in Node (`agent-preview-render.ts`; Redis key, index list, 24h TTL and cap of 20 as in Rails) for `update_user_custom_html` and for `edit_user_custom_html`, with and without the scroll marker. The replay (`agent-custom-html-previews.replay`, 48 tests) replays the POST, ignores the fresh token, and compares the staged document byte for byte with the one Rails staged, plus the index entry and the TTL; the `hands a ...` examples also assert that no preview key was written. Mutation check: renaming the `gumroad-data` script id and changing `posts_total` makes 11 of the 48 replay tests fail.
