Still handed back, on purpose: a page that references prices (`Pages::ProductPrices.build`), a seller who lists a live product or a published post or has the reputation summary on (`ProfileData` needs formatted prices, cover URLs and cache keys), a page that sanitizes to nothing (`Pages::DefaultProfileDocument`), a guarded undo, markup the sanitizer port refuses, a script URL the port cannot read, and a Tailwind manifest or stylesheet it cannot prove. Since almost every real store-agent seller has a live product, most previews still reach Rails; the Node path serves sellers whose earning product is deleted. NOT done: the profile pages themselves (`users#landing_iframe_content`, `user_pages`, the products and posts bridges, `ProductPrices.build`, `ProfileData` with products and posts); they stay in Rails. The recording needed a stub `public/vite-test/.vite/manifest.json` (twelve entrypoints; git-ignored) because the lane has no Vite build and the CSRF fetch renders a help center layout. `pages-profile-data.ts` now counts only `alive` links (not deleted, banned or purchase-disabled), which matches the Ruby scope.

WS-20 paid purchase, charge create (`nextjs/ws20-charge-create-1`, lane 24; Node-only replays, no container, database, Redis or Elasticsearch was created; Stripe only through the replay stand-in and recordings, no live service or credential).
- Decision: paid `POST /orders` stays handed back whole. It does not move to Node until the whole shape, including the lost-confirm outcome, is proven end to end. That outcome is the one Rails has today: the purchase fails with `stripe_unavailable` although Stripe may have charged the card, and the intent id is stored nowhere. Node returns it as `lostIntentId` on the error (an addition, Rails drops it), but nothing persists it yet, so switching would reproduce a possible charge with no record.
- Ported, each replayed against `purchase-paid-order.golden.json` (18 cases) with mutation checks (14 of 14 killed):
  - Stripe client timeline (`stripe/paid-order-timeline.test.ts`, 22 tests): the existing `StripeClient` (4 attempts under one idempotency key on 5xx or a dropped connection, 1 on 429) produces the recorded method, URL, body, headers and attempt counts for every case.
  - `models/charge-create-service.ts` (39 tests): `Charge::CreateService` for a card on the platform account, statement by statement (SQL, jobs, HTTP) for all 18 cases, plus declined, lost-answer and `requires_action` variants. `models/stripe-payment-intent-create.ts` (8 tests): create, confirm when `requires_confirmation`, charge read with the recorded expansions.
  - `models/stripe-chargeable-payment-method.ts` (10 tests): the payment method GET and the card facts Rails stores on the purchase.
- Deviations: the Flipper `buyer_currency_charging` read is hoisted before the first write (Ruby reads it later). After the create, an intent of a shape Node does not read returns `chargeUnreadable` instead of throwing. The settlement-mismatch learned marker is not written. The 429 and invalid-request error mappings are read off the Ruby, not recorded.
- Known post-call refusal: on a definite failure with no charge, a found buyer-currency presentment row throws `NotPortedError` after the Stripe call. Unreachable without the (refused) presentment path, but it is the one place the "refuse before any call" rule does not hold.
- Refused before any write or call: seller's own or destination Stripe account (Connect), UPI, Indian card and mandates, off-session and card-saving charges, FX quote and buyer-currency presentment, saved card (`credit_card_id`), resumed setup, explicit idempotency key.
- Not done: `save_charge_data` and `confirm_charge_intent!` (they live in `Order::ChargeService` and `Order::ConfirmService`, outside the recorded `Charge::CreateService` span); the late confirmation; the TaxJar step; the `/finalize` lane; two-seller carts; the buyer-currency presentment path; persisting the lost intent id. Not run: the Ruby golden re-record, the full parity suite, the typed root typecheck (two pre-existing tsc errors in `purchase-paid-order.golden.test.ts` and `stripe-account-deauthorized.ts`, files not touched here).

WS-17 mobile library and purchase JSON (ws17-mobile-5; lane 27k: `gumroad_test_27` and `gumroad_replay_27`, Redis 11-14 recording and 17 replay, rack-attack 16, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c27k` and `ES_TEST_INDEX_PREFIX=c27k-`, container `gr-ruby-c27k`, `DISABLE_TEST_REDIS_ISOLATION=1`). Five slices, one commit each, recorded in `mobile_purchases_sales_parity_spec.rb` and `mobile_library_writes_parity_spec.rb` (new examples only; the older fixtures were kept) and replayed by `mobile-api.replay.test.ts`. (1) Refunds and reviews: `amountRefundedCentsByPurchase` sums `refunds.effective` (a failed or canceled refund counts unless `balance_reversed_on_failure` is true; a NULL amount counts as zero) and `ratingByPurchase` reads the purchase's own review (a NULL rating is null); a refund whose `json_data` is not a hash or whose flag is not a boolean hands back, because SQL `->>` and Ruby truthiness disagree on a string. Mutation: with the refund sum and the rating forced to 0 and null, 5 of the 6 examples fail (the null-rating one passes either way). (2) Preorders: a purchase with a preorder and no url redirect of its own takes `purchase_id`, `purchased_at`, `user_id`, `is_archived` and the product updates from the preorder's last purchase when it is `successful` (no `preorder_data`), else from its first (the authorization) with `preorder_data { external_id, release_at }`, and has no `custom_delivery_url` either way; the archive and delete writes are recorded with `parity_timeline`. A preorder whose charge purchase has its own url redirect, or whose seller has a post, hands back. Mutation: swapping the source purchase fails 3 examples. (3) Library listing and search: `Purchase.for_mobile_listing` runs in SQL (the flag bits and `NOT_RECURRING_CHARGE` shared with the offer code port), Pagy metadata is rebuilt, the rows go through the same builder as `purchase_attributes` with the product, creator and refund reads shared across the page, `search` leaves `product_updates_data` out; q is `LIKE` with `sanitize_sql_like`, `seller` resolves external ids first, `archived` casts with ActiveModel's false words, `purchase_ids` decodes one id, an unknown `order` falls back to `id DESC`. A page past the last (Rails answers 400), `items` below 1, an array parameter, a membership write, a gift, a post to show and a search whose result spans several sellers hand back (the last because `sellers_from_purchases` groups by seller under `ORDER BY created_at`, a column outside the group, so MySQL's group order is not a thing to copy). Mutation: without the `is_deleted_by_buyer` filter, without the LIKE escaping and without the several-sellers hand-back, 3 examples fail (the LIKE example needed a product named `Gamma 1009x` before it caught it). (4) Media locations: `latestLocationsByFile` keeps the row with the latest `consumed_at` per file (a row without one never joins; two rows tied on it hand back, since `index_by` keeps whichever the join returned last), a location is kept only in a unit the file takes (seconds for audio and video, page_number or percentage for EPUB, page_number for PDF), and an EPUB percentage is read through its CFI spine step (`epubcfi(/6/8[...]!` is page 4; an odd step is dropped; the last page steps back one unless the book is finished, a finished book reports its last page, a one-page book reports nothing). 11 examples; with the page step-back, the odd-step check and the PDF unit check removed, 3 fail. (5) Memberships: `subscription_data` (`ended_at` is `deactivated_at`; the reason is `failed_payment`, `fixed_subscription_period_ended` or `cancelled`), the true original purchase's review for a renewal, and no `file_data` when `Subscription#alive?` (pending cancellation counts as alive) is false; the money check keeps only USD and a price, since none of these fields print a recurrence. A live membership with a url redirect still hands back: every real tier is a purchased variant, and the file list hands back variants, rich content and stamped PDFs (`aliveProductFiles` is shared with the download pages, so it was not widened for the mobile list). Archive, unarchive and delete of a membership purchase hand back before the write: `save!` revalidates the row and a subscription purchase fails with "We couldn't charge your card" in a spec, so the only recording is that 422 (`hands an archive of a membership purchase to Rails`). Mutation: the review holder, the failed-payment reason and the write hand-back reverted fail 3 examples. Known and unrelated: `archives an older purchase of a seller in another time zone` (3 days ago in Tokyo) fails when the replay runs on another local date than the recording (jobs[1].args[1] is a date), and `nextjs/src/server/controllers/mobile-purchases.test.ts` fails to load at the base commit too (a `vi.mock` of `page-props/signed-in/presenters.ts` without `signedInPresenters`). Not done in this lane: sales show (`SalesController#show`: `CustomerPresenter`, charges, emails), variants, rich content and stamped PDFs in the mobile file list, the 400 for a listing page past the last, memberships in the MCP `create_draft_product`, permalink changes in `update_product`, and a Ruby golden for the mobile serializers (the Rails-recorded examples are the proof; `mobile-serializers.test.ts` pins the key order of the new branches).

WS-13 audience 2 (`nextjs/ws13-audience-2`, lane 29: `gumroad_test_29`, `gumroad_replay_29`, container `gr-ruby-c29e`, Redis 17-20 recording and 22 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c29e`; the replay also needs empty `audience_members-c29e` and `purchases-c29e` indices, which the recorder does not create). Three families, each recorded first and replayed after.
- Comments (`comments_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb`, 29 examples). A root comment by a reader enqueues `CommentMailer.notify_seller_of_new_comment` (recorded with `parity_mail_jobs: true`, compared in `audience-mutations.replay` through `unwrapActiveJob`; a seller who turned comment emails off, and a reply, enqueue nothing and the replay asserts that). A reader who can see the post may comment (`visibleToVisitor` moved to `controllers/comment-post-visibility.ts`, shared with the index). A failed save answers 422 `{success: false, error}` (blank, over 10,000 characters, deeper than 4 levels; a blank edit). Deleting a comment with replies runs `mark_subtree_deleted!`: the replies get `was_alive_before_marking_subtree_deleted` in `json_data`, and the ids come back in the order MySQL's `sort_union` returns for ancestry's `LIKE OR = OR id =` condition (descendants by id, the comment last), so the query keeps that text and has no `ORDER BY`. Still handed back: a `purchase_id` or a visitor who bought the post's product (the policy's purchase clauses), a reader who follows, bought from or is an affiliate of the seller (`visible_posts_for` weighs each post's filters), a signed-out visitor, an unresolvable role.
- Follower workflows (`followers_confirm_link_spec.rb`, `followers_create_parity_spec.rb`). `Follower#confirm!` now schedules `SendWorkflowInstallmentWorker.perform_at(confirmed_at + delay, installment, rule version, nil, follower id, nil, nil, confirmed_at.iso8601)` for each alive installment with a rule of every alive follower or audience workflow with the new-customer trigger (`models/follower.ts`); the pushes are computed before the save, so a rule without a delay (Rails raises after the save) hands back ahead of the write. Examples: three on the confirm link (follower, audience and deleted workflows; a member-cancellation trigger; an already confirmed follow) and one on `POST /follow` with a verified session.
- Workflow emails save (`workflows_emails_update_spec.rb`, 19 examples; `audience-next.replay`, 51 tests pass). `PATCH /workflows/:id/emails` runs `Workflow::SaveInstallmentsService` with `replace_all` (`models/workflow-save-installments.ts`): missing emails (the "Installments data is required" alert), the lock and the `send_to_past_customers` update of a never published workflow, deleting the emails left out (rule version advance, email and rule soft delete), adding and updating emails (`inherit_workflow_info`, `SaveFilesService` with no files, the rule save and, for a published email whose delay changed under the `save` action, the `WorkflowInstallmentScheduleIntent`), publish and unpublish, and the validation failures (blank message, name over 255 characters, the whole save rolled back when a later email is invalid). The Inertia errors are the messages grouped by attribute. Handed back before any write: an abandoned cart workflow, an email with files, a preview email (any `send_preview_email` but nil and false), a message holding an upsell card, a delay unit other than hour, day, week and month, and any parameter shape Rails' `permit` would filter differently.
- Mutation checks, each failing the replay: dropping the reply tag (`whole-subtree` case), pushing one workflow job fewer (confirm link and `POST /follow` cases), skipping the delete of removed emails and the schedule intent (`deletes-the-emails-left-out`, `reschedules`).
- Not done, with the reason: the HTML answer of `GET /follow/:id/cancel` (renders after the write), the captcha, waitlist and cutover follows (no recording of Google's verification; the cutover barrier is not ported), `POST /posts/:id/send_for_purchase/:purchase_id` sending (`PostEmailApi` and its `Rails.cache` limit need a recorded stand-in for the mail provider), the abandoned cart and file/preview workflow saves above, and the two throttling examples of `comments_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb`, which fail to re-record in this container (the Rack::Attack store answered 200 instead of 429), so their committed fixtures are the earlier agent's. `embed-form-hands-a-form-post` in `audience-mutations.replay` parses the form-encoded body of the handed-back request as JSON (the test, not these changes; the example is untouched and was not run on a clean checkout).

WS-18 affiliates 2 (`nextjs/ws18-affiliates-2`, lane 29: `gumroad_test_29`, `gumroad_replay_29`, container `gr-ruby-c29d`, Redis 17-20 recording and 22 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c29d`). `POST /affiliates` that saves runs in Node (`models/direct-affiliate-create.ts`, `affiliates-mutations.ts`; flag `nextjs_affiliates_guards`) for one plain product: inside one transaction the product's row lock, the DirectAffiliate (`send_posts` on), the assignment (`affiliates_links`) with the transaction's workflow schedule token, the seller's audience member (inserted, or an existing follower or customer row updated with the new `affiliates` entry and its recomputed columns), then at commit the `AffiliateMailer.direct_affiliate_invitation(id, nil)` mail three seconds out, the audience member's Elasticsearch job and `ScheduleAffiliateWorkflowJobsJob` (after-commit-everywhere, which claims the token with a new `<unix time>:<uuid>`), and the 303 to `/affiliates`. Seven served examples and seven `hands ...` examples in `affiliates_create_parity_spec.rb` (`parity_timeline`, `parity_mail_jobs`) replay in `affiliates-create.replay` (16 tests); with the assignment's fee, the schedule-job guard and the mail arguments changed in Node seven fail. The recorded timeline pins Rails' order (mail, Elasticsearch, token claim, schedule job); the replay sorts jobs because Node's stores keep no common order, so it does not prove the push order. Handed back before any write: all products (the presenter decides the list), more than one enabled product, any destination URL (an empty string is stored as sent), a fee outside 1%..75%, a collab or deleted product, a merchant account in Brazil on either side (checked by country alone, so a Stripe account Rails would not treat as Brazilian also hands back), an email that is not an audience member's, and any live direct affiliation of the account (uniqueness; an affiliate whose products were all removed still holds it). The old "hands a valid affiliate to Rails" guard example is gone. Replays compare the schedule token and the ISO time inside `audience_members.details` by shape, since both change every run; an existing audience member's follower-derived columns (`min_created_at`, `max_created_at`, `follower_created_at`) are not compared, because the seed holds the follower's time as a replay-relative column and as a literal inside `details`. `rubyToI` (fee percentages) is pinned by `parity/golden/ruby-to-i.rb`. Not modelled: the `RecordNotUnique` retry and `LockWaitTimeout` branches of the audience member save (a concurrent insert raises in Node and rolls back, where Rails retries). `affiliates-destroy.replay` failed intermittently when its UPDATE landed in the second of the seed (`updated_at` then does not move, which Rails' recording never shows): the affiliates replays now wait 1.1 s after seeding (`beforeRequest`). Two lanes shared `gumroad_replay_29` for a while and corrupted each other's seeds, so this lane replays against `gumroad_replay_29d`.

WS-18 affiliates 2, second slice: `PATCH /affiliates/:id` that saves runs in Node (`models/direct-affiliate-update.ts`) for an edit that keeps the affiliate, its account and its one product and only moves fees: the product's row lock (only when the assignment changed), the affiliate's UPDATE, the assignment's UPDATE, and `AffiliateMailer.notify_direct_affiliate_of_updated_products(id)` on the `default` queue when the product's fee moved (an assignment with no fee counts as moved), then the 303 to `/affiliates`. An edit that changes nothing writes nothing. Eight served and six `hands ...`/refusal examples in `affiliates_update_parity_spec.rb` replay in `affiliates-update.replay` (16 tests); with the mail decision and the affiliate fee changed in Node five fail. Handed back before any write: another account, an added, removed or switched product, all products, any destination URL on the request or the stored rows, two live affiliations of the account, and the shapes `creationBlocker` refuses. The old "hands an edit that keeps the affiliate's own email" guard example is gone (`affiliates-guards.replay` 25 tests pass). Rails answers a failed PATCH with 303 (Inertia rewrites the 302), not 302. Still handed back in E16 and not started in this lane: `subscribe_posts`/`unsubscribe_posts` (an ERB page with the application layout, which Node has no shell for), collaborators, wishlists, comments, reviews and review responses.

WS-21/WS-22 processors, second pass (`nextjs/ws22-processors-2`, lane 32: `gumroad_test_32`, `gumroad_replay_32`, container `gr-ruby-c32a`, Redis 35, 54, 55, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c32a`; nothing reached a live service: the goldens build models in memory or run the real worker with WebMock answering HTTP, and no case recorded a request).
- E04 slice 1, the payload builders as pure Node functions (`models/stripe-account-payloads.ts`). `accountHash`, `personHash`, `companyHash` and `bankAccountHash` take rows and the Strongbox plaintexts and return the Stripe parameter hash with Ruby's key order (the replay compares JSON text). Golden `scripts/golden/stripe-account-payloads.rb` builds the models in memory (nothing saved; the seller's `alive_user_compliance_info` is the only stub) and records inputs as the database holds them, ciphertexts included, so the test also decrypts with the Node Strongbox: 19 `account_hash`, 30 `person_hash`, 20 `company_hash` and 24 `bank_account_hash` cases (US SSN last four, nine-digit and foreign ids, Japanese kanji/kana with and without city, nationality, NUL and non-breaking-space edges of `strip`, Luxembourg and Dutch postal codes, US/Canadian/Emirati/Indian/Japanese company shapes, the cross-border service agreement, JP/VN/ID/EC holder names, Chile/Colombia account types, Gibraltar routing). The cross-border country list and the prefecture kana table are generated into `stripe-account-payload-data.generated.json` by the same script. 13 mutations of the builders, all killed (the Ecuador-company branch has two cases but was not mutated). A Ruby finding: `bank_account_hash` writes `bank_account_id` into the caller's metadata Hash (`Hash#to_h` returns the receiver), harmless in production where Stripe passes a StripeObject; Node copies, and metadata that already holds the key throws NotPortedError.
- Not done in E04, and why. `UpdateUserComplianceInfo` (690 lines) and `UpdatePayoutMethod` (356) were not ported: they write compliance, bank account and request rows and enqueue jobs, and no recording or golden of them exists yet, so no payload builder has a caller. Bank account classes other than `AchAccount`, `CanadianBankAccount`, `UkBankAccount` and `AustralianBankAccount` (about 100 more, each with its own routing number, country, currency and IBAN/SEPA rules, El Salvador's IBAN builder, `CardBankAccount`'s Stripe token call) throw NotPortedError. The Connect callback and the account-creation route stay handed back whole: the builders are proven in isolation, but the path from the first write to a failure after the account was created (`cleanup_failed_merchant_account`, `Stripe::Account.delete`, the person, guardian and Singapore alias branches, `update_account`) still has no recording, and the one trigger found last pass raises a NoMethodError that leaves no response. A port that built the payload and then failed after `POST /v1/accounts` would leave a Stripe account Rails would have cleaned up, so no part of the route is served. Existing `legalEntityName` in `models/user-compliance-info.ts` strips with JS `\s`, which also removes non-breaking spaces Ruby's `strip` keeps; the new module has its own strip and the old function was left alone.
- E05, `HandleStripeEventWorker` (`models/stripe-account-events.ts`, golden `stripe-account-events.golden.json` from `scripts/golden/stripe-account-events.rb`, 30 cases replayed in `stripe-account-events.test.ts`, the lock cases need `JOBS_REDIS_URL`). Served for a connected account: `capability.updated` when the alive, processor-alive account (last by id) is not Japanese or does not exist, and `account.updated` whose object is a `standard` account. Both enqueue `RefreshMerchantAccountPaymentMethodAvailabilityWorker` (low, `until_executed`) for a Stripe Connect account; `account.updated` then clears `settlement_currency_mismatch_noticed_at` and `_map` (one transaction, the lock-and-merge of `json_data`, the user read of the validation) when `previous_attributes` names `default_currency` or `external_accounts` and a marker is set; Ruby rescues every error there, and the one reachable failure, a validation error from two alive Gumroad-managed accounts sharing a Stripe id, is swallowed in Node too (recorded, no write). Handed back before any statement or job: a non-standard account type (it reads compliance state and sends mail), a Japanese capability (it retrieves the Stripe account), an event whose `data.object` has another shape, a non-String account id, non-Hash `previous_attributes`, and the `capability.updated.x` / `account.updated.x` variants (Rails treats them as no-ops, but no case records it). 9 mutations of the model (Japan guard, previous_attributes check, Connect-only enqueue, swallowing only RecordInvalid, the standard-type guard, enqueue-before-clear order, marker blank check, capability account source): all killed; the first run of the swallowed-validation mutation survived because the golden case seeded the markers on the wrong account, so the seed was fixed and the case now records the `ROLLBACK` Rails sends.
- Not done in E05: `payout.*`, `charge.*`, refunds, `payment_intent.*`, `radar.*`, `capital.*`, `checkout.session`, `customer.subscription.*` (all call the charge or payout processors, ledger writes or Stripe reads, and no golden was started). `HandlePaypalEventWorker` was not started: the legacy IPN path posts to PayPal for verification before anything is decided (a recorded request body and a Node PayPal call would be needed even for the branches that write nothing), and the order and merchant-account event paths are PayPal REST handlers with ledger writes. E06 was not started.

- ws-payments-10 (lane 23: `gumroad_test_23`, `gumroad_replay_23`, Redis 41-45, container `gr-ruby-c23q`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c23q`). The instant payout path runs in Node: `User#instant_payouts_supported?` (`models/instant-payouts-supported.ts`), `instantly_payable_unpaid_balances` (`instant-payable-balances.ts`), `InstantPayoutsService#perform` (`instant-payouts-service.ts`) and the `POST /instant_payouts` handler (`createInstantPayout`, `nextjs_payouts_guards`), each against a Ruby golden (`scripts/golden/payouts-instant-*.rb`, Stripe through WebMock only) with Stripe call order and idempotency keys asserted. `money-hand-backs.replay.test.ts` replays the success recording and the 17 failure recordings (all re-recorded as "serves" examples, with the Sidekiq ActiveJob adapter so the mail jobs are recorded) through the handler: 78 examples pass. Replay-only differences, not port gaps: the harness compares the stamps of rows an instant payout writes loosely (the Stripe gem sleeps between retries and the recorder's setup lands on either side of the 5s `<now>` window) and the time inside a failure comment, and ignores an UPDATE that only moves `updated_at` to `<now>`; the order of writes against Stripe calls is still compared by the timeline. The stand-in now writes WebMock's form of a URL (no bare `?`, `expand[0]` as `expand%5B%5D`) and resets a dropped connection instead of closing it, so the gem reports "Connection reset by peer" as it did in Ruby. Real bug found by the replay: a BigInt user id assigned to an Int foreign key (`Credit#user_id`) reached Prisma; the model runtime now writes an Int column as a number.
- Still handed back to Rails whole, before any write or Stripe POST (`NotPortedError`/`HandBackToRails`): a date that is not a YYYY-MM-DD day; a request that is not HTML when the policy refuses; Connect routing, a retired or foreign-currency Stripe-held balance, a PayPal-held balance and a bank account Node cannot name; balances that share a `created_at` (Ruby's `sort_by` is not stable); a payout above $9,999 that Rails splits into several batches. Not covered by a recording: the branch where `Payouts.create_payments` returns a payment with errors (`outcomes.present?`), which the golden and unit tests cover but no request fixture exercises. After the first payment exists nothing throws: an error is reported and the seller is told "Failed to process instant payout".

WS-14 Devise and admin 1 (`nextjs/ws14-devise-admin-1`, lane 15: `gumroad_test_15`, `gumroad_replay_15`, Redis 56-59 recording and 63 replay, container `gr-ruby-c15x`, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c15x`; no external service was reached, DNS and the breach-password check are stubs in Rails and absent in the replays).

- Devise pages. The only Devise view the app does not override is the gem's resend-confirmation form: sign in, sign up and forgot password are Inertia pages already served from Node, and `User` is not `:lockable`, so there is no unlock page. `GET /users/confirmation/new` (`nextjs_auth_confirmation_new`, `controllers/devise-pages.ts`) is rendered byte for byte against 8 examples (`devise_confirmation_new_parity_spec.rb`, `devise-confirmation-new.replay`, 9 tests; with the analytics-first meta order reverted 6 fail). New pieces: `renderApplicationLayout` (the `application` layout with the base Vite entrypoint: `viteTypescriptTag` reproduces ViteRuby's dependency-first modulepreload order and the `Link` header), per-form CSRF tokens (`perFormCsrfToken`, pinned by a Rails-recorded vector in `csrf.test.ts`), and `analyticsFirst` in `applicationMetaTags` (the controller re-declares three before_actions, which moves them behind `set_analytics_meta_tags`). The replay masks the per-form `authenticity_token` values. Handed back: an Inertia visit and a non-HTML format (406 in Rails).
- Custom domains. `POST /custom_domain/verify` with a text `product_id` no longer hands back: a domain DNS does not accept answers "not configured" whatever the model says; one it accepts is validated through `CustomDomains.valid` on the product's alive domain (or a new row owned by the product's seller) and answers the first error (`custom_domain_verifications_product_parity_spec.rb`, 13 examples, `custom-domain-verifications-product.replay`; 5 fail with the model check removed). The replay answers DNS from the same zone the spec stubs on `Resolv::DNS` through `CUSTOM_DOMAIN_DNS_TEST_ZONE` (read only when `RAILS_ENV=test`). A `product_id` that is not text still hands back. Not done: a domain with a port (the test `ROOT_DOMAIN`) parses differently in `parseDomain` than in PublicSuffix; production domains carry no port.
- Brand accounts. `POST /sellers/brand_accounts` creates the account in Node (`brand-accounts.ts`): `User` with a random password, TOS agreement, owner membership for the creator if missing, admin membership on the new account, confirmation mail, `current_seller_id` cookie and the flash notice; validation failures answer the first message with nothing written. 20 examples in `brand_accounts_parity_spec.rb` replay in `rest-sweep-2.replay` (the mail, the bcrypt hash and the decrypted cookie are checked; with the role changed 7 more fail). `use_existing_payout_setup` copies currency and PayPal address; an alive compliance info or bank account on the creator hands back whole before any write (the copies and `CreateStripeMerchantAccountWorker` are not ported). `GenerateSubscribePreviewJob` is dropped from the compared jobs, as in the auth replay (the factories take its unique lock). The `affiliates_export` replays in that file fail on the date (recorded 2026-11-11), unrelated.
- Not done: Flipper UI (`/admin/features`) and Sidekiq web (`/admin/sidekiq`). They are the gems' own Rack apps (vendor HTML over the Flipper and Sidekiq Redis keys, their own CSRF and live counters); no Rails recording of them exists and a port would copy vendor markup without Gumroad behaviour behind it, so both stay handed back to Rails.


E17 license JSON shapes (`nextjs/ws20-purchase-mgmt-1`, lane 16: `gumroad_test_16`/`gumroad_replay_16`, container `gr-ruby-c16q`, Redis 31-34 recording and 33 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c16q`). `licensePurchaseJson` (the `purchase` of `/v2/licenses/{verify,enable,disable,rotate,decrement_uses_count}`, `nextjs_api_v2_licenses`) now serializes a purchase with one variant (`variants` as `"(Tier)"`, `""` for "Untitled", the ping's `{category => tier}` map; a deleted variant too), an offer code (`offer_code` as `{code, displayed_amount_off, name}` from the cached `PurchaseOfferCodeDiscount` when there is one, else the offer code row; fixed amounts through the money gem's own format, percentages as `"N%"`; a deleted code too) and non-file custom fields (a ping key per field, `custom_fields` as `"name: value"` strings, checkbox and terms cast to booleans). Recorded in `licenses_parity_spec.rb` (84 examples; the "hands" examples for variants, offer code and custom fields, and the enable and rotate ones, became serializing examples) and replayed in `api-v2-ws17c.replay` (`WS17C_SPEC=licenses`, 85 tests). Still handed back before any write: several variants, a SKU, a default-SKU variant, a variant without a category, a discount whose offer code is gone, a file custom field, a custom field whose name is an integer or `__proto__` (a JS object orders integer keys first), a membership purchase with custom fields, plus the earlier gift, preorder, affiliate, shipped product and recurring charge shapes. Mutation checks (variant brackets, percent sign, cents format) each failed the replay.

`api-v2-ws17c.replay` ran `maskGeneratedUids` and `reconcilePermalink` without the fixture's session cookie and the per-example ignore list, so every fixture with a session cookie and every rotate failed; both now take the options the replay itself used.

E17 not done in lane 16: `PUT`/`PATCH /purchases/:id` (needs the version 1 `Purchase#as_json` and the callbacks of a save that changes email, name, address or country: audience member refresh for the old and new email, the Elasticsearch update with the mapped fields, the cutover fence for an opted-out buyer, bundle product purchases, a gift's giftee receipt), `cancel_preorder_by_seller`, the receipt page, `subscribe`/`unsubscribe`, and the `purchase-json.ts` v2 shapes (affiliate, offer code, SKU, several variants, tracking URL, transaction URL). Resend receipt, change can_contact, revoke access, search and export were already in Node; refund, charge, confirm and `update_subscription` stay in Rails by the lane rules.

WS-21/WS-23 payments, twelfth pass: the body of `refund_purchase!` and the affiliate reversal (`nextjs/ws-payments-12`, lane 28: `gumroad_test_28`/`gumroad_replay_28`, container `gr-ruby-c28k`, Redis 45-49, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c28k`; no Stripe call exists in any new code: the processor's refund is an input of the body, and the goldens pass a plain object). `PUT /purchases/:id/refund` stays handed back in Rails before any write or Stripe call, and stays so until the whole path is proven, including the Rails 500 after a Stripe 429; nothing was served and no hand-back was weakened.
- Done, each against a Ruby golden of the real classes replayed statement for statement and job for job (arguments included), with mutation checks. (1) `models/refund-affiliate-balance.ts` + `models/affiliate-partial-refund.ts`: `Purchase#decrement_balance_for_refund_or_chargeback!` for a refund: `seller_balance_update_eligible?`, a lone affiliate or collaborator, a collaborator beside a direct affiliate (`decrement_balance_for_collab_and_direct_affiliate!`, `partial_affiliate_reversals`, the direct share and fee formulas), the affiliate's refund `BalanceTransaction`, the credit's `affiliate_credit_refund_balance`, the `AffiliatePartialRefund` row (with its ES job and the direct-credit field names), then the seller's share. `scripts/golden/refund-affiliate-balance.rb`, 13 cases; mutation runs over the credit, reversal and row code left 0 survivors but one equivalent (the credit fee of a full reversal is read and never used, because a full reversal never writes a partial-refund row). The `AffiliateCredit` validation now reads a persisted credit's affiliate with Ruby's column list, as `affiliate.present?` does. (2) Purchase save callbacks a refund trips, found by the golden: `refresh_high_volume_fee_eligibility` after a commit that changed `stripe_refunded` (the seller's reload, `with_lock` and the month's sum; a seller whose month would change is refused) and `schedule_product_search_refresh_after_refund` (`SendToElasticsearchWorker ... is_recommendable`, registered after commit). (3) `models/refund-purchase-body.ts`: `refund_purchase!` from `reload.lock!` to the VAT credit: the presentment check before `BEGIN`, the flags and the effective sums in the order and count Ruby queries them, the three `build_refund` branches (full, partial, partial-full) reading only the sums their branch reads, `refund.status`/`processor_refund_id`/`is_for_fraud`/`gumroad_funded`/`balance_reconciliation_needed`/`note`, `refunds << refund`, `is_refund_chargeback_fee_waived`, the balance decrement, `save!`, `Credit.create_for_refund_fee_retention!` and `Credit.create_for_vat_exclusive_refund!`, and a refund that cannot be built answering false with a commit. `scripts/golden/refund-purchase-body.rb` runs the real method and stops it where it would send the webhook (the raise rolls back), 15 cases; `purchase-reloaded-associations.ts` models that `reload` drops the loaded seller and link, which Ruby re-queries at the first use and in the save's validations. The caller must pass the unported remainder as `tail`, so the body cannot finish a refund by itself. Body mutation run (24 mutants): 18 killed; the gift and bundle hand-backs gained tests afterwards. Still surviving, because no golden case reaches them: the `!skipSellerBalance` guard on the VAT credit, the two VAT hand-backs (a partial refund of a taxed sale, a refund after the VAT was refunded) and the `> 0` against `> 1` edge of the partial branch (a one-cent refund).
- Refused (NotPortedError), before the first statement: a buyer-currency presentment, a gift sender, a subscription, a bundle, any chargeback, PayPal and other processors. After the lock and before the first write: a partial refund of a sale that charged a Gumroad VAT, a sale whose VAT was already refunded. Inside the transaction after writes (rolled back by the caller): a retained fee that Stripe collects after commit (an account Gumroad manages for the seller), a later credit's currency labels other than USD, a direct affiliate rate not stored on the purchase.
- Still not ported, so the refund stays handed back: `derive_presentment_refund_from_flow_of_funds` and `Purchase::PresentmentRefund` (and so every buyer-currency refund), `mark_giftee_purchase_as_refunded`, `subscription.cancel_immediately_if_pending_cancellation!`, `mark_product_purchases_as_refunded!`, the dispute-win reversal and `reverse_excess_amount_from_stripe_transfer`, the after-commit Stripe collection of a retained fee, `send_refunded_notification_webhook`, `CustomerMailer.refund`/`partial_refund` (critical queue), `update_creator_analytics_cache`, `send_to_elasticsearch("index")`, `enqueue_update_sales_related_products_infos_job`, `LowBalanceFraudCheckWorker`, `disable_attached_license_if_fully_refunded!`, `ContactingCreatorMailer.purchase_refunded`, the PayPal and Braintree refunds, combined charges, the Stripe refund call itself, and the Rails 500 after a Stripe 429. The body and the decrement are called from no route, job or handler.

WS-21/WS-23 payments, thirteenth pass: gift, subscription and bundle effects, the presentment derivation and the tail of `refund_purchase!` (`nextjs/ws-payments-13`, lane 28: `gumroad_test_28`/`gumroad_replay_28`, container `gr-ruby-c28l`, Redis 45-49, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c28l`; no Stripe call exists in any new code: the processor's refund is an input, and the goldens pass a plain object). `PUT /purchases/:id/refund` stays handed back in Rails before any write or Stripe call, and stays so until the whole path is proven, including the Rails 500 after a Stripe 429; nothing was served and no hand-back was weakened.
- Done, each against a Ruby golden of the real classes replayed statement for statement and job for job (arguments and delays included). (1) The four surviving body mutants: golden cases for the `!skipSellerBalance` guard on the VAT credit, a VAT-charging partial refund, a sale whose VAT was already refunded (own account, Connect, managed) and the one-cent partial; what they proved is ported (`refund-purchase-body.ts`), and the managed-account Stripe transfer reversal after a VAT-refunded partial stays a hand-back inside the rolled-back transaction. (2) `models/refund-purchase-effects.ts` (`mark_giftee_purchase_as_refunded`, `mark_product_purchases_as_refunded!`), `models/refund-subscription.ts` (`cancel_immediately_if_pending_cancellation!`, the free-trial review exclusion) and `models/refund-held-associations.ts`, wired into `refundPurchaseBodyBang` in Ruby's order. The Purchase save pipeline (`purchase-create.ts`) now serves a subscription purchase's inventory snapshot and counts, `update_same_purchaser_subscription_purchase_documents` and the review stat for a gift sender or a subscription charge (a giftee that is hidden is handed back). (3) `models/refund-presentment.ts`: `Purchase::PresentmentRefund` (`from_presentment_amount`, `result`, `tax_only_result`, the largest-remainder allocation) and `derive_presentment_refund_from_flow_of_funds` including the error on the purchase and the ErrorNotifier report (25 golden cases). (4) `models/refund-purchase-tail.ts`: `send_refunded_notification_webhook`, `CustomerMailer.refund`/`partial_refund`, `update_creator_analytics_cache(force: true)`, `send_to_elasticsearch("index")`, `enqueue_update_sales_related_products_infos_job`, `LowBalanceFraudCheckWorker`, the post-commit `disable_attached_license_if_fully_refunded!` and `refundPurchaseBang`, which runs body, tail and licence and is proven from `reload.lock!` to `COMMIT` and the after-commit jobs against 11 committed goldens.
- Still handed back: the buyer-currency presentment inside the body (the derivation is ported and proven alone; the body's own presentment path, the refund's settled-amount columns and the presentment balance debit have no golden, so `hasPresentment` still refuses before the first statement); a bundle's licences and a fraud refund of a recurring subscription charge (`licenseDisableRefusal`, checked before the first write); a sale with `purchase_url_parameters` (the ping's `url_params`); a subscription charge that just became successful; a gift whose recipient is hidden or that has no recipient purchase; a free trial whose first charge already counts for reviews; a prior refund that overdraws a buyer-currency component; a refund issued by a team member (it skips the fraud check; no golden); any chargeback; PayPal and Braintree; the dispute-win reversal; the retained-fee collection from Stripe after commit and the managed-account transfer reversal; the analytics cache branch for a purchase from another day is ported but has no golden (every case is the seller's today).
- Mutation pass (20 mutants over the new ports): the 5 that survived were branches no golden reaches; four became hand-backs (above). The fifth, skipping a licence that is already disabled (`unless license.disabled?`), survives because it runs after the processor has refunded and so cannot hand back; it has no golden.
- Not ported: the Stripe refund call itself, the Rails 500 after a Stripe 429, combined charges (`Charge#refund_and_save!`), `ContactingCreatorMailer.purchase_refunded`, and the route. Nothing calls `refundPurchaseBang`; a caller must run `licenseDisableRefusal` and the body's refusals before it asks the processor to refund, because the licence step runs after the processor has moved the money.

WS-21/WS-23 payments, fourteenth pass: scheduled Stripe payouts and the ledger-hold renewal (`nextjs/ws-payments-14`, lane 23: `gumroad_test_23`/`gumroad_replay_23`, container `gr-ruby-c23r`, Redis 41-45, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c23r`; Stripe only through WebMock in the goldens and the replay stand-in, nothing live).
- Done, E02 scheduled payouts. `PayoutUsersWorker` (the weekly and, with `payout_type: "instant"`, the daily payout of one seller) and `ProcessPaymentWorker` for a Stripe payment run in Node (`models/payout-users-service.ts`; the workers call it). Golden `scripts/golden/payout-users-service.rb` runs the real worker classes (Stripe through WebMock, `ErrorNotifier` recorded) for 36 cases, replayed statement for statement, Stripe call for call (idempotency keys numbered by first appearance, statements before each call) and job for job in `payout-users-service.test.ts`: weekly payouts for Stripe-held, Gumroad-held and mixed balances, a seller with no compliance info, nothing claimable, a paused seller, a negative ledger, a seller that does not exist, the daily payout (period ends on the last instantly payable balance, nothing instantly payable, fee refused), every payout failure Stripe can answer (funds, requirements, missing bank account, rate limit, unknown, server error, dropped connection with the `payout_outcome_unknown` hold), the reversal of the internal transfer, and the payment-only path (`ProcessPaymentWorker`). A payment error is reported (`ErrorNotifier`) and the first error raised after the batch, as `process_payments` does.
- Real divergence found by the golden: a destination balance short of the ledger names the seller's retired accounts using `merchant_account.user`; for an account that came from `user.merchant_accounts` Rails has that user loaded and sends no `SELECT users`, Node sent one (and got null under replay). `preparePaymentAndSetAmount` now takes `accountFromSeller`, set by both create_payments callers (`instant-payouts-service.ts`, `payout-users-service.ts`); the prepare golden still pins the standalone case.
- Refused before the first write (`NotPortedError`, the job is handed back whole): any processor but Stripe (PayPal payouts), several user ids in one job (a refusal for the second would come after the first was claimed; `enqueue_payments` enqueues one per job), a retried payout, a payout type other than standard or instant, a date that is not a `YYYY-MM-DD` string or a user id that is not an Integer, and everything `Payouts.create_payments` refuses (Connect routing, retired or foreign-currency accounts, a seller outside the United States, the chargeback-rate reserve). `ProcessPaymentWorker` hands a PayPal payment, and a Stripe payment `perform_payment` refuses before the payout request (retired account, Connect, currency mismatch, no bank account), back after reading it. After the claim commits or the payout request may have left nothing is handed back: a refusal then fails the job with an error, because Rails would pay again (`payout-users-service.refusals.test.ts` pins both sides with mutation checks). A cross-border payment, which Rails defers 25 hours with `ProcessPaymentWorker.perform_in`, cannot exist: the claim refuses every country but the United States, and `cross_border_payout?` throws if one is ever returned.
- Done, payout holds: renewing a stale ledger hold note (`Payouts.create_payments`, a negative ledger whose note is `failed`, or `claimed` more than 15 minutes ago) no longer hands back: the note is claimed again, reported once and marked `notified`. Four new cases in `payouts-create-payments.golden.json` (stale claim, fresh claim, failed, notified). The note's paper_trail snapshot lists `commentable_id` and `json_data` first (Rails materialized them), which the port reproduces with `touch`.
- Recording added: `spec/requests/instant_payouts_failures_parity_spec.rb` "when preparation fails the payment" is the branch where `Payouts.create_payments` returns a payment with errors (the destination balance cannot cover the ledger: the payment fails as `insufficient_funds`, no bank payout is requested, the seller is told the payout failed). It is replayed through `POST /instant_payouts` in `money-hand-backs.replay.test.ts`.
- Lint: `policies/instant-payout.ts` declares `create` as a property (the `unbound-method` of `payouts-guards.ts:88`), and the seven errors of `money-hand-backs.replay.test.ts` are fixed. Left, not instant-payout code: the two `unbound-method` on `balancePolicy.index` and the `require-await` of `completeStripeOauth` in `payouts-guards.ts`, and the `no-unnecessary-condition` / `no-dynamic-delete` in `models/runtime/define-model.ts` (generic runtime, older commits).
- Not done, payouts page branches and remaining reads: the payouts page still hands back a seller who could be supported for instant payouts (it needs `BankAccount#supports_instant_payouts?`, a Stripe read inside a page render, with no Rails recording of that page under WebMock), a daily schedule in `nextPayoutForAdmin`, and Stripe-held balances in `eligibleForInstantPayouts`; the `Payouts.is_user_payable` notes (paused, below minimum, guardian, instant-ineligible), `create_payments_for_balances_up_to_date*` (the cohort walks that enqueue the workers) and `PerformPayoutsForUserSliceWorker` were not started and have no golden. `Gumroad-held money with no account to pay into`, a group that sums to nothing, `MultiplePayoutGroupsError`, the chargeback-rate reserve and `retrying` stay refused in `payouts.ts`/`payout-reads.ts`.

WS-10 settings, third slice (ws10-settings-3). (1) `PUT|PATCH /settings/advanced` with a domain that points at Gumroad now runs in Node: the new or stored row is saved `verified` with its failed attempts reset (a verified row stays verified), certificate reset and `GenerateSslCertificate` as for any renamed domain. Rails verifies the domain as typed, before `stripped_fields` runs, so a name padded with spaces is saved unverified. Five examples in `settings_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb`; the replay answers DNS from the zone the spec stubs on `Resolv::DNS` through `CUSTOM_DOMAIN_DNS_TEST_ZONE`. (2) Snippet fields that are a Float or an integer past 2**53 are stored as Rails casts them (`Float#to_s`, digits), and two Float locations key the duplicate-block check by value, apart from the same Integer; a Float or huge-integer snippet `id` still hands back. (3) A parity limit past 2**53 answers the range validation (`must be less than or equal to 100`, or `greater than or equal to 1` below -2**53), and a feature announcements version past 2**53 reads as changed elsewhere.

WS-13 audience 5 (`nextjs/ws13-audience-3`, stacked on `nextjs/ws13-audience-2`; lane c13c: `gumroad_test_c13c`, `gumroad_replay_c13c`, container `gr-ruby-c13c`, Redis 41-44 recording and 45/46 replay, `ES_TEST_INDEX_PREFIX=c13c-` and `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c13c`; nothing touched a live service). A signed-in visitor hands back (the layout then reads the seller's analytics settings, which no recording pins; example `hands a signed-in visitor to Rails`). The replay (`audience-next.replay`, 71 tests pass) masks the CSRF token and compares the rest of the body byte for byte, and `assertNonceMatchesCsp` checks the nonce. The old `hands a customer ...` examples were renamed to `unsubscribes a customer and shows the confirmation` and `subscribes a customer who unsubscribed before again`. Mutation checks, each failing the replay: dropping the footer (2 examples), always writing `Time.current` (1), skipping the save (2), serving a signed-in visitor (1). (2) `POST /posts/:post_id/comments` for a reader that `User#visible_posts_for` lets see the post (`comment-post-visibility.ts`, `relations: true` from `create` only; `GET /posts/:id/comments` still hands these readers back, so the index replay is unchanged). A confirmed, alive follower (`Follower.active`) or a live `DirectAffiliate` sets `display_audience_posts`, so an audience post that is not on the profile is visible to either; a follower-type post needs the follower and an affiliate-type post the affiliate; seller, product, variant and abandoned cart posts stay invisible (they need a purchase); a workflow post or a deleted one is not visible. 13 examples added to `comments_mutations_signed_in_spec.rb` (`audience-mutations.replay`: 157 of 159 pass, the 2 failures are listed below). Mutation checks, each failing the replay: `relations: false` (11 examples), the follower check of an affiliate post (1), the affiliate check of a follower post (1), the default branch returning true (1), ignoring audience filters (1), serving an unconfirmed follower (1), serving a collaborator (1). Still handed back, each with a `hands a ...` example: a reader with a purchase from the seller (`for_visible_posts` and the per-post purchase filters are not ported), an unconfirmed or removed follower, a collaborator or any affiliate row that is not a live direct affiliate, and a follower-type or affiliate-type post with `created_after`, `created_before`, `waitlisted_products` or `affiliate_products` set (`follower_passes_filters` and `affiliate_passes_filters` are not ported; the example with `created_before` recorded a 200 and is replayed as a hand-back). (3) The preview notice of `PATCH /workflows/:workflow_id/emails` ("A preview has been sent to your email." against "Changes saved!") is now proven: the flash sits in the encrypted session cookie, which the comparison skips, so `audience-next.replay` decrypts the session cookie of the recorded and the replayed response with the replay secret and compares the `flash` entries (and asserts the recorded one is not empty for a save). Changing either constant fails 3 and 10 workflow examples. The replays `audience-next` and `audience-mutations` now take `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX` for the `audience_members` index and pass `ES_INDEX_SUFFIX` to the server, where they used to delete and recreate the unsuffixed `audience_members` index. Pre-existing failures in `audience-mutations.replay`, in code and fixtures this lane did not touch: `deletes the whole subtree of a comment and tags its replies` (the id order of the `ancestry LIKE ... OR` query depends on the MySQL plan: `sort_union`) and `hands a form post of the embed form to Rails` (the replay parses the form body as JSON). Still handed back from audience 4: posts and workflow emails with files (`UrlRedirect`) or thumbnails, upsell cards, and the customer branch of `visible_posts_for`.

WS-14 discover 2 (`nextjs/ws14-discover-2`, stacked on `nextjs/ws14-discover-1`; `gumroad_test_d2`, `gumroad_replay_d2`, Redis 20/21/25/26 recording and 38 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-d2`, container `gr-ruby-d2`): `DiscoverCuratedProducts#curated_products` runs in Node when the alive products of the visitor's cart (`Cart.fetch_by`) and the distinct products they purchased number 3 or fewer, because `fetch_recommended_products` then uses every id and the list is a pure function of `cached_sales_related_products_infos` (counts summed, best seller first, higher id first among equals, cut at 40 before the alive, not-archived, excluded-bundle and `rated_as_adult?` filters). It sets `sort=curated` and boosts the products after the first eight, orders the nav by the curated products' root taxonomies, lists `curated_product_ids`, serves the curated strip and passes the curated ids to `RecommendedWishlistsService` (a wishlist product must match the curated ids and the taxonomy together; without a taxonomy a partial match is topped up). Elasticsearch is deterministic here: the curated query is `_score` then `past_year_fee_cents` over `term _id` boosts, which the replay rebuilds from the seeded rows. The examples are in `discover_parity_spec.rb` (`with curated products` and a guest cart) and replay in `discover.replay.test.ts`. Still hands back: 4 or more associated ids (Rails samples half of them at random), a set `recommended_products_associated_product_ids_limit` (the order of the seeds then matters), counts that are not a map of integers, a curated product without a seller (Ruby raises inside the rescue and returns no list), a curated taxonomy with no root ancestor (Rails' `find` raises) and a curated wishlist match of nothing (Rails 500s on `FIELD(id, )`). Not proved: the 10 second `Timeout` around the fetch.

WS-14 discover 3 (`nextjs/ws14-discover-3`, stacked on `nextjs/ws14-discover-2`; `gumroad_test_c14c`, `gumroad_replay_c14c`, Redis 50-53 recording and 54 replay, `PARITY_ES_INDEX_SUFFIX=-c14c`, container `gr-ruby-c14c`, `ES_TEST_INDEX_PREFIX=c14c-`): the product page and the wishlist page with `layout=discover` no longer hand back every visitor with a cart product or purchase. Both send `taxonomies_for_nav` from `DiscoverCuratedProducts#taxonomies_for_nav`, which is the curated list of discover-2 ordered through its root taxonomies, so `curatedTaxonomyIds` (`page-props/discover/curated-products.ts`) feeds `taxonomiesForNav` for both. The product page (`presenters/product-page.ts`) asks it while building the page; the wishlist page resolves it in its filter (before any write) and carries the ids in the action state. A visitor with 4 or more distinct associated ids, or a set associated-ids limit, still hands back whole. `hasCuratedProductSources` and its test are removed (no caller left). Examples: `product_page_parity_spec.rb` (`for a visitor with curated products`: purchase, signed-in cart, guest cart, three summed seeds, no related products, curated products without a category, and two "hands a visitor with ..." examples for 4 ids) and `wishlist_show_page_parity_spec.rb` (`with the discover layout for a visitor with curated products`: purchase, cart, guest cart, no related products, profile layout untouched, 4 ids handed back); replays 122 and 33 tests. Both spec files were re-recorded whole; only the new fixtures were kept (the rest differ by random emails and passwords alone) and the two old "hands a visitor with a cart" fixtures were deleted. Mutation checks: the product page asking `taxonomiesForNav()` without the ids fails 6 replay tests; lifting the 4-id hand-back fails 2 product and 1 wishlist replay tests; the wishlist passing no ids fails 3; reading the guid from the response headers instead of the request fails the guest example. The guest example caught a real bug in the first draft (`chain.headers` is the response). Unmutated discover replay after the stack: 52 of 52 pass. Not proved: the 10 second `Timeout` around the fetch, and the rescue that turns any Rails error into an empty list (the port hands those back or raises).
